# Part IV — LIMITS

**Admissibility Boundaries, Stop, and Non-Capture**

<a id="chapter-41-why-strata-must-bound-itself"></a>

## 41. Why STRATA Must Bound Itself

PMS-STRATA increases the analytical mobility of PMS. PATH makes temporally distributed configurations reconstructible as sequences, paths, and trajectories. SUB permits provisionally compressed occurrences and composites to be opened under finer granularity. RETYPE permits an origin-typed source object to be tested as performing a bounded function in a declared target context. The same source material may therefore enter more than one analytical movement, and the result of one operation may become the source object of a later operation.

That mobility is the methodological contribution of STRATA. It is also the reason STRATA cannot remain adequately governed by operation signatures alone. A method that can move between temporal scopes, granularities, relative levels, composites, and contextual functions must specify not only how movement becomes possible, but when movement ceases to be warranted, when a prior result remains binding, and when a further transformation would reduce rather than improve analytical discrimination.

The governing question of Chapter 41 is therefore:

> Why must a transformation discipline that increases analytical mobility also make its own stopping and failure conditions constitutive?

The answer begins from a distinction that applies to every operation occurrence and every operation chain:

```text
operation available
≠ operation necessary
≠ operation admissible
```

An available operation identifies a technically expressible analytical move. A necessary operation would require a reason why the current reconstruction cannot adequately answer the declared question without that move. An admissible operation must additionally remain within the STRATA Admissibility Band, preserve the relevant source and type relations, declare Loss, respect source and claim ceilings, and leave Stop and Non-Capture reachable. No one of these statuses follows automatically from another.

Chapter 41 establishes the general risk architecture produced by recursive analytical mobility. Sections 41.1–41.4 address the structural risk of STRATA, recursive availability, infinite decomposition, and unlimited composition. Sections 41.5–41.7 extend the risk architecture to arbitrary projection, vertical authority drift, and analytical self-immunization. Sections 41.8–41.9 state why LIMITS is constitutive of all three operations and formulate the governing LIMITS principle. The detailed lower and upper boundaries, continuity tests, Loss architecture, source limits, anti-immunization procedure, Stop, Non-Capture, and integrated audit remain owned by Chapters 44–53.

Chapter 41 does not introduce a fourth operation. It also does not reopen the definitions of `COMPOSE`, `DECOMPOSE`, or `PROJECT_AS`. It explains why their legitimate use already presupposes limits and why those limits must remain visible when operations are repeated or chained.

<a id="41-1-the-structural-risk-of-strata"></a>

### 41.1 The Structural Risk of STRATA

STRATA is valuable because it permits a reconstruction to change analytical position without pretending that one fixed frame, scale, or object form is sufficient for every question. A temporally distributed structure may need to be composed before its historical load becomes visible. A compressed object may need to be decomposed before its internal asymmetries or reproduction conditions can be inspected. A trajectory, composite, or occurrence may need to be projected as a bounded function before its relevance in another context can be tested.

This mobility is not itself a defect. A method that prohibited all change of granularity, level, or function would preserve apparent stability by making many relevant structures inaccessible. STRATA instead makes such changes explicit and recordable.

The risk arises because the same mobility can be used in two very different ways:

```text
transformation as warranted analytical discrimination
≠
transformation as escape from pressure on a claim
```

A warranted transformation changes the analytical position because a declared praxeological question requires a different object relation, resolution, temporal scope, or target function. It states what becomes newly visible, which prior structure remains preserved, which information is compressed or excluded, and which new claim is being tested. An escape transformation changes position after a claim has been challenged but leaves unclear whether the new position answers the challenge, merely replaces the challenged object, or makes the objection harder to formulate.

#### C41-RISK-01 — analytical mobility is productive and risk-bearing

The structural risk of STRATA does not consist in the existence of several operations. It consists in the possibility that repeated access to those operations makes every claim indefinitely movable.

STRATA can move along at least four declared analytical dimensions:

| Dimension | Productive analytical use | Characteristic risk |
|---|---|---|
| temporal scope | reconstructing sequence, path, trajectory, delay, or sedimentation | replacing a challenged local claim with an ever longer history |
| granularity | opening a compressed occurrence or composite | burying a counterexample in unbounded detail |
| composition | forming a relational or temporal composite | absorbing contradiction into an overbroad macro-object |
| contextual function | testing a bounded target function | assigning a new function whenever an earlier projection fails |

These dimensions are not interchangeable. A temporal extension does not by itself answer a granularity objection. A finer reconstruction does not by itself establish a broader composite. A new composite does not establish a target function. A new target function does not repair a failed source claim. Because the dimensions differ, a move along one dimension must not be treated as if it automatically resolved pressure arising on another.

The risk is intensified by the fact that STRATA objects are derived analytical objects rather than fixed natural units. A path is selectively composed. A trajectory adds a sedimentation claim. A decomposition selects distinctions and relations. A projected function depends on a declared context. These objects can be rigorously reconstructed, but none is protected from revision merely by having been formally declared.

```text
explicit transformation
≠ protected transformation

formal record
≠ substantive passage
```

The Shared Transformation Record makes the move inspectable. It does not guarantee that the move is warranted. Likewise, schema-valid Loss fields show that Loss has been declared; they do not establish that the declaration is complete or that the remaining claim stays within the source basis.

A transformation discipline therefore requires two complementary forms of control.

First, each operation has **operation-local limits**. `COMPOSE` must preserve relevant ordering, selection, relations, heterogeneity, and Loss. `DECOMPOSE` must preserve the same reference object, avoid decomposition of operator types, and show praxeological gain rather than detail accumulation. `PROJECT_AS` must preserve origin type, declare target context, carry a constitutive source trace, and remain functionally bounded.

Second, operation chains require **integrated LIMITS**. A chain may be inadmissible even if one of its component operations was locally admissible. A valid decomposition may supply a new source object for a later projection, yet the projection may fail. A valid composition may create a warranted trajectory, yet a later decomposition may no longer preserve the same reference object. A valid local operation can therefore be followed by an invalid next operation or enter an invalid chain relation.

#### C41-LOCALGLOBAL-01 — local limits and integrated LIMITS are distinct and cumulative

```text
local operation passage
≠ later operation passage
≠ chain passage
```

Integrated LIMITS does not weaken or override local operation results. It preserves them and adds a further question: does the relation among the operations remain admissible? A locally failed operation cannot be converted into a passed operation by chain-level reinterpretation. Conversely, a locally passed operation does not compel the chain to continue.

This distinction prevents two opposite errors. The first error is to treat local checks as sufficient for any later reuse. The second is to treat integrated review as permission to readjudicate or erase local results. STRATA permits neither.

```text
integrated audit
≠ retroactive local override
```

The structural risk of STRATA can therefore be stated precisely: analytical mobility makes it possible to generate a new analytical position whenever pressure arises, while admissibility requires every new position to remain answerable to the question, source, Loss, and prior dispositions that made the move necessary. Without that requirement, mobility becomes elasticity without a failure condition.

<a id="41-2-recursive-availability"></a>

### 41.2 Recursive Availability

A STRATA operation usually produces an object that remains analytically available. A composed trajectory may later be decomposed. A decomposed source object may be recomposed under a different selection rule. A composite may later enter `PROJECT_AS`. A projected source object may be decomposed to test whether the proposed function is actually carried by finer source relations. Nothing in the operation signatures requires every result to become terminal.

This property is **recursive availability**:

```text
result of one operation
→ possible source of a later operation
```

Recursive availability is methodologically useful. It permits correction, refinement, source testing, comparison, and controlled operation chains. It is also bounded by a strict non-equivalence:

```text
recursive availability
≠ recursive necessity
```

The existence of a technically possible next operation does not show that the current claim is incomplete, that more structure would be more discriminating, or that continuation would remain within the source basis. A further operation requires its own justification.

#### C41-RECURSIVEAVAIL-01 — every continuation requires fresh praxeological purchase

Before a new operation occurrence enters the chain, it must state which warranted reconstruction is expected to change and why that change matters for the declared praxis question. The expected gain cannot consist merely in producing more text, more nodes, more distinctions, a larger object, or a different label.

A continuation claim must answer at least:

- What current limitation or unresolved pressure motivates the next operation?
- Which exact source object and prior disposition enter the next operation?
- Which new analytical difference is expected?
- Why is that difference praxeologically relevant rather than merely descriptively available?
- Which source support exists for the new move?
- Which Loss is inherited, and which new Loss may arise?
- Which Stop condition applies if the expected difference is not obtained?

This is a fresh PraxisPurchase burden. It does not require the full mechanics of Chapter 44 to be restated here. It establishes the prior-order rule that later chapters will operationalize:

```text
possible continuation
+ no fresh expected praxeological difference
→ no requirement to continue
```

A recursive move is an **operation occurrence**, not a modification of the operation type. `DECOMPOSE` remains the same operation type whether used first or after `PROJECT_AS`. What changes is the source object, claim, context, inherited history, and position in the chain. For this reason, recursive use never creates a new fourth operation or a hybrid operation type.

```text
PROJECT_AS → DECOMPOSE
≠ new operation type

COMPOSE → PROJECT_AS
≠ compound primitive
```

Each arrow identifies a relation between separate operation occurrences. The chain relation must be declared, but the occurrences remain individually testable.

#### C41-CHAINCLAIM-01 — every recursive occurrence remains a separate claim, Record, Loss, and result

A new recursive operation must not be recorded as an amendment that silently changes the meaning of the earlier operation. It requires a separate claim identity and a separate operation record. Its Loss declaration must distinguish inherited Loss from new Loss. Its result must not overwrite the earlier result.

```yaml
recursive_operation_minimum:
  prior_record:
    claim_id: visible
    operation_result: preserved
    loss: inherited_not_erased
  next_record:
    claim_id: new
    operation: separately_declared
    expected_praxeological_difference: required
    loss: separately_declared
    result: separately_selected_if_executed
  chain_relation:
    reason_for_continuation: required
    prior_disposition_retained: true
    authority_inheritance: prohibited
```

This YAML is an illustrative view of existing Shared Transformation Record duties, not a second record grammar. The actual records remain governed by the canonical schema.

The chapter uses one stable synthetic pressure chain to make the recursion problem visible. It is not a case adjudication and introduces no new Case, Test, or Smoke record.

```text
Q0: a separately warranted PROJECT_AS record
    has the preserved disposition failed_transformation

Q1: DECOMPOSE the source trajectory under finer granularity
Q2: COMPOSE the trajectory with adjacent structures
Q3: PROJECT_AS a source object under a different target function or context
```

Q0 is a stipulated prior disposition for method pressure. Chapter 41 does not create its evidence or readjudicate its failure. Q1–Q3 are analytically available moves. None is therefore necessary, and none is adjudicated here.

The decisive relation is:

```text
Q1, Q2, or Q3 available
≠ Q0 answered
≠ Q0 repaired
```

Q1 may reveal finer source structure. That finer structure could later support a different projection claim, undermine the original source typing, or produce no relevant difference. Q2 may form a warranted broader composite. That composite would be a different source object and could support, fail to support, or leave unresolved a new claim. Q3 may propose a legitimate different target function. Its legitimacy would not alter the fact that Q0 failed.

A later success may therefore coexist with an earlier failure:

```text
Q0 = failed_transformation
Q3 = admissible
```

This combination is coherent when Q3 is genuinely a new claim. What is prohibited is the inference:

```text
Q3 admissible
→ Q0 was not failed
```

Recursive availability also includes the option not to continue. If a further operation produces no expected praxeological difference, would exceed the source basis, or would merely make the claim harder to test, continuation may correctly end before a new operation is executed.

#### C41-COUNTERPRESSURE-01 — more analysis can be unnecessary or less discriminating

Chapter 41 must preserve four counterpressures against automatic continuation:

1. A technically possible operation may be unnecessary.
2. A finer or broader reconstruction may reduce discrimination by adding unsupported or irrelevant structure.
3. A locally admissible operation may enter an inadmissible chain.
4. The correct result may be Stop or Non-Capture rather than another transformation.

These possibilities are not failures of ambition. They are the conditions under which recursive availability remains a controlled methodological resource rather than an obligation to continue indefinitely.

<a id="41-3-the-risk-of-infinite-decomposition"></a>

### 41.3 The Risk of Infinite Decomposition

`DECOMPOSE` permits a provisionally compressed occurrence or composite to be reconstructed at finer granularity. It can reveal internal relations, distributed roles, temporal substructure, asymmetry, repeated reproduction practices, contradiction, and source conditions that the coarser object did not display. It can therefore correct false homogeneity and improve the calibration of later claims.

The risk begins when the ability to introduce a finer distinction is treated as sufficient reason to introduce it.

```text
finer distinction available
≠ finer distinction praxeologically warranted
```

No analytical object is absolutely indivisible within STRATA. Provisional elementarity means only that an object is not further opened for the current claim. This non-ontological position prevents STRATA from declaring final constituents. It does not require endless decomposition.

#### C41-INFINITEDECOMP-01 — infinite decomposition risk is a joint relevance, source, identity, and discrimination failure

Infinite-decomposition risk appears when the decomposition process can always continue formally while the warrant for continuation progressively weakens. Five pressures are central.

#### Declining praxeological relevance

A finer distinction may be accurate at the descriptive level and still make no difference to the warranted praxis reconstruction. It may leave action corridors, costs, roles, asymmetries, expectations, timing, binding, and relevant alternatives unchanged. When this occurs, greater detail does not amount to a further praxis finding.

```text
more distinctions
+ same warranted reconstruction
→ resolution without additional praxeological purchase
```

This risk is not confined to trivial details. A technically sophisticated distinction can remain below the Relevance Floor if it changes no claim-relevant relation.

#### Shrinking source support

Finer granularity usually requires denser source support. Coarse records may establish that an occurrence or composite existed while leaving its internal sequence, role distribution, or reproduction mechanism uncertain. A decomposition that fills these gaps by analytical expectation rather than source evidence increases apparent structure while moving beyond the Source Ceiling.

```text
fine-grained model detail
≠ fine-grained source support
```

The danger is especially strong when a plausible formal relation is treated as evidence that the relation occurred. The PMS grammar may make a candidate relation legible; it does not supply the missing record.

#### Loss of the reference object

`DECOMPOSE` must remain a finer reconstruction of the same reference object. If the operation gradually shifts from the original occurrence or composite to loosely related background structures, the decomposition no longer answers the initial question.

A trajectory may be opened into transitions, Non-Events, roles, and branch points. It may not be replaced by the entire institutional history surrounding it merely because that history contains more explanatory material. At some point the source object can become only a nominal anchor for a different inquiry.

```text
same label retained
≠ same reference object preserved
```

The risk of infinite decomposition therefore includes **reference dilution**: each additional layer remains rhetorically connected to the source, but the constitutive relation becomes weaker until no clear criterion shows what still belongs to the decomposition.

#### Immunization through microcomplexity

A challenged coarse claim can be protected by adding so many microdistinctions that no counterexample appears decisive. Every objection is localized to one subcomponent, every contrary case is redescribed as a different microconfiguration, and the higher claim remains unchanged regardless of the finer findings.

This is not successful decomposition. A valid decomposition must be capable of confirming, revising, reducing, differentiating, or rejecting the source function. If the source claim is invariant under every finer result, the decomposition has ceased to test it.

```text
finer reconstruction always possible
+ source claim never vulnerable
→ microcomplexity as immunization pressure
```

#### False epistemic privilege of the fine-grained view

Finer resolution may reveal structure that a coarser reconstruction compresses. It may also obscure larger relations, overstate uncertain detail, or fragment a function that is visible only at another granularity. STRATA therefore rejects the inference that finer is automatically truer, more fundamental, or more authoritative.

```text
finer granularity
≠ deeper reality
≠ higher truth
≠ greater authority
```

Multiple granularities can be valid for different claims. The relevant comparison concerns discriminative performance, source support, and Loss, not rank.

These pressures can interact. A decomposition may add detail below the Relevance Floor, rely on weaker sources, dilute the reference object, and nevertheless be presented as epistemically superior because it is finer. The combined result is not merely inefficiency. It can make a claim less testable than before.

A bounded decomposition therefore requires a stopping question at every recursive step:

```text
Does the next distinction change a warranted reconstruction
of the same praxis-relevant source object?
```

A negative or underdetermined answer does not always mean that the existing coarse claim is true. It can support different outcomes:

- the coarse claim remains adequate for the current purpose;
- the decomposition is `resolution_neutral`;
- the source basis is insufficient and the claim must be reduced;
- continuation reaches `mandatory_stop`;
- the relevant internal structure remains under `non_capture`.

The exact mapping belongs to later boundary and Stop chapters. The general point is established here: the inability to justify another distinction is not a methodological embarrassment to be overcome by more decomposition. It is information about the limit of the current reconstruction.

The Q0–Q3 pressure chain makes this visible. Q1 proposes a finer reconstruction after a failed projection. Q1 may be independently admissible if it addresses a valid source question. It may reveal why Q0 failed or provide material for a different future claim. But Q1 becomes inadmissible as a repair strategy when its only function is to preserve the desired projected label by moving the disagreement into ever finer source details.

```text
Q1 reveals warranted internal structure
≠ Q0 repaired

Q1 adds microcomplexity without changed source claim
→ no answer to Q0
```

The risk of infinite decomposition is therefore not infinite analytical possibility in the abstract. It is the loss of a warranted stopping relation among source, question, granularity, and claim.

<a id="41-4-the-risk-of-unlimited-composition"></a>

### 41.4 The Risk of Unlimited Composition

`COMPOSE` forms a new analytical object from multiple source structures connected by declared temporal, structural, or relational ordering. It permits STRATA to reconstruct paths, trajectories, relational composites, distributed structures, and other higher-granularity objects that are not visible when each source occurrence is treated in isolation.

Composition is indispensable where the praxis-relevant structure lies in sequence, relation, accumulation, or distributed load. It is not a licence to treat any sufficiently large collection as a coherent object.

```text
more source material
≠ stronger composite
```

The risk of unlimited composition appears when larger scope is treated as self-justifying. Additional events, occurrences, paths, roles, contexts, or time periods can always be added. Without an upper bound, every objection to a composite can be answered by enlarging it until the objection is absorbed as one local variation inside an increasingly elastic macro-object.

#### C41-UNLIMITEDCOMP-01 — unlimited composition risk concerns heterogeneity, contradiction, relation trace, macrofunction inflation, and Loss

Five pressures define the risk.

#### Heterogeneity without a constitutive relation

A valid composite is not a container for everything that is historically adjacent, thematically similar, or jointly describable. Its source objects must stand in relations that are constitutive of the target object.

A long time span, a shared institutional setting, or a recurring label may justify collecting material for investigation. It does not yet establish one path, trajectory, or higher-level composite.

```text
co-presence in one archive
≠ compositional relation
```

When heterogeneity is admitted without a clear formation rule, the composite can survive arbitrary source changes. Such invariance is a warning that the object is carried by the analyst’s label rather than by the source relation.

#### Homogenization of contradiction

A broader composition can hide incompatible subpaths, reversals, role-specific costs, opposing attractors, or unresolved Non-Events. The resulting macro-description may appear coherent only because internal contradiction has been compressed out of view.

Composition is allowed to compress. It is not allowed to convert contradiction into agreement merely by moving to a larger scale.

```text
contradictory source paths
+ one macro-label
≠ integrated composite automatically
```

Some contradictions may themselves be constitutive of the composite. In that case they must remain visible as internal structure rather than be treated as noise. Other contradictions may show that the proposed composite boundary is invalid. The operation must remain vulnerable to both possibilities.

#### Loss of relation trace

As more source objects are added, it becomes harder to reconstruct which relations actually carry the composite. A target object may retain a persuasive summary while its selection rule, ordering rule, and constitutive relation map become unclear.

The Traceability Ceiling is approached when material source changes no longer alter the composite label. A composition that remains identical after removal of alleged turning points, reversal of temporal order, substitution of role relations, or exclusion of central Non-Events lacks adequate source sensitivity.

```text
source set grows
+ relation trace weakens
→ abstraction pressure increases
```

The exact upper-bound test belongs to Chapter 45. Chapter 41 establishes why the risk arises directly from unbounded scope.

#### Macrofunction inflation

`COMPOSE` creates a composite analytical object. It does not automatically assign that object a higher-level function. A trajectory is not thereby a Frame-function. A distributed composite is not thereby a higher-level Asymmetry-function. A recurrent form is not thereby an Attractor-function.

```text
larger composite formed
≠ macrofunction established
```

Macrofunction inflation occurs when the apparent importance, duration, or breadth of a composite is treated as sufficient evidence for `PROJECT_AS`. This collapses the operation boundary between composition and projection.

The risk also appears when a macro-label borrows the semantic force of a PMS operator without a warranted occurrence-level or relation-level trace. The presence of many local asymmetries, for example, does not establish one higher-level Asymmetry-function unless the relational composite and the separate projected function are each warranted.

#### Concealed or irreversible Loss

Every composition selects, orders, compresses, excludes, and leaves uncertainty. Larger composites can make this Loss harder to inspect because local omissions disappear inside a compelling overview.

The canonical Loss remains:

```yaml
loss:
  preserved:
  compressed:
  excluded:
  uncertain:
  irrecoverable:
```

A later enlargement may add source material, but it cannot retroactively restore an `irrecoverable` relation as if it had been preserved in the earlier record. Nor may a larger composite silently absorb excluded counterpaths and then present them as supporting material without a revised source declaration.

```text
broader source boundary
≠ prior Loss erased
```

Unlimited composition risk is therefore not simply the danger of making an object too large. It is the danger that scale substitutes for formation, that heterogeneity substitutes for relation, and that breadth substitutes for traceable load.

The Q0–Q3 pressure chain again provides the minimal pressure form. Q2 proposes to compose the failed-projection source trajectory with adjacent structures. This may be a legitimate new `COMPOSE` claim. It could produce a warranted broader path or relational composite. It may also fail because the additional structures lack constitutive relation, introduce unresolved contradiction, or exceed traceability.

Whatever its result, Q2 does not answer Q0 automatically:

```text
Q2 forms valid broader composite
≠ Q0 projection validated
```

If the broader composite later enters a new `PROJECT_AS` claim, that later projection is Q3 or another separately identified claim. It requires a separate target context, function, source trace, Loss, and result. The chain cannot compress these operations into one narrative of progressive improvement.

A larger composite may sometimes improve discrimination. It may reveal accumulated asymmetry, preserve path-dependent sequence, or show that an apparently local event belongs to a longer trajectory. It may also reduce discrimination by making alternative constructions, role-specific costs, and counterevidence less visible. The decision cannot be made from size alone.

The correct stopping point is therefore relational rather than numeric. STRATA does not define one universal maximum number of components, one maximum time span, or one highest legitimate level. Composition stops when the declared target object no longer gains warranted praxeological structure from additional source material, when source trace can no longer carry the abstraction, or when enlargement would conceal rather than clarify decisive Loss and contradiction.

#### C41-WP1-HANDOFF-01 — WP1 hands forward a stable, unadjudicated recursive-pressure chain

Sections 41.1–41.4 establish the following pressure architecture for WP2:

```text
Q0
= stipulated prior failed_transformation disposition
= preserved and not readjudicated

Q1
= possible finer DECOMPOSE claim
= separate Record, Loss, and result required
= unadjudicated

Q2
= possible broader COMPOSE claim
= separate Record, Loss, and result required
= unadjudicated

Q3
= possible new PROJECT_AS claim
= separate Record, Loss, and result required
= unadjudicated
```

WP1 has not selected an operation result for Q1–Q3, a chain result, a route, or a canonical Output Class. It has not applied the detailed tests of Chapters 44–53 and has not repaired the missing RETYPE lock-critical artifacts. It has established only the general requirement that every recursive move remain independently justified, independently recorded, Loss-bearing, and unable to erase prior dispositions.

WP2 receives four settled propositions:

1. Analytical mobility is productive but creates a structural risk of claim relocation.
2. Recursive availability never establishes recursive necessity or admissibility.
3. Infinite decomposition can reduce relevance, source support, reference integrity, and discrimination.
4. Unlimited composition can erase heterogeneity, contradiction, relation trace, and Loss while inflating macrofunction claims.

WP2 will extend this architecture to arbitrary projection, vertical authority drift, and analytical self-immunization. It receives no permission to adjudicate Q0 again, to adjudicate Q1–Q3 without records, or to convert chain length into analytical authority.

<a id="41-5-the-risk-of-arbitrary-projection"></a>

### 41.5 The Risk of Arbitrary Projection

`PROJECT_AS` is the STRATA operation most directly exposed to semantic elasticity. `COMPOSE` and `DECOMPOSE` alter the analytical object or its resolution through declared source relations. `PROJECT_AS` retains the source object's origin type while testing whether that object performs a bounded function in a target context. Because the target function is contextual rather than primitive, the operation can be expressed in deceptively simple language:

```text
X functions as Y
```

That sentence identifies a proposed target function. It does not yet establish a valid projection. Without further constraints, nearly any source object can be redescribed as nearly any function whose vocabulary fits the analyst's preferred interpretation. A trajectory can be called a frame, a repeated configuration can be called an attractor-function, a breakdown can be called an impulse, and a composite can be called a macro-event without showing which source relations carry the function or why the function belongs to the declared target context.

The risk is not merely loose wording. Arbitrary projection permits a target label to replace the transformation burden. Once that occurs, the source object is no longer doing analytical work; it serves only as a prompt for a new description.

#### C41-ARBITRARYPROJ-01 — the syntax `X functions as Y` is insufficient without bounded projection conditions

A warranted `PROJECT_AS` claim requires at least five jointly necessary conditions:

| Condition | Required burden | Failure pressure |
|---|---|---|
| target context | the context in which the function is claimed must be declared and delimited | the same target label follows the source across unrelated contexts |
| constitutive source trace | concrete source occurrences and relations must carry the proposed function | the target function remains after its alleged source carriers are removed |
| Counterfactual Sensitivity | relevant source or target-context changes must alter, qualify, or defeat the function | the projection is indifferent to material variation |
| TypeIntegrity | the origin type remains visible and is not replaced by the target function | contextual function is rewritten as operator identity, object essence, or new primitive |
| bounded validity | temporal, contextual, source, and claim limits must remain explicit | the function becomes permanent, universal, or authority-bearing |

None of these conditions can be replaced by semantic plausibility. A target function may sound appropriate and still lack traceable load. It may fit one descriptive vocabulary and still collapse origin type into target function. It may be supported in one target context and fail in another. It may survive only because the relevant counterfactual has not been tested.

```text
target-label fit
≠ constitutive Source Trace
≠ functional continuity
≠ admissible projection
```

The source trace must identify more than the source object's name. Where PMS-derived structure is material, the trace must retain the relevant operator-typed occurrences and their relations through the existing source-reference, relation-map, constitutive-source-trace, sensitivity, and Loss positions. An abstract operator label does not count as a carrier merely because the target function resembles the operator's name.

For example, a trajectory may be tested as performing a bounded frame-function in a later scene. The projection is not established by observing that the trajectory influences interpretation. The record must show which historically sedimented relations delimit relevance in the target scene, how a relevant change in those relations would change the proposed frame-function, what remains compressed or excluded, and why the trajectory remains a trajectory as origin type.

```text
trajectory
PROJECT_AS
bounded frame-function in context C

≠

trajectory becomes Frame as origin type
```

Arbitrary projection frequently appears in one of four forms.

**Label substitution** replaces a source-sensitive function with a higher-sounding name. The source object could change substantially while the target label remains stable.

**Analogy inflation** begins from a real structural resemblance and silently upgrades that resemblance into functional identity. Similar shape is treated as sufficient source load.

**Context erasure** presents a locally warranted target function as if it travelled with the source object across every later scene.

**Type absorption** treats the target function as a new description of what the source object really is, rather than as a bounded role it performs under declared conditions.

These errors can coexist. A target label can be analogy-driven, context-free, source-insensitive, and type-absorbing at once. The mere presence of a formal `PROJECT_AS` declaration does not separate them.

The Q0–Q3 pressure chain makes the risk explicit. Q3 is a possible new `PROJECT_AS` claim after Q1 and Q2 have proposed finer and broader source reconstructions. Even if Q1 or Q2 were later warranted, Q3 would still require its own target context, source trace, sensitivity, TypeIntegrity, Loss, alternatives, and bounded result.

```text
Q1 or Q2 supplies a warranted source object
≠ Q3 target function warranted
```

A richer source object can improve the projection test. It cannot substitute for the test. If the target function survives every change in source detail, every rival composition, and every target context, that stability may indicate not robustness but arbitrariness.

The inverse is also important. A valid projection need not claim that no rival projection is possible. More than one bounded target function may be admissible for the same source object in different contexts, or even as competing functions within one context. Rival admissibility does not permit unrestricted elasticity; each projection carries its own source and boundary burden.

```text
multiple bounded projections possible
≠ any projection admissible
```

#### C41-UNNECESSARYOP-01 — Optional Stop remains available where projection adds no praxeological purchase

A further projection can be technically available and still be unnecessary. If the source object's existing type and reconstruction already answer the declared question, adding a target function may contribute only a new label. The correct disposition is then not to force `PROJECT_AS` until it fails, but to stop because no additional praxeological purchase has been established.

This is an **Optional Stop** in the methodological sense established by the minified kernel: continuation is not prohibited because it is already invalid; continuation is unnecessary because the additional operation has no warranted analytical task.

```text
projection expressible
+ no additional functional discrimination
+ no changed praxis reconstruction
→ Optional Stop
```

Optional Stop is not an eleventh Output Class. The underlying claim may remain `admissible`, `admissible_with_bounded_claim`, or another already selected canonical result while the chain is intentionally not extended. Nor does Optional Stop prevent a later projection if a new target context or question supplies fresh praxeological purchase. It prevents availability from being treated as an obligation to functionalize.

The general projection risk can therefore be stated precisely:

> `PROJECT_AS` becomes arbitrary when the target function is selected more freely than the source relations, context, sensitivity, type, and validity boundary can constrain it.

The detailed upper-boundary, continuity, anti-immunization, Stop, and Non-Capture procedures remain assigned to Chapters 45, 47, and 50–52. Chapter 41 establishes the risk architecture: a transformation grammar capable of contextual functionalization must preserve the possibility that no projection, or no further projection, is warranted.

<a id="41-6-vertical-authority-drift"></a>

### 41.6 Vertical Authority Drift

STRATA uses directional language because its operations change analytical position. `DECOMPOSE` often moves toward finer granularity. `COMPOSE` often forms a relatively broader or higher composite. `PROJECT_AS` moves from an origin-typed source object to a target function in another context. These movements are analytical relations, not rankings.

Vertical authority drift occurs when the direction or number of transformations is treated as evidence that the resulting claim possesses greater truth, validity, legitimacy, or application force than its source warrant supports.

The drift can move in either direction. A finer reconstruction may be presented as deeper and therefore truer. A broader composite may be presented as more comprehensive and therefore more authoritative. A projected function may be presented as a higher-order explanation and therefore as entitled to govern lower-level interpretations. A longer operation chain may be presented as more rigorous merely because it contains more transformations.

#### C41-AUTHORITYDRIFT-01 — analytical movement creates no truth, rank, legitimacy, recommendation, sanction, or authority gain

STRATA rejects the following equations:

```text
finer granularity
≠ deeper truth

broader composite
≠ more comprehensive authority

higher relative level
≠ higher validity

bounded target function
≠ origin-type superiority

more transformations
≠ stronger claim
```

The claim ceiling remains determined by the source basis, transformation passage, declared context, and available alternatives. Analytical movement can change what is visible and what kind of claim can be tested. It cannot create an authority surplus.

A useful distinction is required between several forms of authority that are often compressed into one word:

| Authority form | What would be required | What STRATA alone cannot supply |
|---|---|---|
| epistemic warrant | adequate sources and a passed claim-specific transformation | truth beyond the declared source and claim ceiling |
| semantic validity | context-sensitive interpretation and rival-sensitive analysis | final meaning or exclusive interpretation |
| normative legitimacy | an independently warranted normative framework | moral rank, obligation, permission, or blame |
| application authority | domain-specific competence, mandate, evidence, and safeguards | recommendation, intervention, diagnosis, or binding decision |
| governance or sanction authority | legitimate institutional authorization and due process | punishment, exclusion, irreversible labeling, or coercion |

A STRATA result may be structurally admissible while carrying none of the latter forms. Even a strongly supported reconstruction remains a bounded analytical claim. It does not become a mandate.

```text
admissible transformation
≠ authorized application
```

This boundary is especially important where a projected function is expressed in PMS vocabulary. A source object that performs a bounded frame-function, attractor-function, asymmetry-function, or modulating function does not thereby become a superior operator instance, a more real layer, or a basis for person typing. The function belongs to a declared transformation context. It does not inherit the authority of PMS Base, and PMS Base does not inherit warrant from the application.

The same non-inheritance applies to technical and repository layers:

```text
schema validity
≠ substantive validity

formal-model registration
≠ empirical warrant

case readability
≠ proof

Reader navigation
≠ authority

operation-chain completion
≠ permission to act
```

The formal model can preserve an authority ceiling and reject `authority_inheritance`. It cannot determine that a person, institution, group, policy, or intervention is legitimate. A complete record can show that an authority claim has not been supplied; it cannot manufacture the missing authority.

Vertical authority drift is sometimes hidden inside apparently modest comparative language. A macro-object may be called the more complete account. A finer decomposition may be called the real mechanism. A projected function may be called the decisive level. Such formulations can be warranted only if their comparison class and claim scope are explicit. Even then, comparative performance within one task does not establish global rank.

```text
better discrimination for question Q
≠ universally superior analysis
```

A coarse reconstruction can outperform a fine one when local detail obscures the relevant relation. A narrow path can be more traceable than a broad trajectory. A source-type description can be more adequate than an additional projection. Multiple granularities can remain co-valid because they answer different bounded questions.

Authority drift also arises through cumulative presentation. If Q1, Q2, and Q3 were each separately admissible, the chain would contain several warranted claims. It would not contain a single claim whose authority equals the sum of the operations. Each result retains its own source ceiling, context, Loss, and validity scope.

```text
admissible Q1
+ admissible Q2
+ admissible Q3
≠ unlimited chain authority
```

Nor does later success erase an earlier failure. A valid new source object or target function can coexist with the preserved `failed_transformation` disposition of Q0. The later result may answer a new question; it does not gain authority by rewriting the earlier question.

This is why the governance field remains categorical:

```yaml
governance:
  authority_inheritance: prohibited
```

The prohibition is not an ethical add-on to an otherwise complete method. It is required for type and claim discipline. Without it, analytical level, formal complexity, and repository completion could be converted into a hierarchy of truth and action that the transformations themselves do not warrant.

<a id="41-7-analytical-self-immunization"></a>

### 41.7 Analytical Self-Immunization

A method is self-immunizing when pressure on one of its claims is repeatedly converted into permission to alter the analytical coordinates until the objection no longer has a stable target. The method continues producing reconstructions, but no result can remain failed, reduced, stopped, or uncaptured because every challenge triggers a new frame, granularity, level, composite, projection, or object definition.

STRATA is particularly exposed to this risk because coordinate changes are legitimate parts of its method. The same moves that enable correction can also enable evasion. The relevant distinction is therefore not between changing and refusing to change. It is between a new transformation that answers a declared burden and a new transformation that merely relocates it.

#### C41-SELFIMMUNIZATION-01 — coordinate or object change does not answer an objection unless the burden is carried forward

Analytical self-immunization may take several forms:

- a failed projection is replaced by a finer decomposition, while no account is given of how the finer structure changes the failed target-function claim;
- a counterexample to a local claim is absorbed into a broader composite, making the counterexample appear minor without testing its constitutive relevance;
- a source-insensitive target function is moved to a new context after sensitivity failure, while the original context remains unaddressed;
- a reference objection is answered by redefining the object so that the disputed material falls outside the new boundary;
- a Stop is described as temporary incompleteness and analysis continues without a new warrant;
- Non-Capture is recoded as missing detail, guaranteeing that further decomposition is always treated as the cure.

These moves may each become legitimate new investigations. They are self-immunizing only when their novelty is used to conceal discontinuity with the original claim.

The governing question is:

```text
Does the new transformation answer the prior objection,
or does it only create a new object or coordinate system?
```

Answering the prior objection requires an explicit relation between the challenged claim and the new claim. The relation may show that the objection defeats the old claim, narrows it, identifies a source error, establishes that a different object was required, or supports a genuinely new transformation. What it may not do is leave the old disposition indeterminate while presenting the new analysis as its repair.

#### C41-FAILURECONTINUITY-01 — prior failure, reduction, Stop, and Non-Capture remain visible across later transformations

Failure continuity is the requirement that prior dispositions survive analytical movement as dispositions of their original claims.

```text
new frame
≠ prior failure deleted

finer granularity
≠ prior claim reduction restored

broader composite
≠ prior Stop withdrawn

new target function
≠ prior Non-Capture converted into capture
```

Continuity does not mean that every later claim must fail because an earlier claim failed. It means that the identity and result of the earlier claim remain recorded. Later claims may be admissible, may answer related questions, or may provide material for a revised claim. They do not retroactively change what the earlier record established.

The Q0–Q3 pressure chain is deliberately minimal:

| Claim | Status entering WP2 | What a later result may do | What it may not do |
|---|---|---|---|
| Q0 | stipulated prior `failed_transformation` | remain as a visible premise for testing later claims | be readjudicated or erased without its own reopening procedure |
| Q1 | unadjudicated `DECOMPOSE` candidate | reconstruct finer source structure if separately warranted | count as repair merely because more detail exists |
| Q2 | unadjudicated `COMPOSE` candidate | form a broader composite if relations and Loss support it | validate Q0 by scale expansion |
| Q3 | unadjudicated `PROJECT_AS` candidate | test a new bounded target function | inherit validity from Q1, Q2, or chain length |

The chain can therefore contain mixed results. Q1 could be `resolution_neutral`; Q2 could be `admissible_with_bounded_claim`; Q3 could require `claim_reduction_required`; Q0 would remain `failed_transformation`. STRATA must be able to represent that pattern without compressing it into one overall success narrative.

#### C41-CHAINADMISSIBILITY-01 — every operation and every chain relation must pass independently

An operation chain has at least two distinct burdens:

1. each operation occurrence must be admissible on its own terms;
2. the relation that carries one result into the next operation must also be admissible.

```text
local operation passage
+ invalid handoff relation
→ chain not admissible
```

A valid decomposition may produce a source object that is irrelevant to the later projection. A valid composition may alter the reference object so substantially that a later decomposition is no longer opening the same object. A valid projection may be followed by a decomposition that mistakenly opens the target function rather than the origin-typed source object. Local success does not settle chain identity, continuity, or claim relevance.

Conversely, an inadmissible local operation cannot be rescued because the later chain seems coherent. Narrative continuity is not operation passage. The integrated audit in Chapter 53 will formalize the chain-level review; Chapter 41 fixes the non-negotiable architecture that local and relational passage are cumulative.

```text
all local operations admissible
≠ chain admissible automatically

chain appears coherent
≠ failed local operation repaired
```

The requirement blocks a common immunization pattern: treating the chain as the real analytical object and dismissing local failures as provisional steps. STRATA permits provisional work, but provisional status must be declared through the canonical outputs and cannot be used to conceal a selected failure or Stop.

#### C41-STOPNONCAPTURE-01 — Mandatory Stop and Non-Capture are positive method results

A method with no stable Stop or Non-Capture condition cannot preserve claim-level failure or remain bounded by its own records. It can only request another transformation. LIMITS therefore preserves two positive results that analytical mobility tends to treat as embarrassment.

**Mandatory Stop** applies where continuation would be inadmissible: sources are exhausted, TypeIntegrity or reference continuity cannot be preserved, the Traceability Ceiling is exceeded, a prohibited authority inference is required, or the chain would hide rather than resolve a prior failure.

**Non-Capture** applies where the available grammar, sources, calibration, or transformation set cannot produce a bounded capture without distortion. It is not a weak placeholder for future success. It identifies a present capture limit.

```text
mandatory_stop
≠ methodological defeat

non_capture
≠ permission for unlimited recursion
```

A prior Mandatory Stop remains valid for its route even when method work proceeds elsewhere. A Non-Capture result may coexist with partial or analogy-only findings, but it cannot be protected from scrutiny by being made vague. Both results must identify what is stopped or uncaptured and why.

Self-immunization is avoided only if these outcomes remain genuinely reachable. If every Stop is automatically reopened by a new coordinate change and every Non-Capture is automatically translated into a demand for finer detail, the method has converted its limits into fuel for further expansion.

Chapter 50 owns the detailed anti-immunization procedure, including objection lineage, re-entry conditions, and tests for claim relocation. Chapters 51 and 52 own the full Stop and Non-Capture records. The present section establishes why those later procedures are constitutive: without failure continuity, independent chain admissibility, and reachable terminal results, the three operations could generate indefinite analytical motion without a stable claim boundary.

#### C41-WP2-HANDOFF-01 — WP2 hands forward the complete risk architecture without selecting a route or authority claim

Sections 41.1–41.7 now establish five connected risk families:

```text
recursive availability
→ infinite-decomposition risk
→ unlimited-composition risk
→ arbitrary-projection risk
→ authority-drift and self-immunization risk
```

They also preserve the following controls:

1. every operation occurrence remains a separate claim, Record, Loss, and result;
2. every chain relation requires independent passage;
3. prior failure, reduction, Stop, and Non-Capture remain visible;
4. Optional Stop remains available before an unnecessary operation;
5. Mandatory Stop and Non-Capture remain positive results;
6. no vertical movement or chain length creates truth, rank, legitimacy, recommendation, sanction, or application authority;
7. Q0 remains a stipulated prior `failed_transformation` disposition;
8. Q1–Q3 remain unadjudicated.

WP2 has not selected a route, operation result, chain result, or canonical Output Class for Q1–Q3. It has not applied the detailed tests of Chapters 44–53, has not produced any missing RETYPE case package, and has not established Part-IV lock.

WP3 receives the complete Chapter-41 risk architecture. Its task is not to add another risk family or to adjudicate the pressure chain. It must state why LIMITS already governs every operation and chain, preserve LIMITS as a Part rather than a fourth operation or meta-level, and formulate the governing principle that analytical availability never establishes admissibility.

<a id="41-8-limits-as-constitutive-structure"></a>

### 41.8 LIMITS as Constitutive Structure

The preceding risk architecture shows why LIMITS cannot be attached to STRATA only after an operation has produced an analytically attractive result. Infinite decomposition, unlimited composition, arbitrary projection, authority drift, and self-immunization do not arise outside the operations. They arise through otherwise legitimate capacities when the conditions of their use, continuation, and reuse are no longer kept visible.

LIMITS is therefore constitutive in a precise methodological sense. It belongs to what makes an occurrence of `COMPOSE`, `DECOMPOSE`, or `PROJECT_AS` a STRATA operation rather than an unbounded analytical redescription. An operation occurrence is not complete merely because its source and target can be named. It must remain answerable to the source basis, declared claim, reference object, origin type, contextual function, continuity requirements, exact Loss, alternatives, and the possibility that the correct disposition is reduction, Stop, failure, or Non-Capture.

```text
operation signature
+ constitutive limit conditions
→ possible admissible operation occurrence

operation signature
- constitutive limit conditions
→ analytical movement without STRATA passage
```

This does not mean that LIMITS supplies the substantive result in advance. The limits do not decide whether a particular source supports a trajectory, whether a decomposition produces relevant gain, or whether a projected function is actually carried by the source. They require those questions to remain testable and prevent formal movement from being mistaken for passage.

#### C41-CONSTITUTIVELIMITS-01 — LIMITS governs every operation and chain from the outset

LIMITS is active at three connected moments.

**Before an operation**, it governs entry. The operation must have a declared question, source object, expected praxeological difference, source support, and a reason why the proposed movement is needed rather than merely available. Entry can already end in Optional Stop when no additional purchase is expected, or in Mandatory Stop where the move would require a prohibited type jump, unsupported source expansion, authority inference, or erasure of a prior disposition.

**Within an operation**, LIMITS governs formation. Selection, relation, reference, type, context, sensitivity, and Loss must remain inspectable while the target object or function is being formed. The operation may not use its own output label as evidence that its source burden was satisfied.

**After an operation**, LIMITS governs disposition and reuse. The result receives a bounded claim and a canonical output mapping. If the result becomes the source of a later operation, its preserved structure, excluded material, uncertainty, irrecoverable Loss, and prior disposition travel forward as constraints. They do not travel forward as inherited authority.

```text
limit inheritance
=
constraint, Loss, and disposition continuity

limit inheritance
≠
authority inheritance
```

A chain therefore does not begin with a clean slate at each arrow. It carries the history that makes the next operation intelligible. At the same time, inherited limits do not predetermine the later result. A later claim remains independently testable and may pass, require reduction, stop, fail, or remain uncaptured. Constitutive LIMITS preserves both continuity and non-automaticity.

#### C41-NOFOURTHOP-01 — LIMITS is a Part and cross-cutting admissibility structure, not a fourth operation or meta-level

The three operation signatures remain closed:

```text
COMPOSE
DECOMPOSE
PROJECT_AS
```

LIMITS has no independent transformation direction. It does not take a source object and produce a fourth kind of target object. It does not compose, open, or functionally project. It states the conditions under which those operations may be entered, formed, concluded, repeated, and chained.

```text
LIMITS
≠ transformation operation
≠ target-producing operator
≠ superior level above PATH, SUB, or RETYPE
≠ meta-PMS
```

Calling LIMITS a Part serves an architectural purpose. Part IV gathers and systematizes constraints that were already active locally in PATH, SUB, and RETYPE. The Part gives those constraints a common vocabulary, makes their interaction visible across operations, and supplies the later integrated audit. Its position after the three operational Parts is an order of exposition, not an order of authority.

The same distinction applies to the formal model. A Rule Registry, Decision Tree, schema, audit record, Reader route, or package manifest can make limit requirements checkable. None becomes a superior analytical stratum or an additional source of substantive warrant. Formal registration preserves requirements; it does not transform the object or decide the truth of the claim.

#### C41-PARTINTEGRATION-01 — PATH, SUB, RETYPE, and chain limits accumulate without overriding local results

The operational Parts already contain local limit structures:

| Part | Local burden retained by LIMITS | What integrated LIMITS adds |
|---|---|---|
| PATH / `COMPOSE` | warranted ordering and selection, constitutive relations, alternatives, temporal load, exact Loss, path trace | whether the composite remains a valid source for a later operation and whether inherited temporal and alternative structure survives the handoff |
| SUB / `DECOMPOSE` | same reference object, occurrence/composite rather than operator type, functional components and relations, source support, resolution gain, exact Loss | whether the finer reconstruction answers the prior burden, preserves chain identity, and supplies a relevant rather than merely detailed source for later use |
| RETYPE / `PROJECT_AS` | preserved origin type, declared target context and function, Constitutive Source Trace, sensitivity, bounded validity, exact Loss | whether the projection relation remains independent of prior local success and whether later reuse preserves source/function separation and the original result |

Integrated LIMITS does not reclassify a local result because the chain later appears useful. It also does not invalidate a local result merely because the chain later stops. The operation occurrence and the chain relation answer different questions.

```text
local result retained
+ handoff relation tested
+ later operation tested
→ integrated chain disposition
```

This cumulative architecture permits mixed chains without compressing them into one global label. An admissible decomposition can be followed by a failed projection. An admissible composition can be followed by Mandatory Stop because the next source relation cannot be traced. A failed projection can remain failed while a later decomposition of the source is independently admissible. The integrated record must preserve these differences.

The only “inheritance” that LIMITS permits is the inheritance of declared burdens: source limits, claim limits, Loss, continuity requirements, and prior dispositions. No Part, operation, or chain segment inherits an automatic pass from another.

#### C41-RISKTAXONOMY-01 — the Chapter-41 risks form one bounded architecture without replacing later tests

Chapter 41 has identified five connected risk families:

1. **recursive decomposition risk** — the possibility that finer resolution produces detail below the Relevance Floor, exceeds source support, dilutes the reference object, or makes the claim resistant to counterexample through microcomplexity;
2. **recursive composition risk** — the possibility that broader objects absorb heterogeneity and contradiction, lose constitutive relation trace, inflate macrofunction, or conceal irrecoverable Loss;
3. **recursive projection risk** — the possibility that target-label plausibility substitutes for context, Source Trace, sensitivity, TypeIntegrity, and bounded validity;
4. **vertical authority risk** — the possibility that finer, broader, higher, more formal, or more deeply chained analysis is treated as more true, valid, legitimate, or action-authorizing;
5. **self-immunization risk** — the possibility that a new coordinate, object, or transformation replaces the challenged claim without answering its objection or preserving its disposition.

These risks are linked but not interchangeable. A composition can remain traceable yet be unnecessary. A decomposition can produce gain yet fail to answer a projection objection. A projection can be locally bounded yet enter an invalid chain. An integrated chain can be methodically well recorded yet still carry no application authority.

Chapter 41 establishes the taxonomy and its common rationale. It does not fix universal thresholds or reproduce the detailed mechanics that follow. The Relevance Floor, Traceability Ceiling, sensitivity test, continuity matrix, Loss discipline, source and calibration ceilings, anti-immunization procedure, Stop and Non-Capture records, and integrated audit remain separately owned by Chapters 44–53.

#### C41-AUTHORITYBOUNDARY-01 — no operation, chain, level, artifact, or Part transmits authority

The non-inheritance rule applies across every boundary created or used by STRATA:

```text
operation result
→ no automatic authority transfer to later operation

operation chain
→ no authority gain from length or coherence

relative level or granularity
→ no truth or rank increase

composite or projected function
→ no application mandate

formal model or schema
→ no semantic or empirical warrant

Reader, index, audit, or repository completion
→ no validation authority

LIMITS Part
→ no tribunal position above PMS Base or the operational Parts
```

This boundary is stronger than the statement that STRATA is “descriptive.” A structural method can influence decisions even without issuing explicit prescriptions. For that reason, the claim and authority ceilings must remain visible in the record and in any downstream use. A reconstruction may be admissible as a bounded structural claim while remaining unauthorized as diagnosis, ranking, sanction, policy, legal decision, governance verdict, or automatic recommendation.

```yaml
governance:
  authority_inheritance: prohibited
```

The prohibition also prevents a common formal drift: treating the successful completion of more gates, richer records, or a later integrated audit as an increase in external authority. Audit passage supports only the declared STRATA claim within its scope. It does not supply the independent authority needed for application.

<a id="41-9-governing-limits-principle"></a>

### 41.9 Governing LIMITS Principle

The general rationale of Chapter 41 can now be stated as one governing principle.

#### C41-GOVERNINGPRINCIPLE-01 — analytical availability never establishes admissibility

> **A STRATA transformation is admissible only when the specific operation occurrence and every relation by which it enters a chain produce warranted praxeological purchase within the declared source, reference, type, continuity, context, Loss, claim, and authority boundaries. The availability, recurrence, formal expressibility, or apparent coherence of a transformation never establishes its necessity, passage, or authority. Prior dispositions remain attached to their claims. Where these conditions cannot be maintained, the claim must be bounded, reduced, stopped, failed, or marked as Non-Capture rather than rescued through further analytical movement.**

This principle has several immediate consequences.

First, **availability is permissive only at the level of possible inquiry**. It identifies a question that may be formulated. It does not authorize execution or continuation.

Second, **admissibility remains occurrence-specific**. The existence of one admissible `COMPOSE`, `DECOMPOSE`, or `PROJECT_AS` record does not validate another occurrence of the same operation type. Operations do not carry a reusable pass.

Third, **chain admissibility is cumulative and non-compensatory**. Every operation occurrence and every handoff relation must pass its own burden. Strong performance on one segment cannot compensate for failed TypeIntegrity, missing Source Trace, broken reference continuity, exceeded source support, irrecoverable unacknowledged Loss, or prohibited authority inference elsewhere.

Fourth, **prior dispositions are stable but not contagious**. A prior failure remains a failure of its claim; it does not force every later claim to fail. A later admissible claim remains admissible within its scope; it does not repair the earlier failure. The same applies to Claim Reduction, Stop, and Non-Capture.

Fifth, **more analysis is not the default remedy for analytical pressure**. The remedy may be a narrower claim, a different but explicitly new question, Optional Stop, Mandatory Stop, `failed_transformation`, or `non_capture`. A method remains open by preserving these routes, not by guaranteeing that another transformation will eventually succeed.

Sixth, **no admissibility result produces authority beyond its declared claim type**. The method can state that a transformation is structurally warranted, bounded, provisional, reduced, stopped, failed, analogical, partial, or uncaptured. It cannot derive normative legitimacy, person judgment, sanction, policy authority, or automatic action from that classification.

The principle can be reduced to a compact operational form:

```text
available(T, X, C)
≠ admissible(T, X, C)

admissible local operation
≠ admissible next operation
≠ admissible chain automatically

new transformation
= new testable claim

more structure
≠ more authority
```

The full tests are deliberately distributed across the remaining LIMITS chapters. Chapter 41 supplies the rationale and governing relation among them; it does not collapse them into one score or one universal threshold.

#### C41-HANDOFF42-53-01 — Chapters 42–53 receive distinct, non-overlapping LIMITS tasks

The governing principle now hands forward the following ownership structure:

| Chapter | Owned task | Boundary preserved |
|---|---|---|
| 42 | reject an ontology of strata and absolute layer language | analytical relations do not become real layers |
| 43 | reject privilege of finer resolution and higher composition | detail, breadth, and relative height do not become truth ranks |
| 44 | elaborate the Praxeological Relevance Floor | additional distinction must produce warranted praxeological purchase |
| 45 | elaborate the Praxeological Traceability Ceiling | composition and projection must retain constitutive source load |
| 46 | specify Counterfactual Sensitivity | relevant source change must matter without becoming causal proof |
| 47 | specify reference, TypeIntegrity, functional, and temporal continuity | transformation must preserve the identity and boundaries required by its claim |
| 48 | specify selection, compression, and exact five-part Loss | omitted, uncertain, and irrecoverable structure remains visible |
| 49 | specify Source Ceiling and Calibration Boundary | formal precision cannot exceed source and calibration support |
| 50 | specify anti-immunization and failure preservation | coordinate change cannot rescue a challenged claim by relocation |
| 51 | specify Mandatory and Optional Stop, reduction, and re-entry | continuation remains governed and terminal routes remain positive |
| 52 | specify forms and records of Non-Capture | genuine outside and responsible capture limits remain possible |
| 53 | integrate the twelve-stage audit and map results to the closed canonical classes | integrated review preserves local results and adds no tribunal authority |

No later chapter may convert its local test into a superior rule that overrides the others. The Admissibility Band is non-compensatory: relevance does not excuse missing traceability; traceability does not excuse a type jump; continuity does not excuse source overreach; formal validity does not excuse authority drift. Chapter 53 integrates the gates while preserving their distinct ownership.

Chapters 42–43 first protect the anti-ontology and anti-privilege boundary. Chapters 44–49 then specify the operating range and continuity burdens. Chapters 50–52 preserve failure, Stop, and outside. Chapter 53 assembles the integrated audit. This sequence elaborates one constitutive structure; it does not create a hierarchy of increasingly authoritative chapters.

#### C41-HANDOFF-01 — WP3 completes the Chapter-41 rationale and hands WP4 an audit-and-lock task only

Sections 41.1–41.9 now provide the full method-level rationale for LIMITS:

- analytical mobility is productive and risk-bearing;
- recursive availability is not recursive necessity;
- infinite decomposition, unlimited composition, and arbitrary projection are distinct risks;
- vertical movement creates no authority gain;
- coordinate change cannot immunize a claim;
- local operations and chain relations require independent passage;
- prior dispositions and Loss remain continuous;
- Stop and Non-Capture remain positive results;
- LIMITS is constitutive of every operation and chain;
- LIMITS is neither a fourth operation nor a meta-level;
- analytical availability never establishes admissibility;
- Chapters 42–53 have distinct, bounded assignments.

The Q0–Q3 pressure chain remains exactly as it entered WP3:

```text
Q0
→ stipulated prior failed_transformation
→ preserved, not readjudicated

Q1 DECOMPOSE
Q2 COMPOSE
Q3 PROJECT_AS
→ separate new claims
→ unadjudicated
```

WP3 selects no route, operation result, chain result, Local Audit result, or canonical Output Class for Q1–Q3. It creates no Case, Test, or Smoke artifact and does not substitute for the three missing RETYPE lock-critical packages. It also does not claim Part-IV lock.

WP4 receives no new conceptual duty. Its task is to verify the Chapter-41 Contract, redundancy boundaries, exact representation of all twenty-four duties, preservation of Q0 and non-adjudication of Q1–Q3, consistency with the Minified Kernel and formal model, and the exact provisional-lock disposition. Only after that audit may Chapter 42 preparation begin.

Chapter 41 therefore closes its substantive argument with a bounded claim:

> **STRATA remains methodologically mobile only by making the limits of movement part of the method itself.**

LIMITS does not weaken `COMPOSE`, `DECOMPOSE`, or `PROJECT_AS`. It is what prevents their recursive availability from becoming unlimited detail, untraceable abstraction, arbitrary function, authority drift, or immunity from failure.

---

<a id="chapter-42-no-ontology-of-strata"></a>

## 42. No Ontology of Strata

STRATA requires language for moving among parts, composites, local configurations, trajectories, functions, granularities, and relative levels. That language is indispensable. Without it, `COMPOSE`, `DECOMPOSE`, and `PROJECT_AS` could not declare their source and target relations with sufficient precision. Yet the same language creates a recurrent risk: an analytical position may be redescribed as a layer of reality, a selected part as a fundamental constituent, or a bounded composite as a more real whole.

Chapter 42 establishes the anti-ontology boundary for these movements. Its governing distinction is:

```text
analytical relation
≠ ontological layer
```

This distinction is not a denial of structure. STRATA may reconstruct stable relations, persistent configurations, real constraints, recurring trajectories, distributed asymmetries, composite objects, and bounded functions. It may compare objects under different resolutions and locate one object as a constituent of another. What it may not do is infer from the success, stability, or usefulness of such a reconstruction that reality itself is divided into intrinsic, independently existing, universally ordered strata.

The chapter therefore does not replace the coordinate definitions of Chapter 2. Frame, granularity, and relative level retain their established meanings. Nor does it redefine the three operations. It applies the constitutive LIMITS principle of Chapter 41 to a specific inferential pressure:

```text
useful vertical vocabulary
+
reconstructive stability
+
repeated analytical success

≠

warrant for discrete reality layers
```

The pressure can arise in either direction. A finer reconstruction may be treated as access to a deeper reality. A broader composite may be treated as a higher or more complete reality. A projected function may later be treated as a new kind of thing. Chapter 42 blocks all three conversions while preserving the legitimate analytical work performed by fine, broad, and functional reconstructions.

For WP1, the pressure is held in one synthetic packet:

```text
O0
→ institutional arrangement I fundamentally consists of micro-frames
  and exists on a higher real layer

R1
→ within Frame C1 and granularity g1,
  I is reconstructed through selected local practices and relations
  for bounded claim Q1

R2
→ within Frame C1 and granularity g2,
  I is reconstructed through a different supported distinction set
  for a separately bounded claim
```

`O0` is an ontology-pressure statement and is not accepted. `R1` and `R2` are bounded reconstruction candidates and remain unadjudicated. WP1 does not select a preferred granularity, operation result, route, or canonical Output Class. It establishes only the relational conditions under which the reconstructions can be stated without becoming an ontology.

<a id="42-1-analytical-levels"></a>

### 42.1 Analytical Levels

Level language is useful only when its relation is declared. An object is not simply “micro,” “meso,” “macro,” “higher,” or “lower.” It occupies a relative position with respect to a source object, a target object, a comparison axis, a Frame, a granularity, a transformation context, and a Claim Scope.

The same recorded conversation may be relatively composite with respect to its turns, interruptions, and response sequences while remaining one local constituent of an institutional review trajectory. The conversation has not moved between two intrinsic layers. Two different analytical relations have been declared.

```text
conversation
→ relatively wider than selected utterance occurrences

conversation
→ relatively narrower than institutional review trajectory
```

Both statements can be warranted at once. Neither assigns the conversation an absolute level property.

#### C42-ANALYTICALLEVEL-01 — a level is a declared relation, not an intrinsic property

An **analytical level** is the relative position of a declared object within a specified reconstructive relation. At minimum, the relation identifies:

- the reference object;
- the relevant source object or source set;
- the target object or target relation;
- the analytical Frame;
- source and target granularity;
- the comparison or transformation context;
- the temporal and source scope where relevant;
- the Claim Scope for which the position matters.

A level statement is therefore incomplete when it says only:

> `I` is a macrostructure.

The statement does not identify relative to what `I` is macro, which distinctions are being compressed, which relation establishes the position, or what claim depends on that position. A bounded form is:

> Within Frame `C1`, granularity `g1`, and reference set `S`, institutional arrangement `I` is relatively wider than the selected local practice occurrences because the reconstruction relates those occurrences as constituents of `I` for claim `Q1`.

This form does not decide whether `Q1` is true. It makes the coordinate burden inspectable.

Analytical level also does not determine operation identity. A source object may be relatively narrower than a target composite without a valid `COMPOSE` having occurred. A reconstruction may distinguish finer internal structures without satisfying `DECOMPOSE`. A source object may be located within a new target context without a valid `PROJECT_AS` result. Chapter 4 and the operation-specific Parts continue to own those determinations.

```text
relative position declared
≠ operation established
≠ admissibility established
≠ ontology established
```

A level relation may remain stable across repeated analyses. If different analysts using the same Frame, source basis, granularity relation, and Claim Scope repeatedly locate `I` as relatively wider than the selected practices, the relation may become highly reproducible. Reproducibility strengthens the bounded analytical relation. It does not convert that relation into an independently existing layer.

#### C42-LOCALCOORDINATES-01 — every level statement must expose its local coordinates and Claim Scope
