A level statement is admissibly formulated only when the coordinates that make it meaningful remain visible. Chapter 2 already owns the Minimal Level Declaration. Chapter 42 applies its anti-ontology burden through the following readable form:

```text
reference object:
source object or source set:
target object or target relation:
analytical Frame:
source granularity:
target granularity:
relative-level relation:
comparison or transformation context:
temporal scope:
source scope:
Claim Scope:
coordinate changes:
operation status:
Loss and uncertainty note:
```

This is not a second Record grammar. The fields map to existing Shared Transformation Record locations. Their function here is diagnostic: an ontological formulation often appears strongest precisely where the coordinates have disappeared.

Compare:

```text
I exists on a higher layer than its local practices.
```

with:

```text
Within Frame C1 and granularity relation g_local → g_composite,
I is reconstructed as relatively wider than selected local practices
for claim Q1; the relation is source- and claim-bounded,
and no ontological layer is asserted.
```

The second statement remains contestable in the right way. One can challenge the selected practices, the Frame, the relation, the granularity, the source support, the Loss declaration, or the Claim Scope. The first statement conceals those burdens behind a supposed property of reality.

Local declaration also prevents authority drift. A relatively wider position does not raise the Claim Ceiling. A relatively finer position does not lower the Source Ceiling. Coordinate precision may improve inspectability; it creates no legitimacy, recommendation, sanction, person classification, governance competence, or application authority.

```yaml
governance:
  authority_inheritance: prohibited
```

#### C42-RELATIVEVOCABULARY-01 — micro, meso, macro, higher, lower, part, and whole remain relational shorthand

STRATA does not ban ordinary vertical and part-whole vocabulary. Such a ban would make the method cumbersome without solving the underlying inferential problem. The terms remain available as shorthand when their local relation is recoverable.

```text
micro
→ relatively fine or local under a declared comparison

meso
→ intermediate only within a declared relation set

macro
→ relatively wide or composite under a declared comparison

higher / lower
→ positions in a stated analytical relation

part / whole
→ source- and claim-bounded reconstructive relation
```

None of the terms names a universal class. A practice occurrence may be “micro” relative to an institutional arrangement, yet “macro” relative to its own utterance-level constituents. A quarterly review process may be relatively wide in one study and a local episode in a longer governance trajectory in another. An object can occupy several legitimate positions because the comparison relations differ.

The correct rule is therefore not:

```text
never use micro / meso / macro
```

but:

```text
never allow relational shorthand
to conceal the relation that gives it meaning
```

When the relation cannot be recovered, the vocabulary should be expanded or the claim reduced. When several relation sets are in use, each must be declared. If no bounded translation among them is warranted, the analyst may have to retain multiple local descriptions or later mark a capture limit. The vocabulary itself does not force a universal hierarchy.

<a id="42-2-reconstructive-relations"></a>

### 42.2 Reconstructive Relations

STRATA does not merely label objects at different positions. It reconstructs relations among them. These relations may be materially significant and source-supported. Their reconstructive status does not make them fictional or arbitrary. It means that the analysis selects, relates, and claims them under declared conditions rather than discovering a pre-partitioned architecture of reality.

#### C42-RECONSTRUCTIVERELATION-01 — part/whole, sequence/trajectory, source/function, local/macro, and Frame/context are reconstruction relations

Five relation families are especially vulnerable to ontology drift.

**Part and whole.** A source occurrence may be reconstructed as a constituent of a composite object. The relation depends on selection, relation trace, Frame, granularity, temporal scope, and claim. The part is not thereby a final constituent, and the composite is not thereby an ultimate whole. Those operation-specific prohibitions belong to WP2; WP1 establishes the relational premise on which they depend.

**Sequence and trajectory.** Events, non-events, transitions, paths, and trajectories can be related through supported temporal reconstruction. A trajectory is not a deeper layer beneath the sequence, nor is the sequence a more fundamental reality beneath the trajectory. They are distinct analytical objects with different formation burdens.

**Source and function.** An origin-typed source object may later support a bounded target function. The source/function relation is neither identity nor ontological transformation. The function remains dependent on target context, source trace, TypeIntegrity, and bounded validity.

**Local structure and macrofunction.** Local occurrences may jointly carry a wider function, but “local” and “macro” name positions in the declared reconstruction. The wider function is not a superior entity that can override its source relations by virtue of analytical height.

**Source Frame and target context.** A source object may be reconstructed in one analytical Frame and tested in another target context. The relation between them must remain explicit. A changed context does not reveal a hidden layer and does not repair a failed earlier claim automatically.

These relations can be expressed compactly:

```text
part / whole
sequence / trajectory
source / target function
local structure / wider function
source Frame / target context

→ reconstructive relations under declaration
≠ independently existing strata
```

The phrase **reconstructive relation** carries three positive commitments.

First, the relation must be warranted. A part-whole claim requires more than verbal inclusion. A sequence-trajectory claim requires more than chronological order. A source-function claim requires more than analogy. Anti-ontology does not lower the evidential burden.

Second, the relation must remain revisable. New sources, a changed question, a different granularity, or a failed continuity test may alter the reconstruction. Revisibility does not imply that every relation is equally plausible; it prevents the current reconstruction from becoming immune through ontological wording.

Third, the relation must retain Loss and alternatives. Selection makes some structure visible while compressing, excluding, leaving uncertain, or irrecoverably losing other structure. A relation that hides those effects may look ontologically complete precisely because its reconstruction costs have disappeared.

For the pressure packet, `R1` may state:

```text
Within Frame C1 and granularity g1,
institutional arrangement I is reconstructed through
selected documented practices and relations S
for bounded claim Q1.
```

This is a legitimate **form** of claim. It is not yet a selected result. The warrant for the selected practices, their constitutive relation, the Loss profile, and the bounded claim must still be tested. Chapter 42 does not adjudicate them merely because the wording is ontology-neutral.

#### C42-STABILITYNOONTOLOGY-01 — analytical stability does not create an independent stratum

A common ontology drift begins with a legitimate observation: some reconstructive relations are stable. The same institutional arrangement may repeatedly be reconstructed through the same procedural practices, role relations, temporal sequences, and binding conditions. The relation may survive source expansion, rival analysis, and repeated testing. It may be indispensable for explaining the bounded claim.

None of this is a defect. STRATA should be able to identify robust analytical structure. The invalid step is:

```text
stable, recurrent, discriminating reconstruction
→ independently existing layer of reality
```

Stability can arise because:

- the source relations themselves persist;
- the Frame and question remain stable;
- multiple sources support the same bounded relation;
- the reconstruction preserves a useful discriminative distinction;
- relevant alternatives repeatedly fail;
- the target claim is insensitive to non-constitutive variation.

These conditions may strengthen the warranted reconstruction. They do not remove its Frame, granularity, source basis, or Claim Scope. A map can remain reliable without becoming the territory's intrinsic partition. A type system can remain generative without becoming a particle inventory. A composite can remain practically consequential without existing on a higher ontological plane.

The converse must also be preserved:

```text
analytically constructed
≠ unreal
≠ arbitrary
≠ merely linguistic
```

Anti-ontology is not an instruction to weaken every claim into a metaphor. Documented local practices may really constrain action. Institutional arrangements may persist, allocate costs, shape expectations, and close alternatives. A trajectory may carry historical load. A projected function may be consequential in its target context. Chapter 42 denies an unwarranted inference about layer status, not the reality or seriousness of the source-supported praxis structures.

This distinction prevents a false binary:

```text
ontological layer
or
mere fiction
```

STRATA occupies neither pole. It makes bounded reconstructive claims whose strength depends on source support, traceability, continuity, counterpressure, and Claim Ceiling.

<a id="42-3-no-discrete-reality-layers"></a>

### 42.3 No Discrete Reality Layers

Once vertical terms are used repeatedly, they can begin to suggest a universal architecture: micro at the bottom, meso in the middle, macro at the top. The suggestion becomes stronger when analytical operations appear directional—`DECOMPOSE` relatively downward, `COMPOSE` relatively upward, and `PROJECT_AS` across a target context. Chapter 42 rejects the conversion of these local directions into discrete reality layers.

#### C42-NODISCRETELAYERS-01 — STRATA does not posit independently existing or universally ordered micro, meso, and macro strata

STRATA makes no claim that every praxis object belongs to one determinate level in a universal hierarchy. It does not assume that:

- all micro-objects are of one kind;
- all meso-objects mediate the same relation;
- all macro-objects possess a common scale or authority;
- every object has one correct upward and downward decomposition path;
- relative height determines causal priority, truth, fundamentality, or explanatory privilege;
- level boundaries exist independently of Frame, granularity, reference set, and claim.

A universal layer model would require more than STRATA can warrant. It would need stable cross-domain criteria for assigning every relevant object to fixed strata, determining universal boundaries among them, and establishing that the boundaries belong to reality rather than to a chosen reconstruction. STRATA supplies no such criteria and claims no such authority.

The three operations do not supply them indirectly.

```text
DECOMPOSE often moves toward finer reconstruction
≠ descent to a more fundamental layer

COMPOSE often moves toward a wider analytical object
≠ ascent to a more real layer

PROJECT_AS changes bounded contextual function
≠ movement into a new ontological stratum
```

Nor does the Δ–Ψ dependency order settle the matter. PMS Base provides an operator grammar with a declared sequence and organizational layers. Chapter 42 WP3 will state the full grammar/world distinction. For WP1, the relevant constraint is already clear: analytical order and organizational grouping do not by themselves describe the furniture or layer structure of reality.

The absence of discrete universal layers does not prohibit local partitions. A study may distinguish interactional, procedural, and institutional positions because the sources and claim require those coordinates. Another study may use event, episode, and trajectory. A third may use occurrence, composite, and target function. The partitions are admissible when their relation, purpose, sources, and limits are declared.

```text
local analytical partition
→ possible and testable

universal ontological partition
→ not supplied by STRATA
```

The boundary also protects Non-Capture. If one local level scheme fails to coordinate a relevant structure, the analyst may revise the scheme, retain competing descriptions, reduce the claim, stop, or later record a capture limit. The method is not entitled to insist that every object must fit one universal micro–meso–macro ladder.

#### C42-DUALGRANULARITY-01 — two legitimate granularities may coexist without either becoming the real layer

The pressure packet retains two bounded reconstructions of `I`:

```text
R1
→ Frame C1, granularity g1
→ selected local practices grouped into a wider institutional relation
→ bounded claim Q1

R2
→ Frame C1, granularity g2
→ a different supported distinction set within the same reference object
→ separately bounded claim
```

Suppose `g1` distinguishes procedural phases, role positions, explicit decisions, and documented non-decisions. Suppose `g2` distinguishes finer interaction sequences, repair attempts, delays, and local asymmetry shifts. Both may possess praxeological purchase.

`R1` may preserve the wider procedural relation while compressing local interaction. `R2` may preserve local process while making the wider relation less visible. Their five-part Loss declarations will differ:

```yaml
R1_loss:
  preserved: wider_procedural_relation
  compressed: local_interaction_variation
  excluded: unsupported_or_out_of_scope_detail
  uncertain: incomplete_local_transition_evidence
  irrecoverable: distinctions_not_retained_by_the_selected_compression

R2_loss:
  preserved: local_sequence_and_repair_structure
  compressed: wider_procedural_summary
  excluded: relations_outside_the_fine_question
  uncertain: aggregation_to_institutional_function
  irrecoverable: wider_relations_not_reconstructible_from_the_selected_fine_record_alone
```

The example does not adjudicate either Loss profile. It shows why no inference follows from resolution alone.

```text
R1 legitimate
+ R2 legitimate

≠ one must be the real layer
≠ both must be merged
≠ finer must dominate
≠ coarser must dominate
```

The two reconstructions may be compatible, partially overlapping, competing, or non-translatable for a particular predicate. That determination belongs to their substantive comparison and, where performance privilege is at issue, to Chapter 43. Chapter 42 WP1 establishes only the anti-ontology constraint: legitimacy at one granularity does not transform that granularity into reality's privileged resolution.

The same rule applies when one reconstruction is more stable. If `R1` repeatedly supports the bounded institutional claim while `R2` varies with local source detail, `R1` may outperform `R2` for that claim. The result would concern discriminative performance, not ontological superiority. Conversely, if `R2` exposes a decisive asymmetry hidden by `R1`, the finer reconstruction may require qualification or reduction of the coarse claim. That correction still does not reveal a deeper layer of being.

No Output Class is selected for `R1` or `R2` in WP1. The ontology-pressure statement `O0` remains rejected as a permitted inference, while the bounded reconstructions remain open to later testing.

#### C42-WP1-HANDOFF-01 — WP1 hands WP2 a relational boundary without constituent or totality claims

WP1 establishes the following stable boundary:

- analytical levels are locally declared relations;
- every level statement must expose its Frame, granularity, source/target relation, transformation or comparison context, and Claim Scope;
- micro, meso, macro, higher, lower, part, and whole remain relational shorthand;
- part/whole, sequence/trajectory, source/function, local/macro, and Frame/context remain reconstructive relations;
- analytical stability strengthens a bounded reconstruction without creating an independent stratum;
- STRATA does not posit universally ordered discrete reality layers;
- two legitimate granularities may coexist without either becoming the real layer;
- `O0` is not accepted, while `R1` and `R2` remain unadjudicated.

WP1 does not determine whether a decomposition reaches final constituents, whether a composition forms an ultimate whole, or whether a projection ontologically promotes its source. Those operation-specific anti-ontology burdens belong to WP2.

The controlled handoff is:

```text
relational anti-ontology boundary established
+ local coordinate burden established
+ dual-granularity counterpressure preserved
+ O0 prohibited as inference
+ R1 and R2 unadjudicated

→ Chapter 42 WP2
→ §§42.4–42.6
```

WP2 receives no preferred granularity, no selected operation result, no pressure-object Output Class, no final constituent, no ultimate composite, and no world-structure claim. It receives a precise premise: every operation works on declared analytical relations, and no successful operation may convert those relations into an ontology by implication.

<a id="42-4-no-final-constituents"></a>

### 42.4 No Final Constituents

The anti-ontology boundary becomes operation-specific when a compressed object is opened. `DECOMPOSE` may reconstruct finer internal structures, relations, temporal orderings, role distributions, non-events, and reproduction conditions. None of these achievements warrants the claim that the analysis has reached the last constituents of the reference object.

The distinction is categorical:

```text
finer reconstruction
≠ final constituent discovery

successful DECOMPOSE
≠ ontological descent

additional supported detail
≠ fundamental reality
```

A decomposition is a controlled change of analytical resolution. It is not a metaphysical method for discovering what reality is ultimately made of.

#### C42-NOFINALCONSTITUENTS-01 — `DECOMPOSE` never discovers last constituents

`DECOMPOSE` begins from an identifiable occurrence or composite that has been treated as provisionally compressed. It asks whether a finer reconstruction of that same reference object produces warranted praxeological gain. The answer can be strong, weak, neutral, failed, stopped, or non-capturing. It cannot be: “the final parts have now been found,” because the operation has no rule capable of licensing that conclusion.

A finer reconstruction may identify:

- operator-typed occurrences;
- local configurations;
- event and non-event structures;
- transitions and temporal orderings;
- role and resource relations;
- repeated reproduction practices;
- subpaths and internal branch structures;
- distributed asymmetries, bindings, or attractor-supporting relations.

These are analytical components under a declared question. They are not automatically ultimate units. The same component may remain provisionally compressed for another claim. A local practice may be a fine component relative to an institutional arrangement while remaining a composite relative to its own interaction sequence. A documented decision may be treated as an event for one path reconstruction and opened into preparation, delay, negotiation, non-decision, and implementation phases for another.

```text
component at g2
≠ indivisible object

current stopping point
≠ absolute boundary of reality
```

The operation therefore carries **provisional elementarity**, not ontological indivisibility. Chapter 18 owns that definition. Chapter 42 draws its anti-ontological consequence: the absence of a warranted reason to decompose further does not establish that further constituents do not exist; it establishes only that further decomposition is not presently required, supported, relevant, or admissible for the declared claim.

This boundary also preserves PMS Base. `DECOMPOSE` may open concrete operator-typed occurrences and composites structured by Δ–Ψ. It may not decompose an operator type as though Difference, Frame, Non-Event, Attractor, Asymmetry, Temporality, or another base operator were themselves empirical composites whose final ingredients STRATA could reveal.

```text
operator-typed occurrence
→ potentially decomposable

operator type
→ not a DECOMPOSE source object
```

An occurrence-level decomposition can show which supported practices carry a Frame-typed occurrence, which repeated relations stabilize an Attractor-typed occurrence, or which role and cost relations constitute an Asymmetry-typed occurrence. It does not transform that reconstruction into a theory of the final constituents of □, Α, or Ω.

#### C42-DECOMPOSITIONDEPENDENCE-01 — every decomposition remains dependent on Frame, source, granularity, question, and Claim Scope

The anti-finality rule is not merely a philosophical disclaimer. It follows from the declared dependencies of every valid `DECOMPOSE` occurrence.

At minimum, the operation depends on:

```text
reference object
+ source Frame
+ source basis
+ source granularity
+ target granularity
+ decomposition question
+ expected praxeological difference
+ temporal and source scope
+ Claim Scope
+ preservation criterion
```

Change these coordinates and the decomposition claim may change.

A different Frame may make other internal relations relevant. A wider source basis may support distinctions that were previously unavailable or destabilize an earlier component model. A finer granularity may separate previously compressed transitions. A different question may render the same distinctions irrelevant. A narrower Claim Scope may support a local result that cannot carry the earlier broad claim.

Consequently, no component list can detach from the conditions under which it was reconstructed and become the reference object’s unconditional inventory.

```text
components reconstructed for Q1 under C1 and g2
≠ complete inventory of X in all contexts
```

The dependency burden remains visible even where a decomposition is highly stable. Repeated support across sources can justify a strong bounded component-and-relation claim. It does not remove the Frame, source, granularity, question, or Claim Scope from the warrant. Stability strengthens the reconstruction within its declared range; it does not make the range disappear.

The five-part Loss declaration remains mandatory:

```yaml
loss:
  preserved:
  compressed:
  excluded:
  uncertain:
  irrecoverable:
```

A finer reconstruction can still compress wider relations, exclude material outside the question, remain uncertain about intermediate structures, or lose features of the earlier coarse object that cannot be rebuilt from the selected fine record alone. The presence of more detail does not make the Loss field empty.

#### C42-SOURCEOBJECTPRESERVATION-01 — decomposition may fail to retain the claimed reference object

The strongest counterpressure against final-constituent language is that decomposition can fail by losing the very object it claimed to open.

A list of supported fragments is not yet a decomposition of `X`. The operation must preserve a warranted reference relation between the finer structures and the source object. At minimum, it must show how the components and their relations:

- belong to the same historical or analytical reference object;
- carry, revise, differentiate, or defeat the source function;
- preserve relevant temporal and relational continuity;
- remain distinguishable from merely associated detail;
- permit a controlled return to the coarse claim or a justified revision of it.

If the finer record contains many accurate observations but no supported relation by which they constitute the declared source object, the operation does not reveal deeper truth. It fragments the reference.

```text
supported fragments
+ missing constitutive relation
→ source-object preservation failure

more detail
≠ successful DECOMPOSE
```

This failure can require `failed_transformation`, `claim_reduction_required`, `mandatory_stop`, or another canonically warranted result depending on the full Record. Chapter 42 selects no pressure-object Output Class. It establishes only that source-object preservation remains defeasible and that failure cannot be redescribed as discovery of a still deeper layer.

For the anti-ontology pressure packet, the operation view is therefore bounded:

```text
R1 or R2 opened at a finer granularity
→ possible DECOMPOSE claim

final constituents of I discovered
→ prohibited inference
```

`O0` cannot be rescued by saying that the local practices revealed through decomposition are what the institutional arrangement “really is.” The decomposition may support a precise relation between practices and arrangement. It may also revise or defeat the coarse claim. Neither result licenses reduction of the reference object to an ultimate inventory.

#### C42-COARSEOUTPERFORMANCE-01 — a coarser reconstruction may remain more discriminating for the declared claim

The possibility of successful fine analysis does not imply that the finest available reconstruction is the best one for every question. A decomposition can add distinctions while weakening the reconstruction of the relation that matters.

A coarser reconstruction may outperform a finer one when it:

- preserves a stable relation that the fine record fragments;
- supports the declared comparison with less speculative completion;
- retains a trajectory or institutional function that local detail obscures;
- avoids distinctions below the Praxeological Relevance Floor;
- remains more source-calibrated for the available material;
- carries the claim with lower irreversible Loss.

This is not an argument for coarse privilege. Chapter 43 owns comparative performance. Chapter 42 preserves the logical possibility required by its anti-ontology rule:

```text
finer reconstruction available
≠ finer reconstruction privileged

coarser reconstruction more discriminating for Q
≠ coarse layer more real
```

If `R2` provides finer interaction sequences while `R1` more reliably preserves the documented institutional relation relevant to `Q1`, `R1` may later outperform `R2` for that claim. Conversely, `R2` may expose a source-supported asymmetry that requires revision of `R1`. Neither direction establishes fundamentality. Performance remains claim-relative.

The stopping point of `DECOMPOSE` is therefore governed by relevance, source support, continuity, Loss, and Claim Scope—not by a presumption that reality becomes truer as resolution increases.

<a id="42-5-no-ultimate-composite"></a>

### 42.5 No Ultimate Composite

`COMPOSE` creates a new composite analytical object from selected and related source structures. It may form a sequence, path, trajectory, macro-event, distributed arrangement, or another bounded composite. It does not reveal an ultimate whole in which every relevant source, relation, alternative, and context has found its final place.

```text
wider composite
≠ complete totality

successful COMPOSE
≠ ontological ascent

high coverage
≠ final whole
```

A composite is analytically new because selected relations are made jointly legible. It is not ontologically final because its formation remains selective, loss-bearing, source-bounded, and open to rival composition.

#### C42-NOULTIMATECOMPOSITE-01 — `COMPOSE` never forms an ultimate totality

Every `COMPOSE` occurrence answers a bounded formation question:

- Which source objects are included?
- Which relations are constitutive?
- Which ordering rule is used?
- Which Frame and temporal scope define the composition?
- Which praxeological difference should the target object make visible?
- Which Claim Scope may the composite carry?

The resulting object can be robust and indispensable. It can preserve a long trajectory, disclose a distributed cost structure, or show how multiple occurrences jointly carry a function. Its strength does not depend on becoming the final whole.

An ultimate-composite claim would require the operation to establish that:

- all relevant source structures have been included;
- every constitutive relation has been retained;
- no alternative composition could change the claim;
- excluded and uncertain material is irrelevant in every admissible Frame;
- the temporal and contextual boundary is final;
- no outside structure bears on the target object;
- no non-capture remains possible.

`COMPOSE` has no such warrant. Its method requires the opposite: selection, exclusion, uncertainty, Loss, alternatives, and bounded validity must remain visible.

```text
composite adequate for Q
≠ totality of X

current widest reconstruction
≠ widest possible or final whole
```

The same source field may support several composites. One may foreground temporal sequence, another distributed asymmetry, another binding continuity, and another the relation between non-events and changed action corridors. They need not collapse into one super-composite. Attempting to absorb every valid relation into a single object may exceed the Traceability Ceiling and erase the distinctions the analysis was meant to preserve.

#### C42-COMPOSITIONSELECTION-01 — selection, compression, exclusion, uncertainty, Loss, and alternatives remain visible

The anti-totality rule is operationalized through the formation record.

A valid `COMPOSE` must disclose at least:

```text
source objects
selection rule
ordering or relation rule
formation rule
preserved structures
compressed structures
excluded structures
uncertain structures
irrecoverable Loss
alternative compositions
Claim Scope
```

Selection is not a defect that disappears when the analysis becomes more comprehensive. It is constitutive of composition. Without selection, no target object is formed; there is only an unbounded inventory.

Compression likewise remains necessary. A path or trajectory cannot reproduce every source detail at full resolution. The question is not whether compression occurs, but whether the compressed object preserves the relations required by the claim and declares what no longer remains visible.

Exclusion must be distinguishable from denial. Material can fall outside the declared Frame or Claim Scope without being judged unreal or irrelevant in every context. Uncertainty must remain uncertainty rather than being smoothed into macro-coherence. Irrecoverable Loss must remain visible rather than being retroactively filled by the target label.

Alternative compositions provide essential counterpressure. The same source set may admit different bounded ordering, grouping, or relation rules. An admissible composite need not defeat every rival. It must show why its own selection and formation are warranted for the declared claim.

For the pressure packet:

```text
selected practices and relations S
COMPOSE as institutional composite I for Q1
→ possible bounded analytical object

I as the ultimate whole of all relevant praxis
→ prohibited inference
```

The relation between local practices and `I` may be strong. The anti-ontology boundary does not weaken it. It prevents the composite label from concealing what was selected, compressed, excluded, uncertain, or irrecoverably lost.

A macro-object can therefore be more than a convenient summary and less than a totality. It can possess genuine praxeological load because its source relations alter what can be seen, compared, or claimed. That load remains traceable rather than ontologically self-sufficient.

The rule also blocks a common repair strategy: when a composite is challenged, the analyst cannot simply broaden it until the counterexample appears inside the label. Each broadened composition is a new claim with new selection, relation, Loss, and traceability burdens. Unlimited inclusion does not turn a failed composition into an ultimate whole.

```text
broader composition
= new testable claim

broader composition
≠ immunity through inclusion
```

<a id="42-6-no-ontological-promotion-through-projection"></a>

### 42.6 No Ontological Promotion through Projection

`PROJECT_AS` is the operation most directly exposed to ontological promotion because it permits one object to perform a function in another context. The temptation is to convert contextual function into a new kind of being: a trajectory functions as a Frame, therefore the trajectory has become a Frame; a composite functions as an Attractor, therefore it is an Attractor layer; a recurrent profile modulates several occurrences, therefore it constitutes a new operator or stratum.

STRATA rejects each conversion.

```text
bounded target function
≠ new origin type
≠ operator identity
≠ ontological promotion
≠ new reality layer
```

#### C42-NOONTOLOGICALPROMOTION-01 — `PROJECT_AS` adds a bounded function without changing ontology or origin type

A valid projection retains the source object’s origin type and historical reference. It specifies a target context in which concrete source features carry a bounded function. The projection can be analytically powerful. It can show that a completed trajectory structures the relevance field of a later scene, that a composite produces attractor-like stabilization within a target process, or that a bounded profile modulates the weighting of existing operator-typed occurrences.

None of these results changes what the source object is as an origin-typed object.

```text
trajectory PROJECT_AS frame-function
→ trajectory remains trajectory

composite PROJECT_AS attractor-function
→ composite does not become Α as operator type

bounded profile PROJECT_AS modulating function
→ no new PMS primitive
```

This preservation is not semantic caution alone. It is required by `TypeIntegrity`, Reference Continuity, Functional Continuity, Contextual Boundedness, Constitutive Source Trace, and Counterfactual Sensitivity.

The target function must be carried by identifiable source features. Relevant changes in those features must be capable of changing or defeating the function. The function must remain valid only within its declared target context and Claim Scope. If the target label survives independently of the source object, the result has crossed from projection into label substitution or abstraction without traceable load.

Ontological promotion occurs when the contextual target function is allowed to overwrite one or more of these boundaries. Typical forms include:

- the origin type disappears from the formulation;
- the target function is restated as the object’s true identity;
- contextual validity is generalized across scenes without a new test;
- a PMS operator name is treated as a real class of objects;
- a derived function is converted into a new primitive;
- an analytical target level is described as a new layer of reality;
- a person, institution, or practice is fixed by the projected label.

These are not stronger versions of `PROJECT_AS`. They are type, context, traceability, and authority failures.

The anti-ontology pressure packet therefore preserves the following operation view:

```text
R1 or R2 PROJECT_AS bounded target function F in context Ct
→ possible projection claim

source object promoted into a new ontological stratum
→ prohibited inference
```

The operation does not decide whether the source object exists “at” the target level in reality. It decides, if admissible, whether the source object can perform the declared function in that context while remaining traceable as the same origin-typed reference object.

This boundary also prevents projection from becoming covert metaphysics through language. Phrases such as “the trajectory is a Frame,” “the institution is an Attractor,” or “the composite is a higher-order operator” are incomplete unless clearly marked as bounded functional shorthand. The full claim must remain recoverable:

> Within target context `Ct`, source object `X`, whose origin type remains `To`, can be projected as bounded target function `Ft` for claim `Q`, because specified source features and relations carry that function under the declared Source Trace, continuity, sensitivity, Loss, and validity conditions.

The formulation may be shortened in local prose only when those conditions remain available in the Record and surrounding argument.

Projection therefore neither promotes the source object upward nor translates it into a deeper category. It changes the analytical function assigned within a declared context. The movement is contextual and claim-bound, not ontological.

#### C42-WP2-HANDOFF-01 — WP2 hands WP3 operation-specific anti-ontology limits without assigning world structure

WP2 establishes the following stable operation-specific boundaries:

- `DECOMPOSE` reconstructs finer structures but never discovers final constituents;
- every decomposition remains dependent on Frame, source basis, granularity, question, and Claim Scope;
- a decomposition can fail because it does not preserve the declared source object;
- a coarser reconstruction may remain more discriminating for the declared claim;
- `COMPOSE` forms selective, loss-bearing analytical objects but never an ultimate totality;
- selection, compression, exclusion, uncertainty, irrecoverable Loss, and alternative composition remain visible;
- `PROJECT_AS` adds a bounded target function while preserving origin type and reference identity;
- projection creates neither operator identity, new primitive, ontological promotion, nor a new reality layer;
- `O0` remains a prohibited inference;
- `R1` and `R2` remain unadjudicated and no preferred granularity is selected.

WP2 does not yet systematize the distinction between the Δ–Ψ operator grammar and world structure, define the full ontology-drift marker set, or establish the canonical corrective formulation. Those tasks belong to WP3.

The controlled handoff is:

```text
no-final-constituents rule established
+ no-ultimate-composite rule established
+ no-ontological-promotion rule established
+ source-object and origin-type preservation retained
+ coarse-outperformance possibility preserved
+ O0 prohibited as inference
+ R1 and R2 unadjudicated

→ Chapter 42 WP3
→ §§42.7–42.9
```

WP3 receives no ontology claim, no preferred granularity, no pressure-object Output Class, no new operator, no new primitive, and no world inventory. It receives three precise negative limits on the existing operation grammar and the task of stating how that grammar must be distinguished from claims about the structure of reality.

<a id="42-7-operator-grammar-versus-world-structure"></a>

### 42.7 Operator Grammar versus World Structure

STRATA depends on PMS Base, but this dependence must not be converted into a claim that the world itself is arranged as a visible or hidden sequence of Δ–Ψ strata. PMS supplies an operator grammar for rendering praxis structures legible. It does not supply a particle theory, a catalogue of ultimate entities, a universal causal architecture, or an ontological inventory from which every object is literally built.

#### C42-GRAMMARWORLD-01 — the Δ–Ψ grammar is a reconstruction grammar, not a world inventory

The PMS operator sequence

```text
Δ → ∇ → □ → Λ → Α → Ω → Θ → Φ → Χ → Σ → Ψ
```

specifies one systematically motivated dependency path within PMS Base. It states how later operator functions become formally available relative to earlier ones in the grammar. It does not establish that reality is composed of eleven kinds of substance, that every process passes through eleven independently existing layers, or that a reconstructed object contains Δ–Ψ as physical, metaphysical, psychological, or social particles.

The grammar therefore supports claims of the following form:

```text
within Frame C,
source basis S supports an occurrence or relation
that is typed through PMS operator T
for bounded claim Q
```

It does not support the stronger inference:

```text
object X is fundamentally made of operator T
or exists inside a real T-layer
```

This distinction must be preserved even where PMS typing is exceptionally stable. A repeated Frame-typed occurrence can remain a robust and highly discriminating reconstruction. An Attractor-typed recurrence can carry substantial historical load. An Asymmetry-typed relation can organize exposure, role, cost, or exit across a long trajectory. None of these successes converts □, Α, or Ω into a kind of world-stuff.

Operator typing names a structural function in the reconstruction. It does not assert that the operator sign names an independently existing constituent.

```text
operator type
≠ world constituent

operator occurrence
≠ instance of an ontological substance

operator sequence
≠ universal causal history of every object
```

The same boundary applies to operator composition in PMS Base. An expression such as `Σ ∘ Χ ∘ Φ` can specify a formal relation among integration, distance, and recontextualization. It does not imply that a concrete scene contains three separable ontological modules or that its empirical causal order is already proven by the operator word. The operator expression is a disciplined reconstruction proposal whose source support, calibration, alternatives, and Claim Scope remain externally testable.

For STRATA, this has a direct consequence. A transformation may compose, decompose, or project objects that have PMS-typed occurrences or relations. The transformation never gains the authority to redescribe those operator types as the final architecture of reality. `COMPOSE` cannot assemble an ontological PMS whole. `DECOMPOSE` cannot discover PMS operators as last constituents. `PROJECT_AS` cannot promote a bounded target function into a new operator species or reality layer.

The anti-ontology boundary is therefore not external to PMS use. It is a condition of correct PMS use inside STRATA:

```text
PMS typing
+ source trace
+ bounded claim
→ possible structural reconstruction

PMS typing
+ ontological promotion
→ claim failure or mandatory correction
```

This does not weaken PMS Base. A grammar can be powerful, portable, and generative without being an inventory of what ultimately exists. Its strength lies in discriminating and organizing praxis-shaped structures under explicit limits, not in acquiring metaphysical authority through portability.

#### C42-NOOPERATORLAYERREALITY-01 — PMS operator order and `operator_layers` do not define real strata

PMS Base may use ordered or layered organizational language. The canonical sequence gives a dependency architecture, and the formal model may group operators into organizational layers for readability or implementation. These forms must not be imported into STRATA as ontological geography.

The required non-equivalences are:

```text
operator dependency order
≠ order of reality

formal organizational layer
≠ independently existing stratum

later operator in sequence
≠ higher being

higher-order formal function
≠ ontologically superior entity
```

A later position in the Δ–Ψ sequence does not make Ψ more real than Δ, Σ more authoritative than Α, or reflective distance ontologically higher than framed difference. The sequence is systematically motivated within the grammar. It is not a ladder of substance, value, consciousness, maturity, legitimacy, or person rank.

This prohibition is especially important where the organizational vocabulary resembles STRATA language. PMS operator layers, STRATA relative levels, granularities, Frames, and transformation contexts answer different questions:

| Term | What it organizes | What it does not establish |
| --- | --- | --- |
| PMS operator dependency order | formal availability and relation among operator types | temporal history or ontological hierarchy of all objects |
| PMS `operator_layers` | organizational grouping in the model | real layers of the world |
| STRATA relative level | local position in a declared reconstructive relation | universal micro/meso/macro class |
| granularity | resolution of distinctions | depth of reality |
| Frame | boundary of relevance and claim | container existing independently of reconstruction |
| target context | bounded site of projected function | higher or deeper ontological domain |

These distinctions also block authority drift. No claim inherits more truth, dignity, legitimacy, or application force because it invokes a later operator, a higher organizational group, or a broader STRATA level. The PMS order supplies formal discipline. It does not rank persons, practices, institutions, or worlds.

Where a concrete source claim depends on PMS, the occurrence-level route remains binding. The analyst identifies the warranted occurrence, relation, Frame, temporal position, source basis, and relevant dependency context. A complete Δ–Ψ inventory is not required unless the claim itself depends on the complete operator chain. Conversely, merely naming the operator sequence cannot compensate for missing source trace.

```text
claim-relevant occurrence anchoring
≠ decorative operator sequence

organizational layer reference
≠ ontological layer evidence
```

The anti-ontology pressure statement `O0` violates precisely this boundary when it claims that institutional arrangement `I` is fundamentally composed of micro-Frames and exists on a higher real layer. The source materials may support Frame-typed local practices, a wider institutional composite, or a bounded function in another context. They do not support the conversion of the PMS type or STRATA level relation into a real-layer assertion.

#### C42-NOCOUNTERONTOLOGY-01 — rejecting ontological strata does not prove that stable structures are unreal

The anti-ontology rule is itself bounded. STRATA does not replace one metaphysics with another. It does not infer:

- that no stable structures exist;
- that parts and wholes are merely linguistic;
- that all levels are arbitrary;
- that institutions, trajectories, asymmetries, or bindings are unreal;
- that only local occurrences exist;
- that every macroclaim must be eliminated;
- or that reality is necessarily flat, structureless, process-only, relation-only, or inaccessible in principle.

Those would be counter-ontological claims. They exceed the authority of the method in the opposite direction.

STRATA's claim is narrower:

> The existence, fundamentality, or ultimate organization of reality does not follow from STRATA's analytical coordinates, PMS typing, or successful transformations.

A stable reconstruction may possess substantial warrant. It may survive rival comparison, respond to source changes, preserve reference identity, carry traceable load, and distinguish relevant cases. The anti-ontology boundary does not downgrade such a result to fiction. It keeps the claim at the level its sources and method can support.

```text
strong bounded reconstruction
≠ metaphysical assertion

anti-ontology
≠ denial of structure

reconstructive humility
≠ analytical weakness
```

This is also why `R1` and `R2` remain legitimate candidates. Their different granularities may reveal distinct structures of the same institutional arrangement. Either may later be supported, reduced, held provisional, or fail. Their reconstructive status does not depend on being ontological, and their non-ontological status does not predetermine their substantive result.

Chapter 42 therefore protects a middle position:

```text
warranted structure without ontological inflation
+ openness to alternatives and non-capture
+ no metaphysical denial
```

The method may state what can be reconstructed under declared conditions. It does not decide what reality must ultimately be.

<a id="42-8-ontology-drift-markers"></a>

### 42.8 Ontology Drift Markers

Ontology drift often enters through ordinary language before it appears as an explicit theory. A bounded analytical relation is shortened, generalized, or rephrased until its Frame, granularity, source basis, alternative constructions, and Claim Scope disappear. The remaining sentence then sounds as though it names the object's real constitution rather than one warranted reconstruction.

#### C42-DRIFTMARKERS-01 — ontology drift has identifiable linguistic and inferential markers

No phrase is automatically invalid in every context. The same wording can be harmless shorthand where the bounded formulation remains fully recoverable, or it can become an unsupported ontological claim where those limits have disappeared. Drift detection therefore tests the relation between language and the underlying Record.

The following markers require correction or explicit justification.

**1. Fundamentality markers**

```text
"X is fundamentally composed of Y."
"Y is the basic substance of X."
"Below this level lie the real components."
"The analysis has reached the final elements."
```

These formulations turn a chosen decomposition into a claim of last constituents.

**2. Totality markers**

```text
"This composite is the whole system."
"All relevant structures are contained in M."
"The macro-object is the complete reality of the case."
"The composition captures the total process."
```

These formulations erase selection, exclusion, uncertainty, irrecoverable Loss, alternative compositions, and Non-Capture.

**3. Real-layer markers**

```text
"X exists on the macro level."
"The deeper layer explains the surface layer."
"This higher stratum governs the lower one."
"Micro, meso, and macro are the real architecture of the domain."
```

These formulations convert relative analytical positions into independently existing and universally ordered strata.

**4. Identity markers under projection**

```text
"The trajectory is a Frame."
"The institution is an Attractor."
"The composite has become a higher-order operator."
"The projected function reveals what X really is."
```

These formulations may collapse origin type into target function, erase Contextual Boundedness, or create operator identity from functional resemblance.

**5. Reduction markers**

```text
"The macro phenomenon is nothing but its local parts."
"Once the microstructure is known, the wider claim follows."
"The coarse reconstruction is merely an approximation."
"All higher functions reduce without remainder to lower occurrences."
```

These formulations assign automatic privilege to finer resolution and suppress relation, emergence, Loss, or cross-granularity non-equivalence.

**6. Grammar-to-world markers**

```text
"Reality unfolds through Δ–Ψ."
"The operator layers are the layers of praxis itself."
"Every real structure instantiates the complete PMS chain."
"Objects belong to the operator level corresponding to their true form."
```

These formulations convert formal grammar into world inventory or universal causal architecture.

**7. Closure markers**

```text
"Anything relevant can be represented in the grammar."
"What is not captured is only missing detail."
"A failed reconstruction can always be repaired at another level."
"There is no remainder outside the chosen model."
```

These formulations immunize the grammar against Non-Capture and rival models.

A drift marker triggers a question, not an automatic verdict. The analyst must ask:

1. Is the sentence recoverable as a frame-, source-, granularity-, context-, and claim-bounded formulation?
2. Does the Record preserve origin type, alternatives, Loss, uncertainty, and Non-Capture?
3. Would a relevant source change alter the claim?
4. Is the apparent ontological wording only local shorthand, or does the conclusion depend on it?
5. Does the statement claim a type of warrant that STRATA cannot supply?

If the bounded form is recoverable and substantively controlling, the shorthand may be retained with clarification. If not, the claim must be rewritten, reduced, stopped, or marked as outside STRATA's capture.

Ontology drift is therefore not detected by forbidden-word counting alone. Formal tools may flag phrases such as “fundamentally,” “true layer,” “nothing but,” or “complete whole.” They cannot decide whether the substantive use is ontologically inflated. Human review must inspect the actual source relation and claim type.

The pressure statement `O0` displays several markers simultaneously:

```text
I is fundamentally composed of micro Frames
+ I exists on a higher real layer
```

It combines fundamentality, operator-identity, and real-layer drift. Correcting it does not require denying the local Frame occurrences or the institutional composite. It requires separating those warranted reconstructions from the unsupported ontology inference.

#### C42-NONCAPTUREOUTSIDE-01 — relevant structure may remain outside the current grammar or reconstruction

Anti-ontology and Non-Capture are linked. If STRATA treated every relevant structure as necessarily expressible through its current object model, operations, PMS types, and records, the grammar would become a total ontology by methodological default even while verbally denying ontology.

Chapter 42 therefore preserves at least four distinct possibilities:

```text
source material unavailable
→ source_non_capture

relevant distinction unavailable at current resolution
→ granularity_non_capture

required composite destroys constitutive heterogeneity
→ compositional_non_capture

bounded function cannot be traced or calibrated
→ projection_non_capture
```

It also preserves `semantic_non_capture` where the current grammar fails to express a relevant structure without unacceptable distortion. Such a result does not prove that the structure is ineffable, metaphysically unique, or beyond every model. It states that the present reconstruction has reached a justified capture limit.

```text
outside current grammar
≠ outside all possible knowledge

not captured by STRATA
≠ unreal

non_capture
≠ analyst failure concealed by a larger label
```

This boundary matters for PMS operator typing. A source may contain relevant relations that are not adequately captured by the available Δ–Ψ typing, or for which available evidence does not support a responsible occurrence assignment. The correct response is not to invent a new PMS operator, force the material into the nearest label, or treat the remainder as noise. It may remain unresolved, rival-modeled, or explicitly outside current capture.

Non-Capture also blocks retrospective ontology. A structure does not become ontologically basic merely because the grammar cannot decompose it further. Nor does it become unreal because the grammar cannot type it. The result remains a boundary of the present method and claim.

For `R1` and `R2`, Non-Capture remains available at multiple points. Either reconstruction may fail to preserve the institutional reference object, may require unsupported distinctions, may destroy relations through composition, or may not sustain a bounded target function. Chapter 42 does not decide these outcomes. It preserves them as methodologically legitimate possibilities against the totalizing assumption embedded in `O0`.

<a id="42-9-corrective-formulation"></a>

### 42.9 Corrective Formulation

The anti-ontology rule must be usable in actual writing and records. It therefore requires a canonical correction method that preserves the analytical claim where possible instead of merely banning strong language.

#### C42-CORRECTIVEFORM-01 — ontology-prone claims must be rewritten into a bounded reconstruction form

The minimal corrective formulation is:

```text
Within Frame C and granularity g,
X can be reconstructed through Y
for claim Q.
```

This sentence is a minimum, not a complete Record. Where the claim is contested, operationally consequential, or transformation-bearing, the fuller form should be recoverable:

```text
Within Frame C,
using source basis S,
at source granularity g_s and target granularity g_t,
reference object X can be reconstructed through selected structures Y
under relation R
for bounded claim Q,
with alternatives A and declared Loss L.
```

For operation-specific statements:

**DECOMPOSE**

```text
Within Frame C and source basis S,
X is reconstructed at finer granularity g_t
through components and relations Y
for question Q,
without claiming final constituents.
```

**COMPOSE**

```text
Within Frame C and temporal or structural scope T,
selected source objects Y are composed under rule R
as analytical object M
for claim Q,
with explicit compression, exclusion, uncertainty, and irrecoverable Loss,
without claiming an ultimate whole.
```

**PROJECT_AS**

```text
Within target context C_t,
source object X retains origin type T_o
and is projected as bounded target function F_t
for claim Q,
because specified source features carry the function under declared trace,
continuity, sensitivity, and Loss conditions,
without ontological promotion.
```

For PMS-typed material:

```text
Within Frame C and claim Q,
source-supported occurrence x is typed through PMS operator T
because relations r1...rn carry the relevant function.

This typing does not claim that X is fundamentally made of T,
that T names a reality layer,
or that the operator label exhausts X.
```

The corrective form performs several functions at once:

- it identifies the reconstruction as local rather than universal;
- it keeps Frame and granularity visible;
- it names the reference object and source basis;
- it separates source structure from analytical relation;
- it preserves alternatives and Loss;
- it states the Claim Scope;
- it prevents origin type from disappearing under projection;
- it leaves Non-Capture and rival reconstruction available;
- it blocks authority inheritance.

A correction is successful only when these limits govern the actual conclusion. Adding “within a Frame” to an otherwise totalizing assertion is not enough if the argument still treats the selected parts as final, the composite as complete, the target function as identity, or the operator grammar as world architecture.

The `O0` pressure statement can therefore be corrected without adjudicating `R1` or `R2`:

**Ontology-prone form**

```text
Institutional arrangement I is fundamentally composed of micro Frames
and exists on a higher real layer.
```

**Bounded reconstruction form**

```text
Within Frame C1 and granularity g1,
institutional arrangement I can be reconstructed through selected local practices,
Frame-typed occurrences, and their relations
for claim Q1,
subject to source support, relation trace, alternatives, and declared Loss.
```

An alternative remains possible:

```text
Within Frame C1 and granularity g2,
institutional arrangement I can be reconstructed through a different distinction set
for claim Q2,
without treating g1 or g2 as the real layer.
```

These corrections remove the unsupported ontology inference. They do not establish that either reconstruction is admissible, superior, exhaustive, or correctly PMS-typed. `R1` and `R2` remain unadjudicated.

The canonical correction therefore has a precise logical effect:

```text
ontological assertion removed
≠ reconstruction validated

bounded formulation supplied
≠ Output Class selected

anti-ontology correction
≠ preferred granularity
≠ empirical truth
≠ authority gain
```

#### C42-HANDOFF43-01 — Chapter 43 receives anti-ontology without a comparative resolution verdict

Chapter 42 establishes that finer, coarser, lower, higher, local, and composite positions possess no ontological privilege. It does not yet decide how competing granularities should be compared for a declared claim.

Chapter 43 therefore receives the following stable boundary:

- finer reconstruction is not more fundamental;
- coarser reconstruction is not less real;
- broader composition is not more complete or explanatory by default;
- local detail does not automatically entail macrofunction;
- macrofunction does not imply microhomogeneity;
- two granularities may be co-valid;
- performance must remain relative to question, Frame, sources, preserved relations, praxeological purchase, Loss, and Claim Scope.

The handoff is:

```text
no ontological privilege
+ no final constituent
+ no ultimate composite
+ no ontological promotion
+ grammar/world separation
+ drift markers and corrective formulation

→ Chapter 43
→ comparative performance without fine/coarse or high/low privilege
```

Chapter 43 may compare `R1` and `R2` only through declared performance criteria. It may not identify either as the real layer, infer that finer wins, or treat coexistence as automatic equivalence. Chapter 42 supplies the anti-ontological floor; Chapter 43 owns the comparative test.

#### C42-HANDOFF-01 — WP3 hands WP4 a complete anti-ontology method without adjudicating the pressure packet

WP3 completes the substantive Chapter-42 method. The chapter now establishes:

- analytical levels as locally declared relations;
- reconstructive relations without discrete reality layers;
- no final constituents through `DECOMPOSE`;
- no ultimate composite through `COMPOSE`;
- no ontological promotion through `PROJECT_AS`;
- the Δ–Ψ grammar as reconstruction grammar rather than world inventory;
- PMS dependency order and `operator_layers` as formal organization rather than real strata;
- explicit ontology-drift markers;
- a canonical bounded corrective formulation;
- no metaphysical counter-ontology;
- Non-Capture both within and outside the current grammar;
- a clean ownership handoff to Chapter 43.

The pressure packet remains protected:

```yaml
O0:
  status: prohibited_ontology_inference
  substantive_ontology_finding: none

R1:
  status: bounded_reconstruction_candidate
  result: unadjudicated
  preferred_granularity: not_selected

R2:
  status: alternative_reconstruction_candidate
  result: unadjudicated
  preferred_granularity: not_selected
```

WP3 selects no operation result, comparison result, route, Local Audit result, preferred granularity, or canonical Output Class for `R1` or `R2`. It creates no Case, Test, or Smoke YAML and does not repair the RETYPE artifact gap.

The controlled handoff is:

```text
Chapter 42 §§42.1–42.9 substantively complete
+ 24 assigned duties represented
+ O0 prohibited as inference
+ R1 and R2 unadjudicated
+ Chapter 43 comparative ownership preserved

→ Chapter 42 WP4
→ Contract and redundancy audit
→ exact 24-duty verification
→ pressure-packet non-adjudication check
→ provisional-lock decision
→ Chapter 43 Preparation Gate
```

WP4 receives a complete anti-ontology method. It receives no ontology verdict, no preferred reconstruction, no pressure-object Output Class, no Part-IV lock, and no authority to infer what reality ultimately is or is not.

---

<a id="chapter-43-no-privilege-of-finer-resolution-or-higher-composition"></a>

## 43. No Privilege of Finer Resolution or Higher Composition

Chapter 42 removed the ontological inference that finer, coarser, lower, higher, local, and composite positions correspond to independently existing strata of reality. That anti-ontology boundary is necessary but not yet sufficient. Even where an analyst accepts that levels are relational, a second privilege can remain: finer reconstruction may still be treated as epistemically deeper, while broader composition may still be treated as explanatorily stronger. The language of reality layers disappears, but the ranking survives in methodological form.

Chapter 43 rejects that residual ranking. Its governing question is:

> How should competing granularities and compositional scopes be compared when neither detail nor scale carries automatic epistemic priority?

The answer is not that every granularity performs equally well. Symmetrical limitation does not require symmetrical substantive results. A finer reconstruction may reveal decisive production conditions that a coarse reconstruction compresses. A coarse reconstruction may preserve a stable relation that a fine reconstruction disperses across unsupported or claim-irrelevant detail. A wider composite may establish a cross-scene pattern that no local record can carry alone, yet the same composite may lose traceable load through compression, homogenization, or relation dilution. The comparative result must therefore remain relative to the declared question, Frame, source basis, relevant praxis difference, traceability, Loss, alternatives, and Claim Scope.

Chapter 43 does not redefine granularity, relative level, or the three operations. It does not yet operationalize the full Praxeological Relevance Floor of Chapter 44 or the full Praxeological Traceability Ceiling of Chapter 45. It establishes the symmetrical non-privilege rule and the comparative architecture required before either boundary can be applied without directional bias.

For the chapter, one synthetic pressure packet remains stable:

```text
P0
→ finer reconstruction is truer,
  and a wider composite explains more

RC
→ coarse reconstruction candidate
→ stable function F for question QC
→ unadjudicated

RF
→ fine reconstruction candidate
→ heterogeneous production conditions H for question QF
→ unadjudicated

RH
→ wider-composition candidate
→ cross-scene composite K for question QH
→ unadjudicated
```

`P0` is a privilege assertion and is not accepted. `RC`, `RF`, and `RH` are reconstruction candidates. WP1 does not rank them, merge them, select a preferred granularity, decide comparability, adjudicate an operation occurrence, or assign a canonical Output Class. It establishes only why analytical direction cannot decide the comparison in advance.

<a id="43-1-symmetrical-limitation"></a>

### 43.1 Symmetrical Limitation

STRATA permits movement toward finer reconstruction and toward wider composition because both movements can disclose structures that remain invisible at another analytical position. The method would be artificially weak if it treated either movement as suspect by definition. Yet the method would become directionally biased if it treated one movement as presumptively superior.

Symmetrical limitation therefore begins with a double refusal:

```text
finer movement
≠ automatic gain

wider movement
≠ automatic gain
```

The symmetry lies in the burden of justification, not in the expectation of identical outcomes. `DECOMPOSE` and `COMPOSE` have different signatures, different preservation requirements, and different failure modes. A finer reconstruction may fail because it falls below the Relevance Floor, exceeds the Source Ceiling, fragments the source object, or loses the relation that made the source object analytically significant. A wider composition may fail because it lacks a constitutive ordering rule, compresses decisive heterogeneity, removes alternatives, or crosses the Traceability Ceiling. Equal limitation does not erase these differences.

#### C43-SYMMETRICALLIMIT-01 — both analytical directions carry an equal burden of warrant without requiring equal dispositions

The symmetrical rule is:

> A movement toward finer resolution and a movement toward broader composition must each establish their own claim-relative praxeological gain, source support, traceable load, continuity, Loss, and boundedness. Neither movement receives a presumptive pass from its direction alone.

This rule does not imply that every coarse and fine reconstruction should receive the same result. It permits asymmetrical substantive outcomes where the evidence and claim warrant them. For a declared question, `RF` may outperform `RC`; for another question, `RC` may outperform `RF`; for a third, both may remain co-valid, incomparable, underdetermined, or outside adequate capture. `RH` may establish a wider relation, or it may merely include more material without carrying additional explanatory load.

The comparison must therefore preserve two distinct levels of symmetry:

```text
symmetry of justificatory burden
→ required

symmetry of substantive result
→ not required
```

A method that required identical results would be insensitive to actual differences in source support and reconstructive performance. A method that assigned privilege in advance would be insensitive to failure in the favored direction. STRATA permits neither.

The rule applies not only to granularity but also to compositional scope. A wider temporal range, larger source set, or more encompassing target object may be appropriate. It remains a new testable claim. The additional scope must show what relation is gained, what distinctions are compressed, which exclusions remain, and why the resulting object answers the declared question better than the available alternatives.

#### C43-DIRECTIONNOAUTHORITY-01 — analytical direction cannot transmit epistemic, explanatory, normative, or application authority

Directional language can easily produce authority drift even after ontology drift has been removed. Terms such as “deeper,” “higher,” “more comprehensive,” and “closer to the mechanism” may begin as shorthand for granularity or scope and end as unearned rankings of truth or legitimacy.

Chapter 43 therefore extends the Chapter-41 authority boundary:

```text
finer direction
≠ deeper truth
≠ stronger warrant

wider direction
≠ greater explanation
≠ higher authority
```

The prohibition covers several distinct forms of inheritance:

- **epistemic authority:** detail count does not make a claim more true;
- **explanatory authority:** scope does not make a claim more explanatory;
- **normative authority:** neither direction generates an ought, verdict, or legitimacy judgment;
- **application authority:** neither direction authorizes recommendation, intervention, governance, sanction, diagnosis, or person classification.

A finer reconstruction may be more precise and still remain less supported. A coarse reconstruction may be highly source-supported and still remain too broad for a local claim. A wider composite may organize more material and still fail to discriminate among rival explanations. None of these performance differences changes the governance rule:

```yaml
governance:
  authority_inheritance: prohibited
```

The same boundary applies where PMS operator-typed occurrences are relevant. A finer reconstruction of Frame-typed or Asymmetry-typed occurrences does not gain authority merely because it names more local operator occurrences. A wider composite of temporally ordered occurrences does not become more authoritative because it contains a denser Δ–Ψ description. Operator occurrence density is not a substitute for source trace, relation preservation, or claim-relative performance.

#### C43-NONAUTOMATICPREFERENCE-01 — RC, RF, and RH remain unranked until declared comparison criteria are applied

The pressure packet is intentionally structured so that each candidate has a plausible analytical contribution:

- `RC` may preserve a stable institutional function, relation, or trajectory relevant to `QC`;
- `RF` may expose heterogeneous practices, local costs, asymmetries, delays, and production conditions relevant to `QF`;
- `RH` may relate multiple scenes or periods into a wider composite relevant to `QH`.

Plausibility is not ranking. The candidates cannot be ordered by counting distinctions, source documents, operators, components, scenes, or temporal extent. Nor may one candidate borrow the strengths of another before their relations have been tested.

```text
more distinctions in RF
≠ RF preferred

more stable summary in RC
≠ RC preferred

more included scenes in RH
≠ RH preferred
```

A legitimate preference, if one later becomes warranted, must state the question for which the preference holds and the comparison dimensions that support it. It must also preserve the losing or non-selected candidate's distinct claim where that claim remains warranted. Chapter 43 therefore rejects a universal preferred granularity while retaining local, revisable, claim-specific preference.

WP1 does not perform that later comparison. `RC`, `RF`, and `RH` remain unadjudicated and unranked. The chapter-level production status does not flow to the candidates.

<a id="43-2-finer-is-not-better"></a>

### 43.2 Finer Is Not Better

A finer reconstruction often appears methodologically attractive because it increases visible distinctions. It can separate roles previously compressed together, recover temporal order, identify local non-events, expose unequal cost distributions, distinguish repeated practices, and reconstruct the production conditions of a stable function. These are genuine possible gains.

The invalid inference is:

```text
more visible distinctions
→ better reconstruction
```

Additional resolution changes the distinction set. It does not by itself show that the new distinctions are praxeologically relevant, source-supported, relation-preserving, or appropriate to the declared claim.

#### C43-FINERNOTBETTER-01 — finer resolution has no automatic truth, depth, completeness, or authority privilege

Finer resolution is not intrinsically closer to truth. The finest available record may contain more detail while remaining less adequate for a particular reconstruction. It may show every recorded interaction but fail to preserve the procedural relation that makes the interactions intelligible as one institutional arrangement. It may distinguish many local variations while lacking evidence for how those variations connect. It may increase descriptive precision while reducing discriminative performance.

Chapter 43 therefore rejects four automatic privileges:

```text
finer
≠ truer
≠ deeper
≠ more complete
≠ more authoritative
```

**Truth privilege** fails because source density and claim validity are separate. A high-resolution record may be incomplete, selectively produced, unevenly distributed, or poorly calibrated.

**Depth privilege** fails because “deeper” is often an undeclared metaphor. A local practice occurrence is not more fundamental than the relation in which it participates. Chapter 42 has already rejected the ontological version of that inference.

**Completeness privilege** fails because finer reconstruction remains selective. It has its own Frame, granularity, temporal scope, excluded materials, uncertainties, and irrecoverable Loss. Opening one compressed object does not open every relevant relation.

**Authority privilege** fails because analytical resolution cannot raise the Claim Ceiling or create application competence.

A finer reconstruction may still be preferred for a bounded claim. But the preference must be earned through changed reconstruction, improved source trace, better discrimination, or another declared praxeological difference. It is never inherited from fineness itself.

#### C43-FINERCOUNTERPRESSURE-01 — fine detail can obscure relations, overreach sources, generate noise, fragment the object, or lose the source reference

The risks of fine reconstruction are not confined to “too much information.” They concern the structure of the reconstruction.

**Relation obscuration.** A source object may be analytically significant because several elements stand in a particular relation. Finer analysis can make each element more visible while making the relation less legible. A list of local practices does not preserve the institutional arrangement if the coordination, dependency, temporal order, or asymmetry among those practices disappears.

**Source overreach.** Fine distinctions require fine support. If the source material records only broad procedural phases, a reconstruction of minute local motives, role shifts, or operator occurrences exceeds the Source Ceiling. Formal detail cannot repair absent evidence.

**Noise and non-discrimination.** Additional distinctions can multiply variation that does not change any warranted praxis claim. The result may look richer while distinguishing no relevant cases. Chapter 44 will own the full Relevance-Floor procedure; WP1 retains the pressure without adjudicating it.

**Fragmentation.** A compressed occurrence or composite may be opened into components that no longer form a reconstructible source object. Supported fragments alone do not establish `DECOMPOSE` if the relation to the reference object cannot be preserved.

**Source-object loss.** A fine reconstruction can replace the original question with a new object. That move may be legitimate as a separate analysis, but it is not automatically a successful decomposition of the original source.

The counterpressure can be expressed as:

```text
more local detail
+ less relation trace
+ weaker source-object continuity
→ possible analytical loss, not automatic gain
```

The same issue arises with operator-typed occurrences. Suppose `RF` distinguishes many Frame-typed practices, Non-Event occurrences, local Asymmetry shifts, and temporal transitions. The occurrence trace may be valuable. Yet if the reconstruction no longer shows how those occurrences jointly sustain or alter the institutional function claimed in `RC`, it cannot claim to have replaced `RC` merely by being more detailed.

Fine detail therefore carries its own five-part Loss declaration. It may preserve local variation while compressing or excluding the wider relation. It may remain uncertain about aggregation. It may irrecoverably omit relations that cannot be reconstructed from the fine record alone.

The methodological consequence is not distrust of fine analysis. It is disciplined entry. Before further decomposition, the analyst must be able to state which warranted reconstruction may change, which source basis supports the added distinctions, which relation must remain visible, and what would count as resolution-neutral or failed output. Chapter 43 retains this burden without yet applying the complete Chapter-44 lower-bound procedure.

<a id="43-3-coarser-is-not-inferior"></a>

### 43.3 Coarser Is Not Inferior

A coarse reconstruction compresses. Compression is unavoidable whenever a reconstruction preserves a wider relation, compares multiple objects, or states a bounded function without reproducing every local distinction. The presence of compression does not establish analytical deficiency. The relevant question is whether the compression preserves what the claim requires and declares what it does not preserve.

A coarse reconstruction may be stronger for a particular question precisely because it does not reproduce every available detail. It can retain a stable relation, make comparison possible, remain within the Source Ceiling, preserve temporal or functional continuity, and avoid unsupported local inference.

#### C43-COARSENOTINFERIOR-01 — coarse reconstruction may be more discriminating, source-supported, relationally stable, or economical for a declared claim

A coarse reconstruction may outperform a finer one along several claim-relative dimensions.

**Discrimination.** `RC` may distinguish arrangements that sustain a stable function from arrangements that do not, even where local production conditions vary. A finer reconstruction may expose variation without improving that distinction.

**Source support.** The available sources may reliably establish a wider procedural relation while remaining too sparse for fine-grained reconstruction. In that situation, `RC` can be better warranted than `RF` without being more real or universally superior.

**Relation preservation.** A coarse object may preserve the temporal, functional, or compositional relation relevant to the question. The relation can disappear when its elements are inspected separately.

**Economy.** A reconstruction may carry the declared claim with fewer distinctions and fewer unsupported assumptions. Economy is not automatically a virtue, but unnecessary distinction can reduce clarity, comparability, and calibration.

**Comparability.** Coarse reconstructions can make several cases comparable by preserving a common relation while declaring compressed local variation. Fine reconstructions may remain locally strong but non-comparable because their distinction sets differ.
