The bounded claim is:

```text
RC performs better for QC
```

not:

```text
coarse reconstruction is generally superior
```

A local preference for `RC` leaves `RF` available for `QF`. It does not erase the heterogeneous production conditions that `RF` may reconstruct. The two claims may later remain co-valid, compete, or prove non-comparable. WP2 owns that architecture.

#### C43-COARSEPERFORMANCE-01 — coarse performance must be stated without coarse privilege, ontology, or suppression of Loss

Coarse performance is admissibly described only when its basis and limits remain explicit. A statement such as “the coarse model explains the institution” is too broad unless it specifies:

- the question for which the model performs;
- the Frame and reference object;
- the source basis;
- the relation or function preserved;
- the distinctions compressed;
- the excluded and uncertain structures;
- the alternatives retained;
- the Claim Scope and Claim Ceiling.

A bounded form is:

> Within Frame `C`, source basis `S`, and granularity `g_coarse`, `RC` preserves stable relation `F` and discriminates the relevant cases for claim `QC`, while compressing heterogeneous production conditions that remain separately reconstructible under `RF`.

This formulation protects both directions. It permits `RC` to be strong for `QC` without turning coarse scope into truth privilege. It also preserves the possibility that the compressed structures require a separate claim or later correction.

The five-part Loss remains mandatory:

```yaml
RC_loss:
  preserved: stable_relation_or_function_relevant_to_QC
  compressed: heterogeneous_local_production_conditions
  excluded: out_of_scope_or_unsupported_local_distinctions
  uncertain: local_variation_not_resolved_by_available_sources
  irrecoverable: distinctions_not_reconstructible_from_RC_alone
```

This Loss profile is illustrative, not adjudicated. Its function is to show that coarse adequacy does not imply completeness. A stable macrofunction may coexist with heterogeneous local components. The function does not prove micro-homogeneity, and the heterogeneity does not automatically dissolve the function.

Coarse performance also does not mean that the object occupies a more authoritative level. Chapter 42 has already prohibited ontological promotion. Chapter 43 adds that explanatory economy cannot become a hidden authority claim. A coarse reconstruction remains one bounded analytical object among alternatives.

#### C43-WP1-HANDOFF-01 — WP1 hands WP2 symmetrical non-privilege without selecting comparison results or scale-relative criteria

WP1 establishes the following boundary:

- finer reconstruction and broader composition carry symmetrical burdens of warrant;
- symmetrical limitation does not require identical substantive results;
- analytical direction creates no epistemic, explanatory, normative, or application authority;
- finer resolution carries no automatic truth, depth, completeness, or authority privilege;
- fine detail may obscure relations, overreach sources, increase noise, fragment the object, or lose source-object continuity;
- coarse reconstruction may be more discriminating, source-supported, relationally stable, comparable, or economical for a declared claim;
- coarse performance must retain exact Loss, alternatives, and Claim Scope;
- `RC`, `RF`, and `RH` remain unranked and unadjudicated.

The pressure packet remains protected:

```yaml
P0:
  status: privilege_assertion_not_accepted

RC:
  status: coarse_reconstruction_candidate
  result: unadjudicated
  preferred_granularity: not_selected

RF:
  status: fine_reconstruction_candidate
  result: unadjudicated
  preferred_granularity: not_selected

RH:
  status: wider_composition_candidate
  result: unadjudicated
  preferred_scope: not_selected
```

WP1 does not decide which candidate performs better, whether `RC` and `RF` are compatible, whether a wider composition carries additional explanation, or whether any candidate crosses the Relevance Floor or Traceability Ceiling. Those questions remain downstream.

The controlled handoff is:

```text
symmetrical limitation established
+ fine privilege rejected
+ coarse deficiency rejected
+ candidate ranking withheld
+ exact Loss and authority boundaries preserved

→ Chapter 43 WP2
→ §§43.4–43.6
```

WP2 receives no preferred granularity, no selected comparison result, no forced merger, no candidate Output Class, no Relevance-Floor result, and no Traceability-Ceiling result. It receives a precise premise: broader scope does not explain more by definition, performance is scale-relative, and coarse/fine co-validity must remain possible without collapse.
<a id="43-4-higher-composition-is-not-greater-explanation"></a>

### 43.4 Higher Composition Is Not Greater Explanation

A wider composite can extend analytical reach. It may connect scenes previously considered separately, preserve a longer temporal sequence, relate several institutional sites, or show that a local practice participates in a broader functional arrangement. These are possible gains. They do not follow from scope alone.

The invalid inference is:

```text
more included objects, scenes, or periods
→ greater explanation
```

`COMPOSE` creates a new composite analytical object through declared selection, order, and constitutive relations. It does not merely enlarge an existing object without consequence. The wider object carries a new burden of warrant because every additional source structure and every relation among source structures changes what the composite can support.

#### C43-HIGHERNOTGREATER-01 — broader scope or higher composition creates no automatic explanation, warrant, or authority

A higher composition may include more material while explaining less. It can aggregate cases that were discriminating at a narrower scope, obscure a relation that was visible locally, or stabilize a summary that no longer shows how its constitutive differences matter. It can also create apparent coherence by placing heterogeneous structures under one label.

Chapter 43 therefore rejects three automatic inferences:

```text
broader scope
≠ greater explanation

more included elements
≠ stronger warrant

higher composition
≠ higher authority
```

Explanation requires more than inclusion. A composite must identify which relations among its source structures are constitutive for the claim, what alternatives were rejected, what changes under counterfactual variation, and which loss is introduced by the wider scope. Without these burdens, the composite may be descriptively extensive but explanatorily weak.

The same boundary holds where the sources include PMS operator-typed occurrences. A composite containing more Frame-typed, Attractor-typed, Non-Event-typed, or Asymmetry-typed occurrences does not explain more because its operator vocabulary is denser. Nor does a longer Δ–Ψ dependency description inherit greater warrant from its formal range. The relevant question remains whether the occurrence relations carry the declared praxis claim.

A wider composite can also misrepresent temporal structure. More history may be included while less path trace is preserved. If earlier alternatives, transitions, dependencies, or irreversible exclusions are compressed into a smooth macro-narrative, the resulting object may have greater temporal extent but weaker trajectory or path reconstruction.

```text
more history included
+ less transition trace
→ wider narrative, not necessarily greater explanation
```

A local reconstruction can therefore outperform a wider one for a local claim, while the wider composite remains useful for a different claim. This is not an exception to Chapter 43. It is the normal consequence of scale-relative comparison.

#### C43-COMPOSITESCOPELOSS-01 — wider composition must disclose relation dilution, heterogeneity compression, exclusion, uncertainty, and irrecoverable Loss

Every wider composite requires a five-part Loss declaration. The declaration cannot be reduced to a generic note that “detail is lost.” It must show how the new compositional scope changes the analytical object.

**Relation dilution.** Relations that are constitutive at a local scope may become weakly represented when many scenes or structures are combined. A composite may preserve that several practices occurred while losing the dependencies that made one practice a condition for another.

**Heterogeneity compression.** Different production conditions, local costs, asymmetries, temporal rhythms, or operator occurrences may be summarized under one stable macrofunction. The macrofunction may remain warranted, but it does not erase the heterogeneity it compresses.

**Exclusion.** Wider composition remains selective. Some cases, periods, roles, failed attempts, non-events, or competing relations remain outside the composite. Their exclusion must be stated where it could affect the claim.

**Uncertainty.** A wider object can increase uncertainty about whether the same relation holds across all included materials. Similar labels or outcomes do not establish common production conditions.

**Irrecoverability.** Some distinctions cannot be reconstructed from the composite alone once local ordering, role distribution, or source provenance has been compressed. The original source structures may be required for later recovery.

An illustrative record view is:

```yaml
RH_loss:
  preserved: cross_scene_relation_relevant_to_QH
  compressed: heterogeneous_local_production_conditions_and_operator_occurrence_variation
  excluded: unsupported_or_out_of_scope_scenes_and_relations
  uncertain: whether_the_same_constitutive_relation_holds_across_all_included_sources
  irrecoverable: local_ordering_and_role_distinctions_not_reconstructible_from_RH_alone
```

This view introduces no new record grammar. The values belong in the canonical top-level `loss` fields of the Shared Transformation Record.

The wider composite must also disclose whether it preserves, revises, or merely coexists with the narrower reconstructions from which it was formed. `RH` cannot silently absorb `RC` and `RF`. If its claim requires both, their distinct source traces and limits must remain recoverable. If it changes the reference object or question, it is a new analytical object with a new testable claim.

```text
RC + RF + additional sources
→ possible RH

possible RH
≠ RC and RF proven
≠ RC and RF superseded
```

The result is a bounded compositional rule: a wider scope may be useful, but its explanatory performance must be earned through traceable relation and declared loss rather than inferred from size.

<a id="43-5-scale-relative-performance"></a>

### 43.5 Scale-Relative Performance

Once finer resolution and higher composition lose presumptive privilege, comparison requires declared coordinates. “Better” cannot remain an unqualified property of a reconstruction. A reconstruction performs better only relative to a specified question and the burdens that question imposes.

#### C43-SCALERELATIVEPERFORMANCE-01 — performance is bound to question, Frame, source basis, praxis difference, traceability, Loss, and Claim Scope

Chapter 43 uses seven mandatory comparison coordinates:

1. **Question.** What exactly must the reconstruction distinguish, preserve, explain, or render testable?
2. **Frame.** Within which analytical organization are the source object and alternatives being reconstructed?
3. **Source basis.** Which materials support the distinctions and relations claimed at this scale?
4. **Praxis difference.** What difference in practices, possibilities, constraints, costs, sequence, coordination, or function becomes visible or disappears?
5. **Traceability.** Can the claim be traced through the source structures and constitutive relations that carry it?
6. **Loss.** What is preserved, compressed, excluded, uncertain, and irrecoverable at this scale?
7. **Claim Scope.** For which object, time, context, granularity, relative level, and claim type does the result hold?

The comparative form is therefore:

```text
performance(R, Q, C, S, Δpraxis, trace, loss, scope)
```

not:

```text
performance(R) by scale label alone
```

This does not create a universal score. The coordinates structure the comparison; they do not convert heterogeneous judgments into one automatic ranking function. A reconstruction can be stronger on source support and weaker on local differentiation. Another can reveal a relevant asymmetry while losing the wider relation. The comparison must state which dimension matters for the declared claim and why.

A valid local preference has the form:

> For question `QC`, within Frame `C` and source basis `S`, `RC` better preserves relation `F` and distinguishes the relevant cases with lower speculative load than `RF`, while `RF` remains available for the distinct question `QF` concerning heterogeneous production conditions.

This formulation is stronger than a generic claim that coarse analysis is preferable. It also prevents a local preference from becoming a universal ranking.

Scale-relative performance applies to compositional scope as well. `RH` may outperform both `RC` and `RF` for `QH` if the question concerns a cross-scene relation that cannot be reconstructed from either candidate alone. But `RH` cannot borrow that local performance to claim superiority for `QC` or `QF`.

```text
RH better for QH
≠ RH better for QC
≠ RH better for QF
```

The relation among questions must remain explicit. If `QH` presupposes a local claim from `RC` or `RF`, that dependency must be traced rather than hidden inside the composite. If the questions are independent, their outputs should not be forced into one hierarchy.

The chapter also preserves non-compensation. Formal elegance, detail count, wider coverage, or model complexity cannot compensate for missing praxis purchase, traceable load, TypeIntegrity, Reference Continuity, Source support, or bounded Claim Scope. A reconstruction that fails a required condition does not become preferable because it excels on an unrelated presentational dimension.

#### C43-INCOMPARABILITY-01 — non-comparability and underdetermination remain legitimate comparison outcomes

Not every pair of reconstructions supports a direct ranking. `RC` and `RF` may answer distinct questions, use different source subsets, preserve different relations, or incur losses that cannot be ordered under one claim. In such cases, a forced “better/worse” judgment would create a false common metric.

Non-comparability is legitimate where:

- the questions differ materially;
- the reference objects are not the same and no explicit relation is supplied;
- the source bases support different claim types;
- the reconstructions preserve different constitutive relations;
- the losses are not commensurable for the current question;
- or the evidence does not warrant a priority judgment.

```text
not directly rankable
≠ analytically useless
≠ failed transformation
```

Underdetermination is likewise legitimate. Two reconstructions may be comparable in principle but the available sources may not support a warranted preference. The correct result is then that comparison remains underdetermined, not that the analyst may choose arbitrarily.

```text
comparison underdetermined
→ preference withheld
→ alternatives retained
```

This chapter does not select a canonical Output Class for the synthetic candidates. Later instantiated records must map their local findings to the closed ten-class inventory. The method point is that a comparison can legitimately preserve several candidates, reduce a claim, stop, or record Non-Capture rather than fabricate a ranking.

Chapter 44 will own the detailed Praxeological Relevance Floor and Chapter 45 the detailed Praxeological Traceability Ceiling. Section 43.5 uses praxis difference and traceability as comparison coordinates but does not adjudicate either boundary for `RC`, `RF`, or `RH`.

<a id="43-6-coarse-and-fine-co-validity"></a>

### 43.6 Coarse and Fine Co-Validity

A coarse reconstruction and a fine reconstruction can both be warranted without describing identical objects in identical terms. Co-validity is possible when each reconstruction states its own question, coordinates, source basis, relation, loss, and Claim Scope.

The minimal Chapter-43 pattern is:

```text
RC valid for QC
+ RF valid for QF
≠ contradiction by scale difference alone
```

For example, `RC` may reconstruct a stable institutional function across a period, while `RF` reconstructs heterogeneous practices, costs, delays, and asymmetries through which that function is produced. The fine reconstruction need not dissolve the stable function. The coarse reconstruction need not deny the heterogeneous production conditions.

#### C43-COVALIDITY-01 — coarse and fine reconstructions may remain simultaneously valid for distinct claims

Co-validity requires separate warrant. It is not created by declaring two descriptions complementary. Each reconstruction must independently satisfy the conditions appropriate to its claim. `RC` cannot borrow the detailed source trace of `RF` unless the relation is declared. `RF` cannot borrow the functional stability of `RC` merely because both concern the same broad institution.

A bounded co-validity statement is:

> Within Frame `C`, `RC` reconstructs stable function `F` for `QC` at `g_coarse`, while `RF` reconstructs heterogeneous production conditions `H` for `QF` at `g_fine`. Both claims remain locally warranted because their source bases, constitutive relations, losses, and scopes are separately declared.

This formulation preserves plural validity without universal compatibility. The claims may be mutually supporting, merely coexisting, partially tensioned, or non-comparable. Co-validity records that both remain warranted; it does not decide every relation between them.

The relation can therefore take several forms:

```text
co-valid + compatible
co-valid + partially tensioned
co-valid + non-comparable
co-valid + relation underdetermined
```

No form is selected for `RC` and `RF` in the pressure packet.

#### C43-DISTINCTCLAIMS-01 — co-valid reconstructions must retain distinct claims and coordinates

The most common failure of plural analysis is collapse. Once two reconstructions appear useful, they are combined into a single statement whose scale, source basis, and Claim Scope are no longer clear. Chapter 43 prohibits that move.

The coarse and fine claims must retain at least:

- distinct question identifiers;
- declared source and target granularities;
- explicit reference objects or reference relations;
- separate constitutive relations;
- separate source traces;
- separate five-part Loss declarations;
- separate alternatives;
- separate Claim Scopes and Claim Ceilings.

```text
RC claim record
≠ RF claim record

shared reference context
≠ shared claim identity
```

This distinction is especially important where one reconstruction uses PMS operator-typed occurrences. `RF` may reconstruct local occurrences of Frame, Attractor, Non-Event, Transformation, or Asymmetry within the production conditions of `F`. `RC` may reconstruct the wider stable function without assigning one operator identity to the composite. The occurrence analysis and the functional claim remain related but non-identical.

A shared label such as “institutional structure” cannot replace the distinct records. Label continuity is not Reference Continuity, and a common topic is not a common claim.

#### C43-NOFORCEDMERGER-01 — compatible, competing, incomparable, or differently scoped reconstructions must not be forced into one representation

Integration is an analytical operation only where a new composite object is actually warranted. It is not a default response to multiple valid granularities. A forced merger can erase the very distinctions Chapter 43 is designed to protect.

No merger is required where:

- the reconstructions answer different questions;
- one is locally valid and the other temporally or compositionally wider;
- the source bases cannot support a common relation;
- their losses would be hidden by a common summary;
- the candidates are in genuine tension;
- or their relation remains underdetermined.

```text
multiple warranted reconstructions
≠ mandatory COMPOSE
```

Where integration is pursued, it creates a new claim and must pass the full `COMPOSE` burdens. Selection, order, constitutive relations, alternatives, and loss must be declared. The resulting composite cannot retroactively convert `RC` and `RF` into one original claim.

A deliberate non-merger can therefore be the more traceable result. It preserves what each reconstruction establishes and prevents explanatory inflation. Optional Stop remains available where further integration is technically possible but adds no warranted praxis purchase. Mandatory Stop remains available where integration would exceed source, type, continuity, traceability, or claim boundaries. Neither Stop creates an additional Output Class.

#### C43-WP2-HANDOFF-01 — WP2 hands WP3 scale-relative comparison and co-validity without reduction or candidate disposition

WP2 establishes the following architecture:

- higher composition creates no automatic explanatory, evidential, or authority gain;
- a wider composite must disclose relation dilution, heterogeneity compression, exclusion, uncertainty, and irrecoverable Loss;
- performance is relative to question, Frame, source basis, praxis difference, traceability, Loss, and Claim Scope;
- no universal scale score or automatic ranking is available;
- non-comparability and underdetermination are legitimate comparison results;
- coarse and fine reconstructions may remain co-valid for distinct claims;
- co-validity does not establish compatibility, entailment, or common claim identity;
- distinct coordinates, traces, losses, alternatives, and scopes must remain visible;
- multiple warranted reconstructions do not require forced integration.

The pressure packet remains protected:

```yaml
P0:
  status: privilege_assertion_not_accepted

RC:
  status: coarse_reconstruction_candidate
  result: unadjudicated
  preferred_granularity: not_selected

RF:
  status: fine_reconstruction_candidate
  result: unadjudicated
  preferred_granularity: not_selected

RH:
  status: wider_composition_candidate
  result: unadjudicated
  preferred_scope: not_selected

comparison:
  ranking_selected: false
  compatibility_selected: false
  forced_merger: prohibited
  relevance_floor_result: not_selected
  traceability_ceiling_result: not_selected
```

WP2 does not decide whether `RC`, `RF`, and `RH` are admissible, which candidate performs better, whether `RC` and `RF` are compatible, whether micro-support entails a macroclaim, or whether macrofunction requires micro-homogeneity. It selects no route and no canonical Output Class for any pressure object.

The controlled handoff is:

```text
higher-composition privilege rejected
+ scale-relative criteria established
+ co-validity protected
+ distinct claims preserved
+ forced merger prohibited
+ non-comparability and underdetermination retained

→ Chapter 43 WP3
→ §§43.7–43.10
```

WP3 receives a comparison architecture without a comparison verdict. It must establish no automatic reduction, the comparative granularity test, granularity humility, and the LIMITS Record-View control while preserving the closed operation, Rule, audit-stage, Output-Class, sensitivity, loss-field, and schema inventories.

<a id="43-7-no-automatic-reduction"></a>

### 43.7 No Automatic Reduction

Chapter 43 has rejected privilege in both analytical directions. The remaining risk is reduction: the assumption that a finer reconstruction can replace a wider claim merely because it exposes more local structure, or that a set of locally supported findings can be assembled into a macroclaim without a separately warranted relation.

Reduction is not prohibited as a possible result of inquiry. It is prohibited as an automatic inference from granularity alone. A macroclaim may fail when finer reconstruction removes the relations on which it depended. A macroclaim may also remain warranted when finer reconstruction reveals heterogeneous conditions that jointly sustain the wider function. The result depends on the declared claim, reference relation, source basis, and constitutive burden—not on the direction of analysis.

```text
finer reconstruction available
≠ macroclaim replaced

local findings supported
≠ macroclaim entailed
```

#### C43-NOAUTOREDUCTION-01 — macroclaims are neither automatically replaced by microclaims nor automatically derived from them

A coarse or macro reconstruction and a set of fine or local reconstructions are different claims unless their identity has been established. They may concern the same institutional arrangement while selecting different reference relations, temporal spans, constitutive burdens, and Praxis differences.

`RC` may state that a stable function `F` is reconstructible across a declared period for question `QC`. `RF` may state that heterogeneous practices, delays, exclusions, and asymmetries `H` produce or condition that function for question `QF`. Even where both are source-supported, neither statement automatically replaces the other.

Automatic downward replacement fails because a macroclaim may concern a relation not exhausted by an inventory of components. A set of local occurrences can leave unresolved:

- their ordering;
- their coordination;
- their distribution across scenes;
- their persistence across time;
- their mutual compensation;
- their aggregate or emergent functional relation;
- and the Loss introduced when local variation is compressed into a wider claim.

Automatic upward derivation fails for the inverse reason. Local support does not establish that the selected local findings jointly constitute the macrofunction, that omitted local conditions are irrelevant, or that the relation persists across the macroclaim's declared scope.

```text
micro-description completeness
≠ macro-relation warrant

macro-relation warrant
≠ exhaustive micro-description
```

A reduction claim is therefore a new testable claim. It must state at least:

- the source and target claims;
- the alleged replacement or derivation relation;
- the common reference object or explicit reference relation;
- the relevant Frames and granularities;
- the constitutive source trace;
- the preservation criterion;
- the five-part Loss;
- the alternatives;
- and the bounded Claim Scope.

Where those burdens are absent, STRATA preserves the claims separately. It does not use the finer claim as a universal solvent and does not use the wider claim as an unexplained container.

The same rule governs PMS operator-typed occurrences. A fine reconstruction may identify several occurrences typed under □, Α, Λ, Ω, or other unchanged Δ–Ψ operators. Their presence does not by itself entail one wider operator occurrence, one composite operator identity, or one macrofunction. Conversely, a warranted wider function does not require every local component to carry the same operator type.

```text
many operator-typed occurrences
≠ one higher operator occurrence
≠ one new operator type

wider function
≠ uniform local operator typing
```

This is not a denial of dependency or composition. It is a requirement that the relation doing the explanatory work remain reconstructible.

#### C43-MICROMACROBOUNDARY-01 — micro-support remains distinct from macro-entailment, and macrofunction remains distinct from micro-homogeneity

The micro/macro boundary has two independent sides.

First:

```text
micro-support
≠ macro-entailment
```

Micro-support means that local structures, occurrences, relations, or practices are warranted by the declared sources. Macro-entailment would additionally require that those findings support the wider claim through an explicit constitutive relation. The relation may involve sequencing, recurrence, coordination, distributed dependence, selective aggregation, compensatory interaction, or another source-supported organization. Mere co-presence is insufficient.

Second:

```text
macrofunction
≠ micro-homogeneity
```

A stable wider function can be produced through heterogeneous components. Different scenes may carry different operator-typed occurrences, different burdens, different local costs, or different temporal roles while contributing to one bounded wider function. The macroclaim becomes invalid only where the required relation or stability fails—not merely because local sameness is absent.

This distinction prevents two opposite errors:

1. **homogeneity inflation:** inferring that all local components share one form because a macrofunction is stable;
2. **heterogeneity dissolution:** inferring that no macrofunction exists because local components differ.

A valid comparison must therefore ask what form of local variation the wider claim permits, what local changes would defeat it, and what relation carries the function across that variation. This is a Counterfactual Sensitivity burden, not a label-preservation exercise.

For `RC`, `RF`, and `RH`, the boundary remains open. `RF` may support, qualify, contradict, or remain independent of `RC`. `RH` may preserve, dilute, or fail to establish the relation between them. Chapter 43 does not select among those possibilities in the synthetic packet.

<a id="43-8-comparative-granularity-test"></a>

### 43.8 Comparative Granularity Test

Once automatic privilege and reduction are rejected, comparison requires a controlled test. The Chapter-43 test is qualitative and claim-relative. It does not generate a universal granularity score, a scale ranking, or an automatic Output Class. It asks which reconstruction carries the declared claim with the strongest warranted PraxisPurchase and TraceableLoad while preserving type, reference, continuity, source, context, Loss, alternatives, and Claim Scope.

The comparison applies only after the candidates have been delimited. Each candidate must retain its own record or explicitly referenced claim packet. A comparison of blurred candidates cannot establish relative performance because the relevant gains, losses, and constitutive relations are no longer attributable.

#### C43-COMPARATIVETEST-01 — qualitative comparison must examine discrimination, relation preservation, source support, PraxisPurchase, TraceableLoad, Loss, alternatives, and Claim Scope

The test uses eight linked questions.

**1. Discrimination**

Does the reconstruction distinguish materially different praxis configurations for the declared question? Additional detail counts only where it changes a warranted reconstruction, alternative, boundary, or claim consequence. Wider scope counts only where it enables a warranted distinction unavailable at the narrower scope.

```text
more distinctions
+ no changed praxis reconstruction
≠ comparative gain
```

**2. Relation preservation**

Does the reconstruction preserve the relations that make the reference object analytically identifiable? A fine reconstruction that lists components while losing coordination, sequence, dependence, or source function may underperform. A coarse reconstruction that compresses local differences while preserving the constitutive relation may outperform for the declared claim. The inverse remains possible where the coarse representation hides a decisive relation.

**3. Source support**

Which claim is actually carried by the available sources? Source abundance is not equivalent to source relevance. A fine reconstruction can exceed the source resolution. A wide composite can exceed the relations warranted across sources. A coarse claim can be better supported because the sources establish a stable relation but not its full internal production structure.

**4. PraxisPurchase**

What warranted practical difference does the reconstruction make? The test asks whether the candidate changes the reconstruction of action, constraint, option, sequence, dependence, expectation, blockage, distribution, or consequence relevant to the declared claim. Chapter 43 invokes this dimension comparatively but does not perform the full Praxeological Relevance Floor procedure owned by Chapter 44.

**5. TraceableLoad**

Can the candidate carry its claim back to the source structures and constitutive relations that support it? A wider label may remain stable while its source load disappears. A finer account may contain many supported details while the relation to the source object becomes unreconstructible. Chapter 43 invokes traceability comparatively but leaves the full Praxeological Traceability Ceiling procedure to Chapter 45.

**6. Loss**

What is preserved, compressed, excluded, uncertain, and irrecoverable in each candidate?

```yaml
loss:
  preserved:
  compressed:
  excluded:
  uncertain:
  irrecoverable:
```

Comparison must not reduce Loss to a single quantity. A candidate may preserve relation while compressing heterogeneity; another may preserve heterogeneity while losing wider temporal continuity. The relevance of each loss depends on the question and Claim Scope.

**7. Alternatives**

Have plausible rival granularities, compositions, relations, and no-transformation options been retained? A candidate appears superior too easily when alternatives are removed by definition. Comparative warrant requires exposure to at least the strongest supported alternative, including the possibility that no direct ranking is justified.

**8. Claim Scope**

Is the candidate's claim no broader than its source, relation, context, and continuity support? A fine reconstruction may support a local claim but not a period-wide conclusion. A coarse reconstruction may support a stable function but not the internal mechanism attributed to it. A wider composite may support cross-scene comparison but not one homogeneous causal story.

The test returns a reasoned comparison statement, not a scale verdict. Permissible forms include:

```text
RF performs better than RC for QF because ...

RC performs better than RF for QC because ...

RC and RF are co-valid for distinct claims because ...

RC and RF are not directly comparable under the current question because ...

comparison remains underdetermined because ...
```

The final mapping of an instantiated, delimited claim remains restricted to the closed ten-class inventory after the applicable audit. Chapter 43 does not create comparison-specific Output Classes.

#### C43-NONCOMPENSATION-01 — detail, scope, elegance, or strength on one dimension cannot compensate for missing constitutive conditions

Comparison is conjunctive at its load-bearing boundaries. A candidate cannot purchase admissibility through surplus performance on a different dimension.

```text
high detail
+ no PraxisPurchase
→ no compensation

wide coverage
+ no TraceableLoad
→ no compensation

formal elegance
+ failed TypeIntegrity
→ no compensation

strong source support for fragments
+ failed Reference Continuity
→ no compensation
```

The prohibition applies equally to coarse, fine, and wider candidates. A concise reconstruction does not receive a pass for economy if its claim lacks source support. A detailed reconstruction does not receive a pass for richness if it falls below the Relevance Floor. A wide composite does not receive a pass for comprehensiveness if it rises above the Traceability Ceiling. A formally precise projection does not receive a pass if origin type, target context, or Functional Continuity fails.

No weighted score may conceal these failures. The formal model may record comparison dimensions and local findings. It may not assign weights that convert a failed constitutive condition into an overall passing total.

```text
formal comparison record
≠ autonomous ranking engine
≠ substantive superiority proof
```

<a id="43-9-granularity-humility"></a>

### 43.9 Granularity Humility

Granularity humility is the controlled refusal to claim more comparative resolution than the sources, relations, and declared question warrant. It is not generalized skepticism and not a preference for coarse analysis. It preserves the possibility of strong local preference while refusing universal scale hierarchy.

A reconstruction can be clearly better for one question and irrelevant for another. Two reconstructions can be jointly warranted, directly competing, non-comparable, or unresolved. A further operation can be possible yet unnecessary. Current vocabulary can fail to capture the relevant structure. Granularity humility keeps all of these outcomes methodically available.

#### C43-GRANULARITYHUMILITY-01 — comparison must preserve bounded preference, co-validity, non-comparability, underdetermination, Stop, and Non-Capture without expanding the Output-Class inventory

The method must be able to state:

- **finer resolution not warranted** — the finer candidate exceeds the source basis, fragments the reference object, fails to alter the relevant reconstruction, or otherwise cannot carry its stronger claim;
- **coarser reconstruction preferred for the declared claim** — the coarse candidate better preserves the relevant relation, source support, discrimination, or economy within the stated scope;
- **multiple granularities co-valid** — distinct candidates remain separately warranted for distinct claims;
- **non-comparable** — no common question, reference relation, constitutive dimension, or commensurable Loss supports direct ranking;
- **comparison underdetermined** — comparison is meaningful, but the current sources do not support a warranted preference;
- **Optional Stop** — further comparison, decomposition, composition, or integration is available but adds no warranted PraxisPurchase;
- **Mandatory Stop** — continuation would cross source, type, continuity, relevance, traceability, calibration, claim, or authority boundaries;
- **Non-Capture** — the current candidates or grammar do not adequately capture the relevant structure.

These are methodological possibilities, local findings, or controlled formulations. They do not add Output Classes. Instantiated claims continue to map only to:

```text
admissible
admissible_with_bounded_claim
admissible_but_provisional
resolution_neutral
analogy_only
partially_admissible
claim_reduction_required
mandatory_stop
failed_transformation
non_capture
```

The wording “coarser preferred” is always local. It must identify the question, context, compared candidates, and reason. It never licenses:

```text
coarse analysis is generally superior
```

Likewise, “finer not warranted” does not imply that finer structure is unreal, impossible, or permanently unavailable. It records the current claim-source boundary.

Granularity humility also preserves re-entry. New sources, a revised question, a corrected reference relation, or a differently bounded claim can justify a new comparison. Re-entry does not erase the prior result.

```text
new comparison
= new testable claim

later preference
≠ earlier underdetermination erased
```

The same continuity applies to failure, Stop, and Non-Capture. A shift of Frame, granularity, compositional scope, or target function starts a new claim; it does not immunize the previous one.

#### C43-HANDOFF44-45-01 — the lower-bound and upper-bound procedures remain separately owned by Chapters 44 and 45

Chapter 43 has used PraxisPurchase and TraceableLoad as comparative dimensions. It has not defined the full boundary procedures.

The handoff is exact:

```text
Chapter 43
→ compares candidate performance claim-relatively

Chapter 44
→ tests the Praxeological Relevance Floor in detail

Chapter 45
→ tests the Praxeological Traceability Ceiling in detail
```

Chapter 44 must determine when additional differentiation or continuation produces `distinction without praxeological purchase`. Chapter 45 must determine when abstraction, composition, or projection produces `abstraction without traceable load`.

The boundaries are distinct. A candidate can pass one and fail the other. Detail can remain traceable but irrelevant. A broad claim can remain relevant but insufficiently traceable. No strong result on one side compensates for failure on the other.

```text
PraxisPurchase pass
≠ TraceableLoad pass

TraceableLoad pass
≠ PraxisPurchase pass
```

Chapter 43 therefore supplies comparative pressure and candidate separation. Chapters 44 and 45 own the detailed lower- and upper-bound adjudication structures.

<a id="43-10-limits-record-view-control"></a>

### 43.10 LIMITS Record-View Control

Chapter 43 requires comparison views, but it does not introduce a new record grammar. Every coarse, fine, wider, reduction, compatibility, or comparison statement must remain reconstructible from the existing Shared Transformation Record or from explicit relations among separately delimited Records.

A LIMITS view selects and displays fields relevant to a question. It does not create a second object model, new top-level record kind, fourth operation, thirteenth audit stage, or eleventh Output Class.

#### C43-RECORDVIEW-01 — Chapter-43 comparison views remain projections of the existing Shared Transformation Record

The comparison view draws from existing declarations such as:

- source and target objects;
- operation occurrence or integrated-chain scope;
- Frame and transformation context;
- source and target granularity;
- relative-level relation;
- reference identity or explicit reference relation;
- claim and Claim Scope;
- source support and Source Ceiling;
- PraxisPurchase and TraceableLoad findings;
- TypeIntegrity and continuity findings;
- Counterfactual Sensitivity;
- five-part Loss;
- alternatives;
- Stop and Non-Capture assessments;
- local findings, routing readiness, and final Output Class where actually selected.

The view may reference several Records. It must not merge their claims or results merely for display.

```text
comparison view of RC + RF + RH
≠ new composite claim
≠ integrated-chain result
≠ candidate ranking
```

Where `RC`, `RF`, and `RH` correspond to distinct operation occurrences, each occurrence retains its own Record. A comparison artifact references their identifiers and states the comparison question. Where a new `COMPOSE`, `DECOMPOSE`, or `PROJECT_AS` occurrence is actually attempted, that occurrence requires its own Record and audit.

No new schema field is required for Chapter 43. Existing fields can encode candidate identity, source/target granularity, claim segmentation, record relations, alternatives, Loss, and local findings. Human-readable Reference and audit artifacts may present a comparative table, but the table is a controlled view rather than an authority source.

The formal model may validate:

- that referenced Records exist;
- that candidate claims remain distinct;
- that required fields are present;
- that inventory identifiers are valid;
- that no fourth operation, extra audit stage, extra Output Class, or authority inheritance appears.

It may not determine:

- which candidate is substantively better;
- whether a source is materially sufficient;
- whether PraxisPurchase or TraceableLoad is actually present;
- whether the macroclaim is true;
- whether reduction is semantically valid;
- or whether any result authorizes action.

```text
schema-valid comparison view
≠ warranted comparison result
```

This control prevents record proliferation from becoming conceptual proliferation. More views do not produce more primitives, more operations, or more authority.

#### C43-HANDOFF-01 — WP3 hands WP4 a complete comparative method without candidate adjudication

WP3 completes the Chapter-43 method architecture:

- macroclaims are not automatically replaced by microclaims;
- micro-support does not entail a macroclaim;
- macrofunction does not require micro-homogeneity;
- reduction or derivation is a separate testable claim;
- qualitative comparison examines discrimination, relation preservation, source support, PraxisPurchase, TraceableLoad, Loss, alternatives, and Claim Scope;
- no strong dimension compensates for missing constitutive conditions;
- granularity humility preserves local preference, co-validity, non-comparability, underdetermination, Stop, and Non-Capture;
- Chapters 44 and 45 retain the detailed lower- and upper-bound procedures;
- comparison views remain views of the existing Shared Transformation Record.

The pressure packet remains protected:

```yaml
P0:
  status: privilege_assertion_not_accepted
  substantive_comparison_finding: none

RC:
  status: coarse_reconstruction_candidate
  result: unadjudicated
  preferred_granularity: not_selected

RF:
  status: fine_reconstruction_candidate
  result: unadjudicated
  preferred_granularity: not_selected

RH:
  status: wider_composition_candidate
  result: unadjudicated
  preferred_scope: not_selected

comparison:
  ranking_selected: false
  reduction_relation_selected: false
  compatibility_selected: false
  forced_merger: prohibited
  relevance_floor_result: not_selected
  traceability_ceiling_result: not_selected
  output_class_selected: false
```

WP3 does not decide whether `RC`, `RF`, or `RH` is admissible, which candidate performs better, whether the candidates are compatible, whether reduction succeeds, whether a Stop or Non-Capture result applies, or which canonical Output Class should be selected. It creates no Case, Test, or Smoke artifact.

The controlled handoff is:

```text
symmetrical non-privilege
+ higher-composition boundary
+ scale-relative performance
+ co-validity without merger
+ no automatic reduction
+ qualitative comparison test
+ granularity humility
+ Shared-Record view control

→ Chapter 43 WP4
→ integrated Contract and redundancy audit
→ exact 24-duty verification
→ P0/RC/RF/RH non-adjudication check
→ provisional-lock decision
→ Chapter 44 Preparation Gate
```

Chapter 43 is substantively drafted through §43.10. It remains open until WP4 completes the integrated audit and selects the bounded chapter-level disposition without transferring that disposition to `P0`, `RC`, `RF`, or `RH`.
---

<a id="chapter-44-praxeological-relevance-floor"></a>

## 44. Praxeological Relevance Floor

Chapter 43 removed any presumptive privilege from finer resolution, coarser reconstruction, and wider composition. That symmetrical limitation is necessary, but it does not yet answer the lower-bound question. An analyst may still introduce a distinction that is accurate, source-supported, and formally well described while leaving every warranted praxis reconstruction unchanged. The analysis then contains more resolution without containing an additional praxis finding.

Chapter 6 owns the compact definition of the Admissibility Band, the Praxeological Relevance Floor, and PraxisPurchase. Chapter 44 does not create a second admissibility system or redefine those terms competitively. It elaborates the system-wide lower-bound procedure through which an added distinction is tested against a delimited claim, declared analytical coordinates, source support, alternatives, and the exact reconstruction alleged to change.

The governing question is:

> When does an added distinction change a warranted praxis reconstruction rather than merely increase descriptive resolution?

One synthetic pressure packet remains stable throughout the chapter:

```text
L0
→ more detail is automatically more relevant
→ pressure statement not accepted

D
→ proposed component or temporal distinction d
→ unadjudicated

DG
→ candidate claim that d changes a declared praxis reconstruction
→ unadjudicated

DN
→ candidate claim that d is supported but changes no declared reconstruction
→ unadjudicated

DS
→ candidate claim that current sources do not support testing d
→ unadjudicated
```

The packet preserves three questions that must remain separate:

```text
Is D supported by the source basis?
≠ Does D change a warranted reconstruction?
≠ Which canonical Output Class follows after all applicable checks?
```

WP1 establishes the lower-bound architecture, the dimensions of PraxisPurchase, and the claim-relative coordinate burden. It does not decide `D`, `DG`, `DN`, or `DS`; it does not select a preferred granularity, a lower-bound result, a route, or a canonical Output Class. The detailed distinction between added detail and changed reconstruction belongs to WP2. Negative findings, `resolution_neutral`, Shared-Record mapping, Mandatory Lower Stop, and bounded re-entry belong to WP3.

<a id="44-1-definition"></a>

### 44.1 Definition

The Praxeological Relevance Floor is the lower relational boundary below which an added distinction, refinement, composition, or bounded functional claim creates no additional warranted praxis finding for the claim under review. The boundary does not ask whether the distinction can be stated, whether it is descriptively interesting, or whether further analysis is technically possible. It asks whether the distinction changes what may be warrantedly reconstructed about praxis-relevant structure.

Chapter 6 supplies the canonical formulation:

> **Below the Praxeological Relevance Floor, additional resolution is not an additional praxis finding. It is distinction without praxeological purchase.**

Chapter 44 takes this formulation as fixed. Its task is to make the lower-bound burden inspectable without converting it into a universal metric, numerical score, efficiency rule, or preference for coarse analysis.

#### C44-RELEVANCEFLOOR-01 — the lower boundary is crossed only where an added distinction produces no additional warranted praxis finding for the tested claim

The central lower-bound relation is:

```text
added distinction D
+ no changed warranted praxis reconstruction for Q
→ no additional PraxisPurchase for Q
```

This relation is claim-specific. It does not imply that `D` is false, unreal, meaningless, or irrelevant to every possible inquiry. It states only that `D` has not altered the warranted reconstruction carried by the present claim under the declared Frame, granularity, reference object, source basis, and scope.

A below-floor finding can therefore concern an accurate distinction. A meeting record may support a difference of several seconds between two turns, a document archive may support a more precise sequence of internal drafts, or a decomposition may identify an additional component occurrence. If none of those distinctions changes a warranted claim about the relevant action corridor, cost distribution, role relation, temporal formation, function, alternative, or Stop condition, the added resolution does not become an additional praxis finding for that claim.

The floor is not a ban on detail. It is a requirement that an asserted gain identify its praxeological load. Detail may remain useful for documentation, later questions, source preservation, or comparison. The lower-bound finding concerns the stronger claim that the detail improves, revises, narrows, defeats, separates, or stops the reconstruction currently under review.

```text
accurate added detail
≠ additional praxis finding automatically

no additional PraxisPurchase for Q
≠ D false
≠ D permanently irrelevant
```

The boundary applies to all three STRATA operations without changing their signatures. A finer `DECOMPOSE` occurrence must show what warranted source-object reconstruction changes. A broader `COMPOSE` occurrence must show what praxis relation the new selection and ordering establish beyond mere inclusion. A `PROJECT_AS` occurrence must show what bounded target-function claim changes through the projection rather than merely attaching a plausible label. The floor does not become a fourth operation; it tests the claim made by an operation occurrence or by a separately delimited relation among occurrences.

Where PMS operator typing is claim-bearing, the same rule applies at occurrence level. Naming more □-, Α-, Λ-, Ω-, or other Δ–Ψ-typed occurrences does not by itself add PraxisPurchase. The analyst must state which warranted reconstruction changes because the additional occurrence distinction is supported and load-bearing. STRATA never decomposes the operator type itself, creates a new PMS primitive, or treats operator-density as relevance.

```text
more operator-typed occurrence distinctions
≠ more PraxisPurchase
```

#### C44-NOUNIVERSALUNIT-01 — the Relevance Floor has no universal smallest unit, fixed threshold, or scale-independent hierarchy

The floor is relational rather than metrically fixed. STRATA contains no universal smallest praxis-relevant event, shortest relevant interval, minimum component, finest legitimate granularity, maximum useful detail count, or scale-independent threshold at which a distinction becomes relevant.

The same temporal difference may be load-bearing in one claim and neutral in another. A short delay may matter for a reconstruction of turn-taking, procedural exclusion, or bounded non-realization while remaining irrelevant to a claim about a multi-year institutional path. A component distinction may alter a projected function while remaining neutral to a separately bounded identity claim. A broader composition may change an integration claim while adding nothing to a local role reconstruction.

```text
relevant at g1 for Q1
≠ relevant at g1 for Q2 automatically

neutral at g2 for Q2
≠ universally below the floor
```

No number of seconds, documents, components, operator occurrences, relations, or compositional layers can serve as a universal threshold. Nor can a fixed score combine source abundance, detail, technical precision, explanatory appeal, and formal completeness into a substitute for the changed-reconstruction burden. A distinction either has a warranted claim-relative consequence or it does not; strength on unrelated dimensions cannot manufacture that consequence.

This prohibition also blocks a hidden hierarchy of scales. The floor does not say that coarse analysis is generally sufficient or that fine analysis is usually excessive. It permits strong local preference where warranted, but the preference must remain bounded to the question and analytical coordinates. Chapter 43's symmetry therefore remains intact:

```text
no universal smallest relevant unit
+ no automatic fine privilege
+ no automatic coarse privilege
→ claim-relative lower-bound assessment
```

#### C44-COORDINATEDECLARATION-01 — every added distinction and tested reconstruction must retain explicit analytical coordinates

A lower-bound assessment is impossible where the compared claims shift silently. The analyst must declare what is being added, to which object, under which relation, for which claim, and at which source and target coordinates.

At minimum, the assessment identifies:

- the reference object or source set;
- the operation occurrence or non-operational comparison under review;
- the Frame and transformation context;
- the source granularity and candidate granularity or compositional scope;
- the relative-level relation where relevant;
- the proposed distinction `D`;
- the prior warranted reconstruction `Q`;
- the reconstruction alleged to follow after `D`;
- the source basis for both the distinction and the changed relation;
- the Claim Scope and Claim Ceiling;
- the alternatives and five-part Loss relevant to the comparison.

A compact coordinate declaration is:

```text
Within Frame C,
for reference object X,
under source basis S,
from granularity g0 to candidate granularity g1,
D is tested for its effect on claim Q.
```

These coordinates prevent three common substitutions. First, a distinction relevant to a new question may not be presented as though it improved the original claim. Second, a change of reference object may not be hidden as finer reconstruction of the same object. Third, a wider target function may not be treated as though it were merely more detail about the source object's origin type.

```text
new question
≠ improvement of prior claim automatically

new reference object
≠ finer reconstruction of same object automatically

new target function
≠ additional source detail
```

Coordinates make claim relativity criticizable. They do not prove that the distinction is supported or relevant. A formally complete declaration remains only an assessable candidate.

<a id="44-2-praxeological-purchase"></a>

### 44.2 Praxeological Purchase

PraxisPurchase is the positive condition tested at the lower boundary. Chapter 6 defines it compactly as a claim-specific change in the warranted reconstruction of praxis-relevant structure. Chapter 44 elaborates which kinds of reconstruction change can carry that condition and which attractive substitutes do not.

The decisive requirement is not that a description becomes longer, more technical, more vivid, or more useful. The added distinction must require at least one warranted statement about the relevant praxis structure to be formulated differently.

#### C44-PRAXISPURCHASE-01 — PraxisPurchase consists in a supported change to a warranted reconstruction of a declared praxis dimension

An added distinction may possess PraxisPurchase when it changes a warranted reconstruction concerning one or more of the following dimensions:

- **action corridors:** which actions are available, constrained, delayed, costly, closed, or rendered non-responsible;
- **costs:** who bears material, temporal, procedural, reputational, or opportunity costs, and when those costs arise;
- **exposure and risk:** which actors or structures are exposed to uncertainty, dependency, sanction, delay, or irreversible commitment;
- **roles and asymmetries:** who can initiate, block, defer, interpret, transfer, or absorb consequences;
- **expectations and non-events:** which expected occurrence fails to materialize, within which bounded interval, and with what continuing structural effect;
- **bindings and commitments:** which promises, mandates, dependencies, obligations, or self-bindings constrain later possibilities;
- **temporal order and duration:** which sequence, transition, delay, recurrence, branch, or accumulation changes the reconstruction;
- **irreversibility and path formation:** which alternatives become unavailable, which costs are sunk, or which later corridor depends on prior formation;
- **integration and separation:** which structures can or cannot be composed, compared, or retained as separately warranted;
- **target function and projection validity:** which bounded function remains defensible in the declared target context;
- **claim scope and alternatives:** whether the claim must be narrowed, segmented, qualified, or placed beside a rival reconstruction;
- **failure and Stop:** whether continuation, stronger typing, composition, projection, or integration must cease.

The list is not a scoring catalogue. One supported change can be sufficient to establish a lower-bound gain candidate, while many weakly described dimensions can fail to establish any. The burden is constitutive: what changed, why it matters to the claim, and which source relations carry the change?

A change need not strengthen or enlarge the claim. PraxisPurchase can consist in narrowing a claim, separating two claims previously collapsed, revealing a countercase, identifying a hidden Loss, exposing underdetermination, or making Stop necessary.

```text
claim strengthened
or claim narrowed
or claim separated
or claim defeated
or countercase revealed
or Stop required
→ possible PraxisPurchase
```

The positive finding remains local. Passing the Relevance Floor does not by itself establish TraceableLoad, TypeIntegrity, continuity, Counterfactual Sensitivity, source sufficiency for every element, empirical truth, causal validity, or a canonical Output Class. Chapter 45 owns the detailed upper-bound procedure; the later LIMITS chapters and integrated audit retain their own burdens.

```text
positive PraxisPurchase finding
≠ complete admissibility finding
```

#### C44-NOACTIONABILITY-01 — praxeological relevance is distinct from intervention, controllability, usefulness, recommendation, and immediate actionability

PraxisPurchase concerns warranted reconstruction, not the analyst's ability to intervene. A distinction can matter because it shows that a corridor is closed, a cost is already incurred, an obligation has become irreversible, a non-event has stabilized an arrangement, or a source-supported alternative can no longer be responsibly claimed. None of these findings requires an available remedy.

```text
PraxisPurchase
≠ action recommendation
≠ controllability
≠ immediate usefulness
≠ intervention readiness
```

Conversely, an analytically useful distinction may remain below the floor for the current claim. A label may improve communication, a technical subdivision may support data management, or a finer chronology may be convenient for presentation. Those functions do not establish that a warranted praxis reconstruction has changed.

The distinction protects STRATA from two authority drifts. First, relevance may not be reduced to managerial usefulness or the availability of an intervention. Second, a relevant structural finding may not be converted into recommendation, sanction, governance authority, diagnosis, or person evaluation. The governance boundary remains:

```yaml
governance:
  authority_inheritance: prohibited
```

A source-supported claim about an irreversible structure can therefore possess PraxisPurchase while authorizing no action. A proposed intervention can appear practically attractive while possessing no warrant as a STRATA reconstruction. The two questions remain separate.

#### C44-SOURCELIMITDISTINCTION-01 — absent PraxisPurchase must remain distinct from source-limited or currently untestable relevance

The lower-bound question can receive no warranted answer because the current source basis does not support the proposed distinction or does not permit its effect on the claim to be discriminated. This state is not identical to a demonstrated absence of PraxisPurchase.

```text
source does not support testing D
≠ D has no PraxisPurchase
```

Three states must therefore remain distinct:

1. **supported distinction with changed reconstruction:** a possible positive lower-bound finding, subject to all remaining gates;
2. **supported distinction with no changed reconstruction:** a possible neutral or below-floor finding, whose exact interpretation remains WP3's task;
3. **distinction or effect not adequately supported:** a source-limited, underdetermined, or capture-limited state that cannot be relabeled as neutrality.

`DS` represents the third pressure possibility. It does not mean that `D` is irrelevant. It means that the current sources do not carry a warranted decision about the proposed difference or its claimed effect. New sources, a revised claim, or a more defensible coordinate declaration may later support a new test. That later test is a new claim with record continuity; it does not rewrite the earlier source limitation as though the limitation had never occurred.

```text
new source basis S2
→ new testable claim

later support
≠ earlier source limit erased
```

Source abundance is also insufficient. Many citations can repeat the same resolution, depend on the same archive, or document the distinction without carrying the relation alleged to change. Chapter 44 therefore distinguishes source support for `D` from source support for `D → Q'`. Chapter 45 will separately test whether a broader result retains traceable load from those sources.

<a id="44-3-relevance-is-claim-relative"></a>

### 44.3 Relevance Is Claim-Relative

Relevance attaches neither to a detail in the abstract nor to a granularity as such. It is a relation among an added distinction, a source basis, a reference object, analytical coordinates, and a delimited claim. The same source-supported difference can alter one reconstruction, leave another unchanged, and remain untestable for a third.

#### C44-CLAIMRELATIVE-01 — relevance is assessed relative to the declared claim, Frame, granularity, reference, source basis, and scope

For a lower-bound assessment, the minimally delimited object is:

```text
RelevanceFloor(D, X, C, g, Q, S)
```

where:

- `D` is the proposed added distinction;
- `X` is the declared reference object or source set;
- `C` is the Frame and transformation context;
- `g` is the relevant source/candidate granularity or compositional relation;
- `Q` is the claim alleged to change;
- `S` is the source basis.

This notation is conceptual, not a new schema field or record grammar. The existing Shared Transformation Record already provides the source, operation, target, context, claim, admissibility, Loss, alternatives, Stop, and result fields from which the lower-bound view can later be reconstructed.

Claim relativity permits differentiated findings without inconsistency. Consider a supported temporal distinction `D_t`:

```text
D_t relevant to Q_turn_order
D_t neutral to Q_annual_path
D_t source-limited for Q_asymmetry_transfer
```

These findings can coexist because they concern different claims and possibly different source burdens. The analyst may not promote the first finding into the assertion that `D_t` is generally relevant, nor use the second to deny its relevance to the turn-order claim.

Claim relativity is not arbitrariness. The analyst cannot choose a convenient claim after observing the result without preserving the shift as a new testable claim. Nor can the analyst alter Frame, granularity, reference object, target function, or Claim Scope merely to escape an earlier neutral, failed, stopped, or source-limited finding.

```text
changed analytical coordinates
= new testable claim

new claim
≠ prior disposition erased
```

The same rule protects operator-occurrence analyses. A finer distinction among operator-typed occurrences may matter to a claim about local formation while remaining neutral to a claim about a stable composite function. The operator types and their dependency order remain unchanged; only the bounded reconstruction claim differs.

#### C44-WP1-HANDOFF-01 — WP1 hands WP2 the lower-bound and claim-relative architecture without pressure-object adjudication or Output-Class selection

WP1 establishes the following controlled premises:

- the Praxeological Relevance Floor is the lower relational boundary below which an added distinction creates no additional warranted praxis finding for the tested claim;
- the Chapter-6 compact definition remains controlling;
- PraxisPurchase requires a supported change in at least one warranted reconstruction of a declared praxis dimension;
- PraxisPurchase does not require intervention, controllability, usefulness, recommendation, or immediate actionability;
- no universal smallest relevant unit, fixed threshold, scale hierarchy, or compensatory score exists;
- every lower-bound assessment retains explicit reference, Frame, granularity, source, claim, scope, alternatives, and Loss coordinates;
- absent PraxisPurchase is distinct from source-limited or currently untestable relevance;
- relevance is claim-relative but not arbitrary, and changed coordinates create a new testable claim.

The pressure packet remains protected:

```yaml
L0:
  status: pressure_statement_not_accepted
  relevance_privilege: not_granted

D:
  status: added_distinction_candidate
  result: unadjudicated

DG:
  status: changed_reconstruction_candidate
  result: unadjudicated

DN:
  status: neutral_candidate
  result: unadjudicated

DS:
  status: source_limited_candidate
  result: unadjudicated

lower_bound:
  result_selected: false
  preferred_granularity_selected: false
  output_class_selected: false
```

WP1 selects no lower-bound result, no pressure-object disposition, no route, no canonical Output Class, no Relevance-Floor record result, and no Mandatory Lower Stop. It creates no Case, Test, or Smoke YAML and does not repair the missing RETYPE lock-critical packages.

The controlled handoff is:

```text
lower boundary fixed
+ PraxisPurchase dimensions elaborated
+ actionability separated
+ universal threshold prohibited
+ coordinates declared
+ source limitation separated
+ claim relativity established

→ Chapter 44 WP2
→ §44.4 Detail versus Difference
→ §44.5 Lower-Bound Failure Forms
→ §44.6 The Changed-Reconstruction Test
```

WP2 receives no adjudicated `D`, `DG`, `DN`, or `DS`, no selected Output Class, and no permission to infer relevance from detail count, terminology, formal completeness, source abundance, or PMS operator-occurrence density. It receives one exact burden: any asserted gain must identify the defensible statement that changes because of the added distinction.


<a id="44-4-detail-versus-difference"></a>

### 44.4 Detail versus Difference

Additional information and additional praxeological difference are not equivalent. A reconstruction may become longer, denser, more technically precise, more chronologically segmented, or more richly typed without changing any warranted statement about the praxis structure under examination. Chapter 44 therefore treats detail as an input property and difference as a claim-relative result that still requires demonstration.

#### C44-DETAILDIFFERENCE-01 — more information without a changed warranted reconstruction is additional resolution without praxeological purchase

The lower-bound burden is not satisfied by showing that the candidate reconstruction contains more elements than the prior reconstruction. The analyst must show that the added distinction changes what can defensibly be said about the declared reference object for the declared claim.

```text
more observations
or more timestamps
or more components
or more labels
or more operator-typed occurrences

≠ changed warranted praxis reconstruction
```

Let `R0` be a source-supported reconstruction at granularity `g0`, and let `R1` add distinction `D` at candidate granularity `g1`. The mere inequality `R1 ≠ R0` at the descriptive level does not establish a lower-bound gain. The relevant question is whether the warranted claim changes:

```text
R1 contains D
+ Q remains warrantably unchanged
→ additional resolution without demonstrated PraxisPurchase for Q
```

This rule does not deny that `D` exists, that it may be correctly observed, or that it could matter to a different claim. It denies only the inference from descriptive addition to claim-relative gain. A distinction may be source-supported and still fail to alter action corridors, costs, exposure, roles, asymmetries, expectations, bindings, temporal order, irreversibility, integration, alternatives, or Stop for the tested claim.

Detail can also change the surface form of a claim without changing its warrant. Replacing a broad label with several near-synonyms, dividing one interval into smaller intervals that carry the same relation, or listing every local occurrence beneath an unchanged composite function can produce a visibly different document while preserving the same substantive reconstruction.

```text
longer formulation
≠ different claim

more exact notation
≠ stronger warrant

more local occurrence names
≠ additional PraxisPurchase
```

The distinction is especially important when PMS operator occurrences are involved. Identifying more □-, Α-, Λ-, Ω-, or other Δ–Ψ-typed occurrences can sharpen source trace, but occurrence density does not by itself alter the claim. The operator types, their dependency order, and their formal definitions remain unchanged. What must change is a bounded praxis reconstruction carried by the sources: for example, which Frame occurrence constrains a corridor, which Asymmetry occurrence redistributes exposure, or which Attractor occurrence changes the stability conditions of an arrangement. Decorative operator enumeration remains below the burden.

The same limit applies to all three STRATA operations:

```text
finer DECOMPOSE output
≠ automatic lower-bound gain

larger COMPOSE inventory
≠ automatic lower-bound gain

more elaborate PROJECT_AS target description
≠ automatic lower-bound gain
```

A DECOMPOSE result can add fragments while losing the relation that made the source object analytically relevant. A COMPOSE result can include more components while leaving the composite claim unchanged or less traceable. A PROJECT_AS result can add contextual qualifications while failing to change the bounded target function. The operation signature determines what kind of result is being tested; it does not grant PraxisPurchase.

#### C44-NONCOMPENSATION-01 — detail count, technical precision, formal elegance, and source abundance cannot compensate for absent PraxisPurchase

The Relevance Floor is non-compensatory. No amount of formal or descriptive surplus can substitute for the missing changed reconstruction.

```text
high detail count
+ no changed warranted claim
→ no compensation

technical precision
+ no changed praxis relation
→ no compensation

formal elegance
+ no PraxisPurchase
→ no compensation

many citations
+ no source-supported Q → Q′ relation
→ no compensation
```

This is not an anti-formal or anti-empirical rule. Precision, formalization, and source breadth can be necessary for a strong reconstruction. They are simply not sufficient for the lower-bound passage. A perfectly measured distinction can remain neutral to the current claim. A formally elegant partition can fail to alter any praxis dimension. A large source set can repeat the same resolution or document `D` without supporting the claim that `D` changes `Q`.

Non-compensation also protects the Admissibility Band from score substitution. An analyst may not aggregate detail count, citations, terminology, complexity, and operator density into a synthetic relevance score that offsets the absence of PraxisPurchase. Formal strength on one dimension cannot erase failure on another constitutive dimension.

```text
more structure
≠ more relevance
≠ more authority
```

Where a distinction lacks demonstrated PraxisPurchase, the analyst must retain that local finding even if the surrounding analysis is sophisticated. Later TraceableLoad, continuity, Counterfactual Sensitivity, or TypeIntegrity checks cannot retroactively create the missing lower-bound change. Conversely, a possible PraxisPurchase finding cannot compensate for failed TypeIntegrity, Reference Continuity, source sufficiency, or the upper Traceability Ceiling. Each gate retains its own burden.

<a id="44-5-lower-bound-failure-forms"></a>

### 44.5 Lower-Bound Failure Forms

Lower-bound failures recur in recognizable forms. Their names are diagnostic prompts, not automatic verdicts. A marker initiates the Changed-Reconstruction Test; it does not replace source review, coordinate declaration, or claim comparison.

#### C44-LOWERFAILUREFORMS-01 — lower-bound failure forms identify recurrent risks without becoming automatic labels or outputs

Six common forms are especially important.

**1. Event inflation.** A source sequence is divided into an increasing number of events, episodes, turns, or micro-incidents, but the additional divisions do not alter the warranted reconstruction of the trajectory, path, corridor, exposure, or commitment structure.

```text
more event units
+ unchanged warranted temporal relation
→ possible event inflation
```

Event inflation is not present merely because many events are distinguished. A new event boundary can have PraxisPurchase when it changes sequence order, reveals a non-event, identifies an irreversible commitment, separates two paths, or alters a defensible claim about path formation. The marker therefore asks whether the event partition changes the reconstruction rather than presuming that it does not.

**2. Temporal over-resolution.** Time is divided more finely than the source-supported praxis claim can use. Additional timestamps, intervals, or phase boundaries may improve chronology while leaving duration, order, irreversibility, expectation, and transition claims unchanged.

```text
finer time units
≠ changed temporal praxis claim
```

Temporal over-resolution must remain distinct from source limitation. If the sources cannot establish whether the proposed boundary changes the claim, the result is not a demonstrated lower-bound failure. It remains source-limited or underdetermined. Only a supported distinction whose alleged effect fails the test can support a neutral or below-floor finding.

**3. Atomistic listing.** Components or occurrences are enumerated independently while the constitutive relations among them remain absent, unchanged, or analytically unused. The list may be factually correct yet fail to reconstruct how the elements form the same reference object.

```text
supported component list
+ no changed relation claim
→ possible atomistic listing
```

This marker is especially relevant to DECOMPOSE. Opening a composite or occurrence does not require retaining every detail, but it does require preserving or revising the relation through which the finer structures reconstruct the same source object. A list that abandons this relation does not gain PraxisPurchase through quantity.

**4. Role or profile inflation.** The analyst multiplies role names, actor profiles, position types, or subcategories without changing a warranted claim about corridors, bindings, asymmetries, exposure, expectation, or responsibility. This form must not become person typing or diagnosis. STRATA tests structural role distinctions, not personal essence, worth, morality, or condition.

```text
more role labels
≠ more structural differentiation
≠ person taxonomy
```

A role distinction can have PraxisPurchase where it changes who bears a cost, which commitment binds, whose expectation structures a non-event, or how an Asymmetry occurrence is reconstructed. The failure form applies only where the labels accumulate without such a supported change.

**5. Repeated reformulation.** The same substantive reconstruction is restated through new terminology, a new diagram, a new Frame label, a new granularity description, or a new target-function phrase while the claim and its warrant remain unchanged.

```text
new wording
or new representation
or new analytical coordinate label
+ same warranted Q
→ possible repeated reformulation
```

A coordinate change can create a legitimate new claim, but it cannot be used to erase the earlier result. If the new Frame, granularity, reference object, or target function truly changes the question, the analyst must open a new testable claim with explicit continuity to the prior one. Merely renaming the same claim does not cross the floor.

**6. Technical precision without praxis gain.** Measurement, coding, schema detail, mathematical notation, or formal completeness increases while no defensible statement about the praxis structure changes. A schema-valid representation may therefore remain resolution-neutral for the claim.

```text
formal completeness
≠ substantive relevance proof
```

This marker preserves the formal-model boundary. YAML, JSON Schema, decision trees, and validation scripts may require fields, inventories, references, and consistency. They cannot decide whether the added distinction actually changes a warranted praxis reconstruction. Machine-readable precision is not a substantive lower-bound passage.

These six forms are neither exhaustive nor mutually exclusive. One candidate can exhibit several at once, and a suspected form can prove false when the Changed-Reconstruction Test identifies a supported claim change. The labels therefore remain secondary to the test.

A failure marker also does not select a canonical Output Class. `resolution_neutral`, `claim_reduction_required`, `mandatory_stop`, `failed_transformation`, or `non_capture` require their own conditions and later mapping. WP2 identifies the risk architecture but leaves negative-result interpretation and lower Stop to WP3.

```text
failure-form marker
≠ lower-bound verdict
≠ Output Class
```

<a id="44-6-the-changed-reconstruction-test"></a>
