### 44.6 The Changed-Reconstruction Test

The Changed-Reconstruction Test is the central lower-bound procedure. It converts the broad demand for PraxisPurchase into an explicit comparison between a prior warranted reconstruction and a candidate reconstruction containing the added distinction.

#### C44-CHANGEDRECONSTRUCTION-01 — the analyst must identify which defensible statement would be formulated differently because of the added distinction

The test asks:

> Which defensible statement about the declared praxis structure must be formulated differently because of `D`?

A complete test view contains at least:

```text
prior reconstruction R0
+ prior claim Q
+ added distinction D
+ source support for D
+ candidate reconstruction R1
+ alleged changed claim Q′
+ affected praxis dimension
+ constitutive D → Q′ relation
+ alternatives
+ five-part Loss
+ Claim Scope and Claim Ceiling
```

This is a conceptual view of the existing Shared Transformation Record, not a new top-level object or schema field. The test may be applied to one operation record, to a comparison among separate records, or to a later chain record. Each operation and each handoff retains its own claim and disposition.

The minimal sequence is:

```text
1. State Q exactly.
2. State D exactly.
3. Identify the source support for D.
4. Formulate Q′ exactly.
5. Identify the praxis dimension changed by Q → Q′.
6. State the constitutive relation by which D warrants that change.
7. Test alternatives and counterpressure.
8. Declare Loss and remaining uncertainty.
9. Withhold lower-bound and Output-Class mapping until applicable checks are complete.
```

The test is comparative but not numerical. It does not count changed words, added fields, or additional citations. It asks whether the defensible content of the claim changes and whether that change is carried by the sources.

#### C44-CHANGEDCLAIM-01 — the exact changed claim is required; a new label, longer description, or equivalent reformulation is insufficient

The analyst must place `Q` and `Q′` in a form that permits substantive comparison. Vague statements such as “the analysis is richer,” “the picture is more nuanced,” or “the new category matters” do not satisfy the burden.

```text
Q: The arrangement stabilizes through one recurring commitment sequence.

Q′: The arrangement stabilizes only when two temporally ordered commitment sequences remain coupled; separating them removes the claimed stability.
```

Here `Q′` differs in a testable relation, not merely in wording. By contrast:

```text
Q: The arrangement contains several roles.
Q′: The arrangement contains multiple differentiated roles.
```

does not yet identify a changed praxis claim. The second formulation may be stylistically more precise while leaving corridor, cost, asymmetry, expectation, binding, temporal, integration, and Stop claims unchanged.

The changed claim can be narrower, weaker, conditional, separated, or defeated. PraxisPurchase is not restricted to stronger positive claims.

```text
Q′ narrower than Q
or Q′ conditional where Q was unconditional
or Q divided into Q1 and Q2
or Q defeated
→ possible changed reconstruction
```

The exact claim requirement also prevents operator-label substitution. Replacing “stabilizing condition” with “Attractor,” or “constraint” with “Frame,” does not establish a changed claim. A PMS occurrence typing matters only when the source-supported operator function changes the reconstruction while Origin Type, Reference Identity, and bounded Claim Scope remain visible.

#### C44-PRAXISDIMENSIONS-01 — the test examines whether the distinction changes a warranted praxis dimension rather than only descriptive surface

The affected dimension must be declared. Chapter 44 uses the following non-scoring test set:

- **action corridors:** does `D` open, close, separate, or condition a supported corridor?
- **costs:** does `D` redistribute, reveal, defer, concentrate, or make a cost irrecoverable?
- **exposure and risk:** does `D` change who or what remains exposed and under which conditions?
- **roles and asymmetries:** does `D` alter a warranted structural position, dependency, access relation, or burden distribution?
- **expectations and non-events:** does `D` change which expectation is operative or which omission is structurally load-bearing?
- **bindings and commitments:** does `D` change what remains binding, to whom, for how long, or through which relation?
- **temporal order and duration:** does `D` change sequence, phase, delay, persistence, or timing-dependent function?
- **irreversibility and path formation:** does `D` identify a point after which alternatives, costs, or trajectories differ?
- **integration and separation:** does `D` change whether source structures form one composite, several objects, or no warranted composite?
- **alternatives and Claim Scope:** does `D` exclude an alternative, reveal a rival reconstruction, or require claim narrowing?
- **Stop:** does `D` show that continuation, stronger typing, further composition, further decomposition, or projection is no longer warranted?

The dimensions are not a checklist score. One constitutive change can be sufficient; many weakly asserted changes can remain insufficient. The burden is always to state what changes, why that change is praxeological, and how the sources carry it.

#### C44-COUNTERCASEVISIBILITY-01 — a newly warranted countercase, claim narrowing, alternative, or Stop condition can constitute changed reconstruction without presuming lower-bound success

A distinction can create PraxisPurchase by weakening rather than strengthening the analyst's preferred account. It may reveal that the original claim fails under a previously hidden condition, that two cases formerly grouped together require separation, that a rival explanation remains live, or that no further operation is warranted.

```text
D reveals warranted countercase
or D requires claim narrowing
or D restores a source-supported alternative
or D makes Stop necessary
→ possible changed reconstruction
```

This provision prevents confirmation bias. The analyst may not count only distinctions that enrich or support the intended claim. A distinction that defeats the claim can have stronger PraxisPurchase than one that adds descriptive texture.

Countercase visibility still does not presume success. The countercase must be source-supported, refer to the declared object and claim, and alter the warranted reconstruction rather than merely suggest a possibility. Where the sources cannot determine whether the countercase holds, the result remains source-limited or underdetermined. Where the countercase concerns a different object or question, it becomes a new testable claim rather than an automatic revision of `Q`.

The same discipline applies to Stop. Recognizing that no further differentiation is warranted can be a substantive changed reconstruction, but WP2 does not yet select Mandatory Lower Stop. Chapter 44 WP3 owns the exact negative-result, neutrality, Stop, record-view, and re-entry mapping.

#### C44-WP2-HANDOFF-01 — WP2 hands WP3 a complete Changed-Reconstruction Test without deciding negative-result mapping, lower Stop, re-entry, or pressure-object disposition

WP2 establishes the following controlled premises:

- descriptive detail and praxeological difference are non-equivalent;
- more information without an exact changed warranted claim does not demonstrate PraxisPurchase;
- detail count, technical precision, formal elegance, source abundance, or operator-occurrence density cannot compensate for absent PraxisPurchase;
- event inflation, temporal over-resolution, atomistic listing, role/profile inflation, repeated reformulation, and technical precision without praxis gain are non-automatic failure markers;
- the Changed-Reconstruction Test requires explicit `Q`, `D`, `Q′`, source support, affected praxis dimension, constitutive relation, alternatives, Loss, Claim Scope, and Claim Ceiling;
- a changed claim may be stronger, narrower, conditional, separated, defeated, or stopped;
- newly warranted countercases, alternatives, claim narrowing, or Stop conditions can constitute possible changed reconstruction;
- failure markers, changed-reconstruction candidates, and local comparison findings do not automatically map to canonical Output Classes.

The pressure packet remains protected:

```yaml
L0:
  status: pressure_statement_not_accepted
  relevance_privilege: not_granted

D:
  status: added_distinction_candidate
  result: unadjudicated

DG:
  status: changed_reconstruction_candidate
  result: unadjudicated

DN:
  status: neutral_candidate
  result: unadjudicated

DS:
  status: source_limited_candidate
  result: unadjudicated

lower_bound:
  result_selected: false
  preferred_granularity_selected: false
  output_class_selected: false
  mandatory_lower_stop_selected: false
```

WP2 selects no lower-bound result, no pressure-object disposition, no preferred granularity, no route, no canonical Output Class, no Mandatory Lower Stop, and no re-entry decision. It creates no Case, Test, or Smoke YAML and does not alter the artifact-complete RETYPE `mandatory_stop`.

The controlled handoff is:

```text
detail/difference boundary fixed
+ lower-bound failure markers identified
+ non-compensation enforced
+ Changed-Reconstruction Test operationalized
+ exact Q → Q′ burden fixed
+ praxis dimensions declared
+ countercase and narrowing visibility retained

→ Chapter 44 WP3
→ §44.7 Negative Findings
→ §44.8 Resolution Neutrality
→ §44.9 Mandatory Lower Stop and Re-entry
→ §44.10 Shared-Record View and Handoff
```

WP3 receives a complete test architecture but no adjudicated `D`, `DG`, `DN`, or `DS`, no selected lower-bound result, and no permission to collapse source limitation, resolution neutrality, failed transformation, Mandatory Stop, or Non-Capture into one another.
<a id="44-7-negative-findings"></a>

### 44.7 Negative Findings

The Praxeological Relevance Floor is not only a gate for claimed resolution gain. It also preserves warranted negative findings about what an added distinction does **not** change. A lower-bound inquiry can establish that a suspected component, temporal interval, role difference, operator-occurrence distinction, or further compositional separation is not load-bearing for the claim under review. Such a result is analytically substantive because it narrows what must be carried by the reconstruction and prevents unsupported escalation of resolution.

#### C44-NEGATIVEFINDING-01 — supported negative findings are retained when they show that a coarser reconstruction remains sufficient or that a suspected distinction is not load-bearing

A negative finding requires more than failure to notice an effect. The analyst must have a sufficiently supported distinction `D`, an explicit prior claim `Q`, a declared candidate change `Q′`, and a warranted basis for concluding that the alleged `D → Q′` relation does not obtain for the tested claim.

```text
D supported
+ Q declared
+ alleged D → Q′ relation tested
+ no warranted change to Q
→ possible supported negative finding
```

The result can show, for example, that:

- the coarser reconstruction preserves every relation required by `Q`;
- a finer temporal separation changes no action corridor, cost, exposure, expectation, binding, or Stop condition;
- a proposed role difference is descriptively present but not constitutive of the tested asymmetry;
- a further `DECOMPOSE` occurrence produces supported parts without changing the warranted source-object claim;
- a broader `COMPOSE` proposal adds items but no new constitutive relation;
- an additional `PROJECT_AS` distinction changes no bounded target-function claim;
- an additional PMS operator-occurrence typing is accurate but not load-bearing for the declared reconstruction.

This is not a victory of coarse analysis as such. The finding is bounded to the declared question, Frame, source basis, granularity relation, reference object, and Claim Scope.

```text
coarser reconstruction sufficient for Q in C
≠ coarse reconstruction universally superior

D not load-bearing for Q
≠ D never load-bearing
```

A supported negative finding can strengthen confidence in the bounded adequacy of the existing reconstruction by showing that a suspected hidden dependency is absent under the tested conditions. It does not strengthen the claim beyond its existing ceiling, establish exhaustive capture, or prove that no other distinction could matter. It also does not erase Loss already declared for the coarser reconstruction.

The five-part Loss remains mandatory:

```yaml
loss:
  preserved:
  compressed:
  excluded:
  uncertain:
  irrecoverable:
```

If the finer candidate reveals no changed claim but does reveal previously hidden irrecoverable Loss, the finding is not simply neutral. The Loss itself may require narrowing, separation, Stop, or another later mapping. Likewise, if current sources cannot determine whether `D` is load-bearing, the analyst has a source-limited or underdetermined finding rather than a supported negative finding.

```text
no detected change
≠ supported no-change finding

source cannot test D → Q′
≠ D shown not load-bearing
```

Negative findings therefore preserve counterpressure. They can block detail inflation without converting absence of support into evidence of absence, and they can retain a coarser reconstruction without granting it authority by scale.

<a id="44-8-resolution-neutrality"></a>

### 44.8 Resolution Neutrality

Resolution neutrality is the canonical interpretation available when an added distinction is supported and correctly reconstructed but produces no additional warranted praxis finding for the delimited claim. It recognizes that analysis can become more resolved without becoming more praxeologically discriminating.

#### C44-RESOLUTIONNEUTRAL-01 — resolution neutrality remains distinct from resolution gain, failed transformation, source non-capture, and mere incompleteness

The minimal neutral relation is:

```text
D supported
+ reference and type continuity retained
+ exact Q tested
+ no warranted Q → Q′ change
+ no newly load-bearing Loss or alternative
→ candidate resolution-neutral finding
```

The term `candidate` matters. `resolution_neutral` is one of the ten canonical Output Classes, but it may be selected only for a properly delimited routed claim after all applicable checks are complete. Chapter 44 supplies the lower-bound interpretation; it does not bypass TypeIntegrity, Reference Continuity, TraceableLoad, Counterfactual Sensitivity, Source and Claim Ceilings, alternatives, Stop, or Non-Capture.

Resolution neutrality is not resolution gain:

```text
more accurate or finer description
+ unchanged warranted praxis claim
→ no resolution gain
```

It is not `failed_transformation`. The transformation may have been correctly typed, source-supported, and continuous. The result is neutral because the added resolution does not change the relevant claim, not because the operation could not be performed.

It is not source non-capture. Where the source basis cannot support `D`, cannot test the alleged relation, or fails to represent the relevant praxis structure, neutrality is not warranted. The correct response may remain provisional, reduced, stopped, failed, or non-capturing depending on the complete record.

It is not mere formal incompleteness. Missing required fields, unresolved reference identity, absent Loss declaration, or incomplete alternatives do not become neutral results. They remain incomplete formal or substantive burdens.

```text
resolution_neutral
≠ resolution gain
≠ failed_transformation
≠ source limitation
≠ non_capture
≠ incomplete packet
```

A neutral result can be valuable. It can prevent false escalation, preserve the bounded adequacy of a coarser representation, and document that an intuitively attractive distinction was tested rather than ignored. It can also mark an appropriate end to further resolution for the present claim.

Neutrality does not confer general irrelevance. A distinction neutral for one claim may be relevant for another:

```text
D resolution-neutral for Q1 in C1
≠ D resolution-neutral for Q2 in C2 automatically
```

Nor does neutrality authorize deletion of the finer reconstruction. The record may preserve it as supported detail, evidence for a different later question, or a basis for bounded re-entry. What is refused is the stronger claim that the detail adds PraxisPurchase to the current reconstruction.

The class boundary must remain exact. A `resolution_neutral` output is appropriate only where the routed claim is precisely the claim of additional resolution or distinction and where neutrality is substantively warranted. If the original transformation also contains an independently admissible result, the analyst may need claim segmentation rather than one undifferentiated class. If the added distinction exposes a partial failure, a narrower claim, or hidden irrecoverable Loss, `resolution_neutral` may be insufficient.

```text
one record with several delimited claims
→ one Output Class per routed claim

neutral subfinding
≠ entire composite claim neutral automatically
```

Chapter 44 therefore treats resolution neutrality as a positive methodological result: the method has discriminated between supported detail and warranted praxis difference. It is neither a consolation label nor a shield for weak claims.

<a id="44-9-mandatory-lower-stop-and-re-entry"></a>

### 44.9 Mandatory Lower Stop and Re-entry

The Relevance Floor must be able to terminate further differentiation. Without a lower Stop, the analyst could continue decomposing, subdividing, relabeling, or rephrasing indefinitely while presenting analytical availability as cumulative warrant.

#### C44-LOWERSTOP-01 — Mandatory Lower Stop applies to the current route when continued differentiation produces no changed warranted reconstruction, only terminology accumulates, source precision ends, or the same neutral result is repeated

Mandatory Lower Stop is required where continuation would claim or imply additional PraxisPurchase that the record cannot support. Its principal triggers are:

```text
no exact changed warranted claim
or only terminology accumulates
or source precision ends for the proposed distinction
or repeated finer passes reproduce the same neutral result
or further differentiation would break reference continuity
or continuation would hide irrecoverable Loss
→ Mandatory Lower Stop for the current route
```

These triggers do not all carry the same substantive interpretation. Where `D` is supported but changes no reconstruction, the Stop can follow a neutral lower-bound finding. Where source precision ends, the Stop protects against unsupported continuation; it does **not** establish that no relevant distinction exists. Where reference identity would be lost, the Stop protects the source object. Where only terminology or operator labels accumulate, the Stop blocks analytical self-immunization.

```text
Mandatory Lower Stop because sources end
≠ proof that further relevant structure does not exist

Mandatory Lower Stop because Q does not change
≠ permanent ban on all later questions
```

The Stop is mandatory rather than optional when continuation would violate the lower boundary. Optional Stop remains available where further work is technically admissible but unnecessary for the declared purpose. Optional Stop is not an eleventh Output Class; it is a controlled decision not to continue. Mandatory Stop remains the canonical Output Class available when the routed claim requires cessation.

A lower Stop is local. It applies to the delimited distinction, question, operation occurrence, comparison route, or chain handoff recorded. It does not close the entire reference object, all granularities, or all future inquiry.

```text
Stop(D, Q, C, g0 → g1)
≠ Stop(all possible D, all Q, all C, all g)
```

The earlier disposition must remain visible. A later transformation cannot erase a lower Stop by shifting Frame, granularity, relative level, composition, or target function and then treating the prior failure as if it had never occurred.

#### C44-REENTRY-01 — bounded re-entry requires a new source, question, Frame, granularity relation, or claim and preserves the earlier disposition as part of the new record context

Re-entry is permitted only when something claim-bearing changes. Legitimate triggers include:

- a materially new source basis;
- a new question with a distinct PraxisPurchase burden;
- a newly declared Frame or context;
- a different source-to-target granularity relation;
- a different reference object;
- a changed claim or Claim Scope;
- a newly supported alternative or countercase;
- a new operation occurrence with its own warrant.

The re-entry relation is:

```text
prior disposition retained
+ new source/question/Frame/granularity relation/reference/claim
+ new testable claim
+ new or linked Shared Transformation Record
→ bounded re-entry possible
```

Re-entry is not repetition. Rephrasing `Q`, adding a synonym, retyping the same unsupported occurrence, or shifting the target label without new source-function load does not reopen the route.

```text
new wording
≠ new claim

new target label
≠ new PROJECT_AS warrant

same neutral result at finer notation
≠ re-entry
```

A later successful claim may coexist with the earlier Stop or neutral finding. It does not retroactively convert the earlier disposition into success. The record must show what changed and why the new claim is genuinely different.

Chapter 51 owns the integrated Stop, reduction, and re-entry system across all boundary failures and operation chains. Chapter 44 supplies only the lower-bound trigger and the minimum continuity burden for re-entry. It does not settle all collisions among Stop, claim reduction, provisionality, failure, and Non-Capture.

<a id="44-10-shared-record-view-and-handoff"></a>

### 44.10 Shared-Record View and Handoff

The Relevance-Floor procedure is a controlled view of the existing Shared Transformation Record. It does not create a second record grammar, a new top-level object, or an additional schema field.

#### C44-RECORDVIEW-01 — the Relevance-Floor view maps to existing Shared Transformation Record fields and introduces no parallel record or schema

The conceptual view requires the following existing burdens:

```text
reference object
+ source declaration
+ claim declaration Q
+ operation or comparison scope
+ Frame and granularity relation
+ added distinction D
+ candidate changed claim Q′
+ constitutive source relation
+ TypeIntegrity and continuity findings
+ affected praxis dimension
+ alternatives
+ five-part Loss
+ source and claim ceilings
+ audit findings
+ Stop and Non-Capture findings
+ routed Output Class where applicable
```

No field in this view changes the canonical record syntax. `D`, `Q`, and `Q′` may be encoded within existing source, claim, rationale, audit-finding, alternatives, and Loss structures. Where several operation records are compared, the view references those separate records. Where a chain is assessed, each operation and handoff retains its own claim and disposition.

```text
Relevance-Floor view
≠ new transformation record
≠ new operation
≠ new audit stage
≠ new Output Class
```

Formal validation may check that the relevant declarations are present and internally consistent. It may not infer substantive PraxisPurchase, select a preferred granularity, or decide that `Q′` truly follows from `D`.

#### C44-OUTPUTMAPPING-01 — local lower-bound findings map to the ten canonical Output Classes only after all applicable checks and never create a new class

Chapter 44's local findings are not themselves an expanded Output-Class inventory. Their possible later mappings remain bounded:

- a supported distinction with a changed warranted reconstruction may contribute to `admissible`, `admissible_with_bounded_claim`, `admissible_but_provisional`, or another applicable class only after the full audit;
- a supported distinction with no changed claim may map to `resolution_neutral` for the delimited routed claim;
- a claim that must be narrowed because the alleged PraxisPurchase exceeds support may require `claim_reduction_required`;
- continuation below the floor may require `mandatory_stop`;
- a transformation that fails its own operation or continuity burden may remain `failed_transformation` rather than neutral;
- where the relevant praxis structure cannot be adequately represented, `non_capture` remains available;
- partial, analogical, or provisional results remain possible only under their existing canonical definitions.

The complete inventory remains exactly:

```text
admissible
admissible_with_bounded_claim
admissible_but_provisional
resolution_neutral
analogy_only
partially_admissible
claim_reduction_required
mandatory_stop
failed_transformation
non_capture
```

No local label such as “below floor,” “source limited,” “negative finding,” “lower stop,” or “re-entry” becomes an eleventh class. One Output Class is selected per delimited routed claim only after all applicable checks.

#### C44-HANDOFF51-53-01 — the lower Stop and record burdens pass to Chapters 51 and 53 without pre-empting their integrated procedures

Chapter 51 receives:

- the distinction between neutral continuation and mandatory lower Stop;
- the local triggers for lower Stop;
- the requirement that re-entry identify a new testable claim;
- preservation of prior dispositions;
- the prohibition on using re-entry to immunize a failed or stopped claim.

Chapter 53 receives:

- explicit Relevance-Floor coordinates;
- the exact `Q/D/Q′` burden;
- source support for both `D` and the alleged changed relation;
- the affected praxis dimension;
- alternatives and five-part Loss;
- lower-bound finding, Stop, re-entry, and Output-mapping records;
- the requirement that the full twelve-stage audit precede final routing.

Chapter 44 does not perform Chapter 51's integrated collision handling or Chapter 53's complete audit. It supplies their lower-bound inputs.

#### C44-HANDOFF-01 — WP3 hands WP4 a complete lower-bound method without adjudicating the synthetic pressure packet, selecting a route, or assigning a pressure-object Output Class

WP3 completes the Chapter-44 substantive method:

- supported negative findings are retained without turning absent evidence into evidence of absence;
- resolution neutrality is distinguished from gain, failure, source limitation, formal incompleteness, and Non-Capture;
- Mandatory Lower Stop applies to the current route when further differentiation lacks warrant;
- bounded re-entry requires a new claim-bearing condition and preserves the earlier disposition;
- the Relevance-Floor view remains within the Shared Transformation Record;
- local findings map only to the ten canonical Output Classes and only after all applicable checks;
- Chapters 51 and 53 retain integrated Stop/re-entry and twelve-stage-audit ownership.

The synthetic pressure packet remains protected:

```yaml
L0:
  status: pressure_statement_not_accepted
  relevance_privilege: not_granted

D:
  status: added_distinction_candidate
  result: unadjudicated

DG:
  status: changed_reconstruction_candidate
  result: unadjudicated

DN:
  status: neutral_candidate
  result: unadjudicated

DS:
  status: source_limited_candidate
  result: unadjudicated

lower_bound:
  result_selected: false
  preferred_granularity_selected: false
  output_class_selected: false
  mandatory_lower_stop_selected: false
  reentry_selected: false
```

WP3 selects no result for `D`, `DG`, `DN`, or `DS`; no preferred granularity; no route; no Mandatory Lower Stop for the packet; no re-entry; and no canonical pressure-object Output Class. It creates no Case, Test, or Smoke YAML and does not alter the artifact-complete RETYPE `mandatory_stop`.

The controlled handoff is:

```text
complete Relevance-Floor definition and PraxisPurchase architecture
+ claim-relative coordinates
+ detail/difference and Changed-Reconstruction Test
+ negative-finding interpretation
+ resolution-neutral boundary
+ Mandatory Lower Stop and bounded re-entry
+ Shared-Record view and canonical output-mapping boundary
+ Chapter 51 and 53 ownership preserved

→ Chapter 44 WP4
→ integrated Contract and redundancy audit
→ exact 24-duty verification
→ L0/D/DG/DN/DS non-adjudication check
→ provisional-lock decision
→ Chapter 45 Preparation Gate
```

---

<a id="chapter-45-praxeological-traceability-ceiling"></a>

## 45. Praxeological Traceability Ceiling

Chapter 44 established the lower admissibility boundary: additional resolution must change a warranted praxis reconstruction rather than merely add distinction. Passing that lower boundary is necessary but insufficient. A reconstruction may be relevant to the declared claim and still exceed what its sources can carry. A wider composite may name a genuine institutional function while suppressing the relations that form it. A finer reconstruction may enumerate supported fragments while losing the reference object that made them analytically connected. A projected function may fit the target context while becoming indifferent to the source structures from which it is said to derive.

Chapter 6 owns the compact definitions of the Praxeological Traceability Ceiling and TraceableLoad. Chapter 45 does not replace them. It elaborates the system-wide upper-bound procedure through which a transformation result must remain reconstructibly dependent on its declared source structures. The chapter therefore asks not only whether a source can be cited, but whether the target claim still carries an inspectable load-bearing trace of the source features, relations, temporal formation, costs, roles, and asymmetries that warrant it.

The governing question is:

> When does a broader composition, finer reconstruction, or projected function cease to carry reconstructible praxis load from its source structures?

One synthetic pressure packet remains stable throughout the chapter:

```text
U0
→ a source list or citation volume proves traceability
→ pressure statement not accepted

T
→ target candidate said to carry source load
→ unadjudicated

TL
→ candidate Constitutive Source Trace said to support T
→ unadjudicated

TE
→ candidate target label said to remain unchanged under materially opposed source structures
→ unadjudicated

TS
→ candidate finding that current sources do not permit dependency testing
→ unadjudicated
```

The packet keeps four questions separate:

```text
Is a source identified?
≠ Is a load-bearing source trace reconstructed?
≠ Is the target claim structurally or functionally dependent on that trace?
≠ Which canonical Output Class follows after all applicable checks?
```

WP1 establishes the upper boundary, the minimum architecture of TraceableLoad, and the more-than-citation requirement. It does not decide `T`, `TL`, `TE`, or `TS`; it does not select an upper-bound result, route, Stop, Claim Reduction, or canonical Output Class. The detailed Constitutive Source Trace, source-target dependency test, rival-source pressure, source limitation, macro-label elasticity, non-exhaustiveness, upper Stop, and Claim Reduction belong to WP2 and WP3.

<a id="45-1-definition"></a>

### 45.1 Definition

The Praxeological Traceability Ceiling is the upper relational boundary beyond which a transformation result no longer carries reconstructible structural load from its declared source object or source objects. Above the ceiling, a target may remain coherent, familiar, rhetorically persuasive, or descriptively convenient while its constitutive relation to the source has become too weak, elastic, fragmented, or opaque to warrant the claimed transformation.

Chapter 6 supplies the canonical formulation:

> **A composition, decomposition, or projection above praxeological traceability does not carry warranted higher- or finer-level structure. It is abstraction or fragmentation without traceable load.**

Chapter 45 retains this formulation exactly. Its task is to identify what must remain reconstructible and what kinds of source-target disconnection place a claim above the ceiling. The ceiling is not a universal maximum size, complexity, abstraction level, path length, or number of operations. It is relative to the declared source, target, operation occurrence, Frame, granularity relation, temporal scope, target function, Claim Scope, and Claim Ceiling.

#### C45-TRACEABILITYCEILING-01 — the upper boundary is crossed where a transformation result no longer carries reconstructible structural load from its declared source

The minimum relation is:

```text
declared source structure S
+ transformation occurrence T
+ target claim Q
+ no reconstructible load-bearing S → Q trace
→ possible abstraction or fragmentation without TraceableLoad
```

The result may exceed the ceiling through abstraction or fragmentation. Abstraction occurs when a broader object, macrofunction, projected function, or higher-level label remains detached from the source relations that would warrant it. Fragmentation occurs when finer components or intervals are supported individually but no longer reconstruct the same reference object, trajectory, occurrence, or function from which the decomposition claim began.

The two forms are directionally different but methodologically symmetrical:

```text
broader target without source load
→ abstraction risk

finer result without source-object return
→ fragmentation risk
```

Neither direction is inherently above the ceiling. A highly compressed composite can remain traceable when its load-bearing source features and relations are recoverable. A very fine decomposition can remain traceable when the source object and constitutive relations remain reconstructible. Conversely, a modest abstraction or limited refinement may fail when the target becomes source-indifferent or when the fragments no longer return to the declared reference.

The ceiling therefore does not privilege detail over compression or compression over detail. It tests whether the claimed transformation remains anchored to the structures that make it possible.

```text
more detail
≠ more TraceableLoad

less detail
≠ less TraceableLoad automatically

larger target
≠ higher ceiling automatically
```

Traceability is also distinct from truth, causality, and authority. A fully traceable transformation may still be empirically false, causally unsupported, type-confused, contextually unbounded, irrelevant, or beyond its Claim Ceiling. A weakly traceable claim does not become true because its label is useful, nor false merely because the present source basis is incomplete. Chapter 45 addresses the source-load relation only as one constitutive admissibility condition.

The ceiling applies to each STRATA operation without becoming a fourth operation:

- `COMPOSE` must preserve the source selection, order, constitutive relations, heterogeneity, and declared Loss that form the composite analytical object;
- `DECOMPOSE` must preserve return to the same reference object and disclose how finer structures reconstruct, revise, or fail to reconstruct it;
- `PROJECT_AS` must preserve the origin type and show why source features support the bounded target function rather than merely resemble its label.

```text
Traceability Ceiling
≠ COMPOSE
≠ DECOMPOSE
≠ PROJECT_AS
≠ meta-operation
```

Where a chain contains several operations, each operation occurrence and each handoff relation retains its own source-load burden. A traceable first operation does not grant traceability to a later projection. A traceable final target does not retrospectively repair an untraceable intermediate composite. The integrated chain procedure remains later work; Chapter 45 establishes the source-load control that every local occurrence and handoff must carry.

PMS references remain occurrence-based. A source trace may identify concrete □-, Α-, Λ-, Ω-, or other Δ–Ψ-typed occurrences and the relations among them when those occurrences are load-bearing. It may not treat an operator type as a material source constituent, infer TraceableLoad from operator-layer membership, or strengthen a claim by increasing the number of operator labels.

```text
operator occurrence trace
→ possible source-load evidence

operator type name
≠ source-load evidence by itself

more Δ–Ψ labels
≠ more TraceableLoad
```

No PMS operator, dependency, layer, or primitive is added, decomposed, renamed, or retyped by the ceiling test.

<a id="45-2-traceable-load"></a>

### 45.2 Traceable Load

A transformation result possesses TraceableLoad when the target claim remains reconstructibly dependent on identifiable source features and relations that carry its praxis-relevant content. TraceableLoad is not the presence of background material. It is the retained capacity to show what in the source bears the target, how that bearing relation is structured, what has been compressed or excluded, and where uncertainty or irrecoverability limits the claim.

Chapter 6 fixes the minimum elements: constitutive source features are identifiable; relevant relations remain preserved; temporal formation is reconstructible; costs and asymmetries do not disappear; and the transformation result responds to concrete source features. Chapter 45 operationalizes this minimum without requiring exhaustive reproduction or losslessness.

#### C45-TRACEABLELOAD-01 — TraceableLoad requires identifiable load-bearing features, preserved relations and temporality, visible costs and asymmetries, and target dependence

A minimum TraceableLoad account must make five burdens inspectable:

```text
1. identifiable load-bearing source features
2. preserved constitutive relations
3. reconstructible temporal formation
4. visible costs, exposure, roles, and asymmetries where relevant
5. a stated reason the target claim depends on this trace
```

These burdens are jointly oriented but not mechanically additive. Their relevance varies with the source object, operation, and claim. A target need not preserve every source feature. It must preserve or explicitly account for those features whose alteration would change the warranted target claim, its scope, its function, its alternatives, or its Loss profile. WP2 will specify the full source-trace classes and dependency test; WP1 establishes the minimum load architecture.

#### C45-STRUCTURALMAPPING-01 — source features and relations must be explicitly mapped to the target-claim elements they support

Structural mapping identifies which source features support which parts of the target claim. It prevents a transformation from relying on a general atmosphere of evidence while leaving the constitutive link unstated.

A minimal mapping may be represented as:

```text
source feature s1
+ relation r1 to s2
+ declared temporal or functional position
→ target-claim element q1
```

The mapping must distinguish source presence from source function. A documented practice may exist in the source basis but be irrelevant to the target claim. A repeated sequence may support a trajectory reconstruction but not a path-dependence claim. A local cost distribution may support a bounded asymmetry finding but not a totalizing macro-label. A projected frame-function may depend on historical carriers and selective effects, not merely on the presence of a frame-like description.

Structural mapping also preserves alternatives. Where the same source features can support several target reconstructions, the record must not hide that plurality. TraceableLoad can be partial, competing, or underdetermined. The existence of a mapping does not prove that the selected target is uniquely correct.

```text
source-to-target mapping exists
≠ target uniquely warranted
≠ rival target excluded
```

#### C45-FUNCTIONALDEPENDENCE-01 — the account must state why the target claim or bounded function depends on the declared source trace

A source-target relation is not established by proximity, common vocabulary, sequence in a document, or plausible analogy. The account must state a constitutive or functional reason why the target claim depends on the mapped source features and relations.

For a `COMPOSE` occurrence, the reason may concern how selection, ordering, recurrence, interaction, or mutual constraint forms the composite object. For a `DECOMPOSE` occurrence, it may concern how finer parts, intervals, or relations reconstruct the same occurrence or composite and explain a source-object property. For a `PROJECT_AS` occurrence, it may concern how origin-typed source features satisfy a bounded target function under the declared context without becoming that function as an origin type.

The minimum statement has the form:

```text
Q depends on source trace ST because relation R
is constitutive for the declared target object or function
within context C and Claim Scope S.
```

The claim remains bounded. A constitutive reason for one target function does not authorize all functions, all contexts, or an origin-type conversion. A functional dependency does not automatically establish empirical causality. It states the reconstructed load relation required by the transformation claim.

```text
functional dependence for Q
≠ universal causal law
≠ origin-type replacement
≠ application authority
```

#### C45-TEMPORALLOAD-01 — source formation, duration, order, and historical load must remain reconstructible where they are constitutive

Praxis structures are temporally formed. A source trace can therefore fail even when its component inventory is accurate. If the target claim depends on sequence, duration, repetition, delayed realization, non-event structure, sedimentation, irreversibility, or path formation, those temporal relations must remain reconstructible.

A macro-event label that preserves participants and outcomes while suppressing the ordering that made the event possible may exceed the ceiling. A trajectory reconstructed as an unordered list of states may lose the transitions and constraints that carry it. A projected attractor-function may become untraceable if recurrence, return pressure, or stabilized expectation disappears from the source account. A frame-function may lose its historical carriers and selective formation while retaining only a contemporary label.

```text
same components
+ different order, duration, or recurrence
→ possibly different target claim
```

Temporal load does not require narrating every moment. It requires preserving the temporal features that bear the target. Compression remains admissible when it makes its temporal selection visible and retains the sequence, duration, recurrence, or irreversibility necessary for the claim.

#### C45-COSTASYMMETRYLOAD-01 — relevant costs, exposure, roles, and asymmetries must not disappear under abstraction or fragmentation

A target claim may appear coherent while removing the distribution of burdens that gives the source structure its praxeological form. Costs, exposure, access, role differentiation, dependency, exclusion, and asymmetrical option structures are therefore part of TraceableLoad whenever they are constitutive.

A broad institutional composite may preserve formal rules while hiding who bears delay, uncertainty, or compliance cost. A decomposition may list local interactions while losing the role asymmetry that connects them. A projected stabilizing function may describe aggregate persistence while obscuring that persistence is produced through uneven exposure or constrained alternatives. Such omissions do not automatically invalidate the transformation, but they must appear in the source mapping and five-part Loss rather than disappearing behind a smooth target label.

```text
macro coherence
+ erased cost or asymmetry trace
→ possible loss of TraceableLoad
```

The relevant burden is not moral ranking or person evaluation. STRATA reconstructs structural distributions and relations; it does not diagnose persons, assign moral worth, or infer legal or political legitimacy. Costs and asymmetries matter here because they may be load-bearing features of the source-target relation.

TraceableLoad is non-compensatory. Documentation volume, formal elegance, target familiarity, operator density, or strong PraxisPurchase cannot substitute for a missing constitutive trace.

```text
high PraxisPurchase
+ no TraceableLoad
→ no compensation

many sources
+ no structural mapping
→ no compensation
```

Conversely, a source-rich trace does not compensate for absent PraxisPurchase, TypeIntegrity, continuity, Contextual Boundedness, Counterfactual Sensitivity, or authority limits. The Admissibility Band requires both lower- and upper-bound integrity; neither side can inherit a pass from the other.

<a id="45-3-traceability-is-more-than-citation"></a>

### 45.3 Traceability Is More than Citation

Citation identifies where a source claim may be inspected. It does not by itself show how the source carries the transformation result. A bibliography, archive list, quotation set, document count, interview inventory, or dense chain of references can improve auditability while leaving the constitutive source-target relation entirely unspecified.

#### C45-MORETHANCITATION-01 — citation, source listing, and documentation volume are insufficient without structural and functional mapping

The governing non-equivalence is:

```text
citation
≠ Constitutive Source Trace

source list
≠ TraceableLoad

documentation volume
≠ source-target dependency
```

Citation answers a location question: where is the material found? Traceability answers a formation question: which source features and relations bear the target, how are they preserved or compressed, and why does the target depend on them? The first can support the second, but it cannot replace it.

A target may cite every source document and remain untraceable when:

- no source feature is identified as load-bearing;
- the relation among cited elements is unstated;
- temporal order or duration is flattened;
- costs, roles, or asymmetries disappear;
- rival sources are ignored;
- the target label would fit materially different source structures equally well;
- compression and exclusion are not declared;
- the claimed target function is supported only by resemblance.

The reverse is also possible. A concise source basis can carry substantial TraceableLoad when its constitutive features, relations, temporal formation, and limitations are explicit. Quantity is not the criterion.

```text
few sources
+ explicit constitutive mapping
→ possible TraceableLoad

many sources
+ source-indifferent target
→ possible ceiling failure
```

This distinction applies equally to PMS operator references. Citing `PMS.yaml`, naming an operator type, or listing many operator-typed occurrences does not prove that a STRATA target claim carries PMS source load. The record must identify the actual occurrences, the relations among them, the relevant dependency order where claim-bearing, and the precise target-claim elements they support. Operator-layer membership remains organizational, not a source-load shortcut or authority multiplier.

Traceability is also more than rhetorical fit. A label can summarize the material elegantly while remaining elastically applicable to opposed sources. A trajectory can be called a frame, an arrangement can be called an attractor, or a composite can be called a regime in ordinary analytical language. `PROJECT_AS` requires more: a bounded target-function claim with origin type preserved and constitutive source support. WP2 will test dependency and rival-source pressure in detail. WP1 fixes the rule that semantic resemblance and citation proximity are insufficient.

```text
plausible label
+ cited source
≠ warranted PROJECT_AS
```

Citation does, however, remain necessary where a claim depends on external evidence. The more-than-citation rule is not an anti-documentation rule. It prevents documentation from being treated as a substitute for reconstruction. A complete Chapter-45 account therefore keeps both:

```text
source location and support
+ constitutive structural mapping
```

Neither absorbs the other. Unsupported mapping is not rescued by elegance; unmapped citation is not rescued by volume.

The distinction between absent TraceableLoad and source limitation remains open. Where current materials do not permit a dependency test, the correct finding may be underdetermined or untestable rather than traceability failure. WP2 owns that separation. Similarly, the fact that a target appears insensitive to source variation may indicate robustness or elasticity; Chapter 46 owns the full six-value Counterfactual Sensitivity procedure. WP1 does not assign either result.

The synthetic pressure packet therefore remains protected:

```yaml
U0:
  status: citation_sufficiency_statement_not_accepted

T:
  result: unadjudicated

TL:
  result: unadjudicated

TE:
  result: unadjudicated

TS:
  result: unadjudicated

upper_bound:
  result_selected: false
  route_selected: false
  output_class_selected: false
  mandatory_upper_stop_selected: false
  claim_reduction_selected: false
```

#### C45-WP1-HANDOFF-01 — WP1 hands WP2 the upper-bound, TraceableLoad, and more-than-citation architecture without adjudicating the pressure packet or selecting an Output Class

WP1 establishes:

- the Praxeological Traceability Ceiling as the upper relational boundary against source-indifferent abstraction and source-object-losing fragmentation;
- TraceableLoad through identifiable source features, preserved relations, temporal formation, visible costs and asymmetries, and a stated dependency reason;
- explicit structural mapping from source features and relations to target-claim elements;
- functional or constitutive dependence without automatic causal or authority claims;
- temporal and cost/asymmetry load where constitutive;
- citation, source listing, rhetorical fit, documentation volume, and PMS operator density as insufficient by themselves;
- non-compensation between TraceableLoad and other admissibility dimensions.

WP1 does not establish the full Constitutive Source Trace classes, Source-Target Dependency procedure, operation-specific upper-bound variants, rival-source test, source-limitation result, macro-label elasticity test, non-exhaustiveness boundary, Mandatory Upper Stop, Claim Reduction, or final Output mapping. Those remain assigned to WP2 and WP3.

No result is selected for `T`, `TL`, `TE`, or `TS`; no route, Stop, Claim Reduction, preferred target, or canonical Output Class is selected. No Case, Test, or Smoke YAML is created. The three-operation, ten-class, sixteen-Rule, twelve-stage, six-sensitivity-value, and five-Loss-field inventories remain unchanged. `authority_inheritance` remains prohibited.

The controlled handoff is:

```text
upper relational boundary
+ minimum TraceableLoad architecture
+ structural mapping
+ functional dependence requirement
+ temporal and cost/asymmetry preservation
+ traceability more than citation
+ pressure-packet non-adjudication

→ Chapter 45 WP2
→ §45.4 Upper-Bound Failure Forms
→ §45.5 Constitutive Source Trace
→ §45.6 Source-Target Dependency
```

<a id="45-4-upper-bound-failure-forms"></a>

### 45.4 Upper-Bound Failure Forms

The Traceability Ceiling is exceeded neither by abstraction as such nor by decomposition as such. It is exceeded when the transformation result can no longer be returned to a reconstructible source burden adequate to the declared claim. Upper-bound failure is therefore relational: it concerns the connection among source object, operation occurrence, target object or function, and Claim Scope. The same label may be warranted in one record and source-indifferent in another.

#### C45-UPPERFAILUREFORMS-01 — upper-bound failure forms identify recurrent traceability risks without functioning as automatic verdicts

Chapter 45 uses the following recurrent failure forms as pressure markers:

1. **Source-list substitution** — citations or source inventories stand in for a Constitutive Source Trace.
2. **Relation deletion** — source elements are retained, but the relations that make them load-bearing disappear.
3. **Temporal flattening** — sequence, duration, recurrence, delay, non-event structure, sedimentation, or irreversibility is removed although the target depends on it.
4. **Cost and asymmetry erasure** — aggregate coherence is retained while source distributions of burden, exposure, access, role, or constraint vanish.
5. **Source-object fragmentation** — a finer output lists supported fragments but cannot reconstruct how they return to the same reference object or source function.
6. **Macro-label elasticity** — the same target label remains applicable across materially opposed or structurally incompatible source configurations.
7. **Projection by resemblance** — a target function is assigned because the source resembles the label semantically, not because a constitutive source relation supports the function.
8. **Operator-label substitution** — PMS operator names or operator-layer positions replace occurrence-level source mapping.
9. **Compression concealment** — exclusion, uncertainty, or irrecoverable Loss is omitted so that the target appears more source-complete than it is.
10. **Dependency assertion without return path** — the record states that the target depends on the source but provides no reconstructible route from altered source features to altered target-claim elements.

```text
failure-form marker
≠ Traceability-Ceiling verdict
≠ canonical Output Class
```

A marker initiates a source-trace test. It does not decide that the transformation failed, that Claim Reduction is sufficient, or that Mandatory Upper Stop is required. A compressed target may remain fully traceable despite substantial excluded detail. A highly detailed result may exceed the ceiling because it no longer preserves the relation that makes the fragments parts of the same source object. A familiar macro-label may be warranted in one context and elastic in another.

The failure forms are deliberately non-compensatory. A large source list cannot compensate for relation deletion. Temporal precision cannot compensate for loss of the relevant cost structure. A plausible target function cannot compensate for missing origin-type preservation. A large number of Δ–Ψ occurrences cannot compensate for failure to identify which occurrences and relations bear the target claim.

```text
many supporting-looking items
+ no constitutive source route
→ no compensation
```

The markers also do not establish falsity. Where the available source basis cannot determine whether the alleged source-target relation holds, the correct local finding may be source-limited, underdetermined, or currently untestable. The distinction between failed TraceableLoad and unavailable testing remains mandatory throughout WP2.

<a id="45-5-constitutive-source-trace"></a>

### 45.5 Constitutive Source Trace

A Constitutive Source Trace is the minimal reconstructible account of how the declared source carries the target claim. It is not a transcript of the source, a complete causal history, or a lossless duplicate. It records the source features and relations that must remain visible for the target to retain warrant within its declared context and Claim Scope.

#### C45-CONSTITUTIVESOURCETRACE-01 — the source trace must distinguish load-bearing, modulating, compressed, excluded, and uncertain features

Every Chapter-45 source trace must classify relevant source material into five functional trace positions:

```yaml
constitutive_source_trace:
  load_bearing:
  modulating:
  compressed:
  excluded:
  uncertain:
```

These positions are not new top-level schema fields and do not alter the canonical five-part `loss` object. They are a controlled conceptual view of existing source, relation, continuity, Loss, alternative, and claim fields in the Shared Transformation Record.

**Load-bearing features** are those without which the declared target claim or function would require substantive revision, reduction, separation, or withdrawal. They may include source structures, occurrence relations, temporal ordering, recurrent constraints, role distributions, costs, asymmetries, or bounded functional dependencies. The record must state what target-claim element each feature bears.

**Modulating features** alter the degree, range, timing, stability, or contextual expression of the target without independently constituting the target identity. Their role must not be inflated into load-bearing necessity, but neither may they disappear if the claim depends on their modulation.

**Compressed features** remain represented at lower resolution. Compression is admissible when the record states what has been condensed, which relation survives the condensation, and what claim burden the compressed representation can still carry.

**Excluded features** are deliberately left outside the target construction. Exclusion can be warranted by Claim Scope, source limits, non-relevance, or operation design, but it must be declared where it affects alternatives, boundary conditions, costs, asymmetries, or possible countercases.

**Uncertain features** are present or potentially load-bearing but insufficiently established, incompletely mapped, or contested in the available source basis. Uncertainty must remain visible rather than being converted into target coherence.

```text
load-bearing
≠ merely mentioned

modulating
≠ irrelevant

compressed
≠ erased

excluded
≠ disproven

uncertain
≠ freely assertable
```

The trace positions must remain compatible with the canonical Loss declaration:

```yaml
loss:
  preserved:
  compressed:
  excluded:
  uncertain:
  irrecoverable:
```

The two views answer different questions. The Constitutive Source Trace asks how source features function in carrying the target. The Loss object asks what the transformation preserves, compresses, excludes, leaves uncertain, or makes irrecoverable. A feature may be load-bearing and compressed if the compression preserves the relation needed for the target. A modulating feature may be excluded only if the resulting claim is correspondingly bounded. An uncertain feature may become irrecoverable after an undocumented aggregation, which must be declared rather than hidden.

The trace must preserve exact coordinates. At minimum it identifies:

- the source object and target object or bounded target function;
- the operation occurrence or comparison scope;
- Frame, source and target granularity, relative level where relevant, and temporal scope;
- the source basis and Claim Ceiling;
- the relevant source features and their trace positions;
- the constitutive relations among those features;
- the target-claim elements they support;
- alternatives, rival traces, continuity burdens, and five-part Loss.

A Constitutive Source Trace can be concise. Concision is not weakness when the load-bearing path is explicit. Conversely, a detailed narrative can remain untraceable when it does not identify the source-target relation.

```text
short explicit load path
→ possible TraceableLoad

long source narrative without mapping
→ no TraceableLoad proof
```

The PMS boundary remains occurrence-based. A source trace may identify a particular □ occurrence, an Α occurrence, a Λ occurrence, an Ω occurrence, or a relation among operator-typed occurrences where these are claim-bearing. The operator type itself is not a source feature to be decomposed, and operator-layer membership is not a substitute for the occurrence relation. Δ–Ψ and its dependency order remain unchanged.

<a id="45-6-source-target-dependency"></a>

### 45.6 Source-Target Dependency

TraceableLoad requires more than a mapped source inventory. The target must depend on the mapped source trace in a way that is relevant to the declared claim. Dependency is established in principle when a warranted change to a declared load-bearing source feature would require a corresponding change in the target claim or function. Chapter 45 defines this requirement; Chapter 46 owns the complete Counterfactual Sensitivity procedure and its six canonical values.

#### C45-SOURCETARGETDEPENDENCY-01 — relevant source change must imply a specified target-claim effect in the declared context

A minimal dependency statement has four parts:

```text
source feature or relation F
+ warranted modification ΔF
+ target-claim element q
+ expected claim effect Δq
```

The record must state why `F` is load-bearing and what would change if `F` changed. Possible claim effects include:

- target identity no longer warranted;
- target function weakened or strengthened within a bounded range;
- Claim Scope narrowed;
- one claim separated into several claims;
- an alternative restored;
- a continuity requirement failed;
- the operation route changed;
- Claim Reduction or Stop became necessary.

The dependency statement remains source-bounded. It does not authorize invented alternate histories, universal causal claims, or unsupported simulations. A relevant modification must be meaningful within the available evidence and the declared source object.

```text
source-target dependency for Q
≠ causal proof
≠ empirical truth proof
≠ universal necessity
```

The target need not change under every source modification. Only changes to declared load-bearing features are relevant to the dependency claim. Modulating features may alter range, timing, or degree without changing target identity. Excluded features may remain irrelevant within the current Claim Scope. Uncertain features may prevent a decisive dependency result.

#### C45-ASSOCIATIONDEPENDENCE-01 — dependency must remain distinct from association, citation proximity, analogy, and semantic resemblance

The following are insufficient by themselves:

```text
F occurs near Q
F is often cited with Q
F resembles the target label
F and Q share vocabulary
F is temporally adjacent to Q
F is statistically or descriptively associated with Q
```

Association can motivate a dependency test. It does not complete it. The record must reconstruct the relation by which the source feature carries the target claim. For `PROJECT_AS`, resemblance to a frame, attractor, or modulator label is not enough; the origin-typed source object must support the bounded target function through a constitutive relation. For `COMPOSE`, co-presence in an inventory is not enough; selection, order, and relations must constitute the composite. For `DECOMPOSE`, supported fragments are not enough; the finer result must return to the same reference object and preserve the source function relevant to the claim.

```text
association
≠ structural dependence

analogy
≠ projection

label fit
≠ Constitutive Source Trace
```

This distinction preserves analogy as a possible bounded output rather than allowing it to masquerade as functional dependence. Where resemblance is informative but source-target dependency is absent, the eventual result may require `analogy_only`, Claim Reduction, Stop, or another canonical class after all applicable checks. WP2 selects none of them for the synthetic pressure packet.

#### C45-OPERATIONVARIANTS-01 — each core operation carries a distinct upper-bound burden without changing its signature

The common dependency rule takes operation-specific forms.

For `COMPOSE`:

```text
selected source structures
+ declared order and constitutive relations
+ explicit compression and exclusion
→ target composite whose claim changes when a load-bearing source relation changes
```

The upper-bound risk is a composite that survives arbitrary substitution of its components, order, or relation topology. Mere inclusion does not establish dependency. A composite must retain a relation trace adequate to its claim.

For `DECOMPOSE`:

```text
same source occurrence or composite
+ finer supported reconstruction
+ source-function return
→ target claim that changes when a load-bearing finer relation changes
```

The upper-bound risk is fragmentation: source-supported parts remain, but the path back to the same reference object or the function under examination is lost. `DECOMPOSE` never opens operator types; it opens occurrences and composites only.

For `PROJECT_AS`:

```text
origin-typed source object
+ bounded target context
+ constitutive source relation
→ target function whose warrant changes when a load-bearing source feature changes
```

The upper-bound risk is semantic elasticity: the target function remains unchanged despite materially different source structures. Origin type remains preserved, and the target function remains contextual rather than becoming a primitive or operator identity.

The operation variants do not create separate ceilings or new operations. They apply one system-wide Traceability Ceiling to the distinct signatures of `COMPOSE`, `DECOMPOSE`, and `PROJECT_AS`.

#### C45-RIVALSOURCETEST-01 — rival or opposed source structures must be used where available to pressure-test target elasticity

Where the source basis provides rival, contrasting, or opposed configurations, the record must ask whether the same target would still be asserted and why. The purpose is not to demand that every robust target change under every variation. It is to expose labels whose application is independent of the source burden they purport to summarize.

A bounded rival-source test states:

```text
source configuration S1
→ target claim Q1

materially different or opposed configuration S2
→ expected target claim Q2

relation between Q1 and Q2
→ declared and justified
```

Possible outcomes remain open. The target may change substantially, change only in scope or degree, remain stable for a warranted reason, or remain untestable. Stability can indicate robustness when the target depends on a higher-order relation preserved across `S1` and `S2`. It can indicate elasticity when opposed source structures produce the same label without a reconstructible invariant.

```text
target stable across S1 and S2
≠ robustness automatically
≠ elasticity automatically
```

Chapter 46 owns the full sensitivity classification into `strongly_sensitive`, `partially_sensitive`, `weakly_sensitive`, `insensitive`, `underdetermined`, and `untestable`. Chapter 45 requires the dependency and rival-source declaration but does not assign one of those six values here.

Several source traces may support competing targets. Rival-source pressure therefore does not imply that one target must absorb all alternatives. It may reveal bounded co-validity, incompatibility, Claim Reduction, source limitation, or Non-Capture. Those mappings remain downstream.

#### C45-SOURCELIMITDISTINCTION-01 — absent TraceableLoad must remain distinct from source-limited or currently untestable dependency

Three conditions must not collapse:

```text
source trace reconstructed
+ dependency supported
→ possible TraceableLoad

source trace reconstructed
+ dependency not supported under adequate test
→ possible upper-bound failure or Claim Reduction pressure

source basis insufficient to reconstruct or test dependency
→ source-limited / underdetermined / untestable
```

The third condition does not prove that the target is traceable or untraceable. It limits the permissible claim. Missing records, unavailable temporal evidence, uncertain relation topology, or inaccessible cost and asymmetry data may prevent an adequate test. The correct response is to expose that limitation and preserve the Claim Ceiling.

```text
cannot test dependency
≠ dependency absent
≠ target warranted
```

Source limitation also remains distinct from Non-Capture. A known missing source component may block judgment while the grammar remains adequate in principle. Non-Capture may arise when the available transformation grammar or target vocabulary cannot adequately represent the relevant structure. Chapter 52 owns the integrated Non-Capture procedure.

The synthetic pressure packet therefore remains protected:

```yaml
U0:
  status: citation_sufficiency_statement_not_accepted

T:
  result: unadjudicated

TL:
  result: unadjudicated

TE:
  result: unadjudicated

TS:
  result: unadjudicated

upper_bound:
  result_selected: false
  route_selected: false
  output_class_selected: false
  mandatory_upper_stop_selected: false
  claim_reduction_selected: false
```

#### C45-WP2-HANDOFF-01 — WP2 hands WP3 a complete source-trace and dependency architecture without adjudicating elasticity, exhaustiveness, Stop, Claim Reduction, or the pressure packet

WP2 establishes:

- recurrent upper-bound failure forms as non-automatic pressure markers;
- a Constitutive Source Trace distinguishing load-bearing, modulating, compressed, excluded, and uncertain features;
- explicit compatibility between that trace view and the canonical five-part Loss object;
- source-target dependency through a declared source modification and target-claim effect;
- association, citation proximity, analogy, semantic resemblance, and operator-label substitution as insufficient;
- operation-specific dependency burdens for `COMPOSE`, `DECOMPOSE`, and `PROJECT_AS` without changing their signatures;
- rival-source pressure where available, while reserving the full six-value sensitivity procedure to Chapter 46;
- source limitation and current untestability as distinct from absent TraceableLoad.

WP2 does not establish the complete macro-label elasticity test, the non-exhaustive preservation rule, Shared-Record mapping, Mandatory Upper Stop, Claim Reduction, final Output mapping, or the integrated Chapters 46–53 procedures. It does not adjudicate `T`, `TL`, `TE`, or `TS`; select a route; assign a sensitivity value; choose Stop or Claim Reduction; or map a pressure object to a canonical Output Class.

No Case, Test, or Smoke YAML is created. The three-operation, ten-class, sixteen-Rule, twelve-stage, six-sensitivity-value, and five-Loss-field inventories remain unchanged. `authority_inheritance` remains prohibited.

The controlled handoff is:

```text
upper-bound failure-form markers
+ five-position Constitutive Source Trace
+ source-target dependency declaration
+ operation-specific variants
+ rival-source pressure
+ source-limitation distinction
+ pressure-packet non-adjudication

→ Chapter 45 WP3
→ §45.7 Macro-Label Elasticity
→ §45.8 Traceability without Exhaustiveness
→ §45.9 Shared-Record View
→ §45.10 Mandatory Upper Stop and Claim Reduction
→ §45.11 Output Mapping and Handoff
```

<a id="45-7-macro-label-elasticity"></a>

### 45.7 Macro-Label Elasticity

A macro-label becomes analytically elastic when it remains applicable across materially different or opposed source structures without a reconstructible invariant that explains the stability. Elasticity is not mere breadth. A broad target may legitimately cover diverse cases when the same load-bearing relation persists across them. The problem arises when the label survives because its application conditions are too weak, too movable, or too source-indifferent to permit meaningful counterpressure.

#### C45-MACROLABELELASTICITY-01 — macro-label elasticity arises where materially opposed sources leave the target unchanged without a reconstructible invariant or possible countercase

The minimum pressure relation is:

```text
materially opposed source structures S1 and S2
+ same target label Q
+ no reconstructible invariant relation R
+ no possible source-based countercase
→ possible macro-label elasticity
```

Three questions must be kept distinct:

```text
Does Q remain stable across S1 and S2?
≠ Is the stability warranted by a preserved invariant?
≠ Is Q so elastic that source opposition cannot matter?
```

Stable application can be warranted. A target may depend on a higher-order relation that remains present even when local components, temporal sequences, or role distributions differ. In that case, the record must identify the invariant, state why it is load-bearing, and show how the differences are classified as modulating, compressed, excluded, or uncertain rather than silently ignored.

Elasticity pressure increases when:

- mutually opposed source trajectories receive the same path or macrofunction label;
- contrasting local cost and asymmetry distributions leave the target claim unchanged without explanation;
- a projected frame-, attractor-, or modulator-function is asserted under source structures that would ordinarily undermine its constitutive relation;
- the target's conditions are reformulated after each counterexample so that no source configuration can count against it;
- the label preserves rhetorical continuity while Reference, Functional, or Temporal Continuity has disappeared;
- the same target is asserted whether load-bearing source features are present, absent, reversed, or replaced.

The decisive problem is not semantic width but source independence:

```text
broad label
≠ elastic label automatically

stable label with reconstructible invariant
≠ source-indifferent label
```

A valid broad claim therefore states both its invariant and its variation range. It identifies what may change without defeating the claim and what change would require narrowing, separation, retyping of the target function, Claim Reduction, Stop, or rejection of the transformation. A claim without any possible defeating or narrowing condition cannot demonstrate source-target dependency.

This does not authorize an automatic failure verdict. Apparent invariance may reflect incomplete sources, an inadequately specified rival configuration, or a genuinely robust higher-order relation. Chapter 46 owns the full Counterfactual Sensitivity procedure and its six values. Chapter 45 records the elasticity pressure and the required source invariant; it does not assign `strongly_sensitive`, `partially_sensitive`, `weakly_sensitive`, `insensitive`, `underdetermined`, or `untestable` here.

The PMS boundary remains exact. A macro-label cannot gain warrant merely by absorbing more Δ–Ψ-typed occurrences. Nor does recurrence of one operator type prove a stable target function. The record must reconstruct which occurrences, dependencies, and relations carry the target claim and which source changes would matter.

```text
same operator label across opposed occurrences
≠ same target function automatically

more operator occurrences absorbed by Q
≠ stronger Source-Target Dependency
```

The synthetic pressure object `TE` remains unadjudicated. WP3 establishes the method for identifying elasticity; it does not determine whether `TE` is robust, elastic, underdetermined, source-limited, or untestable.

<a id="45-8-traceability-without-exhaustiveness"></a>

### 45.8 Traceability without Exhaustiveness

Traceability does not require the target to reproduce every source feature, relation, interval, document, cost, asymmetry, or uncertainty. Such a requirement would make `COMPOSE`, `DECOMPOSE`, and `PROJECT_AS` impossible whenever they perform genuine analytical work. Transformation necessarily selects, reconstructs, compresses, and bounds. The ceiling is crossed not whenever detail is lost, but when the load-bearing trace required for the declared claim is no longer reconstructible.

#### C45-NONEXHAUSTIVE-01 — TraceableLoad requires preservation of the load-bearing trace, not exhaustive, lossless, or total reproduction

The governing distinction is:

```text
non-exhaustive transformation
+ preserved load-bearing trace
+ explicit Loss
→ possible TraceableLoad

exhaustive inventory
+ no constitutive relation
→ no TraceableLoad by exhaustiveness alone
```

An admissible compression may omit substantial detail while preserving:

- the source features on which the target identity depends;
- the constitutive relations among those features;
- the temporal ordering or duration necessary for the claim;
- relevant role, cost, exposure, and asymmetry distributions;
- the invariant or variation range supporting the target function;
- the exclusions and uncertainties that bound the Claim Scope.

The five-part Loss declaration remains mandatory:

```yaml
loss:
  preserved:
  compressed:
  excluded:
  uncertain:
  irrecoverable:
```

Traceability and Loss answer different questions. Traceability asks whether the target remains reconstructibly dependent on its source. Loss asks what the transformation preserves, compresses, excludes, leaves uncertain, or makes irrecoverable. A target may retain TraceableLoad while carrying major disclosed compression. It may also retain many details while losing the one relation that made the claim possible.

```text
large disclosed compression
≠ ceiling failure automatically

small hidden constitutive loss
≠ harmless simplification
```

The preservation criterion must therefore be claim-relative. For one target, temporal order may be load-bearing while local actor identity is not. For another, a role asymmetry may be decisive while exact chronology can be compressed. For a bounded projection, origin type and the constitutive source relation may be indispensable while many descriptive source attributes remain excluded. No universal list of details must always be retained.

Non-exhaustive traceability also prevents a false choice between total reproduction and arbitrary abstraction. STRATA permits selective representation only when selection is declared, relation-preserving, counterpressured, and bounded by Source and Claim Ceilings. The target must remain inspectable enough that a reviewer can return from the claim to the source features that carry it.

```text
target claim Q
→ inspectable return path to load-bearing source features
→ declared compression and exclusion
→ bounded TraceableLoad claim
```

This return path need not be a single linear chain. Several source traces may jointly support the target, and the same source feature may modulate several target elements. Where the relation is many-to-many, the record must preserve the dependency topology rather than flatten it into a source list.

Exhaustive reproduction is also no guarantee of TypeIntegrity or Reference Continuity. A complete inventory of operator-typed occurrences can still confuse operator types with occurrences, combine distinct reference objects, or project a target function without warrant. Formal and documentary completeness cannot compensate for a missing constitutive relation.

```text
lossless-looking representation
≠ admissibility proof

complete source inventory
≠ valid COMPOSE
≠ valid DECOMPOSE
≠ valid PROJECT_AS
```

<a id="45-9-shared-record-view"></a>

### 45.9 Shared-Record View

The Traceability-Ceiling procedure uses the existing Shared Transformation Record. The conceptual record shown in the Structure blueprint is a reading and audit view only. It does not establish a second grammar, a new top-level object, a seventeenth Rule, a thirteenth audit stage, or an eleventh Output Class.

#### C45-RECORDVIEW-01 — the Traceability-Ceiling view maps to existing Shared Transformation Record fields without a new object or schema field

The conceptual view is reconstructed through existing paths:

```yaml
traceability_ceiling_view:
  target_claim:
    from: claim_and_target_declarations
  constitutive_source_trace:
    from: source_basis_relations_and_admissibility_trace
  preserved_relations:
    from: reference_continuity_and_loss_preserved
  preserved_temporality:
    from: temporal_scope_temporal_continuity_and_trace
  preserved_cost_structure:
    from: affected_praxis_dimensions_trace_and_loss
  counterfactual_dependency:
    from: source_target_dependency_and_counterfactual_sensitivity
  abstraction_risk:
    from: local_upper_bound_findings_uncertainty_and_alternatives
  result:
    from: final_canonical_output_mapping_after_complete_audit
```

The mapping retains the ordinary record sequence:

1. declare the source object and source basis;
2. declare the operation occurrence or comparison scope;
