A debt may reveal an earlier defect where the compression was hidden, the prior claim exceeded the surviving load-bearing structure, or the record falsely implied completeness. But that is a new audit conclusion, not part of the definition of Compression Debt.

```text
later reopening required
≠ earlier failure automatically

later reopening reveals undisclosed claim-defeating omission
→ possible earlier hidden-Loss or overclaim pressure
```

#### C48-NOINVERTIBILITY-01 — Reopening does not imply invertibility or recovery of erased information

A prior compressed result may be reopened by returning to retained sources, linked component records, or newly available evidence. This does not mean the transformation is mechanically reversible.

```text
reopening from retained or new sources
≠ inversion of the prior transformation
≠ recovery of every compressed detail
≠ repair of irrecoverable Loss
```

`COMPOSE` cannot ordinarily be inverted into a unique source sequence merely from the composite. `DECOMPOSE` cannot be assumed to regenerate the exact prior compressed representation from its finer result. `PROJECT_AS` cannot be reversed from a target function into a unique origin object or operator occurrence. Any reopening must therefore be recorded as a new testable claim with its own sources, coordinates, Loss declaration, and disposition.

The earlier result remains part of the audit trail:

```text
T0 with disclosed compression for Q0
+ later reopening T1 for Q1
→ two linked transformation claims

T1 success
≠ T0 rewritten as lossless
≠ T0 disposition erased
```

Where the required relation is no longer recoverable, the record must preserve `irrecoverable` or source-limited status rather than simulate inversion through formal reconstruction. A model-generated completion, familiar PMS label, or plausible narrative cannot restore a missing occurrence-level or relation-level trace.

<a id="48-10-loss-disclosure-rule"></a>

### 48.10 Loss Disclosure Rule

Every STRATA transformation record must disclose the exact five canonical Loss fields:

```yaml
loss:
  preserved:
  compressed:
  excluded:
  uncertain:
  irrecoverable:
```

No field may be omitted because the analyst assumes it is empty, irrelevant, obvious, or recoverable elsewhere. An empty field must still be explicit and, where materially relevant, state why no entry is warranted. This rule ensures that the absence of a declaration is not mistaken for the absence of Loss.

#### C48-LOSSDISCLOSURE-01 — Every transformation occurrence requires the exact five-field Loss declaration

The declaration must satisfy four conditions.

**First, field identity must remain exact.** The canonical names are `preserved`, `compressed`, `excluded`, `uncertain`, and `irrecoverable`. Local labels such as “omitted,” “reduced,” “unknown,” “debt,” “bias,” or “hidden” may describe findings, but they must map into the five-field structure or into separate existing audit and routing paths. They do not create a sixth Loss field.

**Second, entries must be claim-bearing.** A record should not merely list quantities of retained or omitted material. It must identify the structure or relation and explain its possible effect on Reference, TypeIntegrity-view, Function, Temporal Continuity, PraxisPurchase, TraceableLoad, alternatives, or Claim Scope.

**Third, operation and coordinates must be visible.** The same source structure can have different Loss statuses under different operations, Frames, granularities, periods, or target functions. A relation compressed in one `COMPOSE` may be preserved in another reconstruction and excluded in a bounded `PROJECT_AS`.

**Fourth, provenance must be retained.** The record must distinguish Loss inherited from the source basis, produced by an earlier transformation, generated by the current operation, or left attributionally uncertain.

```yaml
loss:
  preserved:
    - structure: constitutive_relation_R
      trace: source_occurrences_and_temporal_order
  compressed:
    - structure: local_variation_V
      claim_effect: retained_as_known_heterogeneity
  excluded:
    - structure: alternative_path_A
      reason: outside_declared_scope
      restoration_pressure: could_narrow_Q
  uncertain:
    - structure: interval_boundary_B
      discriminator_missing: source_conflict
  irrecoverable:
    - structure: missing_relation_M
      loss_origin: inherited_source_loss
```

This representation remains a controlled view of the Shared Transformation Record. Chapter 7 retains record ownership. Chapter 48 specifies the mandatory semantics and disclosure burden of the existing `loss` path.

Disclosure must also remain operation-specific:

- `COMPOSE` must identify component, relation, sequence, periodization, alternative, and heterogeneity Loss;
- `DECOMPOSE` must identify source-object, coarser-function, internal-relation, threshold, and return-path Loss;
- `PROJECT_AS` must identify origin-type, source-feature, rival-function, target-context, and validity-scope Loss.

A complete-looking record with generic statements such as “some detail was lost” does not pass the rule. Formal presence is necessary but not sufficient.

```text
five fields present
≠ substantive Loss disclosure complete

substantive disclosure
≠ Loss admissible automatically
```

<a id="48-11-loss-is-not-automatic-failure"></a>

### 48.11 Loss Is Not Automatic Failure

Transformation necessarily changes representation. A method that treated all Loss as failure would prohibit `COMPOSE`, `DECOMPOSE`, and `PROJECT_AS` in principle. STRATA instead asks whether the Loss is disclosed, bounded, source-traceable, and compatible with the exact claim that survives.

```text
Loss present
≠ failed_transformation automatically

Loss disclosed
≠ admissible automatically
```

The decisive issue is not how much information is lost in the abstract. It is whether the surviving structure still carries the claimed praxeological and traceable load, whether alternatives remain visible, and whether the Claim Scope is calibrated to what the transformation can warrant.

#### C48-LOSSNOTFAILURE-01 — Disclosed Loss may remain compatible with a bounded claim where load-bearing structure survives

A Loss-bearing transformation may remain compatible with a bounded admissibility result only where all applicable conditions remain satisfied:

1. the exact five-field Loss declaration is complete;
2. claim-bearing preserved structure remains identifiable through source, relation, function, reference, or temporal trace;
3. Compression is acknowledged rather than presented as exhaustive preservation;
4. Exclusions are justified and pressure-tested for claim effects;
5. Uncertainty is retained without false precision;
6. Irrecoverability and its origin are explicit;
7. materially relevant alternatives and counterpaths remain visible;
8. the Claim Scope and Claim Ceiling do not exceed the surviving load-bearing structure;
9. TypeIntegrity and applicable Continuity dimensions remain within their bounds;
10. no hidden Loss, authority inheritance, or self-immunizing reframing protects the claim.

A large disclosed Loss may therefore remain compatible with a narrow claim. For example, a long institutional history may be heavily compressed into a bounded claim about one stable coordination function where the source-function relation, major counterpaths, temporal limits, and internal asymmetries remain visible. The result does not claim exhaustive institutional capture.

Conversely, a small exclusion may defeat a claim where it carries the only rival path, decisive Non-Event, critical asymmetry, origin-type limit, or source feature necessary for the projected function.

```text
large disclosed Loss
+ surviving load-bearing relation
+ calibrated claim
→ possible bounded passage

small hidden or excluded relation
+ claim-defeating function
→ possible narrowing, reduction, Stop, or failure pressure
```

Neither pattern is adjudicated for the synthetic packet in this chapter.

#### C48-CLAIMCALIBRATION-01 — Claims must be calibrated to surviving structure, alternatives, uncertainty, and irrecoverability

Claim Calibration requires the analyst to formulate the strongest claim actually carried by the post-transformation record, not the strongest claim suggested by the target label.

The calibration statement must declare:

- what preserved structure carries the claim;
- what compression limits detail or generalization;
- what exclusions bound scope and alternatives;
- what uncertainty prevents stronger formulation;
- what irrecoverability prevents retesting or recovery;
- which contexts, periods, granularities, and functions remain outside validity;
- what finding would require Claim Reduction, Stop, or Non-Capture.

```text
surviving load-bearing structure L
+ declared alternatives A
+ uncertainty U
+ irrecoverability I
→ maximum bounded claim Q_b
```

A calibrated claim may be narrower in temporal scope, functional scope, reference scope, granularity, or confidence. Calibration does not automatically choose `admissible_with_bounded_claim`, `claim_reduction_required`, or any other canonical Output Class. Chapter 49 owns the integrated Source/Claim Ceiling and Calibration Boundary; Chapters 51–53 own later routing and final audit.

A later narrowed or reopened claim remains a new testable claim. It cannot retroactively convert hidden Loss into disclosed Loss or erase an earlier overclaim.

<a id="48-12-hidden-loss"></a>

### 48.12 Hidden Loss

Hidden Loss occurs where the transformation record or prose conceals, misclassifies, or denies a material Loss in a way that strengthens, stabilizes, or immunizes the claim. It is not merely Loss that the analyst has not yet noticed. The relevant pressure is whether the representation presents a stronger continuity, completeness, homogeneity, precision, or functional fit than the source and record can support.

```text
hidden Loss
= material Loss concealed, misclassified, or denied
  in a claim-strengthening way

hidden Loss
≠ every omission
≠ every compression
≠ every uncertainty
≠ every analyst error automatically
```

#### C48-HIDDENLOSS-01 — Hidden Loss must be tested where Loss is reclassified into preservation, disappearance, homogeneity, or false precision

At minimum, the audit must test whether:

- compression is presented as complete preservation;
- excluded alternatives or counterpaths disappear from the accessible record;
- internal heterogeneity is represented as component or operator homogeneity;
- uncertainty is converted into exact sequence, period, relation, number, or operator typing;
- irrecoverable structure is silently reconstructed as if directly observed;
- origin-type limits disappear behind a target-function label;
- `COMPOSE` hides relation dilution, contradictory sources, or asymmetric costs;
- `DECOMPOSE` hides loss of the source object or coarser functional return path;
- `PROJECT_AS` hides rival functions, source independence, or context expansion;
- a familiar PMS operator label substitutes for an occurrence-level trace;
- a new Frame, granularity, periodization, or target function suppresses an earlier failed or stopped claim.

A hidden-Loss finding requires a reconstructible mismatch between the presented Loss status and the source-supported status. Suspicion, interpretive discomfort, or the mere possibility of alternatives is not enough.

```text
represented as preserved
+ source shows material compression or exclusion
+ claim strengthened by misclassification
→ possible hidden Loss
```

Where the source cannot determine whether a material Loss occurred, the correct status may remain `uncertain`, source-limited, or later Non-Capture rather than a hidden-Loss verdict.

Hidden Loss is especially dangerous in operation chains. A locally disclosed compression can become hidden when the next operation treats the intermediate object as complete. Each handoff must therefore inherit the prior Loss declaration and test whether the next claim depends on structure that the earlier operation compressed, excluded, left uncertain, or rendered irrecoverable.

```text
prior Loss declaration
+ later operation treats intermediate object as lossless
→ possible chain-level hidden-Loss pressure
```

The remedy is not automatic restoration of all omitted structure. Depending on the later audit, the method may require a narrower claim, a reopened source record, separated claims, Claim Reduction, Mandatory Stop, or Non-Capture. Chapter 48 establishes the Loss condition; Chapters 49–53 retain ownership of the integrated decision.

#### C48-HANDOFF49-53-01 — Chapter 48 preserves later ownership of ceilings, calibration, anti-immunization, Stop, Non-Capture, and final audit

Chapter 48 hands forward:

- a closed five-field Loss vocabulary;
- explicit Reconstruction Selection and Selection Effects;
- Compression Debt as a later burden without retroactive invalidity by definition;
- mandatory substantive Loss disclosure;
- the distinction between disclosed Loss, potentially admissible Loss, and hidden Loss;
- non-invertible reopening;
- Claim Calibration pressure relative to surviving load-bearing structure.

It does not decide:

- the Source or Claim Ceiling under Chapter 49;
- Anti-Immunization under Chapter 50;
- integrated Stop, Claim Reduction, or re-entry under Chapter 51;
- Non-Capture under Chapter 52;
- the final twelve-stage audit and canonical Output mapping under Chapter 53.

Local findings such as `Compression Debt`, `hidden Loss`, `large disclosed Loss`, `small claim-defeating exclusion`, or `reopening burden` are not new Output Classes.

#### C48-HANDOFF-01 — WP3 hands WP4 a complete Loss method without adjudicating the synthetic pressure packet

WP3 completes the Chapter-48 method by establishing:

- Compression Debt as a later reopening or revision burden;
- reopening without a presumption of invertibility or recovery;
- the mandatory exact five-field Loss Disclosure Rule;
- bounded conditions under which disclosed Loss may remain compatible with a calibrated claim;
- Hidden Loss as claim-strengthening concealment, misclassification, or denial;
- Claim Calibration to surviving structure, alternatives, uncertainty, and irrecoverability;
- controlled handoff to Chapters 49–53.

The synthetic pressure packet remains unchanged:

```yaml
loss_pressure_packet_after_wp3:
  S0: lossless_transformation_assertion_not_accepted
  LP: unadjudicated
  LC: unadjudicated
  LE: unadjudicated
  LU: unadjudicated
  LI: unadjudicated
  CD: unadjudicated
  HL: unadjudicated
  route_selected: false
  stop_selected: false
  claim_reduction_selected: false
  output_class_selected: false
```

No Loss result, Compression-Debt result, Hidden-Loss result, route, Stop, Claim Reduction, or canonical Output Class has been selected. No Case, Test, or Smoke YAML has been created. The artifact-complete RETYPE lock remains `mandatory_stop`; the required lock packages remain `0/3`; Part IV remains open; and Part IV final lock remains unavailable.

```yaml
governance:
  authority_inheritance: prohibited
```

<a id="chapter-49-source-limits-and-calibration-limits"></a>

## Chapter 49 — Source Limits and Calibration Limits

<a id="49-1-source-bounded-reconstruction"></a>

### 49.1 Source-Bounded Reconstruction

STRATA reconstructs relations, temporal structures, composites, finer resolutions, and bounded contextual functions from a declared source basis. It does not convert a source basis into a complete representation of the reference object, and it does not gain warrant merely by expressing a reconstruction in exact prose, a diagram, YAML, a schema-valid record, or PMS terminology.

The governing limit is:

```text
formal precision
≤ epistemic precision of the source basis
```

Epistemic precision concerns what the available materials can responsibly distinguish: which occurrence took place, which relation connected two structures, which temporal order is established, which component is supported, which target function remains source-dependent, and which alternatives or gaps remain live. Formal precision concerns how exactly those distinctions are represented. Formal precision may preserve or expose source precision; it may not create it.

```text
more fields
more decimal places
more operator labels
more exact chronology
more formal detail
≠ more source support
```

#### C49-SOURCEBOUNDED-01 — Reconstruction may not exceed the epistemic precision of its source basis

A source-bounded reconstruction must declare at least:

- the reference object and target claim;
- the source basis and Source Scope;
- the operation occurrence under test;
- what is directly supported;
- what is inferentially reconstructed;
- what structure is missing or inaccessible;
- what remains uncertain or irrecoverable;
- the analytical coordinates under which the sources are used;
- the strongest claim the current source basis can carry.

The limit applies to all three operations.

For `COMPOSE`, the sources must support the selected occurrences, their relevant order or branching, their constitutive relations, and the identity of the proposed composite. A list of similarly labelled events does not by itself support a path, trajectory, attractor, or composite object.

For `DECOMPOSE`, the sources must support both the finer components and the relations by which they remain parts of the same reference object. Finer vocabulary, plausible mechanisms, or analytically convenient distinctions cannot substitute for component and relation support.

For `PROJECT_AS`, the sources must support the origin-typed object, the source feature claimed to carry the bounded target function, and the context in which that function is attributed. Target usefulness or semantic resemblance does not supply a missing source-function dependency.

A source-bounded result may remain coarse, partial, calibration-open, or explicitly uncertain. That limitation is not a defect to be hidden through formal enrichment. Where a stronger reconstruction would require unsupported distinctions, the claim must remain narrower or the additional structure must remain unclaimed.

#### C49-SOURCEPRECISION-01 — Record, notation, and model precision may not outrun source precision

False precision occurs where the representation makes a distinction more exact than its evidential basis. Typical forms include:

- exact dates where only relative order is documented;
- a single sequence where several sequences remain source-compatible;
- a component boundary not present in the materials;
- a definite causal connector where only temporal or structural association is supported;
- an exact PMS occurrence attribution based only on a familiar operator label;
- a fixed target-function relation where rival source features remain plausible;
- a numerical threshold for which no comparison basis has been established.

The remedy is not to remove all formal structure. The remedy is to align the form with the source-supported distinction. A time range may replace a date, a set of live sequences may replace one chronology, an unresolved relation may remain explicit, and a bounded hypothesis may replace a settled structural claim.

```text
source-compatible plurality
→ preserve plurality

source-supported range
→ represent range

source-limited relation
→ retain limitation
```

Machine-readable exactness remains useful because it can expose contradictions, missing declarations, and category errors. Its value is inspectability, not epistemic supplementation. Chapter 49 WP3 owns the full Technical Formalization Boundary; this section establishes only the source-precision constraint that precedes it.

<a id="49-2-source-types"></a>

### 49.2 Source Types

No source type supports every STRATA distinction equally. Source classification is therefore claim- and operation-relative rather than a universal hierarchy of evidential worth. A source may directly support one relation, indirectly support another, and leave a third inaccessible.

#### C49-SOURCETYPES-01 — Source types must be distinguished by the structures and claims they can carry

At minimum, Chapter 49 distinguishes six recurrent source types.

**Direct documentation.** Transcripts, signed decisions, messages, reports, contracts, logs, or contemporaneous notes may support recorded wording, declared commitments, formal dispositions, timestamps, and documented occurrence. They do not automatically establish private motives, unrecorded alternatives, actual implementation, or the completeness of the recorded frame.

**Temporally ordered records.** Event logs, dated correspondence, process histories, version chains, and chronological files may support sequence, duration, delay, repetition, transition, and bounded non-realization. Their order may still be incomplete, administratively generated, or silent about causal and relational structure.

**Institutional materials.** Rules, mandates, role descriptions, procedures, official minutes, and policy documents may support expected occurrences, formal responsibilities, admitted options, and institutional frames. They do not by themselves establish enactment, compliance, practical uptake, or the actual distribution of capacity and burden.

**Observable configurations.** Repeated arrangements, workflows, resource distributions, interaction patterns, or stable role configurations may support structural regularity and asymmetry within the observed field. They do not automatically disclose historical formation, intention, counterfactual alternatives, or an unobserved temporal path.

**Reconstructed transitions.** A transition may be inferred from several materials whose relations jointly support a bounded change even though no source states the transition as a single event. Such reconstruction can be warranted, but its component sources, bridging relations, alternatives, and missing links must remain visible. It may not be relabelled as direct documentation.

**Existing PMS objects.** A previously warranted PMS occurrence, configuration, path, composite, or derived object may serve as a source object for a later STRATA transformation. Its use inherits the earlier record's Source Scope, Loss, uncertainty, type, and disposition. A PMS label does not replenish evidence lost in the earlier transformation and does not convert an abstract operator type into a concrete occurrence.

```text
source type
→ characteristic support and limitation profile

source type
≠ universal evidence rank
≠ automatic claim strength
```

Several source types may be combined. Combination can strengthen a reconstruction where their relations are explicit, but volume or variety alone does not resolve contradiction. An official record and an observed configuration may support different dimensions of the same object. Their disagreement may be a material finding rather than noise to be averaged away.

#### C49-DIRECTINFERRED-01 — Direct support, inferred structure, and missing structure must remain separately declared

A source-and-claim record must preserve at least three statuses:

```yaml
direct_support:
inferred_structure:
missing_structure:
```

**Direct support** identifies what a source establishes without the specific bridging inference made by the transformation. It is still frame- and claim-relative; a direct quotation supports that wording, not every interpretation of it.

**Inferred structure** identifies a relation warranted through comparison, ordering, composition, decomposition, continuity, counterfactual pressure, or source-function reconstruction. The inference must name its bridge and materially plausible alternatives.

**Missing structure** identifies information or relations unavailable within the declared Source Scope. Missing structure may be relevant, irrelevant, later recoverable, uncertain, or irrecoverable. Its status cannot be decided merely from absence.

```text
directly recorded element
≠ all relations involving that element directly supported

plausible inference
≠ direct support

missing structure
≠ negative finding
```

This separation prevents a common ratchet: direct fragments are combined into an inference, the inference is later cited as though directly observed, and a still later transformation treats the derived object as a source free of its original limitations. Every operation-chain handoff must preserve the direct/inferred/missing distinction.

<a id="49-3-missing-source-versus-non-event"></a>

### 49.3 Missing Source versus Non-Event

A gap in the source basis does not establish that an event failed to occur. A source can be silent because the event did not occur, because it occurred outside the record, because the record is incomplete, because the event was not documentable in that medium, or because the declared Source Scope does not reach it.

```text
no record
≠ recorded non-occurrence
≠ structured Non-Event
```

#### C49-MISSINGSOURCENONEVENT-01 — Missing information, unknown occurrence, recorded non-occurrence, and `Λ` remain non-equivalent

The method must distinguish four states.

**Missing information.** The current source basis does not contain the information required to determine occurrence status or a relevant relation.

**Unknown event status.** The analysis cannot establish whether the event occurred within the relevant frame and temporal scope.

**Recorded non-occurrence.** A source supports that a specified realization condition was not met within a declared interval—for example, a deadline log showing no required filing by closure of the period.

**`Λ` Non-Event.** A meaningful failure or delay of an expected occurrence within a frame. In addition to source-supported non-realization, `Λ` requires an identifiable expected occurrence, a warranted expectation relation, a bounded realization condition or window, and praxis-relevant structural consequence.

```text
missing information
→ occurrence status unresolved

recorded non-occurrence
→ realization condition source-supported as unmet

Λ
→ expected occurrence
+ warranted frame
+ bounded window or realization condition
+ source-supported non-realization
+ praxis-relevant difference
```

A document set that contains no follow-up record supports only the statement that no follow-up is present within that set. It does not establish that no follow-up occurred. Even a recorded non-occurrence is not automatically `Λ`: the expectation frame or praxis relevance may be absent.

The distinction is operation-specific. `COMPOSE` may not insert a Non-Event merely to complete a plausible path. `DECOMPOSE` may reveal that a coarse “absence” consists of incomplete records, an open window, and one documented missed condition rather than one Λ occurrence. `PROJECT_AS` may not project an unknown event status as institutional neglect, blocked responsibility, or another bounded function without a source-supported expectation and source-function relation.

A familiar PMS label never supplies missing occurrence evidence:

```text
Λ available in PMS Base
≠ Λ occurred in this source object
```

Where the source cannot discriminate among occurrence, non-occurrence, and incomplete recording, the proper result remains source-limited. Later Chapters 51–53 may determine Claim Reduction, Stop, Non-Capture, or canonical Output mapping; WP1 selects none of them.

<a id="49-4-inferential-distance"></a>

### 49.4 Inferential Distance

Inferential Distance is the source-to-claim distance created by the number and kind of unsupported or only conditionally supported bridges required to move from the available materials to the target claim. It is not a universal numerical score, and it is not identical to complexity, abstraction, granularity, or the number of analytical steps.

A claim can be abstract but near where a stable relation is directly supported. A seemingly concrete claim can be distant where it requires several undocumented transitions, hidden component relations, or target-function assumptions.

```text
inferential distance
≠ word count
≠ model complexity
≠ granularity alone
≠ operation count alone
```

#### C49-INFERENTIALDISTANCE-01 — Greater inferential distance increases justification, Loss, alternative, and reduction burdens

Inferential Distance increases where a reconstruction depends on one or more of the following:

- bridging gaps among sources;
- converting administrative sequence into historical or causal sequence;
- inferring relations among separately supported components;
- moving across Frames, granularities, relative levels, or temporal scopes;
- carrying a prior derived object through another operation;
- selecting one of several source-compatible paths or decompositions;
- treating an origin feature as constitutive of a target function;
- resolving contradiction or silence without an independent discriminator;
- extending a local occurrence claim into a recurrent or general pattern.

As distance increases, the record must increase—not necessarily in volume, but in explicit burden—its source trace, bridging rationale, alternative reconstruction, Counterfactual Sensitivity, Loss disclosure, continuity checks, and claim limitations.

```text
greater inferential distance
→ greater bridge disclosure
→ greater alternative pressure
→ greater Loss visibility
→ stronger justification burden
→ lower tolerance for unmarked precision
```

This relation is not a monotonic score. One independently supported bridge may reduce distance more than several pages of elaboration. Several formal steps may remain near if each is directly traceable. The method therefore records the load-bearing bridges rather than assigning a universal number.

A bounded inferential-distance statement should identify:

1. the directly supported starting points;
2. each material bridging inference;
3. the source or rule supporting the bridge;
4. live alternative bridges;
5. missing discriminators;
6. accumulated Loss and continuity effects;
7. the resulting limit on the target claim.

#### C49-DISTANCECLAIMEFFECT-01 — Unsupported inferential distance must contract Claim Scope and Claim Ceiling

Where a material bridge cannot be independently supported, the method may retain the directly supported structure and any nearer bounded inference, but the target claim cannot inherit the full precision or reach of the proposed chain.

```text
supported source S
+ supported bridge b1
+ unsupported bridge b2
+ target claim Q
→ Q does not inherit support through b2
```

The claim may need contraction by:

- narrowing the reference object;
- shortening temporal reach;
- reducing granularity or component specificity;
- removing causal, necessity, recurrence, or universality language;
- retaining several alternatives;
- marking the relation as provisional or source-limited;
- separating a supported descriptive claim from an unsupported functional claim.

Claim contraction is not automatically a canonical `claim_reduction_required` result at this stage. Chapter 49 establishes the burden; Chapters 51–53 own integrated routing and Output mapping. Similarly, approaching the Source Ceiling does not yet select `mandatory_stop` or `non_capture`.

A later source, newly supported bridge, or revised target claim creates a new testable claim. It does not erase the earlier inferential gap or retroactively convert an unsupported bridge into direct support.

#### C49-WP1-HANDOFF-01 — WP1 hands WP2 a source-bounded architecture without adjudicating the pressure packet

WP1 establishes:

- Source-Bounded Reconstruction and the source-precision constraint;
- six source-type profiles with claim- and operation-specific limits;
- mandatory separation of direct support, inferred structure, and missing structure;
- missing information, unknown event status, recorded non-occurrence, and `Λ` as non-equivalent;
- Inferential Distance as a qualitative source-to-claim burden;
- claim contraction where material inferential bridges lack independent support.

WP2 receives this architecture for Calibration, Calibration Loss, Granularity Calibration, comparability, countercase openness, and calibration openness. WP1 does not define a universal threshold, decide the Source Ceiling, establish a Calibration-Open Result, or pre-empt the Technical Formalization Boundary.

The synthetic source-and-calibration pressure packet remains unchanged:

```yaml
source_calibration_pressure_packet_after_wp1:
  P0: formal_precision_overreach_assertion_not_accepted
  DS: unadjudicated
  IS: unadjudicated
  MN: unadjudicated
  ID: unadjudicated
  SC: unadjudicated
  CA: unadjudicated
  CO: unadjudicated
  TF: unadjudicated
  source_result_selected: false
  calibration_result_selected: false
  route_selected: false
  stop_selected: false
  claim_reduction_selected: false
  non_capture_selected: false
  output_class_selected: false
```

No Case, Test, or Smoke YAML is created. The artifact-complete RETYPE lock remains `mandatory_stop`; the required lock packages remain `0/3`; Part IV remains open; and Part IV final lock remains unavailable.

```yaml
governance:
  authority_inheritance: prohibited
```

<a id="49-5-calibration"></a>

### 49.5 Calibration

Calibration is the controlled relation among a term, its analytical coordinates, the distinctions it is expected to preserve, and the cases that should or should not satisfy it. It does not mean that every structural claim must be converted into a universal numerical threshold. It means that a reader or another analyst can inspect what counted, what did not count, which differences mattered, and what evidence would pressure the result.

```text
Calibration
= comparability
+ relevant differentiation
+ threshold visibility
+ countercase possibility
```

Calibration is claim-relative and operation-relative. The threshold needed to distinguish one recurrence pattern may not be the threshold needed to establish sedimentation, asymmetry, attraction, or a bounded projected function. A term can therefore be locally well calibrated without carrying a universal meaning across all Frames, granularities, temporal scopes, and target functions.

#### C49-CALIBRATION-01 — Calibration preserves comparability, differentiation, visible thresholds, and countercase possibility

A calibrated STRATA claim must make at least four burdens inspectable.

**Comparability.** The relevant terms, source conditions, Frame, granularity, temporal scope, operation occurrence, and Claim Scope must remain sufficiently stable for a meaningful comparison.

**Relevant differentiation.** The method must state which differences would change the claim rather than treating all variation as noise or all resemblance as equivalence.

**Threshold visibility.** The analysis must disclose the boundary, range, condition, or decision rule by which a structure is treated as present, absent, stronger, weaker, repeated, sedimented, asymmetric, attractive, or functionally carrying.

**Countercase possibility.** At least one source-compatible case must remain capable of failing the declared condition. A concept that can absorb every possible observation has not been calibrated.

```text
no possible threshold-failing case
→ no inspectable calibration
```

Calibration does not require a single scalar. A threshold may be qualitative, relational, temporal, conjunctive, or explicitly interval-bounded. For example, recurrence may require more than one occurrence plus a stable relation among them; sedimentation may require persistence across a declared temporal interval and a retained historical load; a projected function may require a specific source feature to remain load-bearing in a named target context. The important requirement is not numerical form but visible decision structure.

Calibration also does not prove empirical truth. It makes the conditions under which a claim would pass, fail, narrow, or remain open inspectable. Source support, Continuity, Loss, Counterfactual Sensitivity, TypeIntegrity, and later integrated routing remain separately necessary.

#### C49-COMPARABILITY-01 — Meaningful comparison requires sufficiently stable terms and analytical coordinates

Comparability fails when two cases are described with the same label but tested under materially different coordinates without disclosure. It also fails when different labels hide equivalent source conditions, or when a term changes meaning between the positive case and its countercase.

A comparison record should therefore hold stable or explicitly vary:

- the reference object and Source Scope;
- the operation type and concrete occurrence;
- the Frame, source and target granularities, relative level where relevant, and temporal scope;
- the tested relation or feature;
- the declared threshold or range;
- the evidence type and inferential distance;
- the Claim Scope and Claim Ceiling.

```text
same label
+ different undeclared coordinates
≠ comparable cases
```

Operations impose distinct comparability burdens. Two `COMPOSE` results are not comparable merely because both are called composites; their selected elements, order, periodization, constitutive relations, and composite identity must be inspectable. Two `DECOMPOSE` results require the same reference object or an explicitly declared reference change, comparable resolution, and preserved return paths. Two `PROJECT_AS` results require visible origin types, source features, target contexts, and bounded functions. A shared target label does not compensate for different or absent source-function dependencies.

For PMS-typed structures, operator labels may stabilize vocabulary but not occurrence evidence. Comparison between two □, Δ, or Λ occurrences still requires concrete source and relation traces. No Δ–Ψ type, dependency, order, or layer is recalibrated by Chapter 49.

#### C49-COUNTERCASEOPENNESS-01 — Calibration must preserve genuine countercases and threshold-failing cases

Countercase openness requires more than mentioning alternatives. The declared conditions must permit a source-supported case to fail without redefining the term after the fact.

A valid countercase may show that:

- repetition occurred without sedimentation;
- attraction language was used where no recurrent directional relation was supported;
- an asymmetry was local rather than constitutive;
- a composite resembled a path but lacked order or dependency continuity;
- a source feature was present but did not carry the proposed target function;
- a missing record could not support a Non-Event;
- a familiar operator label lacked occurrence-level evidence.

```text
countercase appears
+ threshold is redefined only to preserve the original claim
→ calibration-loss pressure
```

The method does not require every countercase to defeat the entire transformation. It may defeat one dimension, one threshold, one Claim Scope, or one operation occurrence. What is prohibited is immunizing the term by turning every apparent failure into a new success condition. Chapter 50 owns the integrated Anti-Immunization procedure; WP2 preserves the necessary calibration-side openness without pre-empting that chapter.

<a id="49-6-calibration-loss"></a>

### 49.6 Calibration Loss

Calibration Loss occurs when the conditions that made a term, threshold, or comparison inspectable are weakened, shifted, hidden, or made analyst-dependent. It is not identical to the five-part transformation Loss inventory. The canonical fields `preserved`, `compressed`, `excluded`, `uncertain`, and `irrecoverable` remain unchanged. Calibration Loss is a method-level finding about loss of comparative and threshold control, mapped to existing record paths rather than added as a sixth Loss field.

```text
Calibration Loss
≠ sixth canonical Loss field
≠ automatic transformation failure
```

#### C49-CALIBRATIONLOSS-01 — Elasticity, post-hoc fit, unknown thresholds, and non-inspectability produce calibration-loss pressure

Calibration Loss can arise through several recurrent mechanisms.

**Elastic terminology.** A term expands or contracts so freely that materially different cases all count as instances.

**Post-hoc accommodation.** The threshold is altered only after a countercase appears, without a new source-supported rationale or a newly declared claim.

**Unknown threshold.** The analysis asserts that a distinction matters but cannot state the condition, range, relation, or evidence by which the distinction is made.

**Non-inspectable records.** Two analysts cannot reconstruct why one case passed and another failed because the coordinates, source bridge, threshold, or exception rule is absent.

**Coordinate drift.** Frame, granularity, temporal scope, or target context changes during comparison without being registered as a new testable claim.

**Positive-case privilege.** The term is defined from the successful example while negative or ambiguous cases are treated under a different standard.

A bounded calibration-loss record should identify:

1. the term or distinction whose calibration weakened;
2. the original coordinates and threshold;
3. the point at which elasticity, drift, or post-hoc accommodation entered;
4. the affected positive and countercases;
5. the source and inferential burden;
6. the resulting Claim Effect;
7. whether recalibration is possible under a new declared claim.

Calibration Loss does not automatically mean that the observed structural material disappears. A recurrent pattern may still be visible while the threshold for calling it sedimented remains unstable. A source feature may plausibly support a function while the degree or scope of that support remains uncalibrated. The correct response may therefore be a bounded open result, claim narrowing, or later Stop; WP2 selects none of these automatically.

```text
structural indication remains
+ threshold control lost
→ calibration problem
≠ empirical absence automatically
```

A newly calibrated successor analysis is a new testable claim. It does not erase the earlier elasticity, post-hoc fit, or non-inspectability.

<a id="49-7-granularity-calibration-and-calibration-openness"></a>

### 49.7 Granularity Calibration and Calibration Openness

Granularity Calibration asks what must be visible at a given resolution for a threshold-bearing distinction to be warranted. A coarse view may support the existence of a pattern while remaining unable to establish its components, internal asymmetry, temporal ordering, or functional carrier. A fine view may expose variation without warranting that every local difference matters to the higher-level claim.

```text
finer granularity
≠ automatic calibration superiority

coarser granularity
≠ automatic loss of validity
```

#### C49-GRANULARITYCALIBRATION-01 — Threshold questions must be operationalized across repetition, attraction, asymmetry, sedimentation, and projection

Different distinctions require different local calibration questions.

**Repetition.** What counts as another occurrence of the relevant structure rather than merely another use of the same label? Which relation, interval, or recurrence condition must remain stable?

**Attraction.** What evidence supports a recurrent directional pull, clustering, or return tendency rather than retrospective grouping? Which non-attracted cases remain visible?

**Asymmetry.** Which role, cost, access, dependency, or burden difference is claim-bearing? At what Frame and granularity does it persist, and which apparent asymmetries disappear under a different resolution?

**Sedimentation.** What temporal persistence, accumulated constraint, path dependence, or historical load distinguishes sedimentation from repetition or duration alone?

**Projection.** Which source feature carries the bounded target function, under which context, and what modification would weaken or remove that function?

These are calibration prompts, not new PMS primitives or operator definitions. They may reference concrete Δ–Ψ occurrences and relations where supported, but they do not rename, reorder, expand, or decompose the operator system.

A threshold declaration should state whether it is:

- directly source-supported;
- reconstructed from several source-supported relations;
- locally stipulated for comparative testing;
- inherited from a domain method;
- still calibration-sensitive.

Locally stipulated thresholds do not become empirical facts merely because they are explicit. Their role, source basis, and countercase implications must remain visible.

#### C49-NOUNIVERSALTHRESHOLD-01 — STRATA rejects universal ex-ante thresholds while requiring local threshold declaration

STRATA does not assume that repetition, attraction, asymmetry, sedimentation, or functional support can be governed by one universal number across domains and contexts. Such a requirement would often create false precision and violate the Source Ceiling.

```text
no universal ex-ante threshold
≠ no threshold discipline
```

The absence of a universal threshold increases rather than removes the local declaration burden. Each claim must identify the condition under which the distinction is being used, why that condition is appropriate to the source and coordinates, and which case would fall outside it.

A threshold may be revised where new evidence, a different Frame, a changed granularity, or a narrower Claim Scope warrants revision. The revision must be registered as a new testable claim, not silently substituted into the earlier analysis.

#### C49-CALIBRATIONOPENNESS-01 — Calibration openness is explicit threshold sensitivity, not vagueness or immunity

Calibration openness is warranted where the structural form can be identified but the available source basis does not yet support one fully settled threshold. The analysis must still specify:

- the structural form currently supported;
- the live threshold range or competing threshold candidates;
- the evidence supporting each candidate;
- the cases whose classification changes across the range;
- the missing discriminator;
- the bounded claim that survives all currently defensible calibrations.

```text
calibration open
→ threshold sensitivity disclosed

calibration open
≠ concept undefined
≠ every case admissible
≠ claim immune to counterexample
```

WP2 establishes the architecture for openness but does not select the canonical Calibration-Open Result owned by WP3. Likewise, an open threshold does not automatically select `admissible_but_provisional`, `claim_reduction_required`, `mandatory_stop`, or `non_capture`.

#### C49-WP2-HANDOFF-01 — WP2 hands WP3 the complete Calibration architecture without adjudicating the pressure packet

WP2 establishes:

- Calibration through comparability, relevant differentiation, threshold visibility, and countercase possibility;
- operation- and claim-relative comparison coordinates;
- Calibration Loss through elasticity, post-hoc fit, unknown thresholds, non-inspectability, and coordinate drift;
- Granularity Calibration for repetition, attraction, asymmetry, sedimentation, and projection;
- rejection of universal ex-ante thresholds together with mandatory local threshold declaration;
- explicit calibration openness as distinct from vagueness, arbitrary accommodation, or immunity.

WP3 receives this architecture for the Source Ceiling, Calibration-Open Result, Source-and-Calibration record view, Technical Formalization Boundary, ceiling distinctions, and handoff to Chapters 50–53. WP2 does not determine whether the Source Ceiling has been reached and does not select Stop, Claim Reduction, Non-Capture, or a canonical Output Class.

The synthetic pressure packet remains unchanged:

```yaml
source_calibration_pressure_packet_after_wp2:
  P0: formal_precision_overreach_assertion_not_accepted
  DS: unadjudicated
  IS: unadjudicated
  MN: unadjudicated
  ID: unadjudicated
  SC: unadjudicated
  CA: unadjudicated
  CO: unadjudicated
  TF: unadjudicated
  source_result_selected: false
  calibration_result_selected: false
  source_ceiling_selected: false
  route_selected: false
  stop_selected: false
  claim_reduction_selected: false
  non_capture_selected: false
  output_class_selected: false
```

No Case, Test, or Smoke YAML is created. The artifact-complete RETYPE lock remains `mandatory_stop`; required RETYPE lock packages remain `0/3`; Part IV remains open; and Part IV final lock remains unavailable.

```yaml
governance:
  authority_inheritance: prohibited
```

<a id="49-8-source-ceiling"></a>

### 49.8 Source Ceiling

The Source Ceiling marks the point at which a further analytical distinction, relation, chronology, component boundary, threshold, or functional attribution would no longer be a source-supported reconstruction under the declared coordinates. Beyond that point, additional precision may still be useful as a model assumption, bounded hypothesis, scenario, or research question, but it may not be presented as though it were carried by the current source basis.

```text
source-supported distinction
→ reconstruction

further distinction requiring an unmarked assumption
→ Source Ceiling reached
```

The ceiling is not a property of an archive or source object in the abstract. It is specific to a claim, relation, Frame, granularity, temporal scope, operation occurrence, and source use. The same source basis may support a coarse sequence while failing to support one internal transition; it may support an institutional expectation while failing to support practical enactment; or it may support the existence of a structural form while leaving its threshold calibration-sensitive.

#### C49-SOURCECEILING-01 — Source Ceiling is reached where further distinctions would be model assumptions rather than source-supported reconstruction

A bounded Source-Ceiling finding should identify:

1. the source-supported structure already established;
2. the next distinction or relation sought;
3. the source bridge required to warrant that distinction;
4. why the bridge is absent, insufficient, contradictory, or non-discriminating;
5. the live source-compatible alternatives;
6. the strongest claim remaining below the ceiling;
7. whether the additional structure is withheld, retained as a bounded hypothesis, or assigned to later evidence collection.

The ceiling may be reached locally. A `COMPOSE` operation may warrant a bounded sequence while reaching the ceiling before path dependence, attraction, or a unique branch relation can be established. A `DECOMPOSE` operation may identify several components while reaching the ceiling before one component boundary or internal dependency is warranted. A `PROJECT_AS` operation may support a contextual resemblance or practical use while reaching the ceiling before a constitutive source-function dependency can be established.

```text
Source Ceiling reached for relation r
≠ all source-supported claims about the object exhausted
```

The correct response is therefore not necessarily to abandon the entire transformation. The analysis may preserve the supported structure, separate stronger and weaker claims, retain alternatives, or stop only the unsupported continuation. Chapters 51–53 own the integrated decision among bounded continuation, Claim Reduction, Mandatory Stop, failure, and Non-Capture.

A source ceiling may shift when a genuinely new source, discriminator, comparison, or independently supported bridge becomes available. That later basis creates a new testable claim. It does not retroactively make the earlier source basis more precise or erase the earlier ceiling.

#### C49-CEILINGDISTINCTIONS-01 — Source Ceiling remains distinct from Relevance Floor, Traceability Ceiling, and Claim Ceiling

Four boundaries must not collapse.

**Praxeological Relevance Floor.** This asks whether a distinction makes a warranted praxis-relevant difference. A source may support a distinction that falls below the Relevance Floor because it changes no reconstruction or claim that matters to the declared praxis problem.

**Praxeological Traceability Ceiling.** This asks whether an abstraction or transformed object still carries reconstructible structural load from its sources. Source evidence may be rich while a highly abstract target label no longer changes when material source relations change.

**Source Ceiling.** This asks whether the source basis can support the next claimed distinction or relation at all.

**Claim Ceiling.** This bounds the reach, precision, generality, causal language, recurrence, necessity, or functional force of the claim that may be made from the surviving support.

```text
Source Ceiling
≠ Relevance Floor
≠ Traceability Ceiling
≠ Claim Ceiling
```

The boundaries may interact without becoming interchangeable. A source ceiling can force a lower Claim Ceiling. A traceability failure can occur below the source ceiling where abundant evidence is mapped into an insensitive macro-label. A distinction can be source-supported and traceable yet fall below the Relevance Floor. Conversely, a praxeologically important question may remain above the Source Ceiling because the evidence required to answer it is unavailable.

No boundary receives authority from another. Formal satisfaction of one check does not compensate for failure of the others.

<a id="49-9-calibration-open-result"></a>

### 49.9 Calibration-Open Result

A Calibration-Open Result is a controlled local finding for cases in which the structural form is sufficiently supported, but the threshold separating stronger classifications remains unresolved under the current source basis. It preserves what is warranted while refusing both false precision and unrestricted elasticity.

```text
structural form supported
+ threshold remains source-sensitive
+ bounded invariant claim survives
→ possible Calibration-Open Result
```

It is not an eleventh Output Class. It does not automatically map to `admissible_but_provisional`, and it is not a substitute for missing concepts, absent criteria, or immunity from counterexamples.

#### C49-CALIBRATIONOPENRESULT-01 — A bounded result may identify structural form while leaving the threshold explicitly calibration-sensitive

A Calibration-Open Result requires all of the following:

- a clearly identified structural form supported by the source basis;
- stable analytical coordinates for the comparison;
- the threshold, range, or decision condition that remains unresolved;
- at least two defensible threshold candidates or a declared calibration interval;
- the source support and inferential burden for each candidate;
- the cases whose classification changes across the candidates;
- the missing discriminator preventing closure;
- the bounded claim that survives every currently defensible calibration;
- the evidence or test that could later resolve the openness.

For example, repeated configurations may be source-supported while the available temporal record cannot discriminate between repetition and sedimentation. The Calibration-Open Result may preserve the recurrence claim and withhold the sedimentation claim. Likewise, a source feature may plausibly carry a bounded target function across several contexts while the threshold for calling the feature constitutive rather than modulating remains unresolved. The supported contextual function may remain, while stronger necessity language is withheld.

```text
classification changes across defensible thresholds
+ core structural claim remains stable
→ preserve core claim
→ disclose threshold sensitivity
```

Calibration openness fails where every threshold is treated as equally acceptable, where no case could fall outside the concept, or where the surviving claim is never stated. It also fails where the analyst changes coordinates, reference objects, or operation occurrences across threshold candidates without declaring a new comparison.

A Calibration-Open Result remains claim-relative. One dimension may be calibration-open while another is adequately calibrated or source-limited. It may coexist with direct support, inferred structure, uncertainty, compression, or a local Source Ceiling. These local findings do not mechanically determine the final canonical Output Class.

Later evidence may close the threshold, narrow the surviving claim, or show that no defensible threshold supports the original classification. Each successor result is a new testable claim. Earlier openness and its affected cases remain part of the record.

<a id="49-10-source-and-calibration-record-view"></a>

### 49.10 Source-and-Calibration Record View

Chapter 49 does not create a second record grammar. Source and Calibration findings form a controlled view over the existing Shared Transformation Record, its source declarations, analytical coordinates, operation occurrence, alternatives, Counterfactual Sensitivity, continuity checks, five-part Loss, Claim Scope, Claim Ceiling, audit findings, and later disposition fields.

```text
Source-and-Calibration view
≠ new Transformation Record
≠ new schema field family
≠ new operation
≠ new Rule
≠ new audit stage
≠ new Output Class
```

#### C49-SOURCECALIBRATIONRECORD-01 — Source basis, inference, ceiling, threshold, confidence, and Claim Effects map to existing Shared-Record paths

A complete view should make the following information reconstructible through existing paths.

**Source identity and scope.** The source object, Source Scope, Source Types, temporal coverage, access limits, and source-specific blind spots map to the existing source-basis and coordinate declarations.

**Support status.** Direct support, inferred structure, missing structure, uncertainty, and irrecoverability map to source notes, reconstruction rationale, alternatives, and the canonical Loss fields. An inference remains marked when passed into a later operation.

**Inferential bridges.** Each material bridge, its source support, rival bridge, and missing discriminator map to rationale, alternatives, Counterfactual Sensitivity, continuity, and audit evidence rather than a new distance score.

**Source Ceiling.** The supported stopping point, unsupported next distinction, surviving bounded claim, and possible evidence need map to Source Ceiling, Claim Scope, Claim Ceiling, Stop pressure, and audit findings. No automatic Stop is inserted.

**Calibration.** Stable coordinates, local threshold or range, threshold provenance, comparison cases, countercases, and affected classifications map to the declared Frame, granularity, relative level, temporal scope, alternatives, test conditions, and claim notes.

**Calibration openness.** Threshold candidates, changing cases, missing discriminator, and the invariant bounded claim map to uncertainty, alternatives, Claim Scope, and provisional findings without creating a new Output Class.

**Confidence.** Where confidence language is used, it must remain qualitative, claim-specific, source-bounded, and reconstructible from the stated support and limitations. It may not be converted into an unexplained probability, global score, or substitute for the Source Ceiling.

**Claim Effects.** Retention, narrowing, separation, removal of causal or universal language, and later routing candidates map to Claim Scope, Claim Ceiling, local findings, and the existing canonical disposition process.

A compact view may therefore be rendered as follows without becoming a new schema:

```yaml
source_and_calibration_view:
  source_basis: existing_source_path
  direct_support: existing_evidence_path
  inferred_structure: existing_rationale_path
  missing_or_uncertain_structure: existing_loss_and_alternative_paths
  inferential_bridges: existing_trace_and_audit_paths
  source_ceiling: existing_source_and_claim_ceiling_paths
  calibration_coordinates: existing_frame_granularity_level_time_paths
  threshold_and_countercases: existing_test_and_alternative_paths
  calibration_open_claim: existing_claim_scope_and_uncertainty_paths
  final_disposition: unselected
```

This is a reading view only. A machine may verify that the referenced paths exist and that controlled values are used. It cannot decide whether the source actually supports the declared relation, whether the threshold is substantively appropriate, or whether the surviving claim is true.

<a id="49-11-technical-formalization-boundary-and-handoff"></a>

### 49.11 Technical Formalization Boundary and Handoff

Formalization is valuable because it makes commitments explicit. Machine-readable records, schemas, type checks, controlled vocabularies, hashes, link audits, and executable decision trees can reveal absent fields, invalid values, inconsistent references, impossible operation signatures, inventory drift, broken dependencies, and contradictions among declared statuses.

Their competence ends at technical and structural inspectability.

```text
machine readability
schema validity
type checking
technical execution
→ possible consistency evidence

≠ empirical truth
≠ causal identification
≠ semantic adequacy
≠ ontological validity
≠ normative validity
≠ application authority
```

#### C49-FORMALIZATIONBOUNDARY-01 — Formal validity remains distinct from empirical, causal, semantic, ontological, normative, and authority validity

A schema-valid record can still contain a false observation, an unsupported bridge, an elastic threshold, a mistaken reference object, a semantically poor projection, or a normatively impermissible use. A technically invalid record may also contain an empirically relevant observation that has been represented incorrectly. Technical and substantive judgments therefore constrain one another without becoming equivalent.

No successful validation run may be described as proof that:

- the reconstructed event occurred;
- the claimed relation is causal;
- a component boundary is empirically real;
- a target function is semantically adequate;
- a PMS operator occurrence has been established;
- a person, institution, or practice should be classified or acted upon;
- the transformation inherits governance, diagnostic, legal, political, or application authority.

```yaml
governance:
  authority_inheritance: prohibited
```

#### C49-TECHNICALCONSISTENCY-01 — Formalization may expose inconsistency without pretending to resolve substantive evidence or thresholds

Formal tools may validly detect that:

- `direct_support` and `missing_structure` make contradictory claims about the same declared relation;
- a Calibration-Open Result lacks threshold candidates or a surviving bounded claim;
- a `PROJECT_AS` record omits the origin type or source-function path;
- a `DECOMPOSE` record has no return path to the reference object;
- a purported `Λ` lacks an expectation frame or bounded realization condition;
- a record uses an eleventh Output Class, sixth Loss field, fourth operation, or thirteenth audit stage;
- a fingerprint, link, schema reference, or controlled vocabulary value is inconsistent.

The tool may reject the inconsistent representation or require the missing declaration. It may not fill the missing evidence, select the correct threshold, infer the historical event, choose the true alternative, or assign the final Output Class on substantive grounds.

```text
formal inconsistency detected
→ representation requires correction

formal consistency achieved
≠ substantive question resolved
```

#### C49-HANDOFF50-53-01 — Chapters 50–53 retain Anti-Immunization, integrated disposition, Non-Capture, and final-audit ownership

Chapter 49 hands Chapter 50 explicit thresholds, countercases, Calibration Loss, source-limit statements, and successor-claim records for Anti-Immunization testing. It hands Chapter 51 local Source-Ceiling, Claim-Ceiling, Calibration-Open, and inferential-distance findings for integrated Stop, Claim Reduction, and re-entry. It hands Chapter 52 source-limited and calibration-limited structures without deciding Non-Capture. It hands Chapter 53 the complete Source-and-Calibration view for the integrated twelve-stage audit.

No handoff changes prior dispositions. A new source, threshold, Frame, granularity, or successor reconstruction creates a new testable claim.

#### C49-HANDOFF-01 — WP3 hands WP4 a complete Source-and-Calibration method without adjudicating the pressure packet

Chapter 49 now contains the complete substantive method for:

- Source-Bounded Reconstruction and source-precision control;
- Source Types and direct/inferred/missing separation;
- missing source versus recorded non-occurrence and `Λ`;
- Inferential Distance and bounded Claim Effects;
- Calibration, Calibration Loss, Granularity Calibration, and countercase openness;
- Source Ceiling and its distinction from the Relevance Floor, Traceability Ceiling, and Claim Ceiling;
- Calibration-Open Result;
- Source-and-Calibration Shared-Record view;
- Technical Formalization Boundary and technical-consistency competence.

The synthetic pressure packet remains unchanged:

```yaml
source_calibration_pressure_packet_after_wp3:
  P0: formal_precision_overreach_assertion_not_accepted
  DS: unadjudicated
  IS: unadjudicated
  MN: unadjudicated
  ID: unadjudicated
  SC: unadjudicated
  CA: unadjudicated
  CO: unadjudicated
  TF: unadjudicated
```

No Source-Ceiling result, Calibration result, Calibration-Open result, route, Stop, Claim Reduction, Non-Capture, or canonical Output Class has been selected for the packet. WP4 receives the complete chapter for integrated Contract, redundancy, ownership, pressure-packet, Shared-Record, inventory, PMS, model, and authority audit and for preparation of Chapter 50.

No Case, Test, or Smoke YAML has been created. The artifact-complete RETYPE lock remains `mandatory_stop`; the required lock packages remain `0/3`; Part IV remains open; and Part IV final lock remains unavailable.
<a id="chapter-50-anti-immunization"></a>

## Chapter 50 — Anti-Immunization

Anti-Immunization is the method-wide discipline that keeps analytical mobility criticizable. STRATA permits changes of Frame, granularity, relative level, composition, operation occurrence, and target function because different questions may require different reconstructions. That permission must never become a device by which every objection is absorbed into a new analytical position. A transformation that begins after a failure, reduction, Stop, uncertainty, or Non-Capture creates a successor claim. It does not retroactively repair, dissolve, or erase the disposition from which it began.

The chapter uses one synthetic pressure packet throughout. It is method pressure rather than an instantiated case and does not create Case, Test, or Smoke YAML:

```yaml
anti_immunization_pressure_packet:
  original_claim: claim_Q0
  original_objection: objection_O
  original_result: unadjudicated
  proposed_successor_reconstruction: claim_Q1

  immunization_assertion_I0:
    statement: analytical_shift_repairs_or_erases_prior_failure
    status: pressure_statement_not_accepted

  original_claim_candidate_OC:
    result: unadjudicated
  objection_disposition_candidate_OB:
    result: unadjudicated
  granularity_escape_candidate_GS:
    result: unadjudicated
  level_or_frame_escape_candidate_LS:
    result: unadjudicated
  projection_rescue_candidate_PR:
    result: unadjudicated
  failure_preservation_candidate_FP:
    result: unadjudicated
  rival_sensitivity_candidate_RV:
    result: unadjudicated
  translation_success_candidate_TS:
    result: unadjudicated
```

No local finding in this chapter mechanically selects Stop, Claim Reduction, Non-Capture, or a canonical Output Class. Those dispositions remain owned by Chapters 51–53.

<a id="50-1-purpose"></a>

### 50.1 Purpose

Transformative flexibility is necessary because one analytical position rarely answers every legitimate question. A coarse trajectory claim may fail while a narrower path claim remains testable. A source typing may fail while a bounded contextual function can still be projected. A composite may be unsuitable for one claim while selected components remain analytically useful. Anti-Immunization therefore does not forbid revision, decomposition, recomposition, reframing, or projection. It requires each move to preserve the conditions under which it can itself be criticized.

#### C50-PURPOSE-01 — Anti-Immunization preserves criticizability under analytical mobility rather than prohibiting revision

The governing distinction is:

```text
revision
≠ immunization automatically

analytical mobility
+ preserved criticism
+ separate successor claim
→ possible warranted revision

analytical mobility
+ disappeared objection
+ inherited repair status
→ immunization pressure
```

A revision remains criticizable when it declares what changed, why the change matters, which new source and praxis burden it introduces, and whether the original objection has been answered. Immunization pressure arises when movement itself is treated as an answer: the analyst changes coordinates, operation, or target function and then presents the new result as though the original claim had passed after all.

The chapter therefore preserves two freedoms together:

1. the freedom to formulate a narrower, differently coordinated, or functionally bounded successor reconstruction;
2. the freedom of the original objection, countercase, rival, or failure record to remain valid against the original claim.

Neither freedom cancels the other. A method that forbids all revision becomes rigid. A method that allows revision without failure preservation becomes self-sealing.

#### C50-ORIGINALCLAIM-01 — The original claim, coordinates, sources, Loss, result, and objection remain separately identifiable

Before a successor reconstruction is evaluated, the record must preserve at least:

- the original claim identifier and exact claim wording;
- Frame, granularity, relative level, temporal scope, source and target coordinates;
- the operation occurrence or operation chain that produced it;
- the source basis, inferential bridges, and Source Ceiling;
- the five-part Loss declaration;
- the local audit and disposition then reached;
- the countercase or objection, including its relevant coordinates;
- the point at which the original claim failed, narrowed, stopped, remained uncertain, or reached Non-Capture pressure.

The original object may be summarized in a successor record, but it may not be replaced by the successor's coordinates. If the original claim concerned a trajectory, the later path claim does not become a corrected version of the same object merely because it is more defensible. If the original claim used a broad institutional Frame, the later local practice claim does not overwrite that Frame. If the original claim projected a strong function, the later analogy cannot be treated as the same successful projection.

A minimal continuity statement is:

```yaml
anti_immunization_view:
  original_claim_ref: existing_claim_path
  original_coordinates_ref: existing_coordinate_paths
  original_source_and_loss_ref: existing_source_and_loss_paths
  original_objection_ref: existing_alternative_or_audit_path
  original_result_ref: existing_disposition_path
  successor_claim_ref: unselected
```

This is a view over existing Shared Transformation Record paths, not a second record grammar.

<a id="50-2-the-granularity-escape"></a>

### 50.2 The Granularity Escape

A granularity shift can be methodologically warranted. Coarse representation may hide a transition, non-event, asymmetry, component relation, or source-function dependency that is indispensable to the claim. `DECOMPOSE` may therefore reveal concrete new structure. Yet the phrase “the analysis was too coarse” can also postpone criticism indefinitely. If every counterexample triggers a finer level without a declared stopping condition, the original claim becomes unreachable by evidence.

#### C50-GRANULARITYESCAPE-01 — Warranted finer reconstruction is distinguished from a shift used only to postpone or dissolve an objection

A finer reconstruction is not a Granularity Escape when all of the following are present:

- the original objection is stated without alteration;
- the finer granularity is declared before or independently of the desired result;
- concrete additional source-supported structure is introduced;
- the new distinction has fresh PraxisPurchase and remains below the Traceability Ceiling;
- the original objection is re-tested at the finer granularity;
- the successor claim is narrower or otherwise explicitly different;
- the original result remains visible;
- further decomposition has a declared Stop burden.

By contrast, Granularity Escape pressure arises when:

- no concrete additional structure is supplied;
- the objection is merely said to disappear at greater detail;
- each finer level changes the claim criteria after the countercase appears;
- the original coarse claim is still presented as vindicated;
- source support decreases while precision increases;
- the new level has no independent praxis difference;
- no granularity could in principle count against the claim.

A bounded positive form is:

```text
Q0: a unified trajectory is claimed
O: a documented branch contradicts unity

DECOMPOSE at finer granularity
→ branch relations become source-visible
→ Q1: one bounded path within the branch is tested

Q1 may pass or fail independently
Q0 remains burdened by O
```

A Granularity Escape form is:

```text
Q0 fails at g0
→ analyst moves to g1
→ O is not re-tested
→ Q0 is described as repaired
```

The amount of detail does not decide the issue. A small but claim-bearing distinction may warrant a finer reconstruction; a very elaborate decomposition may still be an escape if it never returns to the objection.

#### C50-NEWCLAIM-01 — Every shift of Frame, granularity, level, composition, or target function creates a new testable claim

The system-wide identity rule is:

```text
changed analytical coordinate
or changed operation occurrence
or changed composition
or changed target function
→ new reconstruction
→ new testable claim
```

The successor claim must receive its own:

- claim identifier and wording;
- source and target coordinates;
- operation signature and occurrence;
- source basis and bridge declarations;
- PraxisPurchase and TraceableLoad tests;
- TypeIntegrity and continuity checks;
- Counterfactual Sensitivity where relevant;
- five-part Loss;
- Claim Scope and Claim Ceiling;
- local audit and later disposition.

A successor may refer to the original record and inherit source limitations, Loss, unresolved alternatives, and earlier dispositions. It does not inherit successful adjudication, repaired status, or additional authority.

#### C50-DIRECTANSWER-01 — The original objection receives a direct disposition rather than implicit disappearance

A direct disposition states what the objection does to the original claim. At minimum, it must distinguish among:

- answered by new source-supported evidence against the objection;
- accepted and the original claim narrowed;
- accepted and the original claim failed;
- unresolved because sources do not discriminate;
- source-limited or untestable;
- outside the current claim scope but still valid against a broader formulation;
- preserved for Chapter-51 Stop, Chapter-52 Non-Capture, or later audit.

“The new reconstruction works” is not a direct disposition of the original objection. The new reconstruction may be valuable while the original objection remains fully valid.

#### C50-OPERATIONDECLARATION-01 — The successor operation occurrence, changed coordinates, added praxis difference, and independent test burden are declared

Every successor record must identify whether the move is:

- a new `COMPOSE` occurrence with changed selection, order, branch relation, composite identity, or temporal arrangement;
- a new `DECOMPOSE` occurrence with changed target granularity, component boundary, relation set, or Source-Function Return burden;
- a new `PROJECT_AS` occurrence with changed target context, target function, source-feature claim, or validity scope;
- a coordinate change applied without a new core operation but still producing a distinct reconstruction claim.

The declaration must say what additional praxis difference becomes visible and why the new burden is not merely verbal. An operator label alone supplies no such difference. The Δ–Ψ type, name, dependency order, and `operator_layer` remain unchanged. Concrete operator occurrences or relations may be tested only where source-supported.

<a id="50-3-the-higher-level-escape"></a>

### 50.3 The Higher-Level Escape

A broader, composite, or higher-level reconstruction may answer a different question from a local claim. It may reveal emergent coordination, cumulative temporal effects, cross-context function, or a pattern that no isolated occurrence carries. That possibility does not make a local counterexample disappear.

#### C50-HIGHERLEVELESCAPE-01 — Higher-level, composite, or broader claims do not answer local counterexamples automatically

A higher-level claim must distinguish three possibilities:

1. **Different claim:** the broader reconstruction concerns a distinct target object or relation and leaves the local objection untouched.
2. **Contextual qualification:** the local objection remains true, but its relevance to the broader claim is limited by an explicit aggregation or scope rule.
3. **Direct answer:** source-supported evidence shows why the local objection does not defeat the higher-level claim under declared conditions.

Only the third possibility answers the objection, and even then it answers it relative to the higher-level claim. It does not erase the local result.

Higher-Level Escape pressure arises when:

- a local contradiction is re-described as “only local” without a declared aggregation rule;
- a new composite is treated as though it retroactively changed its components;
- a wider temporal scope is used to absorb an inconvenient interval;
- a relative-level change is presented as ontological superiority;
- the higher claim has no independent source or praxis burden;
- the local countercase cannot survive in the record.

For `COMPOSE`:

```text
composite pattern established
≠ every component carries the pattern
≠ local counterexample erased
```

For temporal analysis:

```text
long-run regularity
≠ every interval regular
≠ local transition failure repaired
```

For relative levels:

```text
higher analytical level
≠ truer level automatically
≠ authority over lower-level findings
```

A bounded example is a failed trajectory claim followed by a narrower path claim. Suppose `Q0` claims that several sequences form one trajectory. A countercase shows a branch with incompatible directional logic. A later `COMPOSE` claim may still test whether the branches constitute a heterogeneous composite under a different target question. That composite claim is not a repaired trajectory claim. It is `Q1`, with its own selection, Loss, source basis, and test burden.

#### C50-WP1-HANDOFF-01 — WP1 hands WP2 the original-claim and escape architecture without adjudicating the pressure packet

WP1 establishes that analytical mobility is permissible only under preserved criticizability. It fixes:

- the separately identifiable original claim and objection;
- the new-claim rule for changed coordinates, operation occurrences, composition, or target function;
- direct objection disposition;
- operation and praxis-difference declaration;
- the distinction between warranted finer reconstruction and Granularity Escape;
- the distinction between a higher-level successor claim and automatic repair of a local failure.

The synthetic pressure packet remains unchanged:

```yaml
anti_immunization_pressure_packet_after_wp1:
  I0: analytical_shift_repairs_or_erases_prior_failure_assertion_not_accepted
  OC: unadjudicated
  OB: unadjudicated
  GS: unadjudicated
  LS: unadjudicated
  PR: unadjudicated
  FP: unadjudicated
  RV: unadjudicated
  TS: unadjudicated
```
