No route, Stop, Claim Reduction, Non-Capture, or canonical Output Class is selected. WP2 receives the original-claim, direct-answer, operation-declaration, and escape architecture for Projection Rescue, Moving versus Answering, and Failure Preservation.

<a id="50-4-the-projection-rescue"></a>

### 50.4 The Projection Rescue

`PROJECT_AS` is designed to preserve an origin-typed object while assigning a bounded contextual target function. This makes it possible to retain analytical value after a stronger typing claim fails. The same flexibility can become immunizing if the new function is presented as proof that the original type was correct after all.

#### C50-PROJECTIONRESCUE-01 — Failed source typing is distinguished from an independent bounded `PROJECT_AS` successor claim

The prohibited rescue form is:

```text
Q0: source object X is type T
O: TypeIntegrity or source evidence defeats T

successor statement:
X functions as T-like in context C
therefore Q0 was substantially correct
```

A valid successor form is:

```text
Q0 failed or was reduced
→ original result preserved

PROJECT_AS occurrence P1:
origin-typed X
→ bounded target function Y in context C

Q1 tests Y independently
Q1 does not repair Q0
```

The successor projection must preserve:

- the origin type actually supported after the original failure;
- the source object and Reference Continuity;
- the target context and bounded function;
- the concrete source feature claimed to carry that function;
- Counterfactual Sensitivity of the function to that feature;
- the validity scope and Claim Ceiling;
- the original typing objection and result.

A failed typing followed by an independent projection is therefore a legitimate sequence only when the grammar remains visible:

```text
origin type retained
+ target function bounded
+ original failure preserved
→ possible new PROJECT_AS claim

origin type overwritten by target function
or target function used as retroactive type evidence
→ Projection Rescue pressure
```

Analogy remains separate. If the source-function dependency cannot be reconstructed, an analogy may still be analytically useful. That does not establish a valid projection or repair the prior type claim.

<a id="50-5-moving-versus-answering-an-objection"></a>

### 50.5 Moving versus Answering an Objection

An objection is answered only when the reconstruction engages the proposition, source relation, threshold, countercase, or continuity failure on which the objection depends. It is moved when the analytical position changes while that dependence remains untouched.

#### C50-MOVINGANSWERING-01 — Correcting an objection is distinguished from moving it across Frame, granularity, level, composition, or target function

The following diagnostic table remains qualitative and claim-specific:

| Analytical move | Answering condition | Moving pressure |
|---|---|---|
| Frame change | explains why the original Frame was inappropriate and re-tests the objection | substitutes a new context without disposition of the original claim |
| Granularity change | supplies relevant new structure and re-tests the same objection | postpones the objection to another resolution |
| Relative-level change | states a distinct claim and an explicit cross-level relation | treats a higher level as automatically overriding the local result |
| New composition | declares selection, order, relations, composite identity, and local countercase status | absorbs the countercase into an undifferentiated whole |
| New target function | establishes bounded source-function dependency while preserving origin type | uses functional similarity to rescue source typing |
| New source | identifies how the new evidence bears on the original objection | replaces the source basis and silently redescribes the original record |

A correction may alter the original claim. That is allowed if the alteration is explicit and the earlier claim remains preserved. A moved objection is not necessarily malicious or deliberate; it is a structural failure of record continuity and test design.

A direct Answering Record should make reconstructible:

```yaml
objection_disposition_view:
  original_claim_ref:
  objection_ref:
  objection_target_relation:
  response_evidence:
  original_claim_effect:
  successor_claim_ref:
  unresolved_remainder:
```

No new schema fields are implied. These values map to existing claim, source, alternative, audit, and disposition paths.

<a id="50-6-failure-preservation"></a>

### 50.6 Failure Preservation

Failure Preservation is the record discipline that prevents later analytical success from rewriting earlier results. “Failure” here is broad enough to include `failed_transformation`, Claim Reduction, Mandatory or Optional Stop, `admissible_but_provisional`, `resolution_neutral`, underdetermination, source limitation, and Non-Capture. These results differ substantively, but each must remain visible when a successor reconstruction is introduced.

#### C50-FAILUREPRESERVATION-01 — Earlier failure, reduction, Stop, uncertainty, and Non-Capture remain visible after later transformation

A successor record must inherit references to:

- the earlier claim and operation occurrence;
- the earlier local findings and canonical disposition, where one exists;
- the objection or countercase;
- the five-part Loss and source limitations;
- the exact reason for the earlier reduction, Stop, uncertainty, or Non-Capture;
- any unresolved alternatives;
- the boundary between inherited burden and new evidence.

Preservation does not mean that the earlier result controls every later question. It means the result remains part of the evidential history and cannot be overwritten.

```text
later success
≠ earlier failure erased

later narrower claim
≠ earlier broader claim repaired

new source
≠ old source limitation never existed

re-entry
≠ uninterrupted continuation
```

#### C50-RECORDLINKAGE-01 — Original claim, objection, result, successor operation, successor claim, and disposition continuity map to existing Shared-Record paths

The Anti-Immunization view uses existing paths:

```yaml
anti_immunization_record_view:
  original_claim: existing_claim_path
  original_coordinates: existing_frame_granularity_level_time_paths
  original_operation: existing_operation_path
  original_source_and_loss: existing_source_and_loss_paths
  original_objection: existing_alternative_countercase_or_audit_path
  original_result: existing_local_and_output_paths
  successor_operation: existing_new_operation_occurrence_path
  successor_claim: existing_new_claim_path
  inherited_burdens: existing_dependency_loss_and_disposition_refs
  successor_result: unselected
```

A machine may verify that references exist, that claim identifiers differ, and that controlled values are used. It cannot decide whether the objection was substantively answered or whether the successor claim is empirically true.

#### C50-CLAIMSEPARATION-01 — A successful successor claim does not inherit repair status for the failed original claim

Claim Separation requires distinct identifiers and wording where the following change:

- reference object;
- Frame, granularity, relative level, or temporal scope;
- operation occurrence or chain;
- component selection or composite identity;
- origin type or target function;
- source basis or material inferential bridge;
- Claim Scope or Claim Ceiling.

A successor may be described as “motivated by,” “responsive to,” or “narrower than” the original. It may not be described as “repairing,” “confirming,” or “validating” the original unless the original objection itself has been directly answered and the original claim re-tested under its own coordinates.

#### C50-COUNTERCASEPRESERVATION-01 — The original countercase and its relevant coordinates survive every successor reconstruction

Countercase Preservation requires more than retaining a label. The record must keep:

- what the countercase contradicted;
- the source basis supporting it;
- the Frame, granularity, level, temporal window, and operation occurrence in which it applied;
- which aspect remains relevant to the successor claim;
- which aspect falls outside the successor scope;
- whether a new countercase is needed for the successor.

If a local countercase is outside a broader successor claim, it remains a valid countercase to the original local claim. If a narrower successor avoids it by scope, that is claim contraction, not refutation of the countercase.

#### C50-CHAINIMMUNIZATION-01 — Immunization across operation chains and intermediate objects includes erased inherited Loss or dispositions

Operation chains create a special risk. An intermediate object may carry compression, uncertainty, a failed component relation, or a prior Stop. A later operation can appear clean only because that burden has disappeared from the intermediate representation.

```text
T1 produces intermediate object I
+ I carries Loss or disposition D
+ T2 treats I as lossless or unburdened
→ chain-level immunization pressure
```

Each chain step must therefore preserve:

- predecessor record reference;
- inherited source limitations and five-part Loss;
- unresolved objections and countercases;
- prior local and canonical dispositions;
- the new operation's independent PraxisPurchase and TraceableLoad;
- a statement of which inherited burdens are answered, retained, or outside the new claim.

For example, `COMPOSE → PROJECT_AS` may not use the projected target function to erase selection loss in the composite. `DECOMPOSE → COMPOSE` may not treat reconstructed components as though their uncertain internal relations were directly observed. `PROJECT_AS → DECOMPOSE` may open the source object but cannot retrospectively make the earlier projection's source-function dependency certain.

#### C50-WP2-HANDOFF-01 — WP2 hands WP3 complete failure-preservation and rescue controls without selecting later dispositions

WP2 establishes:

- Projection Rescue versus independent bounded `PROJECT_AS`;
- Moving versus Answering an Objection;
- Failure Preservation across later transformations;
- Shared-Record linkage;
- Claim and Countercase Separation;
- chain-level inheritance of Loss and dispositions.

The pressure packet remains unchanged:

```yaml
anti_immunization_pressure_packet_after_wp2:
  I0: analytical_shift_repairs_or_erases_prior_failure_assertion_not_accepted
  OC: unadjudicated
  OB: unadjudicated
  GS: unadjudicated
  LS: unadjudicated
  PR: unadjudicated
  FP: unadjudicated
  RV: unadjudicated
  TS: unadjudicated
```

No Stop, Claim Reduction, Non-Capture, route, or canonical Output Class is selected. WP3 receives the complete escape, rescue, claim-separation, and failure-preservation architecture for rival sensitivity, no-transformation, translation boundaries, and the six-question test.

<a id="50-7-rival-sensitivity"></a>

### 50.7 Rival Sensitivity

A method becomes immunized when its own transformations are always treated as the privileged response to difficulty. Rival Sensitivity requires source-supported alternatives to remain live: another granularity, composition, path reconstruction, target function, domain method, external conceptual model, or no transformation at all may provide a more parsimonious, more traceable, or empirically better account.

#### C50-RIVALSENSITIVITY-01 — Source-supported rival granularities, compositions, projections, and external models remain live comparative options

Rival Sensitivity does not require STRATA to validate every rival theory. It requires the current claim to state:

- which material rival reconstructions are source-compatible;
- which coordinates differ;
- which observations or relations each rival preserves or loses;
- which claim each rival can and cannot carry;
- what evidence would discriminate among them;
- whether the STRATA reconstruction is more complex than necessary;
- whether a rival performs better on PraxisPurchase, TraceableLoad, TypeIntegrity, continuity, Calibration, or Loss.

A rival may be:

- a coarser reconstruction that preserves the relevant praxis difference without unnecessary decomposition;
- a finer reconstruction that exposes a load-bearing asymmetry;
- a different `COMPOSE` selection or ordering;
- a non-composite account preserving multiple independent paths;
- an analogy instead of `PROJECT_AS`;
- a domain-specific causal, legal, historical, or statistical model outside PMS;
- a description that declines transformation.

An admissible STRATA result does not exclude rival superiority. It means only that the STRATA claim passed its bounded burdens.

#### C50-NOTRANSFORMATIONOPTION-01 — The possibility that no STRATA transformation is required or PMS is not the best capture remains available

“No transformation” can be a positive methodological option when:

- the source object already answers the bounded question;
- additional structure adds no PraxisPurchase;
- decomposition would fall below the Relevance Floor;
- composition would destroy heterogeneity;
- projection would add only analogy;
- source or calibration limits make further transformation misleading;
- a domain-specific representation preserves more relevant load;
- the object's important features cannot be adequately captured within the current PMS-STRATA grammar.

```text
no STRATA transformation required
≠ analytical failure
≠ source object unimportant
≠ PMS disproved
```

The option protects both Optional Stop and Non-Capture without selecting either. It also prevents transformation count from becoming a prestige measure.

<a id="50-8-success-trap"></a>

### 50.8 Success Trap

Translation into a rich formal grammar can be remarkably easy after enough analytical movement. That ease is dangerous when translatability is mistaken for confirmation. A structure may be expressible through PMS operators, STRATA operations, paths, components, or target functions while remaining empirically unsupported, semantically distorted, or less useful than a rival account.

#### C50-SUCCESSTRAP-01 — Successful translation into PMS language does not establish superiority, completeness, or validation

The Success Trap appears when:

- every source distinction is mapped to a PMS operator because the grammar is flexible;
- successful mapping is treated as evidence that PMS discovered the structure;
- non-matching evidence is assigned to a different granularity or target function;
- schema validity is treated as empirical adequacy;
- the absence of a clean translation is blamed on the source rather than considered as possible Non-Capture;
- translation success is used to claim semantic identity or theoretical superiority.

The boundary is:

```text
PMS-expressible
≠ PMS-confirmed
≠ empirically true
≠ semantically identical
≠ complete capture
≠ superior to rivals
```

A successful translation may still be useful. It can provide a disciplined vocabulary, reveal missing declarations, expose Loss, and make comparisons inspectable. Those are methodological gains, not validation of PMS or the translated object.

A required bounded counterpressure is a source structure that can be rendered elegantly in PMS language while the mapping remains analogy-only or source-limited. The correct response is to preserve the translation as a bounded heuristic if useful, not promote it into an established operator occurrence or validated projection.

#### C50-TRANSLATIONBOUNDARY-01 — Translatability, analogy, structural fit, empirical adequacy, and semantic identity remain distinct

These relations form no automatic ladder:

```text
translatable
≠ analogous automatically
≠ structural fit automatically
≠ empirically adequate automatically
≠ semantically identical automatically
```

- **Translatability** means a representation can be expressed in the grammar.
- **Analogy** means selected relations resemble a PMS or STRATA form under declared limits.
- **Structural fit** means source-supported relations satisfy the relevant operation and typing burdens.
- **Empirical adequacy** concerns whether the reconstruction accounts for the evidence under appropriate domain standards.
- **Semantic identity** would require equivalence of meaning or concept and is rarely implied by structural mapping.

A claim may be translatable but not analogous, analogous but not structurally fitting, structurally fitting but empirically underdetermined, or empirically useful without semantic identity. The record must state which relation is claimed.

<a id="50-9-anti-immunization-test"></a>

### 50.9 Anti-Immunization Test

The canonical test contains exactly six questions. It is applied whenever a new Frame, granularity, relative level, composition, operation occurrence, source basis, or target function is introduced after an objection or adverse result.

#### C50-SIXQUESTIONTEST-01 — The six-question Anti-Immunization Test operationalizes criticizability of successor reconstructions

1. **Which original claim was burdened?**  
   State its identifier, wording, coordinates, source basis, operation occurrence, Loss, and result.

2. **Is that claim corrected, narrowed, withdrawn, stopped, unresolved, or merely moved?**  
   Give a direct disposition of the original objection.

3. **Which new operation or analytical change is introduced?**  
   Declare the operation occurrence, Frame, granularity, level, composition, source, or target-function difference.

4. **Does the move create a separately testable successor claim?**  
   Provide a distinct identifier, source burden, PraxisPurchase, TraceableLoad, TypeIntegrity, continuity, Loss, and Claim Ceiling.

5. **Does the original countercase remain visible?**  
   Preserve its coordinates, source basis, relevance to the original claim, and any remaining relevance to the successor.

6. **Can the successor reconstruction fail, stop, remain uncertain, or yield to a rival or no transformation?**  
   State its countercases, Source Ceiling, Calibration limits, Stop conditions, rival options, and possible Non-Capture pressure.

The test does not produce a score. A “yes” to one question does not compensate for a failure on another. A machine may check whether all six answers are present and linked. It cannot decide whether the answers are substantively adequate.

A compact view is:

```yaml
anti_immunization_test_view:
  q1_original_claim:
  q2_original_disposition:
  q3_new_operation_or_coordinate_change:
  q4_separate_successor_claim:
  q5_original_countercase_preserved:
  q6_successor_failure_and_rival_conditions:
```

Again, this is a view over the Shared Record rather than a schema expansion.

<a id="50-10-anti-immunization-rule-and-handoff"></a>

### 50.10 Anti-Immunization Rule and Handoff

#### C50-CANONICALRULE-01 — A new reconstruction does not erase the failure from which it began

The system-wide rule is:

> A change of granularity, level, composition, or target function constitutes a new testable reconstruction. It does not erase the failure of the claim from which the transformation began.

For operational completeness, the rule also covers changes of Frame, relative level, source basis, operation occurrence, temporal scope, and material inferential bridge. These additions do not create new operations. They identify coordinate changes that alter the testable claim.

The canonical logic is:

```text
original claim Q0
+ objection O
+ result D0
+ successor transformation T1
→ preserve Q0, O, D0
→ test successor claim Q1 independently

Q1 success
≠ D0 erased

Q1 failure
≠ Q0 newly failed for a different reason automatically
```

Anti-Immunization is non-compensatory. Formal elegance, additional detail, cross-level reach, translation success, or empirical plausibility in the successor cannot compensate for disappearance of the original claim history.

The rule also constrains authority:

```text
successful successor reconstruction
≠ authority inherited by original claim
≠ authority inherited by PMS
≠ authority over rivals
```

```yaml
governance:
  authority_inheritance: prohibited
```

Anti-Immunization may not rank persons, diagnose states, determine legitimacy, justify sanctions, or create automatic recommendations. It remains a structural method rule.

#### C50-HANDOFF51-53-01 — Chapters 51–53 receive preserved claims, objections, rivals, and unresolved dispositions without pre-emption

Chapter 50 hands Chapter 51:

- original and successor claims as separate records;
- direct objection dispositions;
- Granularity-, Higher-Level-, and Projection-Rescue pressure;
- preserved failures, Loss, uncertainty, Stop history, and countercases;
- successor failure conditions and rival options;
- the six-question test results.

Chapter 51 owns the integrated decision whether continuation is unnecessary, optional, prohibited, or possible after Claim Reduction, and whether later re-entry is warranted.

Chapter 50 hands Chapter 52:

- unresolved source, granularity, composition, projection, calibration, semantic, and rival pressures;
- the no-transformation option;
- records showing which transformations were attempted and what remained uncaptured.

Chapter 52 owns Non-Capture and its forms.

Chapter 50 hands Chapter 53:

- the complete Anti-Immunization view;
- chain-level failure and Loss continuity;
- rival and no-transformation declarations;
- Success-Trap and translation-boundary findings;
- unresolved route and disposition candidates.

Chapter 53 owns the integrated twelve-stage audit and final routing. Chapter 50 selects none of these outcomes for the synthetic packet.

#### C50-HANDOFF-01 — WP3 hands WP4 the complete Anti-Immunization method without adjudicating the pressure packet

Chapter 50 now contains the complete substantive method for:

- criticizability under analytical mobility;
- preservation of original claims, coordinates, sources, Loss, objections, and results;
- Granularity and Higher-Level Escape detection;
- independent successor-claim and operation declaration;
- Projection Rescue prevention;
- Moving versus Answering an Objection;
- Failure, Claim, Countercase, and chain-disposition preservation;
- Rival Sensitivity and the no-transformation option;
- the translation Success Trap and translation boundary;
- the six-question Anti-Immunization Test;
- the canonical system-wide rule and Chapters 51–53 handoff.

The synthetic pressure packet remains unchanged:

```yaml
anti_immunization_pressure_packet_after_wp3:
  I0: analytical_shift_repairs_or_erases_prior_failure_assertion_not_accepted
  OC: unadjudicated
  OB: unadjudicated
  GS: unadjudicated
  LS: unadjudicated
  PR: unadjudicated
  FP: unadjudicated
  RV: unadjudicated
  TS: unadjudicated
```

No Anti-Immunization result, route, Stop, Claim Reduction, Non-Capture, or canonical Output Class has been selected. No Case, Test, or Smoke YAML has been created. The artifact-complete RETYPE lock remains `mandatory_stop`; the required lock packages remain `0/3`; Part IV remains open; and Part IV final lock remains unavailable. WP4 receives the complete chapter for integrated Contract, redundancy, ownership, pressure-packet, Shared-Record, inventory, PMS, model, and authority audit and for preparation of Chapter 51.
## Chapter 51 — Stop Conditions

Chapter 51 integrates Stop, Claim Reduction, preserved results, and re-entry across the complete STRATA method. It does not add a fourth operation, an additional Rule, a thirteenth audit stage, or a new Output Class. It determines when continued transformation is unnecessary, when it would violate a load-bearing boundary, when a narrower claim remains warranted, and what must remain visible if later work resumes.

The governing distinction is:

```text
continuation unnecessary
≠ continuation inadmissible
≠ current claim failed
≠ adequate capture impossible
```

<a id="51-1-stop-as-a-positive-methodological-result"></a>

### 51.1 Stop as a Positive Methodological Result

#### C51-POSITIVESTOP-01 — Stop records a warranted methodological endpoint rather than analytical abandonment

Stop is a positive result because it makes the boundary of warranted continuation inspectable. It may show that the relevant distinction has already been reached, that the present claim is sufficiently answered, that the source basis cannot carry further precision, that additional transformation would lose the reference object or traceable load, or that continued analysis would merely relocate an unresolved objection.

```text
Stop
≠ embarrassment
≠ missing answer automatically
≠ failure automatically
≠ Non-Capture automatically
```

A Stop assessment therefore begins from the current claim rather than from an abstract preference for either more or less analysis. The method asks what further operation would add, which boundary it would pressure, and whether the resulting successor claim would remain source-supported, relevant, traceable, type-integral, calibrated, and independently criticizable.

A valid Stop may preserve a positive result. For example, a PATH reconstruction may already establish an ordered sequence even though the sources do not warrant trajectory direction. Stopping at sequence does not deny the sequence. It prevents an unsupported stronger transformation.

Stop also has a non-authoritative meaning. It constrains a transformation claim; it does not rank analysts, theories, institutions, persons, or domains. A stopped operation may be methodologically disciplined even where the subject remains important or where another method could continue.

#### C51-CURRENTCLAIM-01 — Stop assessment retains the current claim, operation, coordinates, sources, Loss, and unresolved remainder

No Stop assessment is valid without an explicit current object of assessment. The record must retain at least:

```yaml
current_stop_object:
  claim_id:
  claim_text:
  operation_or_chain:
  reference_object:
  frame:
  source_granularity:
  target_granularity:
  relative_level:
  temporal_scope:
  source_basis:
  inferential_bridges:
  loss:
    preserved:
    compressed:
    excluded:
    uncertain:
    irrecoverable:
  current_local_results:
  unresolved_remainder:
```

These are existing Shared Transformation Record paths or controlled views over them. They are not a second Stop schema.

The current claim must remain distinguishable from a possible retained claim and from any future re-entry claim:

```text
claim_before Stop
≠ claim retained after reduction
≠ later re-entry claim
```

Without this separation, Stop could become a rhetorical disappearance of the strongest failed or unsupported formulation. Chapter 50 Anti-Immunization therefore remains active: a later narrower or differently framed claim does not erase the earlier claim, result, Loss, or objection.

<a id="51-2-general-stop-conditions"></a>

### 51.2 General Stop Conditions

#### C51-GENERALSTOP-01 — General Stop integrates distinct trigger families without collapsing them into one automatic route

A Stop assessment may be triggered by any of the following families:

1. **Praxeological Relevance Floor:** further distinction yields no additional praxis purchase for the declared claim.
2. **Praxeological Traceability Ceiling:** further abstraction or transformation would lose reconstructible source load.
3. **Source Ceiling:** the next distinction would require an unmarked model assumption rather than source-supported reconstruction.
4. **Calibration limit:** the classification threshold can no longer be inspected or held open to a genuine countercase.
5. **TypeIntegrity:** origin type, component type, composite type, or operator type could no longer be preserved.
6. **Reference or Functional Continuity:** the same referent or source-function relation would no longer remain identifiable.
7. **Counterfactual Sensitivity limit:** the relevant modification cannot be constructed or remains underdetermined in a claim-defeating way.
8. **Loss and reconstruction selection:** continued work would conceal a load-bearing exclusion, inherited irrecoverability, or hidden Loss.
9. **Anti-Immunization:** continuation would only move the objection through granularity, level, composition, or projection.
10. **Claim Ceiling:** a narrower claim remains warranted but the current stronger claim does not.
11. **Authority Ceiling:** continuation would convert structural reconstruction into person judgment, legitimacy, diagnosis, sanction, or application authority.

These triggers are non-equivalent. A Source Ceiling does not prove irrelevance. A Relevance-Floor result does not prove source exhaustion. Calibration openness does not automatically require Stop. Local uncertainty does not automatically select `mandatory_stop`.

```text
trigger detected
→ Stop assessment required

trigger detected
≠ final route selected automatically
```

The assessment must identify whether the trigger affects the entire current claim, one relation, one operation occurrence, one coordinate, or only the proposed next transformation.

#### C51-UNNECESSARYINADMISSIBLE-01 — Continuation that is unnecessary remains distinct from continuation that would be inadmissible

Two fundamentally different endpoints must remain visible.

**Unnecessary continuation** means that the current claim is already sufficiently answered or that further work would add no relevant difference. Continued work may remain possible, but it is not required for the present claim.

**Inadmissible continuation** means that proceeding would violate a load-bearing boundary: for example, erase the source object, exceed the source basis, overwrite the origin type, hide a countercase, collapse calibration, or confer prohibited authority.

```text
sufficient current answer
+ no additional praxis gain
→ possible Optional Stop

continued operation would violate TypeIntegrity or Source Ceiling
→ Mandatory-Stop pressure
```

An Optional Stop does not imply that no future research is useful. It means only that more transformation is unnecessary for the declared claim under current coordinates. A Mandatory Stop does not imply that every other claim or method is impossible. It prohibits the inadmissible continuation of this operation or chain.

#### C51-NONCOMPENSATION-01 — Detail, formal elegance, operation count, or technical validity cannot compensate for a load-bearing Stop trigger

Stop is non-compensatory. A transformation cannot remain admissible merely because it is detailed, mathematically elegant, technically executable, internally consistent, or supported by many operation steps.

```text
more detail
+ more formal structure
+ more operation occurrences
≠ compensation for lost traceable load
```

Likewise:

```text
schema-valid record
≠ Source Ceiling overcome

fine-grained decomposition
≠ Relevance Floor overcome

successful PMS translation
≠ objection answered
```

Where one load-bearing boundary makes continuation inadmissible, success on other dimensions cannot cancel it. A narrower claim may still be available, but that is Claim Reduction, not compensation.

#### C51-TRIGGERDECLARATION-01 — Every Stop assessment declares the trigger, affected claim, violated boundary, and possible retained claim

A Stop trigger must be stated in a reconstructible form:

```yaml
stop_trigger_view:
  trigger_family:
  trigger_statement:
  affected_claim:
  affected_operation_or_chain:
  affected_coordinate_or_relation:
  boundary_crossed_or_reached:
  source_support:
  countercase_or_alternative:
  continuation_effect:
  possible_retained_claim:
  unresolved_remainder:
```

A vague statement such as “further analysis is unhelpful” is insufficient. The record must specify why further work is unnecessary or inadmissible and what remains warranted.

The possible retained claim may be absent. Where no narrower warranted claim remains, Chapter 51 records that result without automatically deciding whether Chapter 52 Non-Capture or another final Output Class applies.

<a id="51-3-path-stop-conditions"></a>

### 51.3 PATH Stop Conditions

#### C51-PATHSTOP-01 — PATH stops where temporal differentiation no longer yields warranted path structure

PATH-specific Stop pressure arises when further temporal transformation would no longer preserve a source-supported relation among sequence, transition, path, trajectory, alternatives, and Non-Events.

PATH must stop or reduce the claim where:

- additional temporal differentiation produces no new praxis-relevant distinction;
- period boundaries become arbitrary or are selected only to preserve the preferred path;
- a relevant transition cannot be reconstructed from the source basis;
- competing paths or branch points cannot be discriminated;
- a macro-label replaces the occurrence-level sequence;
- historical order is inferred from operator-type order;
- a Non-Event is asserted from missing documentation rather than warranted expectation and non-realization;
- trajectory direction is claimed where only path or sequence is supported;
- path dependence is asserted where only order dependence remains warranted.

A bounded Mandatory-Stop example is:

```text
claim: a trajectory changed direction at t2
source basis: supports t1 and t3 states but no transition relation at t2
proposed continuation: insert an inferred transition to preserve direction
result: continuation would exceed Source Ceiling and create false temporal continuity
→ Mandatory-Stop pressure for the trajectory claim
```

The retained claim may still be:

```text
states at t1 and t3 are documented
or
an ordered sequence is supported
```

An Optional-Stop example is different:

```text
claim: sequence A → B → C is sufficient for the present comparison
further decomposition of intervals adds no praxis-relevant difference
sources could support more detail
→ Optional Stop may be warranted
```

PATH Stop is occurrence-based. It does not alter Δ–Ψ types or infer historical sequence from PMS dependency order.

#### C51-WP1-HANDOFF-01 — WP1 hands WP2 the general and PATH Stop architecture without adjudicating the pressure packet

WP1 has established Stop as a positive result, the current-claim declaration, general trigger families, unnecessary versus inadmissible continuation, non-compensation, trigger declaration, and PATH-specific Stop conditions.

The synthetic packet remains unchanged:

```yaml
stop_pressure_packet_after_wp1:
  J0: further_transformation_is_always_preferable_to_stop_assertion_not_accepted
  GC: unadjudicated
  PS: unadjudicated
  SS: unadjudicated
  RS: unadjudicated
  MO: unadjudicated
  CR: unadjudicated
  RE: unadjudicated
  PR: unadjudicated
```

No Stop mode, Claim Reduction, retained claim, re-entry condition, Non-Capture result, route, or canonical Output Class is selected.

<a id="51-4-sub-stop-conditions"></a>

### 51.4 SUB Stop Conditions

#### C51-SUBSTOP-01 — SUB stops where finer reconstruction loses praxeological function, source support, or the same reference object

SUB does not possess a truth privilege merely because it opens a finer structure. It must stop or reduce the claim when:

- a finer distinction falls below the Relevance Floor;
- proposed components have no declared function for the present claim;
- component boundaries are source-unsupported;
- decomposition loses the return path to the same reference object;
- components are treated as independent objects when their identity depends on the composite;
- finer resolution conceals rather than clarifies a load-bearing relation;
- the source basis cannot support a further component or internal threshold;
- operator types rather than Occurrences or composites are being decomposed;
- repeated decomposition becomes a Granularity Escape from an unresolved objection.

A bounded Mandatory-Stop example is:

```text
source object X is identifiable only through relation R among components a, b, c
proposed DECOMPOSE removes R and treats a, b, c as independent source objects
reference-object return becomes unavailable
→ continuation would violate Reference Continuity
→ Mandatory-Stop pressure
```

A bounded Optional-Stop example is:

```text
components a and b already explain the claim-bearing functional difference
source permits finer subcomponents, but they add no additional praxis purchase
→ Optional Stop may be warranted
```

The method does not prohibit later decomposition under a different claim. That later decomposition requires a new record, fresh relevance and traceability tests, and preserved linkage to the earlier Stop.

<a id="51-5-retype-stop-conditions"></a>

### 51.5 RETYPE Stop Conditions

#### C51-RETYPESTOP-01 — RETYPE stops where the contextual target function is unnecessary, source-detached, type-overwriting, analogy-confused, or arbitrarily bounded

`PROJECT_AS` remains a bounded contextual function assignment. RETYPE Stop pressure arises when:

- no additional target function is needed for the declared claim;
- the proposed target function provides no praxis gain beyond the source description;
- Constitutive Source Trace is missing;
- Counterfactual Sensitivity cannot be constructed where the claim requires dependence;
- origin type is overwritten, hidden, or treated as converted into target type;
- analogy, label substitution, and functional projection cannot be separated;
- the target context is selected arbitrarily or expanded after counterpressure;
- the projected function is portable across unrelated source objects without reconstructible source load;
- the projection is used to rescue a failed origin-type claim;
- an artifact-complete RETYPE lock is claimed while required lock packages remain absent.

The existing RETYPE artifact-complete lock therefore remains a real Mandatory Stop:

```text
required RETYPE lock packages: 0/3
artifact-complete RETYPE lock requested
→ continuation to artifact-complete lock is inadmissible
→ mandatory_stop remains required
```

A projection-to-analogy reduction may remain available:

```text
strong claim: source feature F carries target function Y
source support: structural resemblance is visible, dependency is not warranted
reduced claim: F is analogous to Y in context C
```

This is not a successful `PROJECT_AS`; it is a Claim Reduction from projection to analogy, with origin type and failed stronger claim preserved.

<a id="51-6-mandatory-versus-optional-stop"></a>

### 51.6 Mandatory versus Optional Stop

#### C51-MANDATORYOPTIONAL-01 — Mandatory and Optional Stop are distinguished by the permissibility of continuation

The decisive question is not whether continuation is attractive but whether it remains admissible.

```text
Mandatory Stop
→ continuation would violate a load-bearing boundary
→ canonical Output Class may be mandatory_stop after integrated routing

Optional Stop
→ continuation remains admissible but unnecessary for the current claim
→ no additional canonical Output Class
```

Typical Mandatory-Stop conditions include:

- TypeIntegrity cannot be preserved;
- Reference Continuity would be lost;
- Source Ceiling would be exceeded by an unmarked bridge;
- hidden Loss would be required to maintain the claim;
- calibration would become non-inspectable;
- the original objection would be immunized rather than answered;
- continuation would create prohibited person, normative, governance, diagnostic, legal, political, or application authority.

Typical Optional-Stop conditions include:

- the current bounded claim is sufficient;
- further differentiation adds no praxis purchase;
- additional composition or decomposition repeats already preserved structure;
- the next operation is possible but unnecessary for the declared target;
- a more detailed route would be expensive without changing the claim.

Optional Stop may coexist with a supported or provisional current claim. Mandatory Stop may coexist with a retained narrower claim. Neither mode alone decides empirical truth or complete capture.

<a id="51-7-claim-reduction"></a>

### 51.7 Claim Reduction

#### C51-CLAIMREDUCTION-01 — Claim Reduction preserves a narrower warranted claim when the stronger claim cannot pass

Claim Reduction changes the content, scope, strength, or type of the claim while preserving the earlier stronger formulation and the reason it could not pass.

Canonical examples include:

```text
trajectory → path
path → sequence
strong path dependence → weak order dependence
functional projection → structural analogy
strong target function → limited contextual function
universal recurrence → bounded recurrence within scope C
```

A valid Claim Reduction records:

```yaml
claim_reduction_view:
  original_claim:
  failed_or_unsupported_component:
  reduction_reason:
  retained_structure:
  reduced_claim:
  changed_scope_or_strength:
  new_claim_ceiling:
  inherited_loss_and_uncertainty:
  countercase_preserved:
```

Claim Reduction is not rhetorical retreat. The reduced claim must remain independently source-supported and testable. Where the reduced claim requires new sources, coordinates, or operation occurrences, it becomes a new testable claim rather than a simple reduction within the existing record.

#### C51-SUPPORTDOWNGRADE-01 — Support downgrade changes evidential status rather than claim content

Support downgrade must remain separate:

```text
supported → provisional → underdetermined
```

The claim may retain the same content while its support status changes. That is not Claim Reduction unless the claim itself becomes narrower, weaker, or differently typed.

Example:

```text
claim content: sequence A → B → C occurred
new source conflict: order B/C cannot be uniquely resolved
support result: underdetermined
claim content not yet narrowed
→ support downgrade, not Claim Reduction
```

A later reduction to “A preceded both B and C” would be a distinct Claim Reduction.

#### C51-RESOLUTIONNEUTRAL-01 — `resolution_neutral` is a tested resolution-gain result, not Stop or Claim Reduction

`resolution_neutral` applies where a tested finer or alternate resolution does not improve the claim-bearing distinction.

```text
tested claim: finer resolution will produce relevant gain
local result: no relevant gain
canonical Output Class: resolution_neutral
```

The result does not necessarily reduce the substantive claim and does not require stopping all work. It may support Optional Stop for the present resolution test, but the concepts remain non-equivalent.

```text
resolution_neutral
≠ Claim Reduction
≠ support downgrade
≠ Mandatory Stop
```

#### C51-NOAUTOMATICFAILURE-01 — Stop, reduction, support downgrade, and resolution neutrality do not mechanically become `failed_transformation`

A stopped operation may have produced a sufficient bounded result. A reduced claim may remain admissible. A support downgrade may preserve an underdetermined but useful claim. A resolution-neutral test may have been correctly executed and yielded no gain.

```text
Mandatory Stop trigger
≠ failed_transformation automatically

Claim Reduction
≠ failed_transformation automatically

support downgrade
≠ failed_transformation automatically

resolution_neutral
≠ failed_transformation
```

`failed_transformation` requires its own substantive rationale: the attempted transformation did not produce an admissible result under the applicable operation and claim. Final class routing remains Chapter 53-owned.

#### C51-WP2-HANDOFF-01 — WP2 hands WP3 complete operation-specific Stop and Claim-Reduction controls without selecting final routes

WP2 has added SUB and RETYPE Stop conditions, Mandatory versus Optional Stop, Claim Reduction, support downgrade, resolution neutrality, and the non-automatic-failure boundary.

The pressure packet remains:

```yaml
stop_pressure_packet_after_wp2:
  J0: further_transformation_is_always_preferable_to_stop_assertion_not_accepted
  GC: unadjudicated
  PS: unadjudicated
  SS: unadjudicated
  RS: unadjudicated
  MO: unadjudicated
  CR: unadjudicated
  RE: unadjudicated
  PR: unadjudicated
```

No final route or Output Class has been selected.

<a id="51-8-stop-record"></a>

### 51.8 Stop Record

#### C51-STOPRECORD-01 — The Stop view maps trigger, mode, claim effects, preserved result, remainder, and re-entry conditions to existing Shared-Record paths

The conceptual Stop view is:

```yaml
# Conceptual view only; use existing /admissibility/stop_assessment
# and linked claim, loss, source, and result paths.
stop:
  trigger:
  operation:
  mandatory:
  claim_before:
  claim_after:
  preserved_result:
  unresolved:
  possible_future_reentry:
```

Operationally, the view must include:

- trigger family and source-supported trigger statement;
- affected operation occurrence or chain;
- whether Stop is reached;
- Mandatory or Optional mode where reached;
- claim before Stop;
- reduced or retained claim, if any;
- preserved local results and Output candidates;
- inherited and current Loss;
- unresolved relations, alternatives, and countercases;
- exact conditions under which later re-entry could be tested;
- links to earlier objections, failures, reductions, and Stops.

The Stop Record is not a terminal erasure. It is the mechanism by which the result remains available to later analysis.

The formal model may validate field presence, allowed mode values, required links, and consistency with a selected `mandatory_stop`. It may not substantively decide whether the trigger is real, which claim should be retained, or whether re-entry is warranted.

#### C51-PRESERVEDRESULT-01 — Stop preserves the result, Loss, countercase, and unresolved remainder

At Stop, the method must retain more than the word “stopped.” It must preserve:

```text
what was established
what failed or remained unsupported
what was reduced
what Loss was disclosed
what countercase remained live
what could not be resolved
what future condition could change the assessment
```

Preserved result does not mean every earlier representation is endorsed. It means the record keeps the status of each claim-bearing element visible.

For an operation chain:

```text
T1 result and Loss
+ T2 Stop
→ T1 result and Loss remain visible
→ T2 trigger and remainder are added
```

A later T3 cannot treat the intermediate object as lossless or objection-free.

<a id="51-9-re-entry-after-stop"></a>

### 51.9 Re-Entry after Stop

#### C51-REENTRY-01 — Re-entry is permitted only when a declared material condition changes

Re-entry may be warranted where at least one material condition changes, such as:

- new direct or temporally ordered sources;
- a newly accessible internal relation;
- a genuinely different bounded claim;
- a new relevant countercase or alternative;
- a newly justified granularity or periodization;
- a changed target context with independent Source Trace;
- a new calibration basis or discriminator;
- correction of a demonstrable earlier record or source error.

Mere persistence, preference, technical capability, or a desire to obtain a different result is insufficient.

```text
same sources
+ same claim
+ same coordinates
+ same unresolved trigger
→ no warranted re-entry merely by repetition
```

The re-entry condition must be stated at Stop, where possible, and verified at re-entry.

#### C51-NEWCLAIMREENTRY-01 — Re-entry creates a new testable claim or revised record and never silent continuation

Re-entry requires a new record identifier or a controlled revised-record relation that preserves the earlier record.

```text
earlier Stop record S0
+ materially changed condition ΔC
+ re-entry operation T1
→ new or revised record S1
→ new testable claim Q1

S1
≠ S0 silently reopened
≠ earlier Stop erased
```

The new record must state:

- the earlier Stop identifier;
- the changed material condition;
- what remains inherited;
- which claim is newly tested;
- which operation and coordinates apply;
- new source, Loss, calibration, and failure conditions;
- whether the original countercase still applies.

A successful re-entry does not retroactively make the earlier Stop wrong. It may show that the conditions under which Stop was warranted no longer obtain.

<a id="51-10-no-shame-in-stop"></a>

### 51.10 No Shame in Stop

#### C51-NOSHAME-01 — Stop may indicate sufficiency, irrelevance of continuation, source limitation, underdetermination, or inadmissibility of further work

Stop is not a deficit category. Its meaning must be specified.

Possible meanings include:

1. **Sufficient answer:** the present bounded claim has been adequately established.
2. **No further relevance:** more detail or abstraction adds no praxis purchase.
3. **Source-limited:** the source basis cannot warrant the next distinction.
4. **Underdetermined:** multiple responses or reconstructions remain plausible without a discriminator.
5. **Inadmissible continuation:** proceeding would violate TypeIntegrity, continuity, calibration, traceability, Anti-Immunization, or authority limits.
6. **Optional economy:** additional work could be performed but is unnecessary for the current claim.

The Stop Record must not convert these meanings into one undifferentiated “failure.” Nor may “No Shame” protect a weak claim from criticism. The current claim still requires its own retained, reduced, provisional, failed, or unresolved status.

#### C51-AUTHORITYBOUNDARY-01 — Stop generates no person, normative, governance, legal, political, diagnostic, or application authority

A Stop is a methodological disposition concerning a transformation claim. It cannot establish that:

- a person or group is incapable, irrational, pathological, or illegitimate;
- an institution should be sanctioned or governed in a particular way;
- a political or legal decision is legitimate;
- a diagnostic or clinical conclusion follows;
- an intervention is required;
- PMS or STRATA has authority over a domain or rival method.

```text
methodological Stop
≠ authority over object
≠ authority over persons
≠ authority over action
```

```yaml
governance:
  authority_inheritance: prohibited
```

Machine-detected trigger patterns are not substantive Stop decisions. Human and domain-grounded judgment remains necessary.

#### C51-HANDOFF52-53-01 — Chapters 52–53 receive Stop, retained claims, uncaptured remainder, and unresolved routes without pre-emption

Chapter 51 hands Chapter 52:

- attempted operations and chains;
- Stop triggers and modes;
- retained and reduced claims;
- source, granularity, composition, projection, calibration, semantic, and rival remainders;
- what remained unresolved or inadequately captured;
- re-entry conditions.

Chapter 52 alone determines whether the remainder supports `non_capture`, partial capture, or another bounded capture statement. Stop is not Non-Capture automatically.

Chapter 51 hands Chapter 53:

- general and operation-specific Stop findings;
- Mandatory/Optional distinctions;
- Claim Reduction and support-status distinctions;
- preserved results and Loss;
- Stop Record and re-entry links;
- unresolved final-route candidates.

Chapter 53 owns integrated twelve-stage final routing. Chapter 51 does not mechanically map every local Stop to a final Output Class.

#### C51-HANDOFF-01 — WP3 hands WP4 the complete Stop, Claim-Reduction, and re-entry method for integrated audit

Chapter 51 now contains the complete substantive method for:

- Stop as a positive result;
- current-claim and trigger declaration;
- general, PATH, SUB, and RETYPE Stop conditions;
- unnecessary versus inadmissible continuation;
- non-compensation;
- Mandatory versus Optional Stop;
- Claim Reduction, support downgrade, and `resolution_neutral` separation;
- non-automatic-failure control;
- Stop Record and result preservation;
- changed-condition re-entry as a new testable claim;
- No Shame and authority boundaries;
- Chapters 52–53 handoff.

The synthetic pressure packet remains unchanged:

```yaml
stop_pressure_packet_after_wp3:
  J0: further_transformation_is_always_preferable_to_stop_assertion_not_accepted
  GC: unadjudicated
  PS: unadjudicated
  SS: unadjudicated
  RS: unadjudicated
  MO: unadjudicated
  CR: unadjudicated
  RE: unadjudicated
  PR: unadjudicated
```

No pressure-object Stop, Claim Reduction, retained claim, re-entry, Non-Capture, route, or canonical Output Class has been selected. No Case, Test, or Smoke YAML has been created. The artifact-complete RETYPE lock remains `mandatory_stop`; required lock packages remain `0/3`; Part IV remains open; and Part IV final lock remains unavailable.
## Chapter 52 — Non-Capture

Chapter 52 establishes Non-Capture as a positive, bounded, and criticizable result of PMS-STRATA. It applies where an adequately attempted reconstruction cannot retain a sufficient claim about all or a delimited part of the relevant object without distortion, unsupported extension, false closure, or destruction of a constitutive feature. It does not add a fourth operation, a new PMS primitive, a seventeenth Rule, a thirteenth audit stage, or an eleventh Output Class.

The governing distinction is:

```text
inadequate capture under present tested conditions
≠ analytical mystery
≠ claim immunity
≠ mere absence of evidence
≠ automatic failure
```

<a id="52-1-definition"></a>

### 52.1 Definition

#### C52-DEFINITION-01 — Non-Capture records bounded inadequate capture under present tested conditions

Non-Capture is warranted only for a delimited capture object: a claim, relation, function, temporal load, composite, or semantic remainder that has been subjected to an explicit bounded reconstruction attempt. It states that, under the declared sources, coordinates, operations, and tested alternatives, no adequate retained claim remains for that same delimited object without one or more of the following distortions:

- unsupported extension beyond the Source or Claim Ceiling;
- loss of reference, functional, temporal, or TypeIntegrity continuity;
- concealment of a constitutive feature, counterpath, Non-Event, or asymmetry;
- collapse of calibration or countercase openness;
- replacement of source-supported structure by analogy, label persistence, or model convenience;
- false closure where materially rival reconstructions remain unresolved.

```text
Non-Capture for Q under conditions C
≠ no possible future claim about object X
≠ object X unreal
≠ PMS globally invalid
```

The result is therefore bounded by the attempted claim, operation or chain, frame, granularity, relative level, temporal scope, source basis, and current evidence. It can coexist with captured structure, partial capture, an adequate narrower claim, or an external rival. Where an adequate retained claim remains for the same delimited tested claim, the canonical `non_capture` route is blocked for that claim, though a broader claim may still receive a separate Non-Capture assessment.

Non-Capture remains a method result rather than an authority claim. It does not establish that the object is unknowable, that another grammar is superior, or that no future reconstruction could succeed.

#### C52-ATTEMPTDISCLOSURE-01 — Non-Capture requires disclosure of what was attempted and why capture remained inadequate

A Non-Capture assessment begins from an inspectable attempt record. It must retain at least:

```yaml
non_capture_attempt_view:
  capture_object:
  claim_tested:
  operation_or_chain:
  reference_object:
  frame:
  source_granularity:
  target_granularity:
  relative_level:
  temporal_scope:
  source_basis:
  attempted_alternatives:
  counterfactual_pressure:
  continuity_results:
  calibration_results:
  loss:
    preserved:
    compressed:
    excluded:
    uncertain:
    irrecoverable:
  captured_remainder:
  uncaptured_remainder:
  limiting_condition:
```

These are views over the existing Shared Transformation Record. They do not create a second record grammar.

A statement such as “PMS does not capture this” is insufficient unless it identifies what concrete relation or claim was tested, which operations and coordinates were attempted, which alternatives were examined, and why the remaining limit is not merely the result of an omitted source, an arbitrary granularity, a premature Stop, or an untested projection.

```text
no explicit bounded attempt
→ Non-Capture not yet warranted
```

A method may remain unable to capture something after one attempt, but Chapter 52 requires enough testing to justify the scope of the result. The burden grows with the breadth of the Non-Capture claim.

<a id="52-2-non-capture-is-not-failure-avoidance"></a>

### 52.2 Non-Capture Is Not Failure Avoidance

#### C52-NOFAILUREAVOIDANCE-01 — Non-Capture cannot shield a weak, failed, untested, or immunized claim

Non-Capture is not an escape category for claims that lack source support, fail TypeIntegrity, exceed Claim Ceiling, or move objections across frame, granularity, level, composition, or target function. The original claim and its result must remain visible.

```text
claim Q unsupported
+ analyst declares object uncapturable
≠ Non-Capture warranted
```

The correct result may instead be unsupported inference, Claim Reduction, `failed_transformation`, Mandatory Stop, or an unresolved source limitation. Chapter 52 does not decide those routes mechanically, but it prohibits replacing their burdens with a vague assertion of incompleteness.

An anti-shield counterexample is decisive:

```text
attempt: one coarse COMPOSE without testing an available finer sequence
result: trajectory claim fails
response: “the object exceeds PMS and is non-capturable”
assessment: Non-Capture rejected
reason: relevant operation and granularity alternatives were not tested
```

Likewise, a failed `PROJECT_AS` does not prove semantic or projection Non-Capture where the source-function relation was never specified. A failed `DECOMPOSE` does not prove granularity Non-Capture where only one arbitrary component boundary was attempted.

Chapter 50 Anti-Immunization remains active:

```text
new capture claim
= new testable claim

new capture claim
≠ earlier failure erased
```

#### C52-STOPDISTINCTION-01 — Stop, failure, and Non-Capture remain separate while their records may be linked

Stop concerns whether continuation of a current operation or claim is unnecessary or inadmissible. Failure concerns whether a declared transformation did not satisfy its requirements. Non-Capture concerns whether an adequately attempted reconstruction leaves no adequate retained claim for the same delimited capture object without distortion or false closure.

```text
Stop
≠ failed_transformation
≠ non_capture

failed transformation
≠ Non-Capture automatically
```

The records may be linked. A Mandatory Stop can precede Non-Capture where further continuation would itself violate a boundary and no adequate retained claim remains. An Optional Stop normally does not support Non-Capture, because the current claim is already sufficient. A failed operation can contribute to a Non-Capture assessment only where relevant alternatives, coordinates, and rival reconstructions have also been adequately tested.

A Stop record must therefore preserve its own trigger and retained result. A Non-Capture record must preserve the attempted capture object and uncaptured remainder. Neither result absorbs the other.

<a id="52-3-forms-of-non-capture"></a>

### 52.3 Forms of Non-Capture

#### C52-FORMS-01 — Seven controlled capture forms organize the method without expanding inventories

Chapter 52 recognizes exactly seven capture forms:

```text
source non-capture
granularity non-capture
compositional non-capture
projection non-capture
calibration non-capture
semantic non-capture
partial capture
```

These are diagnostic forms within the Non-Capture assessment. They are not new Rules, primitives, audit stages, operations, or Output Classes. Multiple forms may coexist, but each requires its own evidence and scope.

The forms answer different questions:

- **Source Non-Capture:** do the sources permit an adequate reconstruction of the required relation or load?
- **Granularity Non-Capture:** does any tested relevant resolution jointly preserve relevance, calibration, and source support?
- **Compositional Non-Capture:** can any admissible composite retain load-bearing heterogeneity, alternatives, and Non-Events?
- **Projection Non-Capture:** can any bounded target function retain PraxisPurchase, Source Trace, Counterfactual Sensitivity, and TypeIntegrity?
- **Calibration Non-Capture:** can the necessary threshold or comparison be made inspectable rather than merely left open?
- **Semantic Non-Capture:** can the relevant meaning or domain-specific distinction be represented without semantic substitution or false equivalence?
- **Partial Capture:** which structures are captured, partly captured, and not captured?

The forms are non-exhaustive of every possible reason for an unsuccessful transformation, but they are closed as the Chapter-52 controlled vocabulary. Local reasons map onto one or more of these forms rather than generating an eighth form.

#### C52-PRESSUREPRESERVATION-01 — The synthetic pressure packet remains a test architecture rather than an adjudicated case

```yaml
non_capture_pressure_packet_after_wp1:
  NC0: total_capture_assertion_not_accepted
  SRC: unadjudicated
  GRN: unadjudicated
  CMP: unadjudicated
  PRJ: unadjudicated
  CAL: unadjudicated
  SEM: unadjudicated
  PAR: unadjudicated
  RIV: unadjudicated
```

`NC0` is not accepted because PMS-STRATA does not claim that every relevant structure is adequately capturable within the present grammar. None of the candidate forms is selected for the synthetic object. No rival, Stop, Claim Reduction, re-entry, route, or canonical Output Class is selected.

<a id="52-4-source-non-capture"></a>

### 52.4 Source Non-Capture

#### C52-SOURCENONCAPTURE-01 — Source Non-Capture requires an attempted bounded reconstruction whose required source relation remains unavailable

Source Non-Capture applies where a delimited capture object requires a relation or load that the present source basis cannot adequately reconstruct, despite an explicit bounded attempt and relevant source alternatives.

Typical limiting relations include:

- an unavailable temporal sequence or transition;
- an expectation frame required for a `Λ` Non-Event;
- inaccessible internal structure;
- an irrecoverable constitutive relation;
- historical load necessary for a path or projection claim;
- a missing discriminator among materially different reconstructions.

```text
required relation r
+ bounded reconstruction attempts
+ relevant source alternatives checked
+ r remains unavailable
+ no adequate retained claim for Q without r
→ possible source Non-Capture for Q
```

Source Non-Capture is not established by missing evidence alone. Missing information may remain ordinary uncertainty, an open source request, or a reason for Claim Reduction. The result requires showing that the unavailable relation is necessary for the tested claim and that continued invention would exceed the Source Ceiling.

A bounded example is a proposed path claim for which sources document states at `t1` and `t3` but neither their order nor the transition relation, while no expectation frame supports a Non-Event at `t2`. A sequence or path claim may then be non-capturable under the present source basis even though the separate state descriptions remain captured.

```text
source Non-Capture for sequence claim
≠ state descriptions uncaptured
≠ unknown transition converted into Λ
```

#### C52-WP1-HANDOFF-01 — WP1 hands WP2 the bounded definition, anti-shield control, seven forms, and source architecture

WP1 has established the Non-Capture definition, attempt disclosure, anti-shield control, Stop/failure distinction, exact seven-form vocabulary, pressure preservation, and source Non-Capture.

The synthetic packet remains unchanged:

```yaml
non_capture_pressure_packet_after_wp1_handoff:
  NC0: total_capture_assertion_not_accepted
  SRC: unadjudicated
  GRN: unadjudicated
  CMP: unadjudicated
  PRJ: unadjudicated
  CAL: unadjudicated
  SEM: unadjudicated
  PAR: unadjudicated
  RIV: unadjudicated
```

No capture form, rival, route, Stop, Claim Reduction, re-entry, or canonical Output Class is selected.

<a id="52-5-granularity-non-capture"></a>

### 52.5 Granularity Non-Capture

#### C52-GRANULARITYNONCAPTURE-01 — Granularity Non-Capture requires multiple relevant resolutions and no jointly adequate resolution

Granularity Non-Capture applies only after testing the relevant resolutions needed by the claim. It is not established because one coarse or fine analysis fails.

The assessment must compare at least two materially relevant granularities unless the source basis makes only one available and that limitation is itself documented. For each tested granularity it must ask:

- does the distinction retain PraxisPurchase?
- is the required structure source-supported?
- are comparison and threshold conditions calibratable?
- does the same reference object remain identifiable?
- are constitutive relations, alternatives, and Loss visible?

```text
coarse granularity
→ preserves reference but erases constitutive asymmetry

fine granularity
→ exposes asymmetry but fragments the source object beyond calibration

intermediate tested resolution
→ unsupported by source basis

no tested relevant resolution jointly passes
→ possible granularity Non-Capture for the delimited claim
```

This result remains claim-relative. A coarse descriptive claim may remain captured even where a stronger causal, trajectory, or functional claim is not.

Finer resolution receives no truth privilege. Coarser resolution receives no immunity. Granularity Non-Capture records a tested incompatibility among relevance, source support, traceability, and calibration under the present claim.

<a id="52-6-compositional-non-capture"></a>

### 52.6 Compositional Non-Capture

#### C52-COMPOSITIONALNONCAPTURE-01 — Compositional Non-Capture applies where no admissible composite retains load-bearing heterogeneity, alternatives, or Non-Events

A `COMPOSE` result is not adequate merely because multiple source objects can be ordered under one label. Compositional Non-Capture applies where every tested admissible composition destroys or falsely resolves structure necessary to the claim.

Relevant pressures include:

- incompatible paths that cannot be represented as one trajectory without false direction;
- branch structures whose alternatives disappear under a linear sequence;
- Non-Events whose expectation frames differ across components;
- heterogeneous roles, costs, or asymmetries flattened by aggregation;
- competing periodizations that cannot be merged without arbitrary threshold choice;
- components whose identity or type would be overwritten by the composite.

```text
composite A
→ preserves path p1, erases p2

composite B
→ preserves p2, converts Λ into missing information

composite C
→ retains both labels but invents a common direction

no admissible composite preserves the load-bearing plurality
→ possible compositional Non-Capture
```

The result does not prohibit retaining the separate source records. It means that the proposed composite claim is not adequately capturable under the tested composition space.

<a id="52-7-projection-non-capture"></a>

### 52.7 Projection Non-Capture

#### C52-PROJECTIONNONCAPTURE-01 — Projection Non-Capture requires failure of every relevant bounded target-function candidate across four burdens

Projection Non-Capture applies where no tested bounded target function jointly satisfies:

```text
PraxisPurchase
+ Constitutive Source Trace
+ Counterfactual Sensitivity
+ TypeIntegrity
```

Each candidate must preserve the origin type and declare the target context. A candidate fails if it is merely an analogy, carries no praxis-relevant difference, remains invariant under changes to the supposedly constitutive source feature, or overwrites the origin type.

```text
target function F1
→ relevant but no reconstructible Source Trace

target function F2
→ source-traceable but insensitive to constitutive modification

target function F3
→ sensitive but requires origin-type overwrite

no relevant candidate passes all four burdens
→ possible projection Non-Capture for the target-function claim
```

One failed projection is insufficient. The claim must disclose why the tested candidate set was relevant and whether a source-only description, analogy-only result, no projection, or external domain model remains preferable.

<a id="52-8-competing-reconstructions"></a>

### 52.8 Competing Reconstructions

#### C52-CALIBRATIONNONCAPTURE-01 — Calibration Non-Capture remains distinct from ordinary Calibration Openness

Calibration Openness applies where a stable bounded claim survives across multiple defensible thresholds even though the stronger classification remains open. Calibration Non-Capture applies only where the tested claim itself cannot be made inspectable across relevant threshold candidates.

```text
structural form survives every defensible threshold
+ stronger label varies
→ Calibration-Open Result, not Calibration Non-Capture

material claim changes across defensible thresholds
+ no source-supported discriminator
+ no stable bounded claim remains for the tested object
→ possible Calibration Non-Capture
```

The assessment must preserve threshold candidates, affected cases, missing discriminator, and any invariant remainder. It may not declare Calibration Non-Capture merely because no universal numerical threshold exists.

#### C52-COMPETINGRECONSTRUCTIONS-01 — Equally bounded but non-integrable or non-comparable reconstructions may support a capture limit

Multiple reconstructions may remain individually source-bounded and locally admissible while resisting integration or common calibration. Their coexistence is not automatically underdetermination, compositional failure, or Non-Capture. A capture limit arises where the tested claim requires a single integrated reconstruction and no warranted integration preserves the relevant differences.

The record must state:

- each reconstruction and its coordinates;
- the source support and Loss profile of each;
- why they are not comparable or integrable;
- what claim requires integration;
- which bounded claims survive separately;
- what discriminator or external method might resolve the plurality.

```text
R1 and R2 locally adequate
+ no common frame or threshold without distortion
+ integrated claim Q requires one combined object
→ possible Non-Capture for Q

R1 and R2 remain valid separately
```

#### C52-NOONEFAILUREGENERALIZATION-01 — No single failed operation, granularity, composite, or projection proves a general capture limit

Chapter 52 prohibits the following shortcuts:

```text
one failed granularity
→ granularity Non-Capture

one failed composite
→ compositional Non-Capture

one failed target function
→ projection Non-Capture
```

The generalization burden depends on the breadth of the claim. Relevant alternatives must be tested or explicitly unavailable. Where an alternative has not been examined, the result remains provisional, source-limited, stopped, or failed at the local attempt level rather than generalized to Non-Capture.

#### C52-RIVALSENSITIVITY-01 — Rival and no-transformation possibilities remain open without a superiority conclusion

A Non-Capture assessment must retain the possibility that:

- another PMS-STRATA reconstruction succeeds;
- a narrower claim remains sufficient;
- no transformation is preferable;
- an external domain model captures the object better;
- multiple models capture different aspects without a universal winner.

```text
PMS-STRATA Non-Capture
≠ rival superiority proved
≠ PMS useless
≠ translation impossible
```

A rival-superiority claim requires independent sources, coordinates, comparison criteria, Loss disclosure, and countercases. Chapter 52 keeps that claim possible but unselected.

#### C52-WP2-HANDOFF-01 — WP2 hands WP3 the operation- and coordinate-specific capture limits

WP2 has established granularity, compositional, projection, and calibration Non-Capture, competing reconstruction pressure, the no-single-failure rule, and rival sensitivity.

The synthetic packet remains unchanged and unadjudicated. No local candidate is mapped mechanically to `non_capture` or any other canonical Output Class.

<a id="52-9-partial-capture"></a>

### 52.9 Partial Capture

#### C52-PARTIALCAPTURE-01 — Partial capture separates captured, partially captured, and uncaptured structure with claim effects

Partial capture is not a polite synonym for total failure. It records a differentiated result:

```yaml
capture_status_view:
  captured:
  partially_captured:
  not_captured:
  limiting_condition:
  claim_effect:
```

A bounded example is:

```text
captured:
  documented sequence A → B → C
  role asymmetry between B and C

partially captured:
  transition mechanism at B
  expectation frame for an unrealized branch

not captured:
  constitutive causal relation claimed for the whole trajectory
```

The resulting claim may retain the sequence and role asymmetry while withholding the causal trajectory claim. Partial capture can therefore support Claim Reduction, `partially_admissible`, `admissible_with_bounded_claim`, provisional status, or whole-claim `non_capture`, depending on the delimited claim and Chapter-53 routing. No mapping is automatic.

```text
partial capture of object X
≠ every claim about X partially admissible
≠ whole object failed
```

<a id="52-10-non-operator-remainders"></a>

### 52.10 Non-Operator Remainders

#### C52-NONOPERATORREMAINDER-01 — Material and semantic remainders remain visible without primitive promotion

Praxeologically relevant structure may be operator-readable without being exhaustively reducible to Δ–Ψ. Chapter 52 preserves four broad remainder families:

- material conditions not fully represented by the current operator occurrence record;
- domain-specific mechanisms requiring external concepts or measurements;
- inaccessible internal processes;
- semantic or normative distinctions not captured by structural equivalence.

These are not new PMS operators. They remain source- and claim-bounded remainders.

```text
domain-specific remainder
≠ missing Δ–Ψ primitive

semantic remainder
≠ authorization to expand PMS Base
```

A remainder record must state what structural aspects PMS-STRATA does capture, what remains outside the current grammar, and whether an external vocabulary is needed. It must not convert the remainder into a hidden operator, person type, diagnosis, legitimacy judgment, or authority claim.

<a id="52-11-rival-superiority"></a>

### 52.11 Rival Superiority

#### C52-SEMANTICNONCAPTURE-01 — Semantic Non-Capture requires a material meaning loss, not merely different terminology

Semantic Non-Capture applies where translation into the present grammar would erase a distinction necessary to the tested claim. Different words, disciplinary conventions, or surface labels do not suffice.

The assessment asks:

- does the translation preserve the relevant distinction?
- can the source meaning be reconstructed from the PMS-STRATA result?
- does the translated label import or remove normative, causal, experiential, or domain-specific content?
- does the claim change when the original semantic relation is restored?

```text
different terminology
≠ semantic Non-Capture

translation collapses a claim-bearing distinction
+ no bounded equivalent preserves it
→ possible semantic Non-Capture
```

Semantic Non-Capture does not imply that the structure is non-praxeological or that PMS cannot represent any part of it. It delimits the claim that cannot survive the translation.

#### C52-RIVALSUPERIORITY-01 — Rival superiority remains possible but independently testable and unselected

A rival model may be more economical, empirically closer, semantically adequate, domain-sensitive, or better calibrated. Chapter 52 must allow that possibility because PMS-STRATA claims no total grammar or default superiority.

But superiority requires an independent comparison:

```yaml
rival_comparison_view:
  common_capture_object:
  comparison_coordinates:
  source_basis:
  claims_compared:
  captured_structure_by_each:
  loss_by_each:
  calibration_by_each:
  countercases:
  authority_boundary:
```

A Non-Capture result within PMS-STRATA supplies one input to that comparison. It does not decide the outcome.

<a id="52-12-non-capture-record"></a>

### 52.12 Non-Capture Record

#### C52-NONCAPTURERECORD-01 — The Non-Capture view maps object, attempts, remainder, limit, rivals, claim effect, and re-entry to existing record paths

The canonical view is:

```yaml
# Conceptual view only; map to /admissibility/non_capture_assessment
non_capture:
  object:
  attempted_operations:
  attempted_coordinates:
  captured_structure:
  partially_captured_structure:
  uncaptured_structure:
  limiting_condition:
  capture_form:
  source_and_loss_basis:
  countercases_and_alternatives:
  rival_or_alternative:
  claim_effect:
  prior_failure_or_stop:
  reentry_condition:
```

The view must preserve the earlier transformation records rather than replace them. An operation chain retains one record per operation occurrence, including its sources, Loss, local results, Stops, and objections. The Non-Capture assessment links those records and states the capture boundary.

Machine-readable checks may verify field presence, controlled vocabulary, references, and inventory consistency. They may not decide whether capture is substantively adequate, whether a semantic remainder is material, or whether a rival is superior.

#### C52-REENTRY-01 — Re-entry requires changed conditions and a new testable claim while preserving earlier Non-Capture

Re-entry after Non-Capture requires a materially changed condition, such as:

- newly available sources or a restored relation;
- a newly warranted granularity;
- a new composition preserving prior heterogeneity;
- a bounded target function with new Source Trace;
- a discriminator closing Calibration Non-Capture;
- an external semantic or domain-specific bridge;
- a narrower capture object.

```text
earlier Non-Capture NC0
+ materially changed condition
+ new operation or assessment
→ new record NC1
→ new testable claim

NC1
≠ NC0 erased
```

A later successful reconstruction shows that the earlier conditions changed or the claim narrowed. It does not make the earlier bounded result fraudulent or retroactively complete.

<a id="52-13-non-capture-statement"></a>

### 52.13 Non-Capture Statement

#### C52-STATEMENTHANDOFF53-01 — The canonical statement preserves bounded capture limits and hands them to the integrated audit

> PMS-STRATA preserves the possibility that a structure remains only partially reconstructible, non-projectable, or better captured outside the present grammar.

The statement is not a universal incompleteness claim. It is a governance commitment: STRATA must preserve the possibility of bounded capture limits, rival adequacy, no transformation, Claim Reduction, Stop, and re-entry without inventing source relations or extending PMS Base.

Chapter 53 receives:

- the delimited capture object;
- attempted operations and coordinates;
- captured, partially captured, and uncaptured structure;
- the limiting condition and capture form;
- prior Failure, Stop, Claim Reduction, and Loss;
- rivals and no-transformation alternatives;
- the bounded claim effect;
- re-entry conditions.

Chapter 53 retains ownership of integrated routing and final Output-Class selection.

#### C52-HANDOFF-01 — WP3 hands WP4 the complete Non-Capture method without adjudicating the pressure packet

Chapter 52 now contains all twenty-four duties across §§52.1–52.13. The method preserves exact operation, Rule, audit-stage, Output-Class, sensitivity, and Loss inventories. It creates no new primitive, schema field family, or authority relation.

The synthetic packet remains unchanged:

```yaml
non_capture_pressure_packet_after_wp3:
  NC0: total_capture_assertion_not_accepted
  SRC: unadjudicated
  GRN: unadjudicated
  CMP: unadjudicated
  PRJ: unadjudicated
  CAL: unadjudicated
```
