```yaml
  SEM: unadjudicated
  PAR: unadjudicated
  RIV: unadjudicated
```

No pressure-object Non-Capture form, rival, Stop, Claim Reduction, Failure, re-entry, route, or canonical Output Class has been selected. No Case, Test, or Smoke YAML has been created. The artifact-complete RETYPE lock remains `mandatory_stop`; required lock packages remain `0/3`; Part IV remains open; and Part IV final lock remains unavailable.
## Chapter 53 — Integrated STRATA Admissibility Audit

Chapter 53 integrates the local results of PATH, SUB, RETYPE, and LIMITS into one bounded audit route. It does not replace the operation-specific methods, reopen provisionally locked chapters, or convert formal completeness into substantive warrant. Its task is to preserve every claim-bearing burden while determining which canonical result class, if any, is supported for the audited object.

```text
integrated audit
≠ new operation
≠ replacement of local audits
≠ numerical aggregation
≠ automatic truth decision
```

The chapter audits structures and claims. It does not type persons, diagnose states, decide legitimacy, prescribe sanctions, or inherit authority from analytical resolution.

<a id="53-1-purpose"></a>

### 53.1 Purpose

#### C53-PURPOSE-01 — The integrated audit connects local results without replacing or rewriting them

The Integrated STRATA Admissibility Audit connects the results produced by the existing operation records and LIMITS checks. A local audit remains the canonical owner of its own finding. Chapter 53 may compare, preserve, and route those findings, but it may not edit a local result merely to produce a cleaner final route.

```text
local result R_i
+ explicit handoff
+ integrated compatibility test
→ possible final route

integrated preference
≠ permission to rewrite R_i
```

The audit therefore distinguishes at least four things:

- whether a stage was completed;
- what substantive result the stage produced;
- whether that result is compatible with the other stage results;
- which canonical Output Class is warranted for the delimited audited claim.

A stage can be complete and still remain unresolved. A local method can pass while the operation chain fails at a later handoff. Conversely, a local failure can defeat one claim without erasing narrower claims that remain supported.

The audit is non-compensatory. More detail, more formal structure, more stages passed, or stronger performance elsewhere cannot cancel a load-bearing failure.

<a id="53-2-audit-object"></a>

### 53.2 Audit Object

#### C53-AUDITOBJECT-01 — The audit object and every operation-chain boundary must be explicit

The audit may examine:

- one operation occurrence;
- an ordered operation chain;
- a complete case reconstruction;
- one Shared Transformation Record and its referenced local records;
- a technically formalized STRATA output.

Every audit begins by delimiting the object. The minimum declaration is:

```yaml
audit_object_view:
  audit_object_id:
  reference_object:
  claim_under_audit:
  claim_type:
  frame:
  granularity:
  relative_level:
  temporal_scope:
  origin_type:
  target_function_if_any:
  source_basis:
  operation_records:
  chain_boundaries:
  inherited_results:
  requested_audit_scope:
```

An operation chain requires one record per operation occurrence. A three-operation chain such as:

```text
source configurations
COMPOSE
composite path P

P
PROJECT_AS
bounded contextual function F

F-linked source object
DECOMPOSE
components C1…Cn
```

cannot be represented as one undifferentiated transformation. The records must preserve:

- each operation signature;
- the input and output object of each step;
- the handoff relation;
- the five-part Loss declaration;
- local results, objections, Stops, and Claim Reductions;
- inherited uncertainty and re-entry conditions.

A chain boundary is claim-bearing whenever a later operation depends on an intermediate object. If the intermediate object is compressed, uncertain, provisionally typed, or locally failed, that burden travels with the handoff.

#### C53-STAGEORDER-01 — The exact twelve-stage order is preserved without addition, deletion, or reordering

The integrated audit proceeds through exactly twelve stages:

```text
1.  Source and Claim Entry
2.  Operation Classification
3.  Praxeological Relevance Floor
4.  Praxeological Traceability Ceiling
5.  Continuity and Type Integrity
6.  Counterfactual Sensitivity
7.  Loss and Selection
8.  Alternatives
9.  Source and Calibration Limits
10. Anti-Immunization
11. Stop and Non-Capture
12. Claim and Authority Ceiling
```

The order is functional rather than numerical scoring. Later stages may send the audit back to an earlier record for clarification, but they do not create a thirteenth stage or change the canonical sequence. A return for correction produces an amended or new record, not silent retroactive passage.

```text
stage return for clarification
≠ stage reordering
≠ hidden record repair
```

<a id="53-3-stage-1-source-and-claim-entry"></a>

### 53.3 Stage 1 — Source and Claim Entry

#### C53-SOURCECLAIMENTRY-01 — Stage 1 establishes the object, coordinates, claim, type, scope, and source entry

Stage 1 asks whether the transformation has a sufficiently explicit entry point. It records:

1. the reference object;
2. Frame, granularity, relative level, and temporal scope;
3. origin type and any bounded target function;
4. the exact claim wording and claim type;
5. Claim Scope and Claim Ceiling;
6. source identities, source types, and source scope;
7. direct support, inferred structure, missing structure, uncertainty, and irrecoverability;
8. the prior record or PMS object, where one is reused.

The source entry must distinguish:

```text
direct support
≠ inferred structure
≠ missing source
≠ documented non-occurrence
≠ Λ Non-Event
```

A machine-valid record with every required field can still remain substantively unresolved. For example, a record may correctly declare two reconstructed transitions and a missing discriminator. Schema validity proves that the uncertainty is represented; it does not decide which transition occurred.

If the entry object is not delimited, the audit cannot be rescued by later stages. Stage 1 may require correction, a narrower claim, source limitation, or Stop. It does not automatically select an Output Class.

<a id="53-4-stage-2-operation-classification"></a>

### 53.4 Stage 2 — Operation Classification

#### C53-OPERATIONCLASSIFICATION-01 — Stage 2 classifies exactly COMPOSE, DECOMPOSE, or PROJECT_AS and separates mixed operations

Stage 2 asks:

- Is the occurrence `COMPOSE`, `DECOMPOSE`, or `PROJECT_AS`?
- What is the operation direction?
- Are multiple operations being compressed into one record?
- Does the claimed transformation instead amount to recontextualization, analogy, or label substitution?
- Does each operation have its own object, justification, Loss record, and failure possibility?

The signatures remain:

```text
COMPOSE
many or sequential source structures
→ new composite analytical object

DECOMPOSE
compressed occurrence or composite
→ finer reconstruction of the same reference object

PROJECT_AS
origin-typed source object
→ bounded contextual target function
```

No fourth operation is available. LIMITS remains a Part, not an operation or meta-authority.

For a mixed chain, the audit creates or references separate records. A valid `COMPOSE` result does not automatically validate a later `PROJECT_AS`. A valid `DECOMPOSE` does not retroactively make the source composite lossless. A successful projection does not change the origin type.

A single-operation example is a `DECOMPOSE` occurrence that opens a documented composite event into three source-supported components while retaining the same reference object. Stage 2 can pass the operation classification even if a later continuity test finds that one component relation is uncertain. Operation classification and final admissibility remain separate.

<a id="53-5-stage-3-praxeological-relevance-floor"></a>

### 53.5 Stage 3 — Praxeological Relevance Floor

#### C53-RELEVANCEFLOOR-01 — Stage 3 applies the existing Relevance Floor without redefining it

Stage 3 asks what additional praxeological distinction the transformation produces. The audit records:

- the new difference introduced;
- the PraxisPurchase for the delimited claim;
- the practice dimension affected;
- whether the result corrects, narrows, separates, or clarifies a claim;
- whether the operation only adds detail;
- whether a coarser reconstruction already carries the same claim.

```text
more detail
≠ additional praxeological difference

formal distinction
+ no claim-bearing PraxisPurchase
→ lower-bound pressure
```

The audit does not demand actionability, recommendation, or practical intervention. PraxisPurchase concerns analytical purchase within the declared claim.

A local Stage-3 pass cannot compensate for a later TypeIntegrity failure. Conversely, a Stage-3 failure does not erase all prior source observations. It means the attempted transformation lacks sufficient relevance for the claim under audit.

Where further transformation remains technically possible but produces no additional purchase, Optional Stop may be appropriate. Where continuation would invent distinctions or evade a prior failure, Mandatory Stop may later be required at Stage 11.

<a id="53-6-stage-4-praxeological-traceability-ceiling"></a>

### 53.6 Stage 4 — Praxeological Traceability Ceiling

#### C53-TRACEABILITYCEILING-01 — Stage 4 applies the existing Traceability Ceiling without redefining it

Stage 4 asks whether the target result remains reconstructively anchored in load-bearing source structures. The audit records:

- reference and occurrence traces;
- constitutive relations;
- temporal order, duration, delays, branches, and Non-Events where relevant;
- roles, costs, access conditions, and asymmetries;
- source-function dependencies for `PROJECT_AS`;
- inherited Loss and uncertainty;
- the effect of relevant source modifications.

```text
higher-level readability
+ no reconstructible load path
→ abstraction without traceable load
```

Operator labels are insufficient. The presence of a Δ–Ψ type in both source and target does not establish occurrence identity, historical continuity, or functional dependence.

One local success may survive a chain-handoff failure. For example, a `COMPOSE` record may correctly reconstruct a bounded sequence, but a later `PROJECT_AS` record may omit the source feature claimed to carry the target function. The Stage-4 failure belongs to the projection handoff. The earlier composition remains locally supported; the chain-level functional claim does not.

#### C53-WP1-HANDOFF-01 — WP1 hands WP2 the audit object, exact stage order, and Stages 1–4 without adjudication

WP1 has established the integrated audit purpose, admissible audit objects, exact twelve-stage order, Source and Claim Entry, Operation Classification, Relevance Floor, and Traceability Ceiling.

The synthetic pressure packet remains unchanged:

```yaml
integrated_audit_pressure_packet_after_wp1:
  A0: technical_and_local_consistency_is_sufficient_assertion_not_accepted
  E1: unadjudicated
  E2: unadjudicated
  E3_4: unadjudicated
  E5_6: unadjudicated
  E7_9: unadjudicated
  E10: unadjudicated
  E11: unadjudicated
  E12: unadjudicated
  FR: unadjudicated
```

No stage result, route, Stop, Claim Reduction, Failure, Non-Capture, or canonical Output Class has been selected.

<a id="53-7-stage-5-continuity-and-type-integrity"></a>

### 53.7 Stage 5 — Continuity and Type Integrity

#### C53-CONTINUITYTYPE-01 — Stage 5 preserves reference, TypeIntegrity, functional, and temporal continuity as separate findings

Stage 5 applies the continuity architecture from Chapter 47 and the canonical `TypeIntegrity` Rule. The audit keeps four local fields:

```yaml
continuity:
  reference:
  type_integrity_view:
  function:
  temporal:
```

The fields are not averaged. The audit asks:

- Does the same historical or structural reference object remain identifiable?
- Is the origin type preserved?
- Are component, composite, occurrence, and derived-function types separated?
- Is the bounded target function carried by concrete source relations?
- Are claim-bearing temporal relations retained?
- Are Frame, granularity, and relative-level changes marked?
- Has any derived object or function been promoted to a new primitive?

A mixed result remains possible:

```text
reference continuity: preserved
type integrity: preserved
functional continuity: failed
temporal continuity: partial
```

This result cannot be collapsed into a simple majority pass. It may support a narrower reference claim while defeating the functional claim.

<a id="53-8-stage-6-counterfactual-sensitivity"></a>

### 53.8 Stage 6 — Counterfactual Sensitivity

#### C53-SENSITIVITY-01 — Stage 6 uses exactly six sensitivity values and does not infer causality

The controlled local vocabulary remains:

```text
strongly_sensitive
partially_sensitive
weakly_sensitive
insensitive
underdetermined
untestable
```

Stage 6 records:

- the baseline;
- the claimed load-bearing feature;
- the relevant source modification;
- preserved conditions;
- expected target response;
- source basis for the modification and response space;
- sensitivity value or reason it remains unselected;
- bounded claim effect.

The operation-specific test forms remain distinct:

- `COMPOSE`: modify selection, order, branch, event, Non-Event, or constitutive relation;
- `DECOMPOSE`: modify a component or relation while preserving the same reference object;
- `PROJECT_AS`: modify the source feature claimed to carry the target function.

```text
strongly_sensitive
≠ causal proof

insensitive
≠ robustness automatically

underdetermined
≠ partially_sensitive by averaging

untestable
≠ claim immunity
```

A source-grounded response can pressure a claim. It does not establish causal necessity, empirical universality, or predictive law.

<a id="53-9-stage-7-loss-and-selection"></a>

### 53.9 Stage 7 — Loss and Selection

#### C53-LOSSSELECTION-01 — Stage 7 uses exactly five Loss fields and audits reconstructive selection

The canonical inventory remains:

```yaml
loss:
  preserved:
  compressed:
  excluded:
  uncertain:
  irrecoverable:
```

Stage 7 asks:

- Which structures were selected?
- Which coordinates and periodizations shaped the result?
- What remains reconstructibly preserved?
- What is represented in compressed form?
- What known structure is excluded?
- What remains unresolved?
- What cannot be recovered?
- Did a later chain step silently treat an intermediate object as lossless?

Disclosed Loss is not automatically admissible. Large Loss may remain compatible with a narrow claim where the load-bearing relation survives. A small exclusion may defeat a claim where it removes a constitutive relation or counterpath.

Selection effects must remain visible, including path linearization, Non-Event concealment, asymmetry homogenization, target-function privileging, and operator-label foregrounding.

```text
five fields completed
≠ substantive Loss audit passed
```

<a id="53-10-stage-8-alternatives"></a>

### 53.10 Stage 8 — Alternatives

#### C53-ALTERNATIVES-01 — Stage 8 preserves operation alternatives, rivals, and no transformation

The audit must consider, where relevant:

- an alternative composition;
- an alternative decomposition;
- an alternative bounded projection;
- a competing Frame or granularity;
- a rival domain model;
- a narrower claim;
- no transformation.

Alternatives are not decorative. The record states which alternatives were tested, unavailable, rejected for a bounded reason, or left live.

A no-transformation alternative can be preferable where the source object already supports the claim and further operation adds only complexity or Loss.

```text
technically executable transformation
≠ transformation necessary
```

Competing reconstructions can remain locally admissible without forced merger. The audit does not select PMS superiority merely because a result is expressible in PMS language.

<a id="53-11-stage-9-source-and-calibration-limits"></a>

### 53.11 Stage 9 — Source and Calibration Limits

#### C53-SOURCECALIBRATION-01 — Stage 9 separates Source Ceiling, Relevance Floor, Traceability Ceiling, Claim Ceiling, and Calibration Openness

Stage 9 asks:

- Where does the source basis cease to support the next distinction?
- Which inferential bridges are direct, reconstructed, or unsupported?
- Which cases are comparable under declared coordinates?
- Which thresholds are inspectable?
- Which thresholds remain open?
- Does a stable bounded claim survive all source-supported calibrations?
- Does formal precision exceed source precision?

The four boundaries remain distinct:

```text
Source Ceiling
≠ Relevance Floor
≠ Traceability Ceiling
≠ Claim Ceiling
```

Calibration Openness may retain a stable structural claim while withholding a stronger classification. It is not a safe default, conceptual vagueness, or immunity from counterexamples.

A machine-readable record may correctly represent an open threshold. Technical validity does not close it.

<a id="53-12-stage-10-anti-immunization"></a>

### 53.12 Stage 10 — Anti-Immunization

#### C53-ANTIIMMUNIZATION-01 — Stage 10 preserves the original claim and distinguishes answered objections from shifted objections

Stage 10 records:

- the original claim and coordinates;
- the original objection or countercase;
- the original local and canonical result;
- any new Frame, granularity, level, composition, projection, source basis, or temporal scope;
- the successor claim;
- whether the objection was answered, retained, reduced, stopped, or merely moved;
- whether the successor can independently fail.

```text
new transformation
= new testable claim

new transformation
≠ prior failure erased
```

A finer decomposition that exposes new source-supported structure can support a new claim. It does not repair the failed coarse claim unless that claim is directly re-tested. A successful projection cannot rescue a failed source typing. A higher-level claim cannot dissolve a local counterexample by changing the question without declaring the change.

#### C53-NONCOMPENSATION-01 — No weighting, scoring, majority vote, or cross-stage compensation is allowed

The integrated audit has no numerical score and no compensatory function.

```text
11 stages passed
+ 1 load-bearing stage failed
≠ admissible by majority
```

A canonical example is:

```yaml
stage_summary:
  source_entry: pass
  operation_classification: pass
  relevance_floor: pass
  traceability_ceiling: pass
  continuity_and_type: functional_failure
  counterfactual_sensitivity: underdetermined
  loss_and_selection: disclosed
  alternatives: live
  source_and_calibration: bounded
  anti_immunization: pass
  stop_non_capture: unselected
  claim_authority: bounded
```

The functional failure cannot be cancelled by the remaining passes. The audit may retain a narrower non-functional claim, require Claim Reduction, stop the transformation, or route the functional claim to failure or Non-Capture depending on the complete record.

#### C53-WP2-HANDOFF-01 — WP2 hands WP3 Stages 5–10 and non-compensatory chain logic

WP2 has established continuity and TypeIntegrity, Counterfactual Sensitivity, Loss and Selection, Alternatives, Source and Calibration Limits, Anti-Immunization, and the prohibition on cross-stage compensation.

The pressure packet remains unadjudicated. No stage candidate or final route has been selected.

<a id="53-13-stage-11-stop-and-non-capture"></a>

### 53.13 Stage 11 — Stop and Non-Capture

#### C53-STOPNONCAPTURE-01 — Stage 11 keeps Stop, Claim Reduction, Failure, and Non-Capture distinct

Stage 11 asks:

- Is further transformation necessary for the current claim?
- Would continuation be inadmissible?
- Is Claim Reduction sufficient?
- Has the attempted transformation itself failed?
- Has an adequate bounded capture attempt established Non-Capture?
- What narrower result remains preserved?
- What changed condition could permit re-entry?

The central distinctions are:

```text
Optional Stop
→ continuation admissible but unnecessary

mandatory_stop
→ continuation would be inadmissible

failed_transformation
→ attempted transformation does not meet its declared burden

non_capture
→ adequate bounded attempts cannot retain a sufficient claim for the same delimited capture object
```

A distinction example:

- **Mandatory Stop:** a proposed `DECOMPOSE` would open an abstract Δ–Ψ operator type rather than an occurrence or composite.
- **Failed Transformation:** a declared `PROJECT_AS` lacks a constitutive Source Trace, while a narrower analogy remains expressible.
- **Non-Capture:** several relevant projections, granularities, and rival reconstructions have been tested, yet no bounded result preserves the claim-bearing semantic distinction for the same capture object.

These outcomes are not interchangeable. A local failure does not prove Non-Capture. A Mandatory Stop may preserve a valid narrower claim. Non-Capture requires an adequate attempt record and anti-shield control.

<a id="53-14-stage-12-claim-and-authority-ceiling"></a>

### 53.14 Stage 12 — Claim and Authority Ceiling

#### C53-CLAIMAUTHORITY-01 — Stage 12 limits the final claim and preserves authority non-inheritance

Stage 12 determines the strongest claim supported by the complete record. It asks:

- What claim type is being made?
- Which parts are direct, inferred, provisional, reduced, or unresolved?
- Has increased readability been mistaken for truth?
- Has formal precision exceeded source precision?
- Has a higher level been treated as additional authority?
- Does the wording imply person typing, diagnosis, legitimacy, sanction, intervention, or normative command?
- Is the final validity scope explicit?

The universal governance boundary remains:

```yaml
governance:
  authority_inheritance: prohibited
```

An authority-ceiling counterexample is a structurally admissible reconstruction of role asymmetry that is then used to rank persons, infer a stable person property, or authorize sanction. The structural claim may remain bounded and admissible; the person-level or action-authorizing extension must be rejected, reduced, or stopped.

```text
analytical visibility
≠ application authority
```

<a id="53-15-audit-result-classes"></a>

### 53.15 Audit Result Classes

#### C53-OUTPUTCLASSES-01 — The final route uses exactly ten canonical Output Classes with explicit reasons

The closed inventory is:

```text
admissible
admissible_with_bounded_claim
admissible_but_provisional
resolution_neutral
analogy_only
partially_admissible
claim_reduction_required
mandatory_stop
failed_transformation
non_capture
```

The audit selects a class only after identifying:

- the exact audited claim;
- passed, failed, open, and inapplicable stage results;
- preserved local results;
- load-bearing failure or uncertainty;
- Loss and alternatives;
- Claim Scope and Claim Ceiling;
- Stop, Failure, or Non-Capture basis;
- authority boundary.

No class is a safe default. In particular:

```text
uncertainty
≠ admissible_but_provisional automatically

partial capture
≠ partially_admissible automatically

insensitive
≠ failed_transformation automatically

one failed attempt
≠ non_capture
```

A bounded routing example is:

```yaml
final_route_example:
  source_claim: documented sequence and role asymmetry
  stronger_claim_rejected: constitutive causal trajectory
  preserved_result:
    - sequence
    - role asymmetry
  unresolved:
    - transition mechanism
  final_result: admissible_with_bounded_claim
  route_reason: source and continuity support the bounded structural claim, while causal and universal extensions exceed Source and Claim Ceilings
```

The route is explicit because the remaining claim is identified. The class does not merely express caution.

<a id="53-16-integrated-audit-record"></a>

### 53.16 Integrated Audit Record

#### C53-INTEGRATEDRECORD-01 — The Integrated Audit Record maps to existing Shared-Record paths

The conceptual view is:

```yaml
# Conceptual integrated view only; not a second record grammar.
strata_audit:
  audit_object:
  source_entry:
  claim_scope:
  operation_chain:
  relevance_floor:
  traceability_ceiling:
  continuity:
  counterfactual_sensitivity:
  loss:
  alternatives:
  source_limits:
  calibration_limits:
  anti_immunization:
  stop:
  non_capture:
  authority_ceiling:
  local_results_preserved:
  final_result:
  route_reason:
  reentry_condition:
```

Each field references existing paths in the Shared Transformation Record, operation records, local audit results, and canonical disposition structures. It creates no second record grammar, schema field family, Rule, operation, audit stage, or Output Class.

Machine-readable validation may check:

- field presence;
- controlled vocabularies;
- operation and stage inventories;
- record references;
- Loss fields;
- result-class membership;
- hash and link integrity;
- `authority_inheritance: prohibited`.

It may not decide empirical truth, semantic adequacy, constitutive relevance, causality, person status, normative validity, or application authority.

#### C53-CHAINPRESERVATION-01 — Operation chains preserve local records, handoff failures, Loss, dispositions, and re-entry conditions

For a chain `T1 → T2 → T3`, the integrated audit records separately:

```yaml
chain_audit_view:
  T1:
    local_result:
    loss:
    unresolved:
    output_or_disposition:
  handoff_1_2:
    inherited_burdens:
    continuity_result:
  T2:
    local_result:
    loss:
    unresolved:
    output_or_disposition:
  handoff_2_3:
    inherited_burdens:
    continuity_result:
  T3:
    local_result:
    loss:
    unresolved:
    output_or_disposition:
  chain_result:
```

If `T1` succeeds locally and the `T1 → T2` handoff fails, the local result remains. The chain claim fails or narrows at the handoff. A later `T3` success does not erase that failure.

```text
local success
≠ chain success

later success
≠ earlier handoff repaired automatically
```

Prior Stops, Claim Reductions, Failure, Non-Capture, uncertainty, and re-entry conditions remain part of the provenance.

<a id="53-17-final-audit-questions"></a>

### 53.17 Final Audit Questions

#### C53-FINALQUESTIONS-01 — Ten final questions provide a non-scored completion test

The final completion test asks:

1. Does the transformation produce an additional praxeological distinction?
2. Does the target structure remain traceable to load-bearing source structures?
3. Are reference, origin type, and bounded target function kept distinct?
4. Could a relevant countercase or source modification alter or defeat the result?
5. Are reconstructive selection and all five Loss fields disclosed?
6. Are Source Ceiling, Calibration Limits, and Claim Ceiling visible?
7. Was the original objection answered rather than moved?
8. Is Stop genuinely possible and correctly typed?
9. Is Non-Capture genuinely possible and protected from use as a shield?
10. Has no additional authority been created or inherited?

The completion record is qualitative:

```yaml
final_questions_completion:
  q1_praxeological_distinction: answered
  q2_traceable_load: answered
  q3_type_and_function: answered
  q4_counterpressure: open_with_bounded_effect
  q5_selection_and_loss: answered
  q6_source_calibration_claim_limits: answered
  q7_anti_immunization: answered
  q8_stop_possible: answered
  q9_non_capture_possible: answered
  q10_authority_non_inheritance: answered
  numerical_score: prohibited
  final_route: unselected_pending_substantive_review
```

Completion means the questions have inspectable answers. It does not mean every answer is positive or that the route is admissible.

<a id="53-18-limits-closing-statement"></a>

### 53.18 LIMITS Closing Statement

LIMITS specifies the admissible operating range of PMS-STRATA without becoming a fourth operation, a meta-level, or an authority layer.

It prevents additional resolution from becoming distinction without praxeological purchase, prevents broader composition and projection from becoming abstraction without traceable load, and preserves Reference, Type, Functional, Temporal, Loss, and Authority Continuity across individual occurrences and chains. It also keeps Claim Reduction, Mandatory Stop, Optional Stop, Failure, Non-Capture, and changed-condition re-entry available as distinct results or dispositions.

```text
Praxeological Relevance Floor
─────────────────────────────
admissible transformation
─────────────────────────────
Praxeological Traceability Ceiling
```

The band remains non-compensatory. Detail cannot repair missing PraxisPurchase. Formal precision cannot repair missing TraceableLoad. Continuity cannot repair a type jump. Sensitivity cannot prove causality. Technical completeness cannot grant authority.

## Chapter 54 — The Integrated STRATA Model

<a id="54-1-the-problem-addressed"></a>

### 54.1 The Problem Addressed

#### C54-PROBLEM-01 — STRATA addresses a limited transformation problem without declaring PMS Base defective

PMS Base supplies the unchanged Δ–Ψ grammar through which praxis structures can be reconstructed. It defines operator types, dependencies, layers, and authority boundaries. It does not require that every analytical movement between temporal structures, provisional composites, finer granularities, relative levels, and contextual functions be treated as one undifferentiated act.

STRATA addresses that limited methodological problem. It specifies how analysts may move among already admissible PMS objects while preserving claim boundaries, source burdens, type integrity, loss, and the possibility of Stop, Failure, or Non-Capture.

The problem is not that PMS Base lacks an operator or ontological level. The problem is that unmarked analytical movement can produce avoidable errors:

- chronology can be presented as path or trajectory;
- a composite can be treated as a lossless whole;
- finer detail can be treated as deeper truth;
- a bounded target function can be treated as a new origin type;
- a later operation can erase an earlier failure or handoff burden;
- technical integration can be presented as empirical validation or additional authority.

```text
bounded transformation problem
≠ defective PMS Base
≠ missing operator
≠ missing ontology
```

STRATA therefore extends method, not Basis grammar, ontology, or authority. It asks whether a declared transformation remains both discriminating and reconstructively anchored. Where that question cannot be answered positively, the method must be able to narrow the claim, stop, fail, or retain Non-Capture.

A coherent integrated model may still return `mandatory_stop`, `failed_transformation`, or `non_capture`. Integration makes the burdens more visible; it does not guarantee successful capture.

#### C54-PMSDEPENDENCE-01 — PMS Base remains the sole operator grammar and no authority is elevated through synthesis

Every STRATA object remains dependent on PMS Base for operator typing. Concrete Δ–Ψ occurrences and their source-supported relations may be selected, composed, decomposed as occurrences or composites, and projected as bounded functions. The operator types themselves are neither renamed nor decomposed, their dependencies are not reordered, and their layers are not converted into empirical levels.

```text
PMS Base
→ operator grammar and authority boundary

STRATA
→ bounded transformation discipline
```

The integrated model has no authority to adjudicate PMS Base from a higher position. It cannot infer empirical truth, causality, diagnosis, legitimacy, sanction, intervention, or normative command merely because the transformation record is complete.

```yaml
governance:
  authority_inheritance: prohibited
```

<a id="54-2-the-four-part-architecture"></a>

### 54.2 The Four-Part Architecture

#### C54-FOURPARTS-01 — PATH, SUB, RETYPE, and LIMITS form four mutually dependent Parts

The integrated architecture consists of four Parts:

```text
PATH
→ temporal composition and historical ordering

SUB
→ finer reconstruction of provisionally compressed occurrences and composites

RETYPE
→ bounded contextual functional projection

LIMITS
→ admissibility, continuity, sensitivity, loss, alternatives, Stop, Failure, and Non-Capture
```

The Parts are mutually dependent without being interchangeable.

PATH can form a temporal object, but it does not determine by itself whether that object performs a bounded function in a later context. SUB can open a composite, but it does not grant finer resolution truth priority. RETYPE can project a source object as a contextual function, but it cannot overwrite the source type or repair missing source trace. LIMITS can stop or route a transformation, but it is not a transformation and does not supply empirical content.

```text
PATH ≠ COMPOSE alone
SUB ≠ DECOMPOSE alone
RETYPE ≠ PROJECT_AS alone
LIMITS ≠ operation
```

Each Part contains its own object distinctions, coordinate burdens, audit requirements, and handoff conditions. The integrated model preserves those local definitions rather than replacing them with a shorter superior theory.

#### C54-THREEOPS-01 — The synthesis preserves exactly three operations

Across all four Parts, the operation inventory remains closed:

```text
COMPOSE
DECOMPOSE
PROJECT_AS
```

`COMPOSE` forms a new composite analytical object from many or sequential source structures. `DECOMPOSE` opens a compressed occurrence or composite into a finer reconstruction of the same reference object. `PROJECT_AS` retains the origin-typed source object while assigning a bounded contextual target function.

The integrated model does not add an operation for auditing, limiting, calibrating, stopping, or recording. Those functions remain method controls or dispositions.

#### C54-NOFOURTHOP-01 — Four Parts do not imply a fourth operation

```text
four Parts
≠ four operations

LIMITS
≠ fourth operation
≠ meta-operation
≠ authority layer
```

A LIMITS finding does not transform an object. It evaluates whether a transformation, handoff, chain, or claim remains admissible under the declared conditions. Likewise, a Stop, Claim Reduction, Failure, or Non-Capture result is not an operation occurrence.

#### C54-WP1-HANDOFF-01 — WP1 hands the bounded problem, four-Part architecture, and three-operation inventory forward without adjudication

WP1 establishes the limited problem, PMS dependence, four Parts, and exact three-operation inventory. It selects no result for the synthetic pressure packet. `M0` remains unaccepted; `PD/FA/PC/SD/RP/LB/CL/NI/IM` remain unadjudicated.

<a id="54-3-path-and-compose"></a>

### 54.3 PATH and `COMPOSE`

#### C54-PATH-01 — PATH owns temporal composition without being re-derived in the Conclusion

PATH reconstructs temporal order and the conditions under which configurations can form sequences, paths, trajectories, sedimented structures, and path-dependent relations. It preserves realized and unrealized branches, transitions, Non-Events, thresholds, temporal asymmetries, and historical load where these are claim-bearing and source-supported.

This synthesis does not redefine those objects. Their primary definitions remain in Chapters 9–17. Chapter 54 retains only the integrated role:

```text
PATH
→ forms temporally structured analytical objects
→ preserves the distinction sequence ≠ path ≠ trajectory
→ makes historical load available to later operations
```

PATH does not make every ordered series a path, every path a trajectory, or every trajectory path-dependent. Nor does a complete PATH record determine whether a later `PROJECT_AS` is admissible.

#### C54-COMPOSE-01 — `COMPOSE` retains selection, ordering, composite identity, and Loss burdens

`COMPOSE` takes many or sequential source structures and forms a new composite analytical object. Its result is not a neutral sum. The operation must declare:

- selected source structures and relevant alternatives;
- temporal or constitutive order;
- branch, event, and Non-Event treatment;
- constitutive relations among components;
- composite identity and claim scope;
- what is preserved, compressed, excluded, uncertain, and irrecoverable.

```yaml
loss:
  preserved:
  compressed:
  excluded:
  uncertain:
  irrecoverable:
```

A valid `COMPOSE` occurrence keeps component identities and relations inspectable enough for the declared claim. The composite may acquire a derived analytical identity, but it does not retroactively change the types of its components or automatically acquire a target function.

```text
many source structures
COMPOSE
new composite analytical object

new composite
≠ complete whole
≠ lossless totality
≠ automatic target function
```

A bounded chain example begins with documented configurations `A`, `B`, and `C`:

```text
A, B, C
COMPOSE
P = ordered path object
```

The `COMPOSE` record may preserve the order `A → B → C`, compress internal variation within `B`, exclude one source-supported but non-constitutive side branch, and retain uncertainty about the transition mechanism. Those declarations remain attached to `P` in every later handoff.

<a id="54-4-sub-and-decompose"></a>

### 54.4 SUB and `DECOMPOSE`

#### C54-SUB-01 — SUB owns finer reconstruction under controlled granularity

SUB opens structures that were provisionally treated as analytical units. It asks which finer distinctions produce additional praxeological purchase while retaining the same reference object and a reconstructible relation to the coarser source function.

Its integrated role is:

```text
SUB
→ opens compressed occurrences and composites
→ tests finer distinctions without truth priority
→ preserves or critically revises the coarser source relation
```

SUB does not assume final constituents. It does not make every finer description preferable, and it must stop where additional resolution falls below the Relevance Floor, exceeds source precision, destroys traceability, or becomes a Granularity Escape.

#### C54-DECOMPOSE-01 — `DECOMPOSE` reconstructs the same reference object and never decomposes operator types

`DECOMPOSE` applies to concrete operator-typed occurrences, events, Non-Events, composites, paths, trajectories, and derived analytical objects. It opens their internal components or relations while maintaining or explicitly revising the identity of the same reference object.

```text
compressed occurrence or composite
DECOMPOSE
finer reconstruction of the same reference object
```

It never applies to abstract Δ–Ψ operator types as though those types were empirical containers.

```text
operator occurrence
→ decomposable where source-supported

composite
→ decomposable where source-supported

operator type
→ not decomposable
```

Returning to the compact chain, `P` may be reopened:

```text
P
DECOMPOSE
{A, B1, B2, C, relation set R'}
```

The result is not identical to the original source set. `B1/B2` may reflect a later, source-supported distinction; the earlier excluded branch remains excluded unless reopened; and the transition uncertainty remains inherited. The valid earlier `COMPOSE` result—that `A → B → C` forms a bounded ordered path—can remain locally admissible even if the `P → DECOMPOSE` handoff fails to preserve the same reference object or cannot support the proposed internal relation.

This is the required handoff-failure pattern:

```text
T1 local result valid
+ T1 → T2 handoff fails
→ T1 result preserved
→ chain claim fails or narrows at the handoff
```

<a id="54-5-retype-and-project-as"></a>

### 54.5 RETYPE and `PROJECT_AS`

#### C54-RETYPE-01 — RETYPE owns bounded cross-level functional projection

RETYPE specifies how an origin-typed object may be treated as performing a contextual function at another relative level or within another declared frame. It is not literal retyping of the source object and does not change PMS Base.

Its integrated role is:

```text
RETYPE
→ declares a cross-level functional reading
→ preserves origin type and source reference
→ binds the target function to a context and validity scope
```

RETYPE depends on source trace, functional continuity, contextual boundedness, counterfactual load testing, and an explicit Claim Ceiling. Where those burdens fail, the result may narrow to analogy, stop, fail, or yield a bounded `non_capture` result after adequate attempts.

#### C54-PROJECTAS-01 — `PROJECT_AS` preserves origin type, source trace, target context, and bounded target function

The canonical relation is:

```text
origin-typed source object
PROJECT_AS
bounded contextual target function
```

A valid record keeps separate:

- source reference object;
- origin type;
- source-supported load-bearing features;
- constitutive source-to-function relation;
- target context;
- bounded target function;
- counterfactual sensitivity result;
- validity scope and Claim Ceiling.

```text
target function
≠ origin type

derived function
≠ new PMS primitive
```

For a `COMPOSE → PROJECT_AS` chain:

```text
A, B, C
COMPOSE
P = bounded path object

P
PROJECT_AS
F = orientation frame-function in context C2
```

`P` retains its origin typing and historical load. `F` is not a new origin type of `P`; it is a contextual function warranted only if the declared source features carry that function within `C2`. A relevant source modification must be able to alter the projected result. Successful projection does not establish causality, universal validity, or authority.

<a id="54-6-limits-and-the-admissibility-band"></a>

### 54.6 LIMITS and the Admissibility Band

#### C54-LIMITS-01 — LIMITS controls the admissible range without becoming meta-authority

LIMITS defines when PATH, SUB, RETYPE, their operations, handoffs, and chains remain admissible. Its central bounded structure is:

```text
Praxeological Relevance Floor
─────────────────────────────
admissible transformation
─────────────────────────────
Praxeological Traceability Ceiling
```

Below the lower boundary lies:

```text
distinction without praxeological purchase
```

Above the upper boundary lies:

```text
abstraction without traceable load
```

LIMITS also controls TypeIntegrity, Reference and Functional Continuity, Counterfactual Sensitivity, reconstructive selection, the five Loss fields, alternatives, Source and Calibration Ceilings, Anti-Immunization, Stop, Claim Reduction, Failure, Non-Capture, and Authority Ceiling.

The Part does not produce source evidence, repair a missing relation, or authorize an application. Its audit can show that an integrated representation is technically coherent while still source-insufficient. For example, a complete chain record can have valid operation signatures, all required fields, and consistent links, yet exceed the Source Ceiling for the claimed constitutive relation. The model instance is technically coherent and substantively unwarranted for that stronger claim.

```text
formal integration
≠ empirical adequacy
≠ causal proof
≠ semantic sufficiency
```

<a id="54-7-integrated-transformation-logic"></a>

### 54.7 Integrated Transformation Logic

#### C54-CHAINLOGIC-01 — Every operation occurrence in a chain retains an independent record and failure possibility

Operations may form chains, but no chain is one undifferentiated transformation. For a chain `T1 → T2 → T3`, every occurrence retains:

- its own operation type and source object;
- declaration and justification;
- coordinates and target object;
- source basis and alternatives;
- five-field Loss record;
- local continuity and sensitivity findings;
- validity scope and Claim Ceiling;
- Stop, Failure, or Non-Capture possibility;
- local Output Class or unresolved disposition.

```yaml
operation_chain:
  T1:
    operation: COMPOSE
    record:
  handoff_T1_T2:
    inherited_burdens:
    continuity_result:
  T2:
    operation: DECOMPOSE
    record:
  handoff_T2_T3:
    inherited_burdens:
    continuity_result:
  T3:
    operation: PROJECT_AS
    record:
  chain_result:
```

```text
valid T1
≠ valid T2 automatically

valid T1 and valid T2
≠ valid handoff automatically

all local operations technically valid
≠ chain claim substantively admissible automatically
```

Some operation sequences may be unavailable or inadmissible. A proposed `DECOMPOSE` may lack a same-reference-object return path. A `PROJECT_AS` may have no constitutive source trace. A later `COMPOSE` may conceal prior Loss. The integrated architecture preserves those possibilities.

#### C54-HANDOFFS-01 — Handoffs preserve continuity and inherited burdens

A handoff is itself a claim-bearing relation between records. It must preserve or explicitly revise:

- reference identity;
- origin type and component identities;
- target function where relevant;
- temporal and functional continuity;
- source limitations;
- prior Loss and uncertainty;
- countercases and unresolved objections;
- prior Stop, Claim Reduction, Failure, or Non-Capture;
- re-entry conditions.

```text
new operation
= new testable claim

new handoff
= new testable continuity claim
```

A later success cannot erase an earlier handoff failure. A narrower re-entry can be admissible, but it is a new record with a new claim and must retain the earlier disposition in provenance.

#### C54-WP2-HANDOFF-01 — WP2 hands the complete four-Part chain architecture forward without final synthesis adjudication

WP2 completes SUB, RETYPE, LIMITS, operation-chain, and handoff synthesis. It selects no result for `SD/RP/LB/CL` or any other synthetic candidate. The pressure packet remains unchanged.

<a id="54-8-non-invertibility"></a>

### 54.8 Non-Invertibility

#### C54-NONINVERTIBILITY-01 — Composition, decomposition, and projection are non-invertible transformations

The integrated model preserves three invariants:

```text
DECOMPOSE(COMPOSE(X))
≠ X

COMPOSE(DECOMPOSE(X))
≠ X

PROJECT_AS(X)
≠ X as a new origin type
```

`COMPOSE` selects, orders, relates, and compresses. `DECOMPOSE` reconstructs selectively under a declared granularity and source basis. `PROJECT_AS` foregrounds a bounded contextual function. None is a neutral reversible encoding.

For the valid `COMPOSE → DECOMPOSE` example:

```text
X = {A, B, C, side branch D, unresolved transition relation u}

COMPOSE(X)
→ P = ordered object A → B → C
  D excluded for bounded claim
  u retained as uncertain

DECOMPOSE(P)
→ {A, B1, B2, C, reconstructed relation r}
```

The reopened structure is analytically related to `X`, but it is not `X`. It inherits the original selection, introduces a new granularity, may reconstruct relations unavailable at composition time, and cannot recover every excluded or irrecoverable element.

Non-invertibility is not analytical failure. It is the ordinary consequence of reconstructive selection and bounded claim formation.

#### C54-NOLOSSLESSRETURN-01 — No operation chain licenses a neutral, complete, or lossless return claim

```text
reopening
≠ inversion

same reference object
≠ identical representation

successful chain
≠ lossless return
```

A chain may preserve every structure necessary for a bounded claim and still remain non-identical to its source. Conversely, technical reversibility of a data format would not prove substantive reversibility of the reconstruction.

The five Loss fields remain attached at every operation and handoff. A later operation may disclose inherited Loss, but it cannot retroactively make the earlier transformation lossless.

<a id="54-9-the-shared-admissibility-logic"></a>

### 54.9 The Shared Admissibility Logic

#### C54-SHAREDADMISSIBILITY-01 — Shared admissibility requires PraxisPurchase, TraceableLoad, TypeIntegrity, and ContextualBoundedness

A compact integrated statement is:

```text
Admissible(T, X, C)
only where

PraxisPurchase
and
TraceableLoad
and
TypeIntegrity
and
ContextualBoundedness
```

This formulation is a synthesis of existing controls, not a replacement Rule or algorithm. The four terms are necessary summary burdens, not sufficient conditions for a positive route. Reference and Functional Continuity, Counterfactual Sensitivity, the exact Loss account, alternatives, Source and Calibration limits, Anti-Immunization, Stop and Non-Capture, and the Claim and Authority Ceilings remain separately applicable. Final route selection remains owned by the twelve-stage audit in Chapter 53.

```text
four summary burdens satisfied
≠ admissibility established automatically
```

- **PraxisPurchase** asks whether the transformation produces a claim-relevant distinction.
- **TraceableLoad** asks whether the result remains reconstructibly connected to load-bearing source structures.
- **TypeIntegrity** protects operator types, occurrence identities, composites, origin types, and derived functions from collapse.
- **ContextualBoundedness** restricts claims and functions to declared frames, granularities, relative levels, temporal scopes, and target contexts.

All four burdens are non-compensatory. Formal elegance, detail quantity, or success at another stage cannot repair a load-bearing failure.

```text
more structure
≠ more warrant
≠ more authority
```

#### C54-SENSITIVITYLOADTEST-01 — Counterfactual Sensitivity remains an additional load test without causal proof

Counterfactual Sensitivity asks whether a relevant modification of a claimed load-bearing source feature would alter the transformation result.

```text
relevant source modification
→ expected target-response pressure
```

The closed local vocabulary remains:

```text
strongly_sensitive
partially_sensitive
weakly_sensitive
insensitive
underdetermined
untestable
```

This test can expose label substitution, weak functional dependence, replaceability, underdetermination, or source limitation. It does not prove a causal mechanism or empirical alternate history.

```text
strongly_sensitive
≠ causal proof

insensitive
≠ robustness automatically

untestable
≠ claim immunity
```

<a id="54-10-the-integrated-model"></a>

### 54.10 The Integrated Model

#### C54-INTEGRATEDMODEL-01 — The compact model integrates PMS Base, four Parts, three operations, and bounded dispositions

The complete architecture can be summarized as follows:

```text
PMS Base
→ supplies the unchanged Δ–Ψ operator grammar

PATH / COMPOSE
→ forms temporally structured composite objects

SUB / DECOMPOSE
→ opens provisionally compressed occurrences and composites
  under finer, claim-bearing granularity

RETYPE / PROJECT_AS
→ assigns bounded contextual target functions
  while preserving origin type and source trace

LIMITS
→ controls relevance, traceability, continuity, sensitivity,
  selection, Loss, alternatives, source and calibration limits,
  Anti-Immunization, Stop, Failure, Non-Capture,
  Claim Ceiling, and Authority Ceiling
```

A compact formula is:

```text
PMS Base
+ {PATH, SUB, RETYPE, LIMITS}
+ {COMPOSE, DECOMPOSE, PROJECT_AS}
+ Shared Transformation Records
+ non-compensatory admissibility controls
→ bounded STRATA reconstruction discipline
```

Boundary caveat:

```text
integrated coherence
≠ completeness
≠ empirical validation
≠ full capture
≠ authority elevation
```

The integrated model preserves exactly ten Output Classes, sixteen Rules, twelve audit stages, six Counterfactual-Sensitivity values, and five Loss fields. It creates no new theory source, primitive, operation, schema family, or authority relation.

#### C54-NOAUTHORITYELEVATION-01 — Integration increases internal control, not ontology or authority

A structurally coherent chain can make source, operation, Loss, continuity, and claim boundaries easier to inspect. That gain is internal legibility.

It does not authorize:

- typing or diagnosing persons;
- ranking moral or political legitimacy;
- prescribing intervention or sanction;
- converting a configuration-level function into a person property;
- declaring PMS superior to rival models;
- treating technical execution as cross-domain validation.

An authority-elevation counterexample is a valid reconstruction of a recurrent role asymmetry that is then used to infer a stable personal essence or justify sanction. The structural reconstruction may remain bounded; the person-level and action-authorizing extension exceeds the Authority Ceiling and must be rejected, reduced, or stopped.

```text
more integrated structure
≠ more authority
```

#### C54-HANDOFF55-01 — Chapter 54 hands bounded positive provisions to Chapter 55 without pre-empting them

Chapter 55 receives an integrated but strictly bounded capability basis:

- explicit vertical transformation discipline;
- controlled temporal composition;
- granularity-controlled decomposition;
- bounded functional projection;
- operator-occurrence discipline;
- Loss accounting;
- competing reconstruction support;
- Counterfactual Sensitivity;
- bounded weighting and profile analysis;
- analogy discipline;
- Anti-Immunization;
- Stop and Non-Capture;
- increased internal legibility.

Chapter 54 does not yet define the final positive inventory, its reader-facing formulation, or its symmetry with Chapter 56. Nor does this handoff complete Chapters 55–57, integrated cases, Conclusion lock, Reference Freeze, Corpus Audit, Model Finalization, derivatives, Reader, or release.

#### C54-HANDOFF-01 — The Conclusion lock adjudicates the integrated model without creating a superior layer

All twenty-four Chapter-54 duties are represented across §§54.1–54.10. The exact four Parts, three operations, ten Output Classes, sixteen Rules, twelve audit stages, six sensitivity values, and five Loss fields remain unchanged.

```yaml
integrated_model_pressure_packet_at_final_lock:
  M0: rejected
  PD: passed
  FA: passed
  PC: passed
  SD: passed
  RP: passed
  LB: passed
  CL: passed
  NI: passed
  IM: passed
  selected_result: admissible_with_bounded_claim
```

The integrated model is accepted at final bounded method-and-artifact scope. It synthesizes the established Parts, operations, records, admissibility controls, chain logic, and non-invertibility without introducing new theory, a fourth operation, a superior PMS layer, or additional authority.

The earlier chapter-level `admissible_but_provisional` result remains historically correct for the pre-case and pre-lock evidence state. The final result is a new bounded lock test based on the completed RETYPE and LIMITS dependencies, all six required operation-chain families, direct instantiation of all ten Output Classes, and preserved Failure, Stop, Loss, and Non-Capture traces.

<a id="chapter-54-completion-boundary"></a>

#### Chapter 54 completion boundary

Chapter 54 is final-locked at bounded synthesis scope with `admissible_with_bounded_claim`. It hands Chapter 55 a stable integrated capability basis while preserving the distinction:

```text
integrated coherence
≠ completeness
≠ empirical validation
≠ full capture
≠ authority elevation
```
